## Editor MultiView — Persistence & Keyboard Shortcuts (Task 3)

**localStorage, not a server table, and per-story keys** (`editor-multiview-layout-{storyId}`, `editor-multiview-floating-{storyId}`) — this is the same call already made for every other piece of per-story *workspace UI* state in this app (current chapter, current tool, right-sidebar collapse, in `StoryContext`/`WorkspaceContext`), none of which live server-side. A layout is "how I like to look at this story," not story content — it doesn't need to survive a localStorage clear or sync across the Tailscale-connected machines CLAUDE.md cares about the way chapter text does.

**A hand-rolled structural validator (`layoutValidation.ts`), not zod, for the recursive tree shape.** zod is already a dependency and used elsewhere for API payloads, but a full schema for a recursive tagged-union tree (`EditorLayoutNode`) would be more ceremony than the equivalent handful of small `isRecord`/`isValidX` guard functions, given this only needs to run once per mount rather than validate a wide, evolving external API surface. Deliberately validates *shape* only (right fields, right types, `activePaneId` actually points at a pane that exists in the tree) — not whether a `chapterId` still resolves to a real chapter, since that's a data-freshness question already handled at render time, not a corruption question.

**A layout can now outlive the chapter it points at — restored from a previous session after that chapter was deleted — so `PaneTabContentView`'s `ChapterScopedContent` needed to stop conflating "still loading" with "doesn't exist."** Before persistence, an invalid `chapterId` in a tab was a near-impossible edge case (the in-memory tree never survived long enough to reference something stale). After persistence, it's a realistic scenario the first time. Fixed by checking `isLoading` explicitly and showing "This chapter no longer exists" once the query has genuinely resolved to nothing, instead of spinning forever.

**Both persistence effects are debounced (400ms), not saved on every state change.** `setSplitSizes` fires on every pixel of a resize-handle drag, and a floating panel's `updatePanel` fires on every `pointermove` while dragging/resizing — writing to localStorage on each of those would be wasteful. Split/tab/pane operations are infrequent enough that the debounce is imperceptible.

**Discovered while testing, not something a strict TS type could catch: `react-hotkeys-hook` ignores keydowns from contenteditable/form elements unless `enableOnContentEditable`/`enableOnFormTags` are explicitly set.** Since the Lexical editor *is* a contenteditable, and it's focused the entire time someone is actually writing, every shortcut in this feature would have been silently inert during the one activity MultiView shortcuts are for. Fixed by setting both options on every `useHotkeys` call in `useEditorMultiViewShortcuts.ts`.

**Also discovered while testing: bracket-key shortcuts (`mod+]`/`mod+[`) don't reliably match in this version of the library, including the app's own pre-existing chapter-navigation shortcuts — not just something new here.** `react-hotkeys-hook` matches non-modifier keys by normalizing `KeyboardEvent.code` (e.g. `"BracketRight"` → `"bracketright"`), but a literal `"]"` typed into a hotkey string normalizes to `"]"` — these never match. Rather than risk shipping shortcuts on the same shaky foundation, this feature's tab-cycling shortcuts use vi-style `mod+alt+j`/`mod+alt+k` instead of brackets — letters and arrow keys normalize consistently (confirmed: `"KeyJ"` → `"j"`, `"ArrowRight"` → `"arrowright"`, matching their string-form equivalents exactly). The existing `mod+]`/`mod+[` chapter-nav shortcuts were not touched — that's pre-existing, unrelated code — but this is worth knowing if they're ever reported as flaky.

**All ten shortcuts use `mod+alt+<key>`, a key space nothing else in the app touches**: `mod+alt+right`/`down` (split), `mod+alt+w` (close pane), `mod+alt+j`/`k` (cycle tabs in the active pane), and `mod+alt+l/t/o/p/n/b` (toggle Lorebook/Tags/Outline/POV/Notes/Beats as a tab in the active pane — same content-equality check as the "float" action, so "toggle" means close-if-open-else-open, not always-add). Only mounted while `EditorMultiView` itself is, so there's no separate "is the Editor tool active" guard needed — unmounting already handles it.

**Verified end-to-end in the real app**: built a 2-pane layout with a Lorebook tab, an Outline tool tab, and a floated-and-dragged Beats panel; reloaded the page and confirmed the exact tab structure and the floating panel's exact dragged pixel position both came back unchanged. Verified every shortcut individually after discovering and fixing the contenteditable-focus issue: split right/down, close pane, cycle tabs forward/backward, and all six panel-toggle shortcuts (confirmed each both opens and closes its tab on repeated presses). `tsc --noEmit` and a full-project `oxlint` sweep are both clean — same 21 pre-existing warnings, zero new ones. Test layout state was cleared from localStorage afterward so the next session starts from a clean single pane.

---

## Editor MultiView — Polish & Guide (Task 4)

**The resize handle's hover/drag feedback uses `react-resizable-panels`' own `data-resize-handle-state` attribute (`"inactive" | "hover" | "drag"`), not a hand-rolled `:hover` class.** The handle is a 1px line by design (matching the shadcn convention this component was already built on) with a wider invisible hit-area — coloring it only via CSS `:hover` would work for mouse hover but wouldn't reflect an active drag once the pointer leaves the 1px line (which happens constantly mid-drag, since the whole point of dragging is moving the mouse away from the divider's original position). The library already tracks and exposes exactly this state for exactly this reason; `data-[resize-handle-state=hover]:bg-primary/50` and `data-[resize-handle-state=drag]:bg-primary` just consume it.

**The pane-tab strip picked up a bottom-border accent on the active tab (`border-b-2 border-b-primary`), matching the same "active" visual language as the pane border Task 1 already established** (a colored line indicating "this is the thing your next action targets"), rather than relying on background-color contrast alone to carry that meaning.

**Floating panels now track drag/resize state as real React state (`useState`), not refs, specifically so a "being moved" style could react to it.** Task 2/3 used plain refs for the drag/resize anchor since nothing needed to *render* differently mid-drag — refs were the right call then (no unnecessary re-renders). Task 4 adds a visual cue (deepened shadow + slight opacity drop) while a panel is actively being dragged or resized, which requires a render on drag-start/drag-end, so the anchor moved to `useState`. The panel's position/size were already being updated via context state on every pointermove regardless, so this doesn't add a new render cost, just extends existing renders to also reflect drag state. The frontmost floating panel (last in `FloatingPanelsContext`'s array) also gets a subtle `ring-1 ring-primary/30` so it's visually clear which one "has focus" when several overlap.

**The floating panel's resize corner went from a fully invisible hotspot to a small `MoveDiagonal2` icon.** A 16×16px corner with only a `cursor-se-resize` CSS hint and no visible mark is easy to miss entirely, especially layered under a panel's own scrollable content — a small icon in the corner is a one-line fix for "how do I resize this."

**The Guide entry documents shortcuts using their `Ctrl+Alt` / `⌘+Option` form, not the library's `mod+alt+` syntax** — the audience for user-facing docs doesn't know or care what `react-hotkeys-hook` calls its modifier abstraction; they need to know what to physically press.

**Verified in the real app**: hovering and forcing `data-resize-handle-state` to `"hover"`/`"drag"` on a real handle confirmed the correct color escalation (idle → 50%-opacity primary → full primary) rather than a static color; the active tab's bottom-border accent renders correctly; a floated panel showed the frontmost ring and correctly toggled the dragging shadow/opacity state on pointerdown and cleared it on pointerup; the resize-corner icon renders and remains clickable. The new "MultiView" tab renders correctly in the Guide page alongside the existing seven, with working callouts, tables, and lists via the same MDX pipeline. `tsc --noEmit` (frontend + server) and a full-project `oxlint` sweep are both clean — the same 21 pre-existing warnings elsewhere, zero new ones.

---

## Story Outlining — Visual Outline Foundation (Task 1)

**A new top-level `outlineItems` table, not an extension of `chapters.outline`.** The Main Editor already has a per-chapter free-text outline field (`chapters.outline`, the "Outline" drawer). This feature is a different concern — a story-wide structural planning layer, built before or alongside prose, not a per-chapter scratch note — so it got its own table and its own "Outline" entry in the main left sidebar (next to Chapters/Lorebook), rather than replacing or extending the existing drawer. Both now coexist untouched.

**Exactly two fixed levels (chapter, scene), not an arbitrary-depth tree.** The task asks to "nest scenes/chapters," and a real arbitrary-depth outline tree is both a much bigger UI problem (generic tree DnD) and not what most outlining actually needs. `outlineItems.type` is `"chapter" | "scene"`, with `parentId` only ever pointing from a scene to its chapter — never chapter-to-chapter or scene-to-scene nesting further down. This mirrors the project's general bias (see the TTS/Humanizer/RAG decisions above) toward the concrete, tractable version of a feature over the maximally general one.

**`parentId` is a plain column, not a self-referencing FK.** This app runs with SQLite foreign-key enforcement off database-wide (see the `concreteBeats.characterId` comment) — a self-ref would need an awkward forward-declared callback for a constraint that isn't actually enforced at runtime anyway. Deleting a chapter row cascades to its scenes in application code (`routes/outline.ts`'s custom `DELETE /:id`, which overrides the generic CRUD delete by being registered first).

**Chapters reorder via drag; scenes reorder within their own chapter via a separate, chapter-scoped drag context; moving a scene to a *different* chapter is an explicit "Move to chapter" select, not a cross-context drag.** A single unified tree-DnD system that lets you drag a scene out of one chapter and into another mid-list is a substantially harder problem (tracking drop targets across nesting levels, recomputing two different parents' sibling orders from one gesture) for a feature whose actual requirement is just "reorder and nest." Two independent `dnd-kit` `SortableContext`s (top-level chapters; each chapter's own scenes) plus one small dedicated reparent control gets the same practical outcome — restructure freely — without that complexity, and follows the exact single-context sortable pattern already established by `ChaptersTool.tsx`.

**`OutlineItem.chapterId` optionally links an outline "chapter" row to a real `chapters` row, but nothing requires it.** Outlining can happen entirely before any prose exists (chapterId stays null throughout) or as a structural map layered onto chapters that are already written — the schema doesn't force either workflow.

**Bug found during verification: the generic CRUD helper's POST only auto-populates `id`/`createdAt`, not `updatedAt`, so every create 500'd against `NOT NULL constraint failed: outlineItems.updatedAt`.** Every other table with an `updatedAt` column (`notes`, `storyTtsPreferences`, `codexPendingChanges`'s `resolvedAt`) either doesn't require it NOT NULL or is written through a dedicated (non-generic-CRUD) route — `outlineItems` is the first table to combine "required `updatedAt`" with "created via the shared `createCrudRouter`." Fixed by giving `routes/outline.ts` its own `POST /` and `PUT /:id` overrides (registered first, same precedence trick as the `DELETE /:id` override above) that always stamp `updatedAt`, rather than loosening the column's NOT NULL constraint or teaching the generic CRUD helper about it for one table.

---

## Story Outlining — Character Arc Tracking (Task 2)

**A single junction table (`outlineItemCharacters`) with one free-text `arcNote` field, not a separate "arc" entity.** A character's "arc overview" is just the ordered sequence of outline items they're linked to, each with a short note on their development at that point — there's no separate arc record to create or manage, which keeps the mental model to "link a character to a beat in the outline, optionally say what happens to them here."

**`storyId` is denormalized onto the junction table** (derivable from `outlineItemId`, but stored directly anyway) — the same call `concreteBeats` already makes by storing both `storyId` and `chapterId` even though the latter implies the former. It lets the arc-overview query (`GET /outline/story/:storyId/arc/:characterId`) filter directly without a join back through `outlineItems`.

**The arc's "order" is computed server-side by flattening the two-level outline into reading order** (chapters by their own order, each immediately followed by its scenes by theirs) **and then filtering down to linked items** — not stored as a separate sequence number on the link itself. A link's position in the arc is always exactly wherever its outline item sits in the story, so there's nothing to keep in sync if the outline is later reordered.

---

## Story Outlining — AI-Assisted Outlining (Task 3)

**Same `source`/`status` convention as Concrete Beats, applied to outline rows instead of beats**: `source: "manual" | "ai_suggested"`, `status: "confirmed" | "pending" | "rejected"`. A generated chapter and its generated scenes are inserted immediately as `pending` rows (not held in-memory until the triggering request completes) — the same rationale as `detectConcreteBeatsForChapter`: generation can be slow, and suggestions need to survive a reload while awaiting review.

**Accepting a suggested chapter does not cascade-accept its suggested scenes.** Each row's `status` is independent, so a user can keep a generated chapter's shell while rejecting or hand-editing individual scenes — matches Concrete Beats' one-suggestion-at-a-time review model rather than an all-or-nothing accept.

**The generation prompt is intentionally open-ended (no fixed taxonomy to validate against), unlike `beatDetector`'s strict "concrete only" system prompt.** An outline chapter/scene is free-form narrative planning — there's no equivalent of Concrete Beats' seven-type allowlist to enforce; the only structural validation is that returned JSON parses and each item has a non-empty title.

**Context sent to the model includes both already-written chapters (from `chapters`) and already-planned outline chapters (from `outlineItems` itself), separately labeled**, so a second "Generate with AI" pass doesn't propose something that duplicates either what's already prose or what's already planned but unwritten.

---

## Story Outlining — Polish & Guide (Task 4)

**No virtualization library added for "large outlines."** Given this app's actual scale (a personal/small-team writing tool, not a platform with thousands of outline rows per story), the performance answer is grouping the flat query result into the two-level render shape exactly once per query result via `useMemo` (`groupOutlineItems`), rather than recomputing it per row or reaching for `react-window`/similar. Row components (`OutlineChapterCard`, `OutlineSceneRow`) are otherwise plain — no premature `React.memo` micro-optimization where the actual cost is the one grouping pass, not re-renders of individual rows.

**Guide entry follows the exact structure of the Concrete Beats guide** (numbered sections, a comparison callout distinguishing it from a similarly-named existing feature, a pending-suggestion action table, a troubleshooting table) — consistent doc shape across features that share the same manual/AI-suggested review pattern.

**Verified end-to-end in the real app**: created chapters and a nested scene through the actual dialogs (this is what surfaced the `updatedAt` bug above), linked a character to a chapter via the picker popover and confirmed it in the Character Arcs tab with an editable, saves-on-blur arc note, confirmed the "Generate with AI" soft-fail path shows the exact `outline_generation`-specific message when no provider is configured, seeded a pending AI-suggestion row directly and confirmed the dashed-amber-border/"AI Suggested" treatment plus "Reject All Suggestions (N)" count plus Accept flipping it to a normal confirmed entry, and confirmed deleting a chapter cascades to its scene and character link while leaving sibling chapters untouched (checked directly via the API, not just visually). Reorder/reparent were verified by calling `PATCH /outline/reorder` directly rather than simulating a real pointer-drag gesture — `dnd-kit`'s `PointerSensor` needs genuine pointer capture that this session's browser-automation tooling can't reliably produce, so the endpoint's correctness was confirmed at the API level instead of via an actual drag. The new "Outline" tab in the Guide page renders correctly (confirmed via keyboard activation after discovering the automation tooling's synthetic clicks don't reliably land in the dense 9-column tab-trigger grid — a tooling limitation, not an app bug, since the 2-tab Outline/Character-Arcs tabs on the feature page itself did register real clicks fine). All test data was deleted afterward via direct API calls, confirmed empty. `tsc --noEmit` (frontend + server) and a full-project `oxlint` sweep are both clean — the same 22 pre-existing warnings elsewhere (one of which, `server/db/schema.ts`'s `max-lines`, was already over the limit before this feature added its two tables — not a new violation), zero new ones.

---

## Deep Writing Sessions — Focus Mode Foundation (Task 1)

**A new "Start a Writing Session" flow, distinct from the pre-existing toolbar "Maximize Editor" button, not a replacement for it.** The toolbar already had a `Maximize`/`Minimize` icon button (tooltip was literally "Focus mode") that collapses the workspace's left and right side panels via `WorkspaceContext.isMaximised`. That's a much shallower layout toggle than what this feature asks for ("hide sidebars, minimal UI, full-screen editor" plus timers/goals/theming/wellbeing) — so rather than overload one button with two different meanings, the existing button's tooltip was renamed to "Maximize Editor" / "Restore Sidebars" (its actual behavior, unchanged) and a new `Focus` icon button opens `StartFocusSessionDialog` right next to it. A session, once started, also implies maximised layout (`isMaximised || sessionActive` is passed to `EditorMultiView`), so the two features compose rather than conflict.

**`FocusSessionProvider` is mounted once in `Workspace.tsx`, above both `Sidebar`/`TopBar` (which it hides) and `StoryEditor` (which reads it to hide its own right-rail and render the HUD)** — the common ancestor of both subtrees that need to react to session state. Chrome-hiding is deliberately scoped to `sessionActive && currentTool === "editor"`: switching to another tool (e.g. checking Lorebook) mid-session restores the chrome there rather than stranding the user in a chrome-less view of an unrelated tool, while the session itself (timer, word tracking) keeps running in the background regardless of which tool is on screen.

**Live word count for the goal-tracking HUD comes from a new tiny cross-component store (`chapterWordCountStore.ts`), not `ToolbarContext`.** `ToolbarContext`'s `wordCount` already exists and updates live, but it's scoped to a single Lexical composer tree (one per open MultiView pane) — unreachable from a HUD that renders outside any one pane's component tree without threading pane-id plumbing through everything in between. `WordCountPlugin` (already computing and debouncing word count every 500ms) now also reports into this `useSyncExternalStore`-based module-level `Map<chapterId, count>`, following the exact pattern `useAutoDetectBeats.ts` established for a single boolean, generalized to a per-key value. A session snapshots the *starting* count via a plain (non-hook) `getChapterWordCount` read the instant it begins, so "words written" is always relative to session start, not the chapter's total.

**A session is scoped to whichever chapter was open when it started (snapshotted, not live-tracked against "whatever the current chapter is now"), and does not persist across a page reload.** Both are deliberate scope decisions: chapter-scoping avoids needing pane-id plumbing (see above) and matches "one session, one piece of writing"; not persisting active-session state (unlike Editor MultiView's layout, which *does* persist) reflects that a session is closer to a live sprint/timer than to "how I like to look at this story" — reloading mid-session ending it is the expected, simple behavior, not a bug to fix later.

---

## Deep Writing Sessions — Adaptive Theming (Task 2)

**Three new themes (Warm, Low Contrast, Night) are CSS-variable overrides scoped to a wrapper class on the editor's root div, never applied to `<html>`/`<body>`, and a fourth option ("Default") is simply the absence of a class.** This means picking a focus theme can never fight the app-wide `ThemeProvider`/`ThemeToggle` (light/dark/system) — the two systems don't know about each other, by design. Each theme class redefines the *same* variable names `index.css` already defines (`--background`, `--foreground`, `--card`, `--primary`, etc.), so every existing Tailwind utility class (`bg-background`, `text-muted-foreground`, ...) and every `PlaygroundEditorTheme.css` rule (which already reads `hsl(var(--foreground))` etc.) picks up the new palette automatically — no new theme-aware components needed anywhere else in the editor.

**Smooth transitions come from plain `transition: background-color/color` on the themed subtree, not from animating the CSS custom properties themselves.** CSS custom properties don't animate directly without `@property` type registration (patchy browser support) — but `hsl(var(--background))` *resolves* to a concrete color at computed-style time, and transitioning the resulting `background-color`/`color` property works perfectly well across a class swap. The transition rule is scoped to descendants of the three theme classes only (`.focus-theme-warm *`), not the whole app, so it costs nothing outside an active session.

**The theme switcher lives in two places (Start Session dialog, in-session HUD) sharing one `FocusThemeSwitcher` component** — changing your mind about the theme mid-session doesn't require ending the session first.

---

## Deep Writing Sessions — Wellbeing Features (Task 3)

**All three wellbeing knobs (break reminders, session-length check-in, content check-in) default OFF and are configured per-session from the same dialog as the writing goal** — nothing about wellbeing messaging appears unless explicitly turned on, matching this app's established bias (see the Concrete Beats auto-detect toggle, off by default for the same reason: an opt-in feature respects the user's own judgment rather than assuming everyone wants to be interrupted).

**Break reminders recur (rescheduling immediately when one fires, regardless of when — or whether — the user acts on it); the session-length check-in and goal-reached celebration each fire exactly once per session.** A break reminder is inherently periodic ("every 30 minutes"); a session-length check-in is a single, one-time "how are we doing" moment, not a recurring nag, and firing it more than once per session would undercut the "supportive rather than intrusive" requirement.

**Reaching a Timer or Word Count goal shows a dismissible celebration (`GoalReachedToast`), not an automatic session end.** The goal is a target to notice, not a leash — "Time's up!" / "Word goal reached!" offers **Keep Writing** or **End Session**, same as the session-length check-in, and typing is never interrupted to show it.

**"Content check-in" was interpreted as a private, writer-facing wellbeing nudge, not a reader-facing content-tagging/warning system.** Given this fork's explicit focus on long-form erotic/psychological fiction (see CLAUDE.md), a literal reader-facing "content warning" feature would mean a new content-tagging data model on chapters/stories — a much larger, unrelated feature with no existing schema support. Read narrowly as "give the *writer* a way to check in with themselves while working through emotionally intense material," it needed no new persisted data at all: it's a single boolean that adds one supportive, dismissible line to the existing break-reminder and session-limit toasts. This is a scope judgment call, flagged here explicitly in case the intent was actually the broader reader-facing feature — that would be a separate, considerably larger effort (new schema, new Lorebook/chapter UI, no wellbeing-toggle framing).

**Toasts are custom JSX content via `react-toastify`'s `toast(<Component />, options)`, not the plain-string `toast.success()`/`toast.error()` calls used everywhere else in this app.** This is the first place in the codebase needing toast content richer than a message plus a color (buttons for Snooze/Dismiss/Keep-Going/End-Session) — `react-toastify` already supports this natively, so no new toast/notification library was needed, just a different call shape than existing call sites use.

---

## Deep Writing Sessions — Polish & Guide (Task 4)

**One new keyboard shortcut, `Ctrl+Alt+F` (`⌘+Option+F` on Mac), toggling a session on/off using the last-used config** — same `mod+alt+<letter>` key space Editor MultiView's shortcuts already established (documented there as the space to use since most `mod+<key>` combos are taken elsewhere); "f" for focus was free. Uses the same `enableOnContentEditable`/`enableOnFormTags` options Editor MultiView's shortcuts needed to fire while the Lexical editor itself is focused.

**The HUD fades and slides in on session start (`animate-in fade-in slide-in-from-top-2`, via the already-installed `tailwindcss-animate` plugin) rather than appearing instantly** — consistent with "polish all UI elements... calm, focused" framing; an abrupt UI pop-in is a small but real jolt right at the moment the whole point is to reduce distraction.

**Guide entry follows the same structure as Concrete Beats/Outline** (a comparison callout distinguishing this from the pre-existing "Maximize Editor" button, numbered sections, action tables, a troubleshooting table) — consistent doc shape across every feature in this app that pairs a UI affordance with optional/configurable behavior.

**Two real bugs were found and fixed during verification, not just discovered-and-documented.** First: the wrapper div that carries a focus theme's class (`focus-theme-warm` etc.) didn't itself declare `bg-background`/`text-foreground` — so while every *descendant* using a Tailwind `bg-*`/`text-*` utility correctly re-resolved the overridden CSS variables (custom properties inherit; `background-color`/`color` don't, but they're recomputed fresh wherever a class asks for them), the wrapper's *own* background/text color was just passively inheriting `body`'s already-computed color from *outside* the override's scope — so the editor surface itself never actually changed color, even though everything nested inside it should have. Fixed by adding explicit `bg-background text-foreground` to the same div that carries the theme-override class, forcing it to resolve its own colors using the locally-overridden variables. Second: `StoryEditor.tsx` crossed the project's 250-line `max-lines` lint limit once the HUD/theme/shortcut wiring was added — fixed by extracting the right-rail tool sidebar, its five drawers/sheet, and the mobile floating-menu equivalent into a new `EditorToolsPanel.tsx`, leaving `StoryEditor.tsx` as just layout/session wiring.

**A pre-existing, unrelated bug was discovered (not fixed) during verification: `WordCountPlugin`'s word count reads as `0` for at least the demo story's chapters, regardless of this feature's changes.** Confirmed pre-existing (not introduced here) by temporarily reverting this feature's only edit to that file via `git stash` and reproducing the identical `Words: 0` — the bug is in the plugin's existing traversal logic (it reads `editor._editorState._nodeMap` directly rather than using Lexical's normal `$getRoot()`-based accessors), which this feature's code doesn't touch. Because of this, the Word Count goal type's live progress number could not be visually verified end-to-end in the browser — verification instead used the Timer goal type (which has no dependency on word counting) to confirm the HUD, progress bar, and goal-reached celebration all work correctly, and the word-count *plumbing this feature adds* (`chapterWordCountStore.ts`) was verified by code review and type-checking rather than a live number. This pre-existing bug is flagged separately for its own fix, out of scope here.

**Verified end-to-end in the real app**: started a session from the toolbar's new Focus icon with a Timer goal (1 minute, for a fast test), Warm theme, and both Break Reminders and Content Check-in enabled; confirmed the left sidebar, top bar, and right-rail tool panel all disappeared and the floating HUD appeared showing live elapsed time and time remaining. Switched the HUD's theme selector through Warm → Night → Low Contrast and confirmed (via computed-style inspection, not just visual impression) that both the editor surface's background-color and text color actually changed to the correct values for each theme — this is what caught the inheritance bug above. Let the 1-minute timer run out for real and confirmed the "Time's up!" `GoalReachedToast` appeared with working **Keep Writing** (dismisses the toast, session continues — confirmed the HUD was still present and counting afterward) and **End Session** (confirmed via a second session: sidebar/top-bar/right-rail all fully restored). Verified `Ctrl+Alt+F` both starts a session (using the last-used persisted config — confirmed the Timer/Warm/toggle choices survived a full page reload) and ends one on a second press. Cleaned up test text typed into the demo chapter during verification, confirmed via direct API check that the chapter's saved content matches its pre-verification state exactly. `tsc --noEmit` (frontend + server) and a full-project `oxlint` sweep are both clean — the same 22 pre-existing warnings elsewhere, zero new ones (the transient `StoryEditor.tsx` max-lines warning introduced mid-feature was fixed via the extraction above, not left as a "pre-existing, not mine" excuse).

---

## RAG Index Freshness (Lorebook Auto-Index, Reindex-on-Approve) & Agent Framework Direction

**Two real staleness gaps found while auditing the RAG pipeline for a strategy/direction discussion, both fixed.** Chapters already auto-index on save (`SaveChapterContent/index.tsx`'s 8s debounce → `ragApi.indexChapter`), but `ragIndexService.ts`'s `indexLorebookEntry` was only reachable through the manual `POST /api/rag/index/lorebook/:entryId` route — nothing called it after a lorebook entry was created or edited through the normal CRUD form, so entries silently never entered the index unless someone hit that route by hand. Fixed by calling it (fire-and-forget, via the `attemptPromise` convention already used elsewhere for non-blocking calls) from `lorebook.ts`'s custom POST and PUT handlers, mirroring the chapter pattern exactly.

**Approving a Codex proposal also left the index stale.** `codexService.ts`'s `approvePendingChange` applies the proposed fields to the live lorebook entry and snapshots the result, but never told the RAG index the entry's content had changed — a hybrid search run immediately after approval could still surface the pre-approval description. Fixed with the same fire-and-forget `indexLorebookEntry` call, placed inside the `if (hasFields(changes))` branch so a no-op approval (snapshot-only, no actual field changes) doesn't trigger a pointless reindex.

**Guardrail recorded, not acted on: no manual "run scan" UI gets built going forward.** `ragScanner.ts` is backend-complete (`scan/chapter`, `scan/story`, persisted `ragScanIssues`, progress polling) but has zero frontend — confirmed nothing in `src/` calls any `/api/rag/scan/*` or `/api/rag/issues/*` route. Building a manual trigger button now would be thrown away the moment background scanning (below) exists, so it stays deliberately unbuilt until the agent framework owns it.

**Known gap found, not fixed here (pre-existing, out of scope for this pass): deleting a lorebook entry doesn't clean up its RAG index.** Verification below deleted a test entry via the normal `DELETE /api/lorebook/:id` route (generic CRUD, no custom delete handler in `lorebook.ts`) and found its `ragChunks`/`vec_chunks`/`fts_chunks` rows were left behind — `removeEntityFromIndex` is never called from the delete path. Cleaned up manually for this test; flagged here as a real follow-up (the fix is the same shape as the two above: call `removeEntityFromIndex("lorebook_entry", id)` from the delete handler) rather than bundled into this pass, which was scoped to freshness-on-write, not the delete path.

**Agent Framework — direction recorded now, not implemented.** The longer-term goal is a background layer that keeps the RAG index fresh, runs scanners without manual triggers, does light DB housekeeping, and accumulates a per-writer/per-project memory the assistant can draw on across sessions. Rather than invent new primitives, the design deliberately reuses what's already in the codebase:
- **One `agentJobs` table, generalizing the existing `ragScans` precedent.** `ragScans` (`status: running|completed|failed` + progress polling, driven by a fire-and-forget async IIFE in `ragScanner.ts`'s `scanStory`) is already the only async/background-job pattern anywhere in `server/`. The generalized table would carry `jobType` (`rag_scan_chapter`, `reconcile_index`, `prune_history`, future types), a polymorphic `storyId`/`entityId` scope, `payload`/`result` JSON, and timestamps — `ragScans`/`ragScanIssues` becoming one `jobType` among several instead of a one-off table.
- **An in-process poller (`jobRunner.ts`), not new infra.** No queue library, `worker_threads`, or cron exists anywhere in this codebase today, and the app is a single Docker container against one SQLite file (`docker-compose.yml`: 2 services, app + languagetool) — a second process contending for the same DB file adds risk with no real benefit at this scale. The runner would claim `queued` rows via an atomic status-flip and requeue anything left `running` after a crash/restart, which the current fire-and-forget `ragScanner.scanStory` pattern can't do (a scan dies silently if the server restarts mid-run).
- **Agent memory rides the existing RAG pipeline instead of a parallel one.** Extending `RagEntityType` (currently `"lorebook_entry" | "chapter"` in `ragRepository.ts`) with `"agent_memory"` means distilled facts about a project or writer become ordinary `ragChunks` rows, retrievable through the same `hybridSearch` — no new retrieval mechanism to build or maintain. Cross-project memory would reuse the nullable-`storyId`-means-global convention `prompts`/`aiChats` already established, rather than a new global/scoped split.
- **Endpoint routing needs no new mechanism.** `buildClientForFeature`'s per-feature override system (already resolving distinct endpoints for `rag_scanner`, `embedding`, `humanizer`, etc., per CLAUDE.md's "writing model and scanner model can run on different machines" requirement) just needs new `FeatureKey`s added when the runner is actually built — zero infrastructure changes.

None of `agentJobs`, `jobRunner.ts`, or the `agent_memory` entity type exist yet. This section records the committed shape so later work doesn't build something incompatible with it — it is not a claim that any of it is built.

**Verified end-to-end against the real running dev server and DB, not just by reading the code**: logged in as the existing dev account, created a lorebook entry via `POST /api/lorebook` and confirmed a `ragChunks` row appeared for it (`entityType: "lorebook_entry"`, `embeddedAt` set) with no manual index call; updated its description via `PUT /api/lorebook/:id` and confirmed `ragChunks.contentHash` changed to a new value; proposed a Codex change via `POST /api/codex/:entryId/propose` and approved it via `POST /api/codex/pending/:id/approve`, and confirmed `ragChunks.contentHash` changed a third time to reflect the approved (not the originally-proposed-but-superseded) content. This is also what surfaced the delete-path gap noted above. All test data (the lorebook entry, its snapshot/pending-change rows, and its orphaned RAG chunks) was removed afterward via direct API calls plus a manual cleanup pass for the chunks the delete route doesn't touch — confirmed empty via a direct query.

## Lorebook Image Support — Manual Upload & AI Generation (Both Built)

**Original decision:** Lorebook entries had no image field — name/description/category/tags/metadata/codexState were all text/JSON. The user asked about adding portrait/reference images (prompted by importing a PDF character sheet whose actual content is ~1 page of text plus ~67 pages of reference images, which the import feature correctly ignores — see "Multi-format Lorebook Import" above). Built as two capabilities, in order:

1. **Manual image upload — built.**
2. **AI-generated images — built.** The AI Settings model catalogue already lists real Grok image-generation models (`grok-imagine-image`, `grok-imagine-image-quality`) once Grok OAuth is connected. The integration shape is genuinely different from every existing AI feature here — `buildClientForFeature`'s whole pattern (`server/services/aiClientFactory.ts`) is built around `client.chat.completions.create(...)`; xAI's image generation is a different, separate endpoint (`POST {baseURL}/images/generations`, confirmed against xAI's own docs rather than assumed, since getting the shape wrong would waste a real billed call) not exposed through the `openai` npm SDK's typed `images.generate()` at all. An explicit user-triggered action ("Generate from Description" button reading the entry's own saved description as the prompt), matching this app's established pattern for every other AI action (Humanizer, Beat Detection, Outline Generation are all opt-in buttons, never automatic) — not something that fires on save automatically.

### Manual upload — implementation

**Storage lives under the existing SQLite DB's Docker volume, not a new one.** New `data/uploads/lorebook/` directory, resolved the same way `DATABASE_PATH` already is in `server/db/client.ts` (`process.env.UPLOADS_DIR || path.join(process.cwd(), "data", "uploads", "lorebook")`) — `docker-compose.yml`'s `${DATA_PATH:-./data}:/app/data` volume already covers it, zero new Docker config needed. New `server/services/lorebookImageStorage.ts` owns filename generation (`{randomUUID}.{ext}`, extension derived from an allowlisted mimetype — jpeg/png/webp/gif only), saving, and best-effort deletion.

**Served through an authenticated API route (`GET /api/lorebook/:id/image`), not a public `express.static` mount.** `server/index.ts` only ever serves static files for the production SPA build. A bare public static mount for uploads would reopen the exact gap the Basic Login entry above already called out and closed ("protecting only two route groups... would be a false sense of security") — this is a private writing tool, character portraits shouldn't be reachable without a session. Browsers send the session cookie automatically on same-origin `<img src>` requests, so this needed no special frontend handling — confirmed by testing `<img>` tags in the real app load correctly while a direct unauthenticated `curl` to the same URL correctly gets a 401. `res.sendFile` handles Content-Type detection automatically from the file extension, no manual mimetype map needed.

**Three new routes in `server/routes/lorebook.ts`** (which already had a multer instance and a `customRoutes` extension point from the earlier document-import work): `POST /:id/image` (upload/replace — deletes the previous file first if one exists, so replacing never orphans a file), `GET /:id/image` (stream), `DELETE /:id/image` (remove without deleting the entry). Also **a custom `DELETE /:id` override** (registered in `customRoutes`, taking precedence over the generic CRUD delete per `server/lib/crud.ts`'s "custom routes first" ordering — same trick `server/routes/outline.ts`'s own delete override already uses) that deletes the image file before the row, so deleting an entry doesn't orphan its image — built in now rather than becoming a second version of the already-documented "delete doesn't clean up RAG chunks" gap.

**Deferred-until-save on the frontend, same pattern `codexState` already established.** `CreateEntryForm` gained `imageFile?: File | null` — `undefined` means no change, a `File` means a new upload, `null` means explicit removal — applied in `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`'s `handleSubmit` after the base entry create/update succeeds, using the same `entryId = entry?.id ?? randomUUID()` sequencing already in place for Codex state. New `ImageUploadField.tsx` shows the existing image (via the API route) or a local `URL.createObjectURL` preview of a newly-picked file, with Upload/Replace/Remove controls; `LorebookEntryList.tsx` cards show a small thumbnail when set. One image per entry, no resizing/compression on upload (display-time CSS scaling only) — matches this app's "avoid new dependencies" bias (`sharp` or similar would be new, heavier tooling for a v1); both are easy to extend later without a schema rework.

**`lorebookApi` was extracted into its own `lorebookClient.ts`** (same reasoning as `codexApi`/`ttsApi`/etc. before it — `client.ts` was already over the project's line limit and this feature was about to add two more functions to it) while adding `uploadImage`/`removeImage`/`imageUrl`.

**Verified end-to-end in the real app, not just via curl**: uploaded a real 1x1 test PNG through the actual `ImageUploadField` control (simulated file selection + native change event, not a direct API call), confirmed the object-URL preview and Replace/Remove controls appeared immediately, saved, and confirmed via direct DB/filesystem checks that the file landed on disk and `imageFilename` persisted. Reloaded and confirmed both the card grid thumbnail and the editor's preview independently re-fetched and displayed the same image (`naturalWidth`/`naturalHeight` checked to confirm real image data loaded, not a broken `<img>`). Clicked Remove and saved again, confirmed the column cleared and the file was deleted from disk. Separately verified via direct API calls: replacing an image deletes the old file (not just adds a new one), and deleting an entry entirely removes its image file too. `tsc --noEmit` (frontend + server) and a full oxlint sweep are clean.

### AI generation — implementation

**New `FeatureKey`: `"image_generation"`** (`src/types/aiSettings.ts`), routed through the *existing* per-feature editor (`FeatureEndpointsCard.tsx` — see "Per-Feature Grok Routing Gap" below) — no new settings UI needed, it already lists every `FeatureKey`. Scoped to Grok only for this pass: no other configured provider (local/openai/openrouter) has image-generation models surfaced anywhere in this app, so a generic multi-provider system would be speculative; the per-feature-endpoint system already gives this room to grow later without a redesign.

**Reused `buildClientForFeature("image_generation")` as-is** rather than a parallel resolution path — it already handles per-feature override + global fallback + Grok OAuth token refresh. The returned `OpenAI` instance's `.baseURL`/`.apiKey` are real public properties (confirmed by reading the SDK's own TypeScript source, not assumed), so a raw `fetch()` to `${client.baseURL}/images/generations` reuses all of that resolution work for free.

**New `server/services/grokImageService.ts`**: `generateLorebookImage(entryId): Promise<void>` — looks up the entry, resolves the connection, POSTs the entry's own `description` as the prompt with `response_format: "b64_json"`, decodes the base64 response into a `Buffer`, sniffs its actual format from magic bytes (new `sniffImageMimetype` in `lorebookImageStorage.ts` — xAI doesn't guarantee a specific format the way a browser's `File.type` does for manual uploads), then calls the *existing* `saveLorebookImage`/`deleteLorebookImage` exactly like the manual-upload route does — same replace-deletes-old-file behavior, same DB update. New route `POST /lorebook/:id/generate-image` in `server/routes/lorebook.ts`'s `customRoutes`, no request body needed.

**Frontend, same deferred-until-save shape `imageFile` already established.** `CreateEntryForm` gained `generateImageOnSave?: boolean` — setting it clears any pending `imageFile` selection and vice versa (generating and manually picking a file in the same edit session are mutually exclusive; last action wins). `ImageUploadField.tsx` gained a "Generate from Description" button, disabled when the description field is empty, with a Sparkles icon placeholder and a "Will generate a new image from the description when you save" hint shown while pending. `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`'s `handleSubmit` gained a branch calling `lorebookApi.generateImage(entryId)` after the existing image-file branch, plus a dedicated `isSubmitting` state so the Save button reflects the whole async submit (generation can take several seconds), not just the create/update mutations' own `isPending`.

**Fixed a real gap while touching this code anyway:** `handleSubmit`'s `if (error) { /* Error toast handled by mutation */ }` block was stale for the codex and image calls specifically — those are raw API calls, not `useMutation`s with `onError` toasts, so a Codex-state or image failure previously failed silently. Replaced with a real `toastCRUD.saveError("entry", error)` call (reusing the existing standardized CRUD-toast helper in `utils/toastUtils.ts` rather than a bespoke message).

**Verified end-to-end in the real app**: clicked "Generate from Description" on a real entry with a real 613-character description, confirmed the Sparkles placeholder and hint text appeared immediately, saved, and confirmed via network inspection that `POST /lorebook/:id/generate-image` fired correctly. No `image_generation`-specific feature endpoint was configured in this dev environment, so `buildClientForFeature` correctly fell back to the global default (confirmed directly via a script: resolved to the local LM Studio endpoint, `http://localhost:1234/v1`) — the resulting `fetch` to a local server with no `/images/generations` support failed cleanly, and the fix above surfaced it as a real, visible toast (`"Failed to save entry: fetch failed"`) instead of the previous silent swallow, alongside the base entry's own separate "updated successfully" toast confirming the rest of the save still completed normally. This is the intended, correct error path when no Grok connection is configured for this feature yet — a real generation call was deliberately not spent verifying the success path, matching this session's established practice of not spending billed API calls just to prove a request shape already confirmed against the provider's own docs. `tsc --noEmit` (frontend + server) and a full oxlint sweep are clean — zero new warnings or errors in any touched file.

## Per-Feature Grok Routing Gap (Fixed) & the Missing Feature-Endpoint Editor UI

**Bug found while diagnosing a user report, now fixed.** A user configuring the `document_import` feature (see "Multi-format Lorebook Import" above) to use Grok got the same generic `"Connection error."` they'd see from an unreachable local LM Studio endpoint. Traced to two compounding gaps in `server/services/aiClientFactory.ts`:

1. **Per-feature overrides couldn't be set to Grok at all.** `FeatureProvider` (`src/types/aiSettings.ts`) was `"local" | "openai" | "openrouter" | "grok"`, and `clientFromEndpoint`'s switch already supported a `"grok"` case — but `server/routes/admin.ts`'s validation for writing a per-feature override only accepted `"local" | "openai" | "openrouter"`, rejecting `"grok"` before it ever reached the factory.
2. **The global default's provider priority is hardcoded `local → openai → openrouter → grok`** (`clientFromGlobalSettings`, left as-is — see below for why), checked unconditionally — if `localApiUrl`/`defaultLocalModel` are populated at all (even stale/unreachable ones left over from earlier testing), local wins regardless of what the user actually wants. `document_import` had no override, fell into this resolver, and silently hit the same dead local endpoint every time — never actually attempting Grok.

**Deeper finding while investigating the fix: there was no UI to set a per-feature override at all**, for any feature, ever. `PreferencesSidebar.tsx`'s "Feature Endpoints" card only displayed current overrides read-only; its "Configure Endpoints" button opened `/settings` in a new tab, but `SettingsPage.tsx` had zero editing UI for them. The backend (`PUT /api/admin/feature-endpoints/:feature`) and the frontend API client (`featureEndpointsApi.setFeature`) both worked and had since the per-feature-endpoint system was first built — nothing in the app ever called them. So even fixing the Grok validation bug alone wouldn't have solved the user's actual problem.

**Fix, both pieces, done together (user chose "build the real editor" over "just fix the global default" when asked):**
- `FeatureProvider` extended to `"local" | "openai" | "openrouter" | "grok" | "grok-oauth"` — `"grok-session"` deliberately excluded: it isn't a simple `new OpenAI({baseURL, apiKey})` client (it proxies through grok.com server-side via a bespoke SSE conversion, see `grokSessionClient.ts`) and is already flagged unofficial/fragile in the UI, so it doesn't fit this system's shape without inventing a second one just for it.
- `clientFromEndpoint` (`aiClientFactory.ts`) is now async and takes the full settings row, handles a new `"grok-oauth"` case via `getFreshGrokOAuthToken` (transparently refreshes and persists a rotated token, same refresh-then-persist pattern `server/routes/ai.ts`'s `GET /settings` already used for the settings-page display — now factored so `aiClientFactory.ts` shares the same behavior instead of inventing a second copy), and fixes a related latent gap where `apiKey ?? ""` never actually fell back to the global key for openai/openrouter/grok per-feature overrides despite the type's own doc comment saying it should.
- `admin.ts`'s two provider-validation checks now check against a shared `VALID_FEATURE_PROVIDERS` list instead of hardcoded `!==` chains.
- New `FeatureEndpointsCard.tsx` in Settings (placed after the provider connection cards): one row per `FeatureKey`, a provider select (including "Global default" to clear the override), a model combobox scoped to that provider's cached models, an optional API URL field for `"local"` specifically (the actual "run this on a different machine" case this whole system exists for), and a Save button. New `useFeatureEndpointsQuery`/`useSetFeatureEndpointMutation`/`useRemoveFeatureEndpointMutation` hooks. The dashboard's read-only `PreferencesSidebar` display and this new editor were wired to the same TanStack Query key (`aiSettingsKeys.featureEndpoints()`, previously a private key only `useDashboardData.ts` knew about) so saving here now correctly refreshes that summary too.
- **Deliberately left unchanged:** `clientFromGlobalSettings`'s hardcoded fallback order. The user chose the per-feature-editor fix specifically over a "just add a global preferred-provider setting" alternative, so the global chain still means "whatever's configured first in local → openai → openrouter → grok wins" — the new editor is how you route around that per feature, not a change to what "the default" means.
- `SettingsPage.tsx` was already over the project's 250-line lint limit before this session touched it again; extracted its "Local Models" card into `LocalModelsCard.tsx` (which also absorbed the `openSections`/`toggleSection` state that existed solely for that one card's collapsible) while adding the new card, landing the file at 221 lines instead of pushing it further over.

**Verified end-to-end for real, not just against a mock**: set `document_import` → Grok (xAI OAuth) → `grok-4.3` through the actual Settings UI, confirmed the "Feature endpoint saved" toast and the persisted override via a direct API check, then confirmed `buildClientForFeature("document_import")` now resolves to `baseURL: "https://api.x.ai/v1"` with a real access token (not the stale local endpoint) via a direct script. Then ran the actual `POST /api/lorebook/import/document` endpoint against a real file through the newly-fixed routing and got a genuine, successful Grok completion back — first with a small test doc, then with the full Vesper "Vex" Thorne PDF from the "Multi-format Lorebook Import" verification above: name, description (narrative only), and codexState all extracted correctly, with the PDF's "Core Identity" table landing exactly in `customFields` (Age/Role/Aesthetic/Education/Occupation) as designed. This is the first time this session's document-import feature was proven against a live model rather than only the error path. `tsc --noEmit` (frontend + server) and a full oxlint sweep are clean — 13 warnings now (one fewer than the prior 14-warning baseline), zero errors, zero new warnings.

**Separately confirmed (unrelated to this bug, still worth recording):** the docked-chat model picker (`ChatSystemPromptControl.tsx`'s explicit per-message model dropdown) threads `selectedModel.provider` directly through `AIProviderFactory` client-side with no priority fallback and correctly reaches `api.x.ai`/grok.com for all three Grok variants already. A report of Grok "not responding" from *that* picker specifically is a genuine credential/token problem (missing/invalid `grokKey`, expired `grokOAuthAccessToken`, or a stale `grokSessionCookie`), not this routing gap.

## Three Small Fixes: Codex-Proposal `scopeId`, Brainstorm Chat List Vanishing, Lorebook Refresh

Three issues the user hit while testing live, found and fixed in one pass.

**1. World-Building/Editor chat Codex proposals always failed for `level: "story"` (the common case).** Server logs showed `Chat error: Error: scopeId is required for level 'story'` on every attempt. Root cause: `CODEX_PROPOSAL_INSTRUCTIONS` in `chatContextService.ts` — the system prompt that tells the AI how to emit a `codex-proposal` fenced block — shows the model an example with no `scopeId` field at all, and tells it to default to `level: "story"`. But the AI has no legitimate way to know the story's UUID (an internal DB id, never surfaced in conversation), so it faithfully omitted it every time, and `POST /api/chats/:chatId/codex-proposals` (`server/routes/chats.ts`) required it unconditionally. Fixed by defaulting `scopeId` server-side from the chat's own `chat.storyId` when `level === "story"` and none was supplied — the chat already knows what story it belongs to, so the model was never the right place to source this from. Verified directly: POSTed the exact payload shape the AI's prompt instructs it to produce (`{type: "new_entry", level: "story", name, description, category}`, no `scopeId`) against the real endpoint — got a 500 before the fix, a real `201` with `scopeId` correctly resolved to the chat's story after (test entry created and deleted via direct API calls, not left in the demo data).

**2. Brainstorm's chat-history sidebar appeared to vanish once a chat was selected.** Reported as "needs a back button to get back to the chat list" — but the list was never gone, it was scrolled off the top of the page. `MainContent.tsx` has a `needsBoundedHeight` switch: most tools let their wrapper grow to fit content and the page-level `<main>` scrolls (right call for list/form-heavy tools), but Editor/Outline/Lorebook opt into a real `h-full overflow-hidden` wrapper because they lay out fixed-position UI (resizable panels, open-tabs strips) against it. `BrainstormTool.tsx` docks a persistent `ChatList` sidebar next to the message thread — same shape as those three — but was never added to the bounded-height list, so its wrapper just grew to fit the entire chat transcript instead of clipping it. `ChatMessageList.tsx`'s own scroll-to-bottom effect (`messagesEndRef.current?.scrollIntoView(...)`) then had no properly-bounded scroll container to act on, so the browser walked up to the nearest real scrollable ancestor — the page-level `<main>` — and scrolled *that* to the bottom instead, dragging the sidebar off-screen along with the rest of the page every time a message arrived. `ChatList.tsx` was already built assuming a bounded parent (`h-full overflow-y-auto` on its own content div) — this was a wiring gap, not a missing component. Fix: added `"brainstorm"` to `needsBoundedHeight`. Verified live: selected a chat with a long transcript, confirmed the message list now scrolls within its own contained area and the sidebar never moves, and confirmed via direct DOM inspection that the page-level `<main>`'s `scrollTop` stays `0` throughout (previously scrolled to `973px`).

**3. No manual refresh for the Lorebook browse grid.** Entries can now change from several places that don't all share cache invalidation with the browse view (chat-approved Codex proposals, AI-generated images, edits from an open entry tab) — the user asked for a way to force a refetch rather than reloading the page. Added `onRefresh`/`isRefreshing` props threaded from `LorebookPage.tsx` (using the already-available `refetch`/`isFetching` off `useHierarchicalLorebookQuery`/`useSeriesLorebookQuery` — no new query logic needed) down to `LorebookBrowsePanel.tsx`, with a new "Refresh" icon button (spinning `RefreshCw`, matching the existing Export/Import/New Entry button row) placed first in that row. Verified live: clicked it, confirmed via network inspection that `GET /api/lorebook/story/:id/hierarchical` fires.

All three: `tsc --noEmit` (frontend + server) and a scoped oxlint pass on every touched file are clean — the only warnings present are the same 2 pre-existing `chats.ts` curly-brace style warnings from before this pass (unrelated, not touched).

---

## Follow-up: Same Sidebar-Vanishing Bug on the Lorebook Entry Page, Plus a Per-Entry Refresh

The user reported the Brainstorm sidebar fix above didn't cover the *individual* Lorebook entry page's docked World-Building chat panel, and asked for the new browse-grid Refresh button to also be available while editing a single entry.

**Same bug family, different axis.** `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`'s docked chat panel (`WorldBuildingChatPanel`) renders `ChatInterface` + `ChatList` (`side="right"`, a fixed `w-[300px]`) side by side inside a fixed `w-[420px]` wrapper. The flex row holding them (`<div className="flex h-full border-l">`) had `overflowX: visible`, and `ChatInterface`'s own wrapper (`flex-1 h-full min-h-0`) had `min-h-0` but not `min-w-0` — so in a *row* flex container, the interface's intrinsic content width refused to shrink below its natural minimum, the row's total content exceeded the 420px budget, and the fixed-width `ChatList` sidebar visually overflowed past the container's right edge instead of ever being clipped or reflowed — landing off-screen at `x: 1614` in a 1600px-wide viewport, confirmed via direct `getBoundingClientRect()` inspection rather than assumed from the screenshot alone. Same root cause as the Brainstorm bug (a flex item needs an explicit `min-w-0`/`min-h-0` on the *shrinking axis* to respect its container instead of overflowing), just the horizontal counterpart. Fix: added `min-w-0` alongside the existing `min-h-0`. This immediately exposed a second, real problem the overflow had been silently masking: 420px total was only ever sized for the chat interface *alone* (from before this panel doubled up with a chat list); once correctly clipped, the interface was left with ~120px — unusably narrow, one word per line. Widened the panel to `w-[680px]`.

**Per-entry Refresh, same underlying `refetch` as the browse grid.** `LorebookEntryTab.tsx` gained an `onRefresh`/`isRefreshing`-driven "Refresh" icon button next to the entry name/level badge, wired from `LorebookPage.tsx` to the exact same `refetchEntries()` already added for the browse panel above — the hierarchical/series query is shared, so refetching it updates `activeEntry` (derived via `entries.find(...)`) for whichever view is showing it. This surfaced a **pre-existing, unrelated bug while wiring it up**: `LorebookEntryTab` was never actually `key`ed on entry identity by its caller, despite `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`'s own doc comment explicitly claiming "both callers key this component on entry identity (LorebookEntryTab) or on open-transition (CreateEntryDialog)" — that was only ever true for `CreateEntryDialog`. Since `useForm`'s `defaultValues` are only read once at mount, a `Refresh` that pulled back genuinely changed data (or, more subtly, switching between two already-open entry tabs) would silently keep showing stale form values with no error. Fixed by keying `LorebookEntryTab` on `` `${activeEntry.id}-${activeEntry.updatedAt ?? ""}` `` — covers both the tab-switch case (different `id`) and the refresh-after-external-change case (same `id`, new `updatedAt`) with one key, matching the pattern the comment already (incorrectly) claimed was in place.

**Verified live**: reproduced the sidebar overflow first (created a real chat with a long synthetic transcript via direct API calls, confirmed the `ChatList` panel's bounding rect sat past the viewport edge at multiple viewport widths, ruling out "just a narrow test window" before touching any code), then confirmed after the fix that the panel renders fully on-screen with both panes readably wide, the chat still auto-scrolls to the latest message within its own contained `ScrollArea` (not the page), and the page-level `<main>` never scrolls. Clicked the new per-entry Refresh button and confirmed via network inspection it fires the same `GET /api/lorebook/story/:id/hierarchical` call as the browse grid's button. Test chat and any test data cleaned up via direct API calls afterward, not left in the demo dataset. `tsc --noEmit` and a scoped oxlint pass on every touched file are clean.

---

## Chat Context Anchoring: Ground World-Building Chats in the Entry + Project

**Problem, confirmed by tracing the code (not assumed):** opening a World-Building chat from a specific Lorebook entry (e.g. "Elena Cross," via the docked panel in `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`) gave the AI almost no reliable grounding. Chat creation only stored `storyId`/`chatType`/`templateSlug`/`title` — nothing linked the chat back to the entry it was opened from. `chatContextService.ts`'s `getChatContext` did one RAG hybrid search seeded by the chat's generic template title (e.g. "Character Development," not the entry's actual name), once, on open, never refreshed — whether the entry itself surfaced was down to semantic-similarity luck. `LorebookEntry.metadata.relationships` (a real, already-existing field) was never read anywhere in this path, so declared character connections were invisible to the AI. Separately, `stories.synopsis` (shown on the dashboard card) was never injected into any chat's context, despite being a zero-cost source of baseline project grounding. This is request-time context assembly, not agentic/background work — unrelated to the separately-recorded, deliberately-not-yet-built Agent Framework above.

**Fix — three layers, in priority order:**
1. **Always-on project baseline**: `story.synopsis` now injected into every story-scoped chat's context unconditionally (Editor chats included, not just World-Building — no reason writing assistance should have less project grounding).
2. **Anchor entry**: new nullable `aiChats.anchorEntryId` column. `WorldBuildingChatPanel` (`LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`) threads the entry it's docked to through chat creation (`WorldBuildingChatPanel` → `useCreateChatMutation` → `chatsApi.create` → `POST /api/chats` → `createWorldBuildingChat` → `chatRepository.createChat`, one new optional field at each step, pure pass-through). `getChatContext`'s new `resolveAnchorAndRelated` fetches that entry directly — full current description, not a RAG excerpt — and includes it unconditionally, tagged `role: "anchor"`. A brand-new unsaved entry has no id yet, so this degrades to no anchor with zero special-casing (matches "only for non-new entries").
3. **One-hop relationship walk**: if the anchor entry has `metadata.relationships` (`{targetId, type, description?}[]`), those targets are resolved to their current name/category/excerpt too, tagged `role: "related"` — so "who is this character connected to" is a direct lookup, not RAG luck. Stale targets (entry since deleted) are silently skipped.

`ChatContextCodexEntry` gained a `role: "anchor" | "related" | "search"` field so `resolveCodexEntries` (the existing RAG-result resolver, now taking an `excludeIds` set to avoid double-listing) tags its own results `"search"`. `ChatContext` gained `projectSynopsis: string | null`. `ChatInterface.tsx`'s context-assembly effect (still keyed on `[selectedChat.id]` only, unchanged) formats these into distinct sections — "Focused entry (treat as current, authoritative)," "Entries connected to the focused entry," "Other relevant Codex entries," "Project synopsis" — instead of one flat list, and a small "Focused on: {name}" line renders in the chat header so anchoring silently failing (e.g. the entry gets deleted mid-conversation) is visible to the user rather than just an unexplained drop in the AI's apparent knowledge.

**Explicitly deferred**: per-message RAG re-seeding (using recent conversation content instead of the static chat title as the search query). The `query` param plumbing already exists end-to-end and needs no changes, but re-seeding is its own design problem (what counts as "recent," per-message latency/cost) orthogonal to fixing the anchor-vs-luck problem this pass targets — and this pass's `role` tagging is actually a prerequisite for doing it cleanly later (only `search`-role entries would ever need to churn mid-conversation).

**A real bug found and fixed mid-implementation, not just a design footnote:** the original plan called for `anchorEntryId.references(() => lorebookEntries.id, { onDelete: "set null" })`, based on this file's own `concreteBeats.characterId`/`outlineItems.parentId` comments claiming "SQLite foreign_key enforcement off database-wide." **That claim is false for this connection** — confirmed live via `db.pragma('foreign_keys', { simple: true })`, which returned `1`; `better-sqlite3` defaults FK enforcement ON, and nothing in `server/db/client.ts` ever turns it off. Worse, even with `onDelete: "set null"` declared, SQLite's `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` syntax doesn't support inline `ON DELETE` clauses at all — confirmed by generating the migration and comparing its actual output against every other `ALTER TABLE ADD`-style FK migration in this repo's history (`0016_concrete_beats_character_ref.sql` included), none of which ever emit one despite their schema declaring `onDelete`. First live test (delete an entry that's an active chat anchor) reproduced exactly this: a 500 `FOREIGN KEY constraint failed`, not the intended graceful null-out. **Fixed by dropping the `.references()` call entirely** — `anchorEntryId` is a plain, unconstrained `text` column (still indexed), matching the exact precedent `outlineItems.parentId` already established in this same file for the identical reason ("a self-ref requires an awkward forward-declared callback for no real benefit here; cleaned up in application code instead"). `resolveAnchorAndRelated` already degrades gracefully on a missing/stale anchor id, so application-level handling was already correct — the schema-level constraint was the actual bug, not a missing safety net. **Flagged, not fixed here (pre-existing, out of scope for this pass): `concreteBeats.characterId` likely has this exact same latent bug** — same `ALTER TABLE ADD ... REFERENCES ... { onDelete: "set null" }` pattern, same confirmed-on FK enforcement, so deleting a Lorebook entry that's referenced by any `concreteBeats` row would plausibly also 500 instead of nulling out. Not verified directly (out of scope for this pass), but the mechanism is identical and worth checking before it surprises someone.

**Verified end-to-end against the real running dev server and DB:** `PRAGMA table_info(aiChats)`/`PRAGMA index_list(aiChats)` confirmed the column and index landed correctly after the corrected migration. Created a chat anchored to "Marcus Webb" (real demo data, with real `metadata.relationships` pointing at Elena Cross and Sir Geoffrey Hastings) via a direct `POST /api/chats` call, then `GET .../context` and confirmed `projectSynopsis` was populated with the story's real synopsis and `relevantCodexEntries` contained Marcus tagged `role: "anchor"` plus both relationship targets tagged `role: "related"`, each excerpt correctly prefixed with the relationship type/description. Edited Marcus's description via `PUT /api/lorebook/:id` and re-fetched context, confirming the anchor excerpt reflected the new text immediately (proves a live fetch, not a cached snapshot) before restoring his original description. Created a throwaway test entry, anchored a chat to it, deleted the entry, and confirmed the delete succeeded (`200`, after the schema fix — it 500'd before) and the re-fetched context simply omitted the anchor rather than erroring. Confirmed in the real browser: opened Marcus Webb's entry, selected the anchored test chat from the docked panel's chat list, confirmed the "Focused on: Marcus Webb" line rendered and `GET /api/chats/:id/context` fired (`200`) via network inspection. All test chats and entries deleted afterward via direct API calls, confirmed empty via a follow-up query — nothing left in the demo dataset. `tsc --noEmit` (frontend + server) and a scoped oxlint pass on every touched file are clean (the one `server/db/schema.ts` `max-lines` warning is pre-existing, confirmed via the same file at 437 lines — already over the 400 limit — before this change added its 2 net lines).

## Chapter Anchoring for Editor Chat

Same gap as the Lorebook case, one tool over: opening an Editor Chat while focused on a specific chapter gave the AI no structural link to it — `chatContextService.ts`'s `resolveChapterPassages` only ever surfaced chapters via RAG relevance ranking against the chat's generic title, so whether the chapter actually being written showed up was down to luck. `StoryEditor.tsx` already tracked `currentChapterId` (confirmed live-synced to whichever pane/tab is actually focused, via `EditorPane.tsx`/`PaneTabStrip.tsx`'s `setCurrentChapterId` calls, not just an initial value) but never passed it to `EditorChatRail`.

**New `aiChats.anchorChapterId`** — plain column, no `.references()` FK, matching `anchorEntryId`'s already-corrected precedent directly (no repeat of that mistake this time). Threaded through `EditorChatRail` (`storyId`) → `StoryEditor.tsx`'s `currentChapterId` at the call site → `useCreateChatMutation` → `POST /api/chats` → **`createGenericChat`, not `createWorldBuildingChat`** — Editor chats route through the generic path (`chatType !== "worldbuilding"`), a real distinction from the entry case that the plan caught before implementation rather than after. `OutlinePage.tsx` (shares the same `EditorChatRail` component) deliberately passes nothing — no single focused chapter there, same "naturally undefined, no special-casing" degrade as a new Lorebook entry.

**A genuinely different design question than the entry case, resolved by reusing existing infrastructure rather than inventing new truncation logic.** Lorebook descriptions are short enough to include raw and unconditionally; chapter `content` can run to many pages, so including it raw risked bloating the prompt. Rather than add new size-capping logic, `resolveAnchorChapter` (new, `chatContextService.ts`) pulls the anchor chapter's own `ragChunks` rows directly (`entityType: "chapter"`, ordered by `chunkIndex`) — bypassing RAG relevance ranking entirely (it's the chapter actually being worked on) while still reusing the indexing pipeline's existing per-chunk size bound, the same "bypass ranking, keep the real underlying representation" move `resolveAnchorAndRelated` already makes for entries. Deliberately unbounded (all chunks, not `RELEVANT_ENTRIES_LIMIT`-capped) so a long chapter isn't silently truncated.

`resolveChapterPassages` gained the same `excludeIds` exclusion guard `resolveCodexEntries` already has, and both entry/chapter context types gained a `role` field (`ChatContextChapterPassage`: `"anchor" | "search"`, no `"related"` — chapters have no relationships concept). `getChatContext` runs `resolveAnchorChapter` unconditionally alongside the existing anchor-entry/synopsis work — no `chatType` branching needed, since `anchorChapterId` is only ever set by `createGenericChat`'s editor path in the first place.

`EditorChatRail.tsx` previously relied on `ChatList`'s own default "New Chat" button/handler (unlike `WorldBuildingChatPanel`, which already overrode it for its template picker) — now overrides `renderNewChatAction` itself with a plain button that includes `anchorChapterId`, keeping `ChatList` itself anchor-agnostic rather than leaking this concern into a component every other non-anchored chat list also shares. `ChatInterface.tsx`'s "Focused on" indicator (renamed `anchorEntryName` → `focusedOnLabel`) now also checks for an anchor chapter (`Chapter: {title}`) when there's no anchor entry — a chat only ever has one or the other, never both, so one shared indicator is correct.

**Verified end-to-end against the real dev DB and server:** `PRAGMA table_info`/`index_list` confirmed the column+index landed with a clean `ALTER TABLE ADD` (no FK, matching the corrected `anchorEntryId` shape exactly). Indexed a real demo chapter ("The Drop," 4 chunks) directly via `POST /api/rag/index/chapter/:id` (bypassing the debounced autosave), created a chat anchored to it via direct API, and confirmed `GET .../context` returned all 4 chunks in `chunkIndex` order tagged `role: "anchor"`, matching the chapter's real content. Renamed the chat's title to exactly match the chapter's own title (maximizing the chance RAG search would also surface it) and confirmed the chunk count stayed at exactly 4 with no `role: "search"` duplicates — the exclusion guard holds. Edited the chapter's content, re-indexed, re-fetched context, confirmed the anchor excerpt reflected the change immediately, then restored the original content and re-indexed, confirmed via a follow-up fetch that `contentLength` matched the pre-test value exactly (`7920`) with no test text (`"submarine"`) left behind. Confirmed live in the browser: opened the Editor tool with "The Drop" focused, clicked the real "New Chat" button, confirmed the created chat's `anchorChapterId` matched via a follow-up API check, and confirmed "Focused on: Chapter: The Drop" rendered. Switched to the Outline tool (same `EditorChatRail` component) and confirmed a chat created there has `anchorChapterId: null` and shows no indicator. All test chats deleted afterward. `tsc --noEmit` (frontend + server) and a scoped oxlint pass on every touched file are clean — only the same pre-existing `chats.ts` curly-brace warnings and `schema.ts` `max-lines` warning, both predating this change.

**Recorded but deliberately not implemented this pass: Brainstorm's missing `story.synopsis` baseline.** Brainstorm (`src/features/brainstorm/`) is a fully separate system with explicit, checkbox-driven manual context selection (`ContextSelector.tsx`, resolved by `BrainstormContextResolver` in `src/features/prompts/services/resolvers/BrainstormResolvers.ts`) — since the user already picks what's included, an automatic *anchor* would be redundant there. But confirmed by reading the resolver directly: `story.synopsis` is never injected into Brainstorm's context in either its "Include Full Context" branch or its manual-selection branch, the same zero-cost baseline win already shipped for World-Building/Editor chats. Not implemented now because the user is actively rethinking Brainstorm's workflow — a concrete plan risks being built against a shape that's about to change. When that settles, the fix is small and self-contained: fetch `synopsis` in `BrainstormContextResolver.resolve` and prepend it unconditionally to both branches' `parts` array — no schema changes, no new resolver class needed.
