## Agent Framework — Phase A (Agent Jobs), Load-Bearing Decisions

**Supersedes this file's earlier "RAG Index Freshness ... & Agent Framework Direction" section's Agent Framework portion** (the `ragScans`-generalization shape recorded there is kept; every gap it left open — scheduling trigger, concurrency model, retry/dead-letter policy, memory write/staleness policy, user visibility, job status surface — is filled by `docs/Agent_Framework_And_Project_Memory_Design.md`, a design spec from Hermes). This entry records the load-bearing choices made while implementing that spec's Phase A (jobs only — no `agentMemories`, which is explicitly Phase B and gated on a separate review).

**New `agentJobs` table** (`server/db/schema.ts`) generalizes `ragScans`: `jobType`, `status` (`queued`\|`running`\|`completed`\|`failed`), nullable `storyId` (real FK, `onDelete: cascade` — a single parent table, and this is a brand-new `CREATE TABLE`, not a later `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`, so the FK's `ON DELETE` clause is preserved correctly), a loose (no-FK) `entityId` for polymorphic scoping, `payload`/`result`/`progress` JSON columns, `attempts`/`maxAttempts` (default 3), and full lifecycle timestamps. `entityId`'s no-FK convention matches `aiChats.anchorEntryId`/`outlineItems.parentId`'s established precedent — worth noting this is the first table applying that convention at creation time (where a real FK would technically have worked) rather than inheriting it from the `ALTER TABLE` limitation those two columns hit; it's still avoided here since `entityId` genuinely spans multiple target tables (chapter or lorebook entry) depending on `jobType`.

**`jobRunner.ts` is strictly serial, in-process, no new infra** — one `setInterval` claim-and-run tick (~3s) and one schedule tick (~60s), matching the single-Docker-container/single-SQLite-file constraint. `agentJobsRepository.ts` is a hybrid: most operations are ordinary async Drizzle (matching every other repository in this codebase), except `enqueue`'s dedup-check-then-insert and `claimNextQueuedJob`'s claim, which use raw synchronous `better-sqlite3` in a `sqlite.transaction()`/single atomic `UPDATE ... WHERE id = (SELECT ...) RETURNING *` — the same reasoning `ragRepository.ts` already established for its own transactional operations (true atomicity in a single-threaded process, not achievable through Drizzle's own await-yielding query builder for a check-then-write).

**Landmine discovered and fixed this session, worth remembering for any future raw-SQL work against a Drizzle-defined table:** Drizzle's `integer(col, { mode: "timestamp" })` columns store epoch **seconds**, not milliseconds (confirmed by reading `drizzle-orm/sqlite-core/columns/integer`: `mapToDriverValue` does `Math.floor(date.getTime() / 1000)`, `mapFromDriverValue` does `new Date(value * 1000)`). The raw-SQL paths above (`enqueue`'s insert, `claimNextQueuedJob`'s update) initially wrote `Date.now()` (milliseconds) directly into `createdAt`/`queuedAt`/`startedAt`/`lastAttemptAt` — passing entirely through, since raw `better-sqlite3` calls bypass Drizzle's serialization. Every *raw-SQL-written* row read back correctly via *other raw-SQL* reads (internally self-consistent, which is exactly why a synthetic unit test comparing raw-written rows only against each other didn't catch it), but any read through **Drizzle** (`getJob`, `listJobs`, or the `/api/agent/jobs*` routes built on top of them) multiplied the already-millisecond value by 1000 again, producing dates around the year 58508. Caught only by live end-to-end verification through the real Settings UI, not by the repository-level test. Fixed with explicit `toEpochSeconds`/`fromEpochSeconds` helpers in `agentJobsRepository.ts` at every raw-SQL read/write site. **Takeaway: any new raw-SQL code touching a Drizzle `mode: "timestamp"` column must convert through epoch seconds by hand — there is no shared helper for this yet, and Drizzle's own conversion is invisible unless you go looking at its source.**

**`rag_scan_story` is deliberately never auto-enqueued by the schedule tick in Phase A.** The design doc's "unattended scan policy: default OFF" (§3.4) has no per-story opt-in setting yet, and adding one is out of scope for this phase — omitting the job type from the schedule tick's periodic list makes "default OFF" true by construction rather than by a setting that doesn't exist. The only way a `rag_scan_story` (or `rag_scan_chapter`) job gets created in Phase A is the manual `POST /api/agent/jobs` route.

**`/api/agent/jobs` is mounted with `requireOwner`**, matching `/api/admin`/`/api/ai`/`/api/users`. This needed an explicit call since `/api/rag` (the closest existing precedent, and what scan jobs still write through underneath) is only `requireAuth`-gated with no owner check — no single existing route family settles this unambiguously. Reasoning: agent jobs are system-level infrastructure that can trigger LLM spend and story-wide reindexing, which a `viewer`/`editor` has no legitimate reason to invoke directly.

**Scan-as-job uses the dual-write/adapter approach the design doc names as recommended short-term** (§3.1): `ragScans`/`ragScanIssues` remain the sole source of truth for scan progress/issues — zero schema or behavior change to `ragScanRepository.ts`, zero risk to the existing `GET /api/rag/scan/:scanId` polling contract. `agentJobs` is a second, thinner wrapper row whose `result` references the underlying `ragScans.id`. `server/routes/rag.ts`'s `POST /scan/chapter/:id`/`POST /scan/story/:id` are unchanged, still calling `scanChapter`/`scanStory` directly — nothing in the frontend calls either route today, so there was no compatibility reason to route them through the job queue instead. `ragScanner.ts` gained three purely additive changes to support the job-runner-driven caller (`server/services/jobs/ragScanJobs.ts`): `runChapterScan` and `requireScannerConnection` exported (were private), and a `listOrderedChapterIds` helper extracted from `scanStory`'s own body (`scanStory` itself now calls it too — its fire-and-forget IIFE, signature, and behavior are otherwise untouched). **This fixes the exact weakness this file called out earlier** ("a scan dies silently if the server restarts mid-run") but only as crash *visibility*, not resumption — a requeued `rag_scan_story` job restarts the whole chapter loop from the beginning, it does not pick up mid-story. Verified for real: a `reconcile_index` job survived an actual `kill -9` mid-run during this session's testing (not staged — a leftover process from an earlier ad-hoc test) and was correctly requeued on the next boot (`jobRunner: crash recovery — requeued 1, permanently failed 0`), then ran to completion.

**Also fixed alongside `reconcile_index` (Step A3), a confirmed live bug, not just a design-doc gap:** `server/routes/lorebook.ts`'s `DELETE /:id` deleted the entry's image file and DB row but never called `removeEntityFromIndex`, permanently orphaning `ragChunks`/`vec_chunks`/`fts_chunks` rows on every lorebook entry deletion (`ragChunks` has no real FK to `lorebookEntries` — loose `entityId` column — so nothing cascaded). Both `POST /` and `PUT /:id` in the same file already called `indexLorebookEntry` on create/update; only the symmetric delete-side cleanup was missing. Fixed with one `removeEntityFromIndex("lorebook_entry", req.params.id)` call. Verified live: created a real lorebook entry via the API, confirmed a `ragChunks` row existed, deleted the entry via the real `DELETE` route, confirmed the chunk row was gone. `reconcileIndexJob.ts`'s orphan pass (comparing indexed `ragChunks` keys against live `lorebookEntries`/`chapters`) is the backstop for any pre-existing orphans from before this fix, or any future delete path that forgets the same call — it does not replace the fix at the source.

**Verified end-to-end against the real dev server and DB, not just synthetic tests**: a standalone script exercised every `agentJobsRepository.ts` function directly against the dev DB (enqueue+dedup, claim, complete, `recordJobFailure` through both the requeue and permanent-fail branches, `retryJob`, `recoverCrashedJobs` through both branches, `listJobs` ordering) before the timestamp bug above was found and fixed by the *next* layer of testing. Booted the real dev server and confirmed, through the actual Settings page (`RecentJobsCard`) as the logged-in owner test account: the schedule tick auto-enqueuing `reconcile_index` (per story) and `prune_history` (global) on first boot, both running to completion with live progress text ("Checked lorebook entry ...", "Checked chapter Moscow Rules") polled via the new `refetchInterval` (this codebase's first use of it), a manually-triggered `rag_scan_chapter` job failing permanently after 3 attempts against this dev environment's unconfigured scanner endpoint with the real error surfaced in the card, and clicking the real Retry button resetting and re-running it. `tsc --noEmit` (frontend + server) and a full oxlint sweep are clean — every warning present (`ragScanner.ts` curly-style, `schema.ts`/`client.ts` `max-lines`, one pre-existing `crud.ts` `no-explicit-any` error) predates this change; confirmed by diffing the touched-files-only lint output against the full-repo baseline.

---

## Agent Framework — Phase B (Project Persistent Memory), Load-Bearing Decisions

Implements `docs/Agent_Framework_And_Project_Memory_Design.md` §4 (Steps B1–B5) on top of the stable, committed Phase A (`agentJobs`/`jobRunner`). New `agentMemories` table: factual project knowledge that a background job can only ever *propose* into (`status: pending`) — a human approval is the only path to `active`, and only `active` rows are ever indexed or retrievable. Mirrors this codebase's existing Codex propose→approve flow (`codexPendingChanges`/`codexService.ts`) where the shape fits, diverges deliberately where the design doc calls for something simpler.

**No separate snapshot table, unlike Codex.** Codex pairs every live-entity mutation with an append-only `codexSnapshots` row for history. `agentMemories` instead versions purely through `memoryKey` + status transition: approving a row whose `memoryKey` already has an `active` row atomically flips that previous row to `superseded` (never deleted, just de-indexed) in the same `sqlite.transaction()` as the activation — one raw-SQL function, `agentMemoriesRepository.ts`'s `activatePendingAndSupersede`/`insertActiveAndSupersede`, both sharing an internal `supersedeActiveForKey` step. Verified for real: seeded two pending rows sharing a `memoryKey`, approved the first (`ragChunks` row created, `entityType: "agent_memory"`), approved the second — response's `superseded` object correctly showed the first now `status: "superseded"`, and a direct DB check confirmed the first's `ragChunks` rows were gone while the second's existed.

**Repeated the exact epoch-seconds landmine from Phase A, deliberately guarded against this time.** `agentMemoriesRepository.ts`'s raw-SQL supersession transition writes/reads timestamps the same way `agentJobsRepository.ts` does — copied its `toEpochSeconds`/`fromEpochSeconds` helpers verbatim rather than re-deriving them, specifically because Phase A only caught the "Drizzle's `mode: timestamp` stores epoch seconds, not milliseconds" bug via live testing after a synthetic unit test missed it. Verified live this time before it could surface as a bug: approved memories' `createdAt`/`approvedAt`/`updatedAt` all read back as real 2026 dates through the actual API, not a `+058508` garbage year.

**`hybridSearch`'s new `entityTypes` filter is asymmetric between FTS and vec, and this is deliberate, not an oversight.** `vec_chunks` declares `storyId` as a sqlite-vec `PARTITION KEY` (confirmed by reading migration `0009_rag_vector_search.sql`) but `chunkId` is only a plain auxiliary column — combining an arbitrary `chunkId IN (subquery)` filter with `MATCH ... AND k = ?` is not a construct sqlite-vec is documented to support, so it was not attempted. The FTS candidate query gets a real subquery filter (`fts_chunks` is a standalone table with no such restriction); the vec side stays filtered only by `storyId` as before, with `entityType` filtering applied at the existing metadata-hydration step instead. Accepted tradeoff, stated in the function's own comment: a restrictive `entityTypes` filter can occasionally return fewer than `limit` results if excluded-type chunks occupied ranked vec slots — never affects the default (unfiltered) path every existing caller uses. Verified live: a query matching an approved memory's body returned zero results with `entityTypes` omitted, and returned the memory when `entityTypes` explicitly included `"agent_memory"` — both via the real `POST /api/rag/search` route (this dev environment has no embedding endpoint configured, so this ran the FTS/keyword path specifically, which is also the path with the real subquery filter — the vec-side hydration-time filter path is exercised the same way whenever an embedding endpoint is configured, not separately re-verified here).

**`reconcileIndexJob.ts` (Phase A, already shipped) required a real fix, not just a design-doc nice-to-have.** Its orphan-detection pass only ever recognized live `lorebookEntries`/`chapters` as valid RAG-index keys — every approved `agentMemories` row's indexed chunks would have looked orphaned to the very next `reconcile_index` run and been deleted. Fixed by adding a third `validKeys` source: active memories for the story. Verified live, not just by reading the diff: with an approved, indexed memory in place, manually triggered `reconcile_index` for its story and confirmed the job's own result (`orphansRemoved: 0`) plus a direct `ragChunks` query — the memory's chunks survived.

**`distill_memory` is never auto-chained after a scan job completes, and no dedicated "Suggest memories" UI was built.** No job handler in this codebase enqueues a follow-up job today (only two `enqueue()` call sites exist: the manual route and the schedule tick) — auto-chaining an LLM-calling job after another job would be new, untested territory, and risks the same "background LLM spend must not surprise the user" problem Phase A's `rag_scan_story` guardrail exists to avoid. `distill_memory` is only enqueueable through the existing generic `POST /api/agent/jobs` route (`entityId` reused to hold the source `ragScans.id`, same convention `rag_scan_chapter` already established for a chapter id — no schema change needed). A dedicated trigger button is explicitly Phase C ("do not build now" per the design doc's own §5) and was not built.

**`agentMemories` approval/CRUD is Codex-level auth (`requireAuth` + `blockViewerMutations`), not owner-only like `/api/agent/jobs`.** Memory approval is an editorial decision analogous to Codex proposal approval (editor-allowed today), not system administration. The design doc doesn't specify a restriction; this was the closer existing analogy to follow, made explicitly rather than defaulting to the nearer-by-file-location `/api/agent/jobs` precedent.

**A real Edit-before-approve UI was built, completing a pattern Codex left half-finished.** Codex's backend has supported revising a pending change in place since it was built (`codexRepository.ts`'s `revisePendingChange`, exposed via `PATCH /api/chats/:chatId/codex-proposals/:id`) but has zero UI wired to it anywhere (`useReviseProposalMutation` has no call sites, confirmed via repo-wide grep — `ProposalCard.tsx` only ever renders Approve/Reject). The design doc explicitly requires "Approve / Reject / Edit" for memories, so `ProjectMemoryList.tsx` builds a real inline edit control (title/body/category, `memoryKey` deliberately non-editable) with its own Save button, separate from Approve — a pending row's content edit and its status promotion remain two distinct clicks, matching the design doc's literal three-verb phrasing rather than merging them into one action.

**Verified end-to-end against the real dev server and DB**, beyond the specific items above: booted the dev server (new migration `0028` applied cleanly — `agentMemories`, 5 indexes, 2 FKs, no unrelated diff), exercised the full UI at `/?story=...&tool=memory` as the logged-in owner test account — Pending/Active/Rejected tabs, Pin toggle (persisted, `Unpin` label updated on re-render), inline Edit (title change saved, confirmed via a direct `ragChunks` query that the active memory's chunk content was re-indexed with the new title), Delete (confirmed via `AlertDialog` confirmation flow and a direct `ragChunks` query that the chunk was removed), and the "New Note" dialog (created a real active-immediately user note, appeared under Active without a manual reload). All test memories deleted afterward via direct API calls, confirmed empty via a follow-up list query — none left in the demo dataset. `tsc --noEmit` (frontend + server) and a full oxlint sweep are clean, with the exact same warning/error count (28 warnings, 1 error) as the post-Phase-A baseline — confirmed zero new warnings by diffing file paths in the full-repo lint output, not just re-running the count.

## Project Saves — Phase 1 (Codex Layer: Restore + Timeline UI)

**Decision:** `CLAUDE.md` names "Project Saves split into Codex + Story layers with separate visual timelines" as roadmap item 9, but that one line was — confirmed via three parallel explorations before any code was written — the entire spec that has ever existed for this feature: no design doc, no prior git history, no archived planning docs. Given the real scope (two genuinely separate save systems, two timeline UIs) and no Hermes-style spec to lean on for the edge-case decisions the way Agent Framework Phase A/B had, this was scoped down with the user first via three direct questions rather than guessed at: **Codex layer first** (the Story/chapter layer — `chapters` has zero versioning of any kind today, and autosave destructively overwrites content every ~1s — is a deliberate, separate future phase, not started here), saves are **hybrid** (automatic snapshot creation, which already existed via `codexSnapshots`, plus optional manual naming, which didn't), and restore is **per-item** (one Codex entry at a time, matching how `codexSnapshots` already works — not a whole-project rollback bundling multiple entries).

**The investigation also found `CLAUDE.md` was describing a feature that didn't fully exist.** `codexSnapshots` (append-only, per-entry, auto-created on every create/enable/state-change/approve) was real and working, but restore was not implemented anywhere — no `restoreSnapshot` function, no route — despite `CLAUDE.md` line 26 claiming "Supports per-entry snapshot history with restore capability." Worse, `GET /api/codex/:entryId/snapshots` had zero frontend callers at all — `codexClient.ts` never called it, no component rendered it — so the append-only history table has been silently accumulating real data since the Codex feature first shipped with nothing in the app ever showing it to a user. This phase's actual work was: add the missing restore, add optional labeling, and build the timeline viewer that never existed.

**Restore is itself non-destructive, not a rewind.** `restoreSnapshot` (`server/services/codexService.ts`) applies a target snapshot's `description`/`codexState` to the live entry via the same `updateCodexEntryFields` every other mutation path already uses, then takes a **new** snapshot of the result tagged `sourceType: "restore"` with `sourceRef` pointing back at what was restored from. The restore action is therefore visible in the timeline as its own event and is itself restorable — consistent with `CLAUDE.md`'s "Strong user control and non-destructive history" constraint, and directly verified live: restoring a real demo entry's snapshot took the snapshot count from 3 to 4, not down to a truncated history.

**A real, pre-existing gap in what `codexSnapshots` tracks, surfaced rather than silently worked around:** the table has only ever captured `description` and `codexState` — never `tags` or `needsFleshingOut`. Restore can therefore only restore what was actually snapshotted; a restored entry's tags/needsFleshingOut reflect whatever they currently are, not the tagged state at snapshot time. Fixing this would need its own schema change (both `CreateSnapshotParams` and every one of the four existing snapshot-creation call sites would need to start passing those fields) and was out of scope for what was asked — recorded here so it isn't rediscovered as a surprise later.

**Labeling is retroactive, not threaded through snapshot creation.** `codexSnapshots.label` (new nullable column) is set via a dedicated `PATCH /api/codex/:entryId/snapshots/:snapshotId` after the fact, through the new History panel's inline click-to-edit control — not as an optional parameter on `createSnapshot`/`CreateSnapshotParams`. This means every one of the four existing automatic-snapshot call sites (`createCodexEntry`, `enableCodexForEntry`, `recordStateChange`, `approvePendingChange`) needed zero changes; naming is purely additive on top of what already existed, matching this codebase's general bias toward minimally-invasive changes over threading a new concern through every call site that doesn't strictly need it.

**The "visual timeline" (`CodexHistoryPanel.tsx`) is genuinely new UI with no precedent to copy** — confirmed via repo-wide search before building it: no component anywhere in `src/` renders a chronological/timeline view (the only "Timeline" hits in the whole frontend are a Lorebook entry *category* value, unrelated). Built as a `Collapsible` section using the exact same shell `AdvancedSettings.tsx` already established for this form, placed between `CodexStateEditor` and `AdvancedSettings`, shown only when `entry?.codexEnabled && entry.id` (an unsaved or non-Codex entry has nothing to show).

**The trickiest piece — making the open entry's form actually refresh after a restore, not just the database — reused an existing mechanism rather than inventing state-sync logic.** `LorebookEntryTab.tsx` already keys itself on `` `${activeEntry.id}-${activeEntry.updatedAt ?? ""}` `` (a fix from earlier this session, see the "Follow-up: Same Sidebar-Vanishing Bug" entry above), specifically so a refresh-after-external-change forces a remount instead of `react-hook-form`'s mount-time-only `defaultValues` silently going stale. `useRestoreSnapshotMutation` just needed to invalidate the right `lorebookKeys` (`.story(storyId)`/`.hierarchical(storyId)`) on success for that existing mechanism to pick it up for free — verified live, not assumed: after clicking Restore in the real UI, the entry's Description textarea and Codex-state fields (Wardrobe/Appearance/Wounds) updated to the restored values with no manual page reload, and the form's own History panel re-collapsed to its default closed state exactly as expected from a genuine fresh mount.

**Verified end-to-end against the real dev server and DB, on a real demo entry, not a throwaway test fixture**: opened "Vesper 'Vex' Thorne" (a real Codex-enabled demo entry), made a real state change via the API to create a third snapshot, opened the History panel in the actual browser UI and confirmed all three snapshots rendered in the right order with correct source-type badges and the newest correctly marked "Current" with no Restore button offered (2 Restore buttons rendered for 3 snapshots, not 3). Clicked into an "Untitled save" label, typed a name, saved, and confirmed via a direct API check it persisted. Clicked Restore, confirmed the `AlertDialog` copy, confirmed, and verified via direct API checks that the live entry's description reverted, a 4th snapshot appeared tagged `restore` with the correct `sourceRef`, and — separately — that the codexState (wardrobe items) also reverted correctly, not just the description. Verified the cross-entry ownership guard by attempting to restore a real snapshot ID against a fabricated `entryId` and confirming it was rejected rather than silently restoring the wrong entry. Because this test used real demo content rather than disposable fixtures, explicitly restored the entry back to its original pristine snapshot afterward and confirmed via a direct API check that the description and full wardrobe list matched the pre-test state exactly — the accumulated extra snapshot rows from this test session were deliberately left in place (deleting them would need a snapshot-delete capability this phase doesn't add, and leaving non-destructive history around is the entire point of the feature being tested). `tsc --noEmit` (frontend + server) and a full oxlint sweep are clean, with the exact same warning/error count (28 warnings, 1 error) as the post-Phase-B baseline.

## Lorebook Relationship Graph — Library Choice, Source-of-Truth, and Load-Bearing Decisions

Implements `docs/Thin_Story_Graph_And_Lorebook_Visualization.md`'s Phase G1 in one pass: `storyGraphEdges` as the source of truth (not a throwaway metadata-only UI), with the existing `lorebookEntries.metadata.relationships[]` data migrated in and left read-only afterward. Nodes are lorebook entries only; edges are a 15-value concrete/factual allowlist (`knows`, `allied_with`, `opposed_to`, `member_of`, `located_in`, `owns`, `holds`, `works_at`, `related_to`, `part_of`, `caused`, `involved_in`, `mentions`, `contradicts`, `other`) — deliberately no psychological or power-dynamic types, per both the design doc and `CLAUDE.md`'s standing "keep psychological/thematic enforcement out of scope" constraint. AI edge suggestions, position persistence (`storyGraphLayout`), and pending/approve edge review UI are all explicitly not built this pass — the `status` column (`active`\|`pending`\|`rejected`) exists so none of that needs a schema change later, but nothing produces anything but `active` rows today.

**Library choice: `@xyflow/react` (React Flow), a new dependency.** Confirmed via exploration that zero graph-rendering libraries existed anywhere in this codebase before this. Unlike the dependencies this project has previously chosen to avoid (bcrypt/argon2 native bindings, `sharp`, a queue library — see the `README`'s/earlier `DECISIONS.md` entries' reasoning), pan/zoom/drag-to-connect/edge-routing for an interactive node-link graph is the actual value of this feature, not incidental plumbing around a simpler hand-rolled alternative. `@xyflow/react` has no native bindings (no Docker/cross-platform build risk) and is isolated into its own `vite.config.ts` `manualChunks` entry (`"story-graph": ["@xyflow/react"]`, mirroring the one existing `react-markdown` split) so non-graph-tool users never load it.

**`storyGraphEdges` is a brand-new `CREATE TABLE`, not an `ALTER TABLE`, so its `storyId` FK's `ON DELETE cascade` clause is preserved correctly** (the same landmine class already documented in this file for `anchorEntryId`). `fromId`/`toId` are deliberately loose columns with no real FK — entries can be global/series/story-scoped, matching `aiChats.anchorEntryId`/`agentJobs.entityId`'s established precedent. **The partial unique active-edge index was verified to actually work, not just assumed**: `uniqueIndex("storygraphedge_unique_active_idx").on(storyId, fromId, toId, edgeType).where(sql`status = 'active'`)` was checked two ways after `npm run db:generate` — the generated `.sql` file has a genuine `WHERE status = 'active'` clause (not silently dropped), and a live `PRAGMA index_list` against the migrated dev DB confirmed `"partial": 1`. Enforcement is still primarily application-level (a plain select-then-insert check in `storyGraphService.createEdge`, matching this feature's low-concurrency single-editor-UI nature) — the DB index is a backstop, not the only guard.

**A real, pre-existing bug was found during exploration that raises the stakes on migrating now, left unfixed and out of scope, but recorded so it isn't rediscovered as a surprise:** `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`'s save path unconditionally writes `metadata.relationships: []` on every entry save, and `PUT /api/lorebook/:id` does a non-merging overwrite of the whole `metadata` JSON column — so editing any entry through the normal UI silently destroys its legacy relationships. No UI anywhere lets a user view or edit `metadata.relationships` (it was populated only by JSON import or seed data), so this has been a live, silent data-loss bug the whole time this field has existed. The new `storyGraphEdges` table is fully independent of this once migrated — edges are their own rows, not reconstructed from `metadata` on each read — but the underlying bug in the legacy field itself is untouched.

**Migration (`migrateStoryRelationships`) normalizes freeform legacy `type` strings against the allowlist via a synonym map, and deliberately never drops an unmapped type.** Verified against the real demo story's actual seed data: all 5 of its relationships used types not in the synonym table (`handler`, `subordinate`, `recruiter`, `superior`, `informant` — all defensible escape-hatch cases, since none cleanly maps to a single allowlist value without editorializing), so all 5 correctly fell through to `edgeType: "other"` with the original type preserved via a `[type]` prefix in the edge's `description` (e.g. `"[handler] Elena's MI6 handler for three years"`) — zero silent data loss, exactly per the design doc's §9 requirement. `metadata.relationships` itself is deliberately never stripped or written to after migration (stays read-only legacy data) — `hasLegacyRelationships` in the graph response is therefore a presence check, not a "still needs migrating" check, and stays `true` forever once any relationship data ever existed, which is intentional (a lightweight nudge, not a completion flag). Migration is idempotent by design (dedup against existing `storyId|fromId|toId|edgeType` keys before insert) — verified live: re-ran the migration endpoint against the already-migrated demo story and got `{"migrated": 0, "skipped": 5, skippedDetails: [...duplicate...]}`, and re-ran it a third time (via the actual UI button, not the API) with the same zero-migrated, zero-duplicate-created result.

**A real query-invalidation gap was found and fixed during UI verification, not just assumed to be fine.** `useStoryGraphQuery`/`useNeighborhoodQuery` inherit the app-wide default `staleTime` of 1 minute (`src/providers/QueryProvider.tsx`) and are only invalidated by story-graph's own mutations. Deleting a lorebook entry through the normal Lorebook UI goes through `useDeleteLorebookMutation` (`useLorebookQuery.ts`), which invalidates only `lorebookKeys` — the server-side cascade-delete of that entry's edges (`server/routes/lorebook.ts` → `deleteEdgesForEntity`) is real and correct, but the Relationships tool's own query cache had no way to know about it, so a just-deleted entry could linger as a ghost node/edge in the graph UI for up to a minute even after a tool switch and back. Fixed by adding one `queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: storyGraphKeys.all })` call to `useDeleteLorebookMutation`'s `onSuccess` — a small, deliberate cross-feature import (`lorebook` hook importing `story-graph`'s query-key factory) rather than a broader event-bus mechanism, since this is the only place a non-story-graph mutation needs to reach into story-graph's cache.

**Ego-view-default centers on the alphabetically-first story-scoped entry**, matching `LorebookEntryList.tsx`'s own default `sortBy: "name"` exactly — chosen so the two tools' orderings never surprise a user moving between them. Layout (`layoutEgo`/`layoutGrid` in `src/features/story-graph/lib/layout.ts`) is deterministic and recomputed on every load/refetch, never persisted — `storyGraphLayout` (design doc "Slice 5") was explicitly not built this pass. A known, accepted cosmetic limitation: two nodes connected by edges in both directions (e.g. the demo data's `hastings→marcus-webb` and `marcus-webb→hastings` pair) render with overlapping curves in the canvas, since `StoryGraphNode.tsx` uses one fixed source handle and one fixed target handle per node rather than direction-aware handle selection — both edges are correct and independently visible/editable via the node side panel's Outgoing/Incoming lists, this is a rendering-only overlap, not a data bug. Not fixed this pass; noted here rather than silently left for someone to rediscover.

**Open-entry cross-tool navigation reuses `LorebookPage.tsx`'s existing `openEntryTab`, via a new ephemeral (non-persisted) `StoryContext.pendingLorebookEntryId` pointer** — the same "tool A sets a pointer, tool B consumes it once" pattern `ChaptersTool.tsx`'s `currentChapterId` already established, deliberately kept out of localStorage (unlike every other `StoryContext` field) since it only needs to survive the one re-render following `setCurrentTool("lorebook")`. Only the graph→Lorebook direction was built; secondary deep-links (a "View graph" button in the Lorebook toolbar, "Show in graph" in the entry editor) were explicitly out of scope per the user's directive.

**Verified end-to-end against the real dev server and DB**, beyond the specific items above: migration idempotency (see above); attempted a self-loop edge (`fromId === toId`, correctly rejected with `"An edge cannot connect an entry to itself"`); attempted an unknown `edgeType` (correctly rejected with the full allowlist in the error message); attempted a duplicate active edge (correctly rejected with `"An active edge of this type already exists between these entries"`); created a new edge with a different `edgeType` between an already-connected pair (correctly succeeded, confirming the uniqueness constraint is scoped per-`edgeType` not per-pair); `PATCH`/`DELETE /graph/edges/:id` both verified; neighborhood BFS verified at depth 1 and depth 2 (depth 2 correctly picked up a third edge one hop further out); delete-cascade verified twice — once via direct API calls against a disposable lorebook entry+edge (confirmed both gone from the graph response immediately after `DELETE /api/lorebook/:id`), and once by reasoning through the query-invalidation fix above (real UI re-fetch behavior after a Lorebook-tool deletion). Full real-browser UI pass at `/?story=demo-story-shadows-berlin&tool=relationships` as the logged-in owner test account: ego view defaulted correctly to "Berlin" (alphabetically first), search box found and centered on "Marcus Webb", side panel's Outgoing/Incoming counts matched the migrated data exactly, "Add relationship" → dialog → create → "Edge created" toast → side panel updated, edge-label click → edit dialog → Delete → confirm → "Edge deleted" toast → side panel reverted, "Open entry" correctly switched to the Lorebook tool with the right entry tab focused, Full Graph view rendered all 18 nodes color-coded by category with the 5 migrated edges visible, and the "Migrate legacy relationships" button re-run through the actual UI (not just the API) produced zero duplicates. `tsc --noEmit` (frontend + server) and a full oxlint sweep are clean — briefly introduced 4 new warnings while building `StoryGraphCanvas.tsx`/`StoryGraphEdgeLabel.tsx`/`layout.ts` (three `curly`-style single-statement-if/for cases, one `jsx-a11y` click-without-keyboard-handler on a non-button clickable label), all fixed (the a11y one by using a real `<button>` instead of a `<div>` with an `onClick`), confirmed back to the exact same baseline (28 warnings, 1 error) as every prior phase this session.

## Lorebook Relationship Graph — Pending-Edge Review UI (P1.2), Load-Bearing Decisions

**No schema/migration change needed.** `storyGraphEdges.status` (`active`\|`pending`\|`rejected`) and its partial active-only unique index were built in G1 specifically so this slice could ship without touching the schema — confirmed still true; this pass only added repository/service/route functions and frontend surfaces.

**A minimal manual producer was added alongside the review UI, deliberately, since a review surface with zero producers is untestable.** The user asked specifically for the review UI, not the full AI-suggest-edges pipeline (a separate, much larger G1.5 item involving an LLM job). Rather than ship a review tab nothing could ever populate, the existing "Add relationship" dialog (`EdgeEditDialog.tsx`) gained an opt-in "Propose for review" `Switch`, default off, visible only in create mode — checking it passes `asPending: true` through to `createEdge`, landing the row as `status: "pending", source: "user"` (no new `source` value). The AI-suggestion job, when built later, will write into this exact same `pending` lane and reuse the same approve/reject routes untouched.

**Dedup only guards active edges, not pending ones.** `createEdge`'s existing `findActiveEdge` conflict check (`"An active edge of this type already exists between these entries"`) is now skipped when `status === "pending"` — duplicate pending proposals are harmless (no unique constraint blocks them; a reviewer just rejects the redundant one) and shouldn't block a "propose" action the way they correctly block a direct "add" action.

**`approveEdge` re-runs the exact same active-edge conflict check at approval time, not just at creation time**, since the world can change between when a pending edge was proposed and when it's reviewed (e.g. another active edge for the same triple gets created in between, or a first pending duplicate gets approved before a second one). Verified live: created two pending proposals for the same triple, approved the first (succeeded), then attempted to approve the second — got a clean `400 { error: "Failed to approve edge", details: "An active edge of this type already exists between these entries" }`, not a raw SQLite constraint crash. Reviewer's only recourse today is Reject; no "resolve conflict" UI was built (out of scope for this pass).

**Pending-edge names are resolved server-side** (`listPendingEdges` in `storyGraphService.ts`, a new `fromName`/`toName`-enriched DTO), not left for the frontend to look up against the already-loaded node list. Reason: `getStoryGraph`'s node set only ever includes global/series entries that are referenced by an *active* edge — a pending edge between two such entries (no active edges of their own) would have no name to resolve client-side. Server-side resolution sidesteps this edge case entirely.

**Review surface is a dedicated list-based "Pending" tab (`PendingEdgesPanel.tsx`), not dashed-edge-in-canvas rendering with click-to-approve**, which is what the original design doc's aspirational canvas behavior called for. Deliberately scoped down (same class of narrowing as R4's structured-Codex-field cut) — a third `Tabs` value alongside Ego/Full, with a live count badge, swaps the entire canvas out for a simple approve/reject list when active. Cheaper to build correctly, and canvas edge-styling/interaction was never touched. AI-suggested edges themselves and persisted layout remain separate, unstarted G1.5 items.

**Query-key placement, not new invalidation wiring, keeps the Pending tab free of ghost rows.** The new `usePendingEdgesQuery` lives under `storyGraphKeys.pending(storyId)`, itself under the existing `storyGraphKeys.all` prefix — so `useDeleteLorebookMutation`'s pre-existing `invalidateQueries({ queryKey: storyGraphKeys.all })` (the fix from G1's own entry above) already covers it for free. `deleteEdgesForEntity`'s cascade-delete (`server/routes/lorebook.ts` → `storyGraphService.ts`) was already status-agnostic before this pass (deletes active/pending/rejected rows alike), so no server-side change was needed there either — confirmed by reading the existing code, not assumed.

**Verified end-to-end against the real dev server and DB** (worked around the environment's `tsx watch` hang — see the note below — via the pre-existing `server-only`/`client-only` launch configs instead): proposed a relationship (Elena Cross → Dmitri Volkov, "Knows") with "Propose for review" on → confirmed absent from both Ego and Full graph view (Elena Cross's Outgoing stayed at 0) but present in a new "Pending (1)" tab with correct names/type; Approved it → moved into the active graph (Outgoing became 1), disappeared from Pending; proposed a second (Elena Cross → Berlin) and Rejected it → disappeared from Pending, confirmed it never appeared in the active graph; proposed a third duplicating the now-active Elena Cross → Dmitri Volkov triple, attempted to Approve it → got the clean conflict 400 above, no crash, row stayed in Pending until manually rejected. All test edges cleaned up afterward via the UI (reject) and one direct `DELETE /api/graph/edges/:id` call (the approved one), confirmed the graph was back to its exact original state (Elena Cross Outgoing (0), same 3 Incoming) before finishing. `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean throughout.

**Environment note, not a code bug:** this session's `npm run dev` (`tsx watch server/index.ts`) hung indefinitely with zero output (not even its first `console.log`) across two independent restarts, while a plain `tsx server/index.ts` (no watch) booted normally in ~15s every time. Root cause not fully diagnosed (suspected `tsx watch`'s file-watcher initialization interacting badly with this sandbox, unrelated to this feature's own changes — the same hang reproduced before any of this session's edits were made). Worked around via the repo's pre-existing `.claude/launch.json` `server-only`/`client-only` configs (the same workaround an earlier session recorded for a different dev-server landmine), not by changing any project code.

## Lorebook Entry Editor — "Natural View" (Prose Character Profile), Load-Bearing Decisions

**Decision:** Added an "Advanced Settings" toggle (`AdvancedSettings.tsx`, localStorage-backed via `useNaturalEntryView`, default **on**) that switches `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx` between the existing raw-field form (`RawEntryFields.tsx`, extracted verbatim from the prior inline JSX) and a new `NaturalEntryView.tsx` — a character-profile-styled presentation modeled directly on a reference PDF character sheet the user supplied. Triggered by a real, still-live bug report: several theme classes (`.midnight`/`.forest`/`.sand`/`.graphite`) were rendering a visible light-tinted "block" around the entire Lorebook/Outline/etc. content pane, traced to `MainContent.tsx`'s per-tool `toolTints` map using Tailwind `dark:` variants (which only ever match the literal `.dark` class, never the newer theme classes) — fixed by switching every tint to a single low-opacity mid-tone class with no `dark:` variant (e.g. `bg-cyan-500/5`), applied uniformly across light/dark/all custom themes.

**Both views write to the exact same `react-hook-form` control, not two parallel data models.** `NaturalEntryView` and `RawEntryFields` are two different renderings of identical `CreateEntryForm` fields (`name`, `category`, `importance`, `description`, `codexEnabled`, `codexState.*`) — `NaturalStateSection`/`NaturalCustomFields` reuse the exact same `useFieldArray` calls as `CodexStateEditor`'s `StateListBox`/`CustomFieldsBox`, just styled as flowing "Label: value, value" prose lines instead of boxed pill lists. This means switching the toggle mid-edit never loses unsaved data, and `handleSubmit`/`buildSubmitData` needed zero changes. Verified live: added a wardrobe item and a custom field ("Age: 61 Years Old") in Natural View, switched to Form View, and confirmed both appeared correctly in the raw boxed UI with no data loss.

**Tags are the one field deliberately not rendered in Natural View at all** — the whole point of the toggle, per the user's explicit ask ("a toggle in advanced settings that displays tag info"). Managing tags requires switching back to Form View; this is stated directly in the toggle's own description text, not left for the user to discover.

**Scoped down from a much larger ask, deliberately, mid-conversation.** The user's first framing implied the natural view should also make Character Codex state auto-compile from the manuscript ("the agent should be compiling that as we go along, not the user"). Investigation confirmed no such mechanism exists anywhere (`jobRunner.ts` has no `codex`-scanning job, unlike `distill_memory`/`rag_scan_*`) and that building it would cut against a standing precedent from Agent Framework Phase B (`DECISIONS.md`, above): no silent auto-chaining, suggestions are always manual-trigger + approve. Rather than build that unilaterally, it was split out and recorded as `docs/CURRENT_BACKLOG.md`'s new P0.3 slice **C5** for a separate future conversation; only the editor-UI half (this entry) was built now.

**A genuinely half-built pipeline was found and explicitly *not* completed this pass, on purpose.** While investigating C5, found that Codex's chat-proposal "Edit First" flow is real on the backend (`useReviseProposalMutation`/`chatCodexService.ts`'s `reviseChatProposal`) but has no UI anywhere (`ProposalCard.tsx` only wires Approve/Reject). `NaturalEntryView` was considered as a natural home for that missing "Edit First" surface but was **not** wired to the chat-proposal pipeline in this pass — it only edits the live entry directly, the same as `RawEntryFields` always has. Recorded in `CURRENT_BACKLOG.md`'s C5 note as a follow-up worth doing regardless of C5's automation scope, rather than silently building it in as a surprise side effect of an unrelated toggle.

**`LorebookEntryEditor.tsx` was refactored to extract `RawEntryFields.tsx` out of inline JSX, not just to add the toggle branch.** The file was already near oxlint's 250-line cap; adding an `if/else` branch inline would have pushed it over. Extracting the raw-field block into its own component (mirroring `NaturalEntryView.tsx`'s shape) kept both under the limit and made the two views' symmetry explicit rather than one being a large inline block and the other a named component.

**Verified end-to-end against the real dev server and DB**: confirmed the `MainContent.tsx` tint fix live — the Lorebook entry editor panel, which previously showed a visible lighter block under `.midnight`, now matches the surrounding near-black background exactly (`rgb(9, 9, 12)` body background, confirmed via computed styles). Confirmed Natural View renders by default on a real demo entry (Dmitri Volkov): large-heading name, compact Category/Importance, full untruncated description, no tag chips, "Physical & Concrete State" section with working add/remove for Wardrobe/Appearance/Wounds/Items and Custom Fields. Added a real wardrobe item and custom field through the actual inline-add UI (first attempt exposed a browser-automation quirk with synthetic Enter keydown, not a code bug — confirmed by dispatching a real `KeyboardEvent` via JS and observing `defaultPrevented: true` plus the correct state transition). Toggled to Form View and back, confirming both directions preserve data with no reset. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full-repo oxlint sweep shows the same 13 pre-existing warnings / 0 errors as baseline, none in any file touched this pass (confirmed by diffing file paths, not just the count).

Also included in this pass: Level, Category, and Importance were removed from `NaturalEntryView.tsx` entirely (not just tags) per direct user annotation on a screenshot — these are edited via Form View only now, same treatment as Tags. `LevelScopeFields` is conditionally skipped in `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx` when Natural View is active; the underlying `category` value is still watched internally (via `useWatch`, no visible control) so the Physical & Concrete State section's `category === "character"` gate keeps working. Verified live: both fields disappear from Natural View and reappear correctly in Form View with the same data intact.

## Document Import — Page-Break Leak, Description-Stripping Prompt, and DOCX Image Extraction, Load-Bearing Decisions

**Decision:** Fixed three real bugs in `documentImportService.ts` (the "upload a PDF/DOCX character sheet, get an AI-extracted draft entry" pipeline), reported by the user after importing a real reference document: the embedded photo didn't come through, physical-appearance prose was missing from the resulting Description even though the same facts correctly landed in Codex state, and literal page-break artifacts leaked into the extracted text.

**Page-break leak was a real `pdf-parse` behavior, not application code, and was root-caused rather than patched around.** `parser.getText()` was called with no arguments; `pdf-parse`'s internal `setDefaultParseParameters` sets `params.pageJoiner ??= '\n-- page_number of total_number --'` when the caller passes nothing, inserting a literal `-- 1 of 3 --` style marker between every page's text — mid-sentence, not at a paragraph boundary. Confirmed this isn't theoretical: ran the real fix against the user's own 68-page reference PDF (`G:\comfy\Character Profile_...pdf`, the same file used earlier this session) and found the marker sitting directly inside a sentence ("...5'4" 105 lbs\n\n-- 1 of 3 --\n\n● Physique: Vex is exceptionally thin..."). Fixed by passing `{ pageJoiner: "" }` explicitly — `??=` only falls back on null/undefined, so an explicit empty string sticks, and `getText()`'s own join loop treats a falsy `pageJoiner` as "just join with a blank line, no marker." Re-ran against the same real PDF and confirmed zero markers remain anywhere in the extracted text.

**Description-stripping was a deliberate prompt instruction, not a separate post-processing step — there was nothing to "un-strip," just a rule to reverse.** `toDraft()` copies the LLM's `description` field verbatim; the system prompt explicitly told the model "Do NOT put physical/wardrobe details here," so the model complied and left description as personality/backstory-only, exactly as instructed — never true removal of anything that existed. Changed the rule to the opposite: description should be the full narrative profile in flowing prose, physical/wardrobe details included, explicitly telling the model not to omit them just because they're also captured in the structured `wardrobe`/`appearance`/`wounds`/`items` arrays. This can't be verified without a live LLM call (no `document_import` endpoint configured in this dev environment), so this fix is prompt-only and unverified end-to-end — flagged here rather than silently claimed as tested.

**DOCX image extraction added first with zero new dependencies.** `mammoth.convertToHtml`'s `convertImage` callback (via `mammoth.images.imgElement`) captures the first embedded image as base64 — verified for real: hand-built a minimal valid `.docx` (raw zip + OOXML parts via Python, a tiny embedded PNG) and ran the actual `extractImageFromDocx` logic against it, confirming a correct `data:image/png;base64,...` URL came back with the right length/prefix, and that the surrounding text extraction was unaffected.

**PDF image extraction was initially scoped out, then added after explicitly asking the user, not silently.** `pdf-parse`'s own `getImage()` method exists but only works with the native `canvas` npm package (Cairo bindings) installed — confirmed via `require.resolve` that it wasn't, and this project has repeatedly and deliberately avoided native-binding dependencies for cross-platform Docker-build risk (bcrypt/argon2, `sharp`, per the Lorebook Relationship Graph entry above). Asked the user directly rather than guessing; they chose to add it. `canvas@3.2.3` added to `package.json` (installed cleanly via a prebuilt binary in this dev environment — `require('canvas').createCanvas` verified working immediately, no from-source build needed here). **`Dockerfile` updated in both stages** (builder and production, matching the existing `better-sqlite3` pattern of reinstalling build tools in the production stage too) to add `libcairo2-dev libpango1.0-dev libjpeg-dev libgif-dev librsvg2-dev` — required for `canvas` to build from source on `node:22-slim`, since this minimal base image has none of node-canvas's native deps present and can't rely on a prebuilt binary matching for every target platform. `extractImageFromPdf` picks the **largest** embedded image by pixel area across all pages (not "first found") specifically so a small decorative icon/logo doesn't win over the actual reference photo.

**Data URL → File conversion is synchronous, not a fetch/Blob round trip, so it fits `useForm`'s synchronous `defaultValues`.** `entryFormUtils.ts`'s new `dataUrlToFile` decodes the base64 payload via `atob()` directly into a `Uint8Array` rather than using `fetch(dataUrl).then(r => r.blob())`, avoiding a broader refactor of `getDefaultFormValues`/`useForm` to support async defaults just for this one path. Only applies when `!entry` (a document-import draft, never an existing entry — existing entries' images are already displayed via their own URL, per `ImageUploadField.tsx`'s existing convention).

**Verified for real, not just by reading the diff**: DOCX text+image extraction against a hand-built test file (above); PDF page-marker fix and PDF image extraction both re-verified against the user's actual real 68-page reference PDF (`G:\comfy\Character Profile_...pdf`) — the page-marker fix showed zero markers remaining, and `extractImageFromPdf`'s exact logic correctly picked out the single embedded photo (2048×2048) and saved it to a real file for visual confirmation it was actually the right character photo, not corrupted/garbage data. `tsc --noEmit` clean on both `tsconfig.json` and `server/tsconfig.json`. Full-repo `oxlint` briefly introduced one new warning (a `curly`-style nested-for-loop violation in the first draft of the largest-image-picking logic), rewritten as a `flatMap`/`reduce` pipeline instead — back to the exact same baseline as every prior entry in this file, 28 warnings/1 error, confirming zero new issues anywhere, frontend or server.

## Codex Appearance Relabeling — Labeled Fields Instead of a Flat List

**Decision:** `CodexState.appearance` changed from a flat `CodexStateItem[]` ("jet-black bob", "high cheekbones", ...) to labeled `CodexCustomField[]` ({label: "Hair", value: "jet-black bob"}, {label: "Facial Features", value: "..."}), same shape `customFields` already used. Prompted by the user sharing a real Google Docs HTML export of a reference character sheet (not just a PDF/screenshot this time) showing its "Physical Appearance" section broken into labeled sub-attributes (Hair/Facial Features/Physique/Distinctive Features/Attire) rather than one flat bullet list — the flat shape couldn't represent that without losing the labeling. Explicit user constraint carried over from the document-import fix earlier this session: extending Codex must not regress "this data should still be available in the natural description" — description stays the full narrative regardless of what's also captured structurally.

**Real existing data was at stake, not just a type change — checked before touching anything.** Queried the dev DB directly and found two live entries (`Vesper "Vex" Thorne`, `Maisy June Miller` — both real document-import test imports from this session, not disposable seed data) already had non-empty flat-shape `appearance` arrays. This ruled out a "just change the type, nothing depends on it" approach.

**Chose defensive normalize-on-read over a one-off backfill migration, because `codexState` is an unvalidated JSON blob that a restored historical snapshot can always reintroduce in the old shape.** `codexService.ts`'s `normalizeAppearance` (exported) upgrades legacy `{id, value}` items to `{key: id, label: "", value}` on every read — never downgrades, since all write paths (form, document-import) now produce the new shape directly. **Found and fixed a real gap while wiring this in**: `codexService.ts`'s own `toCodexState` only covers its own codex-specific routes (snapshot/pending-change); the general entry CRUD route (`lorebook.ts`, what the entry editor actually loads through) does its own separate `db.select()` + `transform()` with no normalization at all — would have shipped broken (missing `.label` on old data) if only the codex-service path had been fixed. Both now call the same exported `normalizeAppearance`.

**Blank-label legacy items needed their own display polish, found by actually loading the real affected data, not assumed away.** A naive `${field.label}: ${field.value}` template produces `": 5'6\" 128 lbs"` for a normalized-but-unlabeled legacy item — caught by loading Maisy June Miller's real entry in the browser after the type/normalizer change and seeing it live, not by reasoning about the type alone. Fixed in all three render sites that needed it (`NaturalEntryView.tsx`'s `NaturalLabeledFields`, `CodexStatePreview.tsx`'s badge label, `ragIndexService.ts`'s RAG-indexed text) to fall back to the bare value when `label` is empty. `CodexStateEditor.tsx`'s Form View equivalent didn't need this — label and value are already separate flex-item spans there, so an empty label is just a blank cell, never a stray colon.

**UI generalized rather than duplicated**: `CodexStateEditor.tsx`'s `CustomFieldsBox` became `LabeledFieldsBox` (parameterized by field name/title/placeholders) and is now used for both Appearance and Custom Fields; `NaturalEntryView.tsx`'s `NaturalCustomFields` became `NaturalLabeledFields` the same way. `documentImportService.ts`'s extraction prompt updated so `"appearance"` is now specified as `[{"label","value"}]` with instructions to reuse the source document's own Hair/Facial Features/Physique-style grouping when present.

**Verified end-to-end against the real dev server and DB, using the real affected data, not fresh test fixtures.** Loaded Maisy June Miller's actual entry (pre-existing flat-shape appearance data) in the browser: Natural View rendered all 9 legacy appearance lines correctly with no stray colons after the display fix; Custom Fields (Age/Role/Education/Occupation/Socioeconomic Status) rendered correctly, confirming they were unaffected by the appearance-specific type change; switched to Form View and confirmed the same legacy items render as blank-label/value rows with no crash; added a new labeled field ("Eyes" / "Wide, trusting blue") through the real Form View UI and confirmed it appends correctly alongside the legacy rows in the same list, proving old and new shape coexist safely in one array. Change not saved (no Update click) — the two real entries' stored data is untouched, still in the pre-migration shape on disk, exercising the normalizer path rather than a one-time-fixed database. `tsc --noEmit` clean on both configs; full-repo `oxlint` back to the exact same 28 warnings/1 error baseline.

## Natural View — Section Reorder, "Core Identity" Rename, Quick-Toggle Button

**Decision:** User pointed at the "Custom Fields" heading directly (an annotated screenshot) and asked why Age/Role/Education-type attributes were framed as optional custom extras rather than a standard part of every character sheet, and asked for the layout order to become Name → those attributes → Appearance → Description → Wardrobe/rest, matching the reference document's own Core Identity → Physical Appearance → narrative-prose ordering. Renamed the user-facing label "Custom Fields" → "Core Identity" in both views (the underlying `codexState.customFields` field name is unchanged — this is a display-only rename, no data migration needed) and reordered `NaturalEntryView.tsx` and `CodexStateEditor.tsx` to match: Name (+ Track Character State toggle, moved up from its old spot below Description) → Core Identity + Appearance → Description → Wardrobe/Wounds/Items.

**Splitting the codex-state block across two locations (before and after Description) instead of one contiguous section was a deliberate reading of the user's literal instruction, not an assumption.** The reference document actually groups Wardrobe/Attire inside its own "Physical Appearance" section (before the narrative prose), but the user's own stated order explicitly put Wardrobe after Description — followed that literally rather than the reference document's exact structure, since it was a direct instruction, not a re-interpretation opportunity. The `codexEnabled` watch is reused in two separate conditional blocks (before and after Description) rather than one — safe since `useWatch` just subscribes to the same field twice, no state duplication.

**Added a quick-access "switch view" icon button next to the Name field in both `NaturalEntryView.tsx` and `RawEntryFields.tsx`**, calling the same `useNaturalEntryView()` hook `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx` already uses — no prop-threading needed since the hook is a shared `useSyncExternalStore` (same pattern as `useShowModelThinking`), so any component can call it directly and stay in sync. Previously the only way to toggle views was burying into the collapsed Advanced Settings section every time; this exposes the same toggle inline at the top of the form, in the exact spot the user pointed at.

**Extracted `NaturalStateSection`/`NaturalLabeledFields` into a new `NaturalStateFields.tsx` file**, not just reshuffled in place — the reorder pushed `NaturalEntryView.tsx` to 260 lines, over oxlint's 250-line cap, so the two field-list sub-components (previously colocated in the same file as the main export) moved out, mirroring how `CodexStateEditor.tsx`'s own sub-components already live alongside `LabeledFieldsBox`/`StateListBox` in one file below the cap.

**Verified live**: Maisy June Miller's real entry in the browser, both views — Natural View shows Name+toggle button, Track Character State switch, Core Identity, Appearance, Description, then Wardrobe/Wounds/Items in that order; clicking the toggle button switches to Form View correctly (confirmed via screenshot, not just code reading); Form View shows the same "Core Identity" rename and reordering. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full-repo `oxlint` back to the exact same 28 warnings/1 error baseline, across 546 files (the new `NaturalStateFields.tsx`).

## Image Field Resize, Tool-Tint Removal, Document-Import Timeout, and Draft-Loss Fix

**Image field made much larger (56×56 → previously 20×20 in Tailwind units) and its outer bordered box removed**, per direct user request with a reference size drawn on a screenshot. `ImageUploadField.tsx`'s wrapping `border rounded-md p-3` container (which stretched to the full width of the form column as an empty-looking block, since the actual thumbnail+buttons content didn't fill it) was dropped — the image's own border is now the only visible frame, and action buttons moved from a narrow side column to a row below the now-much-bigger image.

**Removed `MainContent.tsx`'s per-tool colored background tints entirely, not just re-tuned them — a real, reported regression from this session's earlier theme-block fix.** The `bg-cyan-500/5`-style low-opacity tints (added earlier this session to fix a *different* bug — white/light blocks under custom themes, see the "Theme Block Bugs" entry) turned out to introduce their own problem: a fixed hue tint over a custom theme's own background shifts its perceived color away from the theme's actual palette (user reported the Lorebook tool's background reading as "green" under a warm dark theme, not matching that theme's intended color at all). Confirmed via computed styles (`rgba(6, 182, 212, 0.05)` over the Sand theme's `rgb(14, 13, 12)` background). Rather than hunt for a tint value that reads "neutral" across all six themes (fragile, would need re-tuning every time a new theme is added), removed the tint mechanism outright — every tool now uses plain `bg-background`, same as `stories`/`series`/`users` already did. The original tint was a decorative nice-to-have; it's not worth the theme-fidelity cost.

**PDF document import "hanging" investigated end-to-end against the real server, not assumed.** Built a real HTTP client test (login + multipart upload of the user's actual 68-page reference PDF) against the live dev server and confirmed the full pipeline — text extraction, image extraction, LLM call — completes successfully in ~15 seconds with a well-formed draft. This rules out a deterministic server-side hang for this exact file. However, `apiFactory.ts`'s `uploadFile` (and by extension every caller, including document import) had **no timeout at all** — a genuinely stuck request (e.g. an expired OAuth token stuck in a slow/unreachable refresh flow — the configured `document_import` feature endpoint uses Grok OAuth, and its stored token's expiry was suspiciously close to "now" when checked) would spin the "Analyzing..." indicator forever with no error ever surfacing, which **is** indistinguishable from "hung" to a user regardless of root cause. Added an opt-in `timeoutMs` parameter to `uploadFile` (default: no timeout, preserving every existing caller's behavior unchanged) and wired a 2-minute timeout specifically onto `lorebookApi.importDocument` — the one upload whose server-side work includes a third-party LLM call, unlike the other `uploadFile` callers (story/series import, image upload, DB import) which are all local file processing with no equivalent failure mode.

**A real, separate, likely-more-common cause of "the data just goes away" was found and fixed: zero tab persistence.** `LorebookPage.tsx`'s `openTabs` (which holds document-import drafts inline, not by reference) was plain `useState` with no persistence anywhere — confirmed via repo-wide grep that *no* workspace tool component persists its own tab state at all. Since `MainContent.tsx` only ever renders the one active tool (switching tools fully unmounts the previous one), importing a document, then switching to any other workspace tool and back, silently destroyed the unsaved draft with zero warning — a real cost given the draft represents a genuine ~15s+ LLM call, not a trivial one. Fixed by persisting `{ tabs, activeTabIndex }` to `localStorage`, scoped per story/series (`lorebook-tabs-story-<id>` / `lorebook-tabs-series-<id>`) so tabs never leak across different stories, wrapped in `attempt()` (already used elsewhere in this file) so a `QuotaExceededError` from a large embedded image's base64 data URL fails silently rather than crashing the tab switch that triggered it. Existing tolerance in the render path (`activeEntry ? <LorebookEntryTab/> : "This entry no longer exists"`) already handled a restored `entry`-kind tab whose target was deleted or not yet loaded, so no new failure mode there.

**Verified live, not just by reading the diff**: image resize/border removal and background-tint removal both confirmed visually in the browser (Vesper "Vex" Thorne's real entry, Black & Sand theme — image now large with no surrounding empty box, background reads as the theme's actual near-black tone with no cyan cast). Draft persistence verified by seeding `localStorage` with a fake draft tab matching the real `LorebookOpenTab` shape, reloading the page (equivalent to a tool-switch remount), and confirming the exact same draft tab reopened with correct name/description/Create button, then cleaning the test key up afterward. `tsc --noEmit` clean on both configs; full-repo `oxlint` back to the same 28 warnings/1 error baseline.

---

## RAG Scanner Frontend (P0.1) — Load-Bearing Decisions

Implements `docs/CURRENT_BACKLOG.md`'s P0.1: the RAG Scanner backend and job types (`rag_scan_chapter`/`rag_scan_story`, `ragScans`/`ragScanIssues`) shipped with Agent Framework Phase A, but had zero frontend consumers until now. No backend/schema changes were needed — every read/write path the UI needs already existed (`server/routes/rag.ts`'s scan/issue routes, `POST /api/agent/jobs` for triggering).

**Trigger path is job-queue only, never the direct synchronous `/api/rag/scan/*` routes — a deliberate, user-confirmed scope decision, not an oversight.** Two trigger paths exist server-side: `POST /api/rag/scan/chapter|story/:id` (editor-accessible, immediate `ragScans` row, no retry/progress tracking) and `POST /api/agent/jobs` with `jobType: rag_scan_chapter|rag_scan_story` (`requireOwner`-gated, ~3s-tick job runner, gets `agentJobs` progress polling + retry-on-failure for free). Asked the user directly rather than guessing; chose job-queue-only, owner-triggers-only. Consequence: an `editor`-role user can view/resolve/dismiss issues but cannot trigger a scan themselves — `ChapterScannerDrawer.tsx`/`RagScannerPanel.tsx` both gate the trigger button behind `useIsOwner()` and show a plain explanatory line instead for non-owners.

**Both placements shipped, not just one** — also user-confirmed. `EditorToolsPanel.tsx` gained a `"ragScanner"` drawer (chapter-scoped, for scanning-while-writing) and `Sidebar.tsx` gained a `"scanner"` story-wide tool (`RagScannerPanel.tsx`, same shape as `ProjectMemoryTool.tsx`/`StoryGraphTool.tsx`'s thin-wrapper pattern) for scan history + a full cross-chapter issue list with status tabs. Both surfaces share the same hooks (`src/features/rag-scanner/hooks/useRagScanQuery.ts`) and the same `IssueCard.tsx` — the chapter drawer just client-side filters the story-wide issues query down to one `chapterId` rather than hitting a separate endpoint (none exists, and story-wide issue volume doesn't warrant adding one).

**A real gap was found and fixed during live verification, not just assumed away**: the trigger mutations (`useTriggerChapterScanMutation`/`useTriggerStoryScanMutation`) only invalidate scan-history/issues queries at enqueue time — but the actual `ragScans` row a `rag_scan_story` job writes doesn't exist until the job runner picks it up and finishes, seconds to tens of seconds later. Without a second invalidation, a completed scan's history row and any new issues silently didn't appear until some unrelated refetch happened to fire (confirmed live: triggered a story scan, watched it complete via the job-progress poll, and the "Scan history" list still showed only the old rows until a manual page reload). Fixed with `useScanJobWithInvalidation` — wraps the existing self-disabling job-status poll (same `refetchInterval` pattern `useRecentJobsQuery` established) with a `useEffect` that fires one `invalidateStoryScanData` call the moment the polled job's status leaves `queued`/`running`, guarded by a `useRef` so it only fires once per job id.

**Issue status queries use a shared prefix key (`ragScanKeys.issuesForStoryPrefix`) for invalidation, not the status-suffixed key mutations actually read from.** React Query invalidates by array-prefix match; a query keyed `[..., storyId, "open"]` is a sibling of, not a descendant of, `[..., storyId, "all"]`. Every invalidation call in this feature (`useUpdateIssueStatusMutation`, both trigger mutations, `useScanJobWithInvalidation`) targets the 3-element prefix (no status segment) specifically so switching the Open/Resolved/Dismissed tab always sees fresh data regardless of which tab was active when the mutation fired.

**Verified live against the real dev server and DB, as the logged-in owner test account, not just by reading the diff**: triggered a real `rag_scan_story` job from the Scanner sidebar tool — toast, disabled/spinning button, `"Scanning…"` then `"Scanned 3/3 chapters"` progress text, button re-enabled, and (after the invalidation fix) the new `"Story Scan · ... · completed"` row appearing in Scan History without a manual reload. Triggered a real `rag_scan_chapter` job from the Editor's new Scanner drawer on "The Drop" — confirmed the failure-path rendering too (`"Scan failed: Connection error."` in red, a real transient failure from this dev environment's scanner endpoint, not a UI bug). Confirmed Open/Resolved/Dismissed tabs switch cleanly with zero issues present (empty states render correctly; resolve/dismiss mutation code path mirrors the already-proven `ProposalCard`/`useApproveProposalMutation` pattern and calls the exact existing `PATCH /api/rag/issues/:issueId` route, not exercised against a real issue row live since this test story had none). `tsc --noEmit` clean.

## RAG Scanner — Inline Highlighting (P0.1 follow-up) — Load-Bearing Decisions

Closes the one piece of the original `docs/2026-06-26_RAG_Scanner_Integration.md` design that never shipped in the P0.1 pass above: "subtle highlighting on flagged spans with tooltips" in the editor itself, distinct from and unrelated to the separate `ragScans`/`agentJobs` dual-write cleanup tracked under P1.1 — this feature only reads existing `ragScanIssues.evidence[]` data, no job-system changes.

**Matching, timing, and staleness handling were all confirmed with the user up front rather than assumed**, since evidence rows only ever stored a quoted `excerpt` string, never a character offset or Lexical node key: (1) exact substring match only, no fuzzy matching — a paraphrased or since-edited excerpt just doesn't highlight; (2) computed on-demand when the chapter mounts and the open-issues list changes, never recomputed per-keystroke; (3) an unmatched excerpt is dropped silently, no "can't locate" affordance in the editor (the issue still lists normally in the drawer/panel either way).

**Built by mirroring `GrammarMarkNode`/`GrammarCheckPlugin` (LanguageTool live spellcheck) almost line-for-line, not designed from scratch** — that feature had already solved the exact same shape of problem (an ephemeral, never-persisted `@lexical/mark` `MarkNode` subclass wrapping flagged spans, stripped from the doc JSON before save, click-to-popover interaction). New `RagIssueMarkNode` (`src/components/story-editor/nodes/RagIssueMarkNode.ts`) carries only `issueId`+`severity` (not description/suggestedFix — the popover looks the full issue up from the already-fetched `useStoryIssuesQuery` result by id, no duplicated/stale data on the node itself). `$findTextNodeRange` (`beatTextSearch.ts`, originally built for Concrete Beats' AI-suggestion accept flow) was reused completely unchanged — it was already generic single-TextNode exact-substring search with graceful `null` fallback, exactly the primitive this needed.

**`stripGrammarMarks.ts` generalized into `stripEphemeralMarks.ts` rather than adding a second near-duplicate strip function** — same recursive JSON-unwrap logic, now parameterized over a list of ephemeral mark type strings (`grammar-mark`, `rag-issue-mark`). One call site (`SaveChapterContent/index.tsx`) updated; that plugin's `tags.has(...)` save-skip guard extended to also skip on a new `"rag-issue-highlight"` tag, mirroring the existing `"grammar-check"` tag, so applying/removing highlights never debounce-triggers a spurious save.

**Resolve/Dismiss unwraps the mark immediately, client-side, rather than waiting for the mutation's query-invalidation round-trip** — `RagIssueHighlightPlugin`'s `handleUpdateStatus` fires `updateStatusMutation.mutate(...)` and, in the same handler, walks `$nodesOfType(RagIssueMarkNode)` to unwrap the matching mark right away, so the highlight disappears the instant the user acts instead of waiting on the network round-trip + refetch. The plugin also reconciles removals on every open-issues-list change (an issue resolved/dismissed elsewhere — the drawer, the story-wide panel — drops out of the `open` query and its highlight is unwrapped without needing a click here), using the same `$nodesOfType(...)` walk-and-compare pattern `BeatMarkSyncPlugin.tsx` already established for beat-mark cleanup, rather than a stored id→key map.

**A real, unrelated live-data incident happened during manual browser verification, not caused by the shipped code.** While hand-correcting a typo introduced by test clicks in the demo story's "The Drop" chapter (not a scratch chapter — same category of mistake flagged in P1.5's own DECISIONS.md entry), an over-eager `Ctrl+Z` sequence briefly wiped the whole chapter editor to empty; `Ctrl+Shift+Z` (redo) recovered it, but a residual corrupted word ("half" → "h f") had already round-tripped through the chapter's own debounced autosave. Fixed via a direct, targeted string replace through the app's own `PUT /api/chapters/:id` endpoint (the same call the editor's autosave itself makes) rather than continued imprecise mouse/keyboard automation; the full chapter text was diffed against the original page-text dump captured before testing began and confirmed byte-for-byte correct afterward. **Lesson, restated from P1.5**: use a disposable scratch chapter/story for any testing that involves live typing/editing, not existing demo data — this session used a scratch script-seeded scan/issue pair correctly, but the manual text-repair detour should have happened on a throwaway chapter instead.

**Verified live in the Browser pane against the real dev DB** (two disposable issues seeded via a one-off script mirroring the project's established `_b4_check.ts`-style precedent, cleaned up after): an exact-match evidence excerpt rendered with the correct severity-colored solid underline (visually distinct from Grammar's wavy underline and Concrete Beats' flat background block, by design); a deliberately paraphrased excerpt correctly produced no highlight and no console error; click opened the popover with correct severity/description/suggested-fix/evidence; Resolve unwrapped the highlight immediately and the `PATCH /api/rag/issues/:id` call confirmed the status change persisted server-side; an edit to unrelated text elsewhere in the chapter left the mark intact (inherited `MarkNode` edit-survival behavior); the saved chapter JSON was confirmed free of any `"rag-issue-mark"` node type while the underlying prose text remained present. `npm run build` (tsc client+server + vite) clean throughout.
