## Story-Layer Chapter Versioning (P0.2) — Load-Bearing Decisions

**Scope correction mid-session, recorded because it changed the whole feature shape.** P0.2 in `docs/CURRENT_BACKLOG.md` was originally scoped (and partially implemented — schema, repository, routes, all discarded) as linear chapter history: auto-snapshot-on-a-timer + restore, directly mirroring Codex's `codexSnapshots`/restore precedent. The user corrected this mid-build: this feature is chat-branching-style alternate drafts, not undo history — a chapter can have several parallel "temp" versions shown as tabs next to the main chapter, created by AI regenerate or manual duplicate, independently editable, and only ever reach the live manuscript via an explicit one-way "compile" action. The two are genuinely different data models (siblings vs. a timeline) and the discarded linear-history code shared nothing with what got built. Confirmed via direct check: the backlog's own reference to a `2026-06-26_Versioning_Branching_Design.md` doc for the "don't conflate with this" warning does not exist in the repo — it was an aspirational placeholder, so there was no prior design surface to reconcile against either way.

**Scoped via 4 direct questions rather than guessed, all answered as recommended-with-edits:** (1) versions created via AI regenerate and manual duplicate, both; (2) flat list, not a branching tree — "temp files to be compiled to the final chapter output"; (3) main chapter is authoritative the whole time, versions are inert until an explicit compile; (4) view/edit in tabs by default, with an optional side-by-side compare toggle rather than always-on diffing.

**New `chapterVersions` table, deliberately unlike `codexSnapshots` in one key way: no `sourceRef`/restore-chain field.** Codex snapshots form a chain (`restoreSnapshot` creates a new snapshot referencing what it restored from) because Codex is linear history. Chapter versions are flat siblings with one one-way exit (compile) — there's nothing to chain. Columns: `chapterId` (FK, cascade), `content` (Lexical JSON, same shape as `chapters.content`), `sourceType` (`'ai' | 'manual'`), `label` (nullable, user-settable at any time — not retroactive-only like Codex's, since a manual "Save Version" moment has an obvious point to ask for a name), `createdAt`/`updatedAt` (versions get their own ongoing edits, unlike Codex's append-only snapshots, so — unlike `chapters`, which has no `updatedAt` at all — this table needed one from day one).

**Compile is genuinely irreversible, not "non-destructive" like the rest of this project's save/history features — called out explicitly in its own confirmation dialog.** It copies a version's content into `chapters.content` with no automatic backup of what's being replaced (`compileVersionToChapter` in `chapterVersionsRepository.ts`). This is a deliberate departure from CLAUDE.md's "non-destructive history" principle for Codex/RAG-scanner-adjacent features, accepted because the user explicitly scoped this phase as not linear undo history — pairing compile with real undo would mean building the P0.2-that-wasn't (chapter snapshots) after all. Worth revisiting if this friction shows up in practice; noted here rather than silently decided.

**Trigger path for AI regenerate mirrors Humanizer, not the streaming "Generate with prompt" path** — non-streaming `buildClientForFeature("chapter_version")` call (new `FeatureKey`), whole rewritten chapter returned in one response with a loading spinner in `GenerateVersionDialog.tsx`. Simpler for a first pass; a multi-thousand-word wait with no progressive feedback is the accepted tradeoff, flagged as a candidate for streaming later if it feels slow in practice.

**A hand-rolled plain-text to Lexical converter (`textToLexicalContent` in `chapterVersionAiService.ts`), not `@lexical/headless`.** The AI service returns plain prose text (via the existing `extractTextFromLexical` reused unchanged for the other direction); turning that back into editable Lexical JSON server-side needed some conversion, and `@lexical/headless` isn't a dependency of this project. Rather than add it for one call site, the exact minimal paragraph-node shape was confirmed by inspecting real stored chapter content directly (`server/data/demo-story-shadows-berlin.json`) and hand-built: split on blank lines into paragraph nodes, one text child each. Deliberately not a markdown parser — good enough for a first AI draft; the user's own editing (bold/italic/etc.) takes over from there.

**Version tabs get a bespoke, deliberately lighter Lexical composer (`VersionEditor.tsx`), not the main editor's `App.tsx`/`Editor.tsx` reused wholesale.** Checked first: `Editor.tsx` hardcodes several plugins — `WordCountPlugin`, the focus-session button in `ToolbarPlugin` — that resolve "which chapter" via `useEditorChapterId()`'s fallback to the global `StoryContext` when no `EditorPaneProvider` wraps them. A version tab isn't (and shouldn't be) wrapped in one, so reusing those plugins as-is would silently report a version's word count, or let a focus session start, against whatever chapter happens to be globally active — wrong data, not just wrong UX. `VersionEditor.tsx` reuses only the content-agnostic pieces (`PlaygroundNodes`, `PlaygroundEditorTheme`, `RichTextPlugin`, `HistoryPlugin`, `MarkdownShortcutPlugin`, `AutoFocusPlugin`) plus a new minimal `VersionToolbar.tsx` (undo/redo/bold/italic/underline only) and forked `LoadChapterVersionContentPlugin`/`SaveChapterVersionContentPlugin` (same debounce/`toJSON` shape as the chapter originals, scoped to a `versionId` instead, with no beat-detection/RAG-reindex side debounces — a draft isn't part of the manuscript yet). Not integrated into `EditorMultiView`'s pane/split/floating-panel/layout-persistence system, despite that system already supporting "two independent live editors side by side" (which the Compare toggle also needed) — grafting a version concept into machinery built for "which chapter is this pane showing" was judged more structural risk than the toolbar duplication it would have saved.

**A real bug found and fixed during live verification, not just assumed away.** `useUpdateVersionContentMutation` (a version's autosave) originally did nothing to the React Query cache on success, on the theory that the actively-focused editor already has the latest content live and doesn't need it reflected anywhere else. This broke the moment a second editor instance for the same version mounted — e.g. toggling Compare — which loads its initial content from `LoadChapterVersionContentPlugin`'s `content` prop, sourced from the (now-stale) cached version list. Confirmed live: edited a version, toggled Compare, and the second pane showed content from before the edit. Fixed by having the autosave mutation's `onSuccess` patch the list cache in place via `queryClient.setQueryData` (not a full invalidate/refetch, which would either do nothing useful for the already-loaded editor or thrash unnecessarily) — mirrors `useUpdateChapterMutation`'s own `setQueryData` pattern in `useChaptersQuery.ts`.

**A separate, pre-existing data-quality issue was found while chasing the above, confirmed unrelated to this feature, and left alone.** `demo-chapter-1`'s own `chapters.content` (not `chapterVersions`) already contains a structurally malformed paragraph — three originally-separate paragraphs collapsed into one text node joined by a literal double-newline, with a stray extra period and an incorrect italic flag — verified directly against `GET /api/chapters/demo-chapter-1` outside of any version. Every new duplicate/generate/save path here faithfully round-trips whatever the source chapter contains, corruption included, without introducing any new corruption of its own (confirmed by a controlled single-character edit test whose save came back byte-for-byte correct apart from the intended change). Root cause not investigated further — it predates this session and sits in the main chapter editor's save path, out of scope for chapter versioning.

**Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server and DB, as the logged-in owner test account:** manual duplicate (toast, new tab, content matches Main, edits fully isolated from Main confirmed by switching tabs both directions), a real AI-generate call with an instruction (`buildClientForFeature("chapter_version")` against this dev environment's actual configured model, roughly 15-20s wait, genuinely different and coherently rewritten prose, correctly split into clean paragraph nodes), the Compare toggle (side-by-side Main + version panes, independently scrollable), Compile (confirmation dialog copy read correctly, real content transfer into `chapters.content` confirmed by switching to Main and seeing the AI draft's text, `["chapters"]` cache invalidation reflected without a manual reload), and Delete (confirmation dialog, tab removed, toast, Main unaffected). `tsc --noEmit` and `oxlint` both clean (extracted `chaptersApi` out of `src/services/api/client.ts` into its own `chaptersClient.ts` file, matching that file's existing per-feature-file precedent, since this feature's one-line re-export addition pushed it over the 250-line cap; fixed an `autoFocus` a11y warning and a `versions` exhaustive-deps warning introduced along the way).

## Chapter Content Undo/Restore (P0.2b) — Load-Bearing Decisions

Implements the backlog item split out after P0.2's scope correction (see the "Story-Layer Chapter Versioning (P0.2)" entry above) — linear undo history for the *main* chapter's own content, mirroring the Codex `codexSnapshots`/restore precedent this time for real. Reused the three scoping answers the user gave earlier in that same corrected session (coarse ~15min auto-checkpoints + manual named saves; unbounded retention like Codex; view/restore only, no diff view) rather than re-asking, since they were answered in service of exactly this feature even though the build that immediately followed went a different direction.

**New `chapterSnapshots` table, closely mirroring `codexSnapshots`** — `id`, `chapterId` (FK, cascade), `content` (Lexical JSON), `sourceType` (`'auto' | 'manual' | 'restore'`), `sourceRef` (the snapshot restored from, or the compiled version id for a pre-Compile safety checkpoint), `label`, `createdAt`. Unlike `chapterVersions` (P0.2, flat siblings, no chain), this table forms a restore chain the same way Codex's does.

**`'auto'` snapshots are throttled server-side (~15min per chapter), computed inside the chapter content `PUT` route itself** — `chapters.ts`'s generic CRUD `PUT /:id` is overridden in `customRoutes` (same override-and-delegate pattern `lorebook.ts` already established) to call `maybeAutoSnapshot(chapterId, oldContent, newContent)` before applying a content change, snapshotting the *old* content once the last snapshot (of any type) is stale enough. Chapters autosave continuously while typing, unlike Codex's discrete per-mutation snapshots, so a naive "snapshot on every save" policy would produce hundreds of rows per session — flagged as a real requirement during P0.2b's scoping and confirmed by measuring real demo content (~18 bytes/word as full Lexical JSON).

**Manual "Save Version" reads the chapter's already-autosaved DB content, not a value pushed from the live editor** — `POST /:chapterId/snapshots` takes no content in its body. Considered threading the live editor's `editor.getEditorState()` through instead (to avoid the ~1s autosave-lag edge case), but the History drawer lives in `EditorToolsPanel.tsx`, a sibling of the `LexicalComposer` tree, with no clean access to the live editor instance without new plumbing — accepted the small lag risk rather than add that.

**Compile (from P0.2) and Restore both now take an unconditional safety checkpoint before their one-way overwrite** — `compileVersionToChapter` and `restoreSnapshot` both call a new `safetySnapshot` helper that bypasses the 15-minute throttle entirely, since these are rare, deliberate, high-stakes actions rather than routine autosaves. This directly closes the gap flagged when Compile shipped in the P0.2 entry above ("a one-way, un-backed-up overwrite... adds one more way to lose the previous state, not fewer"). Restore's confirmation dialog can now honestly say "nothing is lost," matching Codex's own restore copy, instead of inheriting Codex's *implicit* safety (every mutation snapshotted) without actually having it.

**A real ordering bug was found and fixed during live verification**: `chapterSnapshots.createdAt` has the same 1-second resolution as every other timestamp column in this codebase (Drizzle's `mode: "timestamp"` floors to epoch seconds), and both `restoreSnapshot` and `compileVersionToChapter` create two rows back-to-back in one request — easily landing in the same second. Confirmed live: a restore's safety-checkpoint and its result snapshot tied on `createdAt`, and `ORDER BY createdAt DESC` alone returned them oldest-first instead of newest-first (SQLite's default tie-break happened to preserve insertion order, not reverse it). Fixed with a secondary `ORDER BY ..., desc(sql\`rowid\`)` (drizzle-orm's `sql` template tag for a raw column reference) — SQLite's implicit rowid increases monotonically with insertion, giving a reliable tie-break. Confirmed via direct API inspection before and after the fix (`{auto, restore}` order → `{restore, auto}` order).

**The "Current" badge is based on an actual content comparison against the live chapter, not list position** — a real bug caught live before it shipped: `CodexHistoryPanel.tsx`'s precedent marks `index === 0` (newest in the list) as current, which is safe for Codex because every mutation is snapshotted, so the newest snapshot genuinely is always current. Chapter snapshots are throttled, so the live chapter can easily have moved on from the most recent snapshot with no new one created yet — confirmed live: right after a manual save, a further (unsnapshotted) edit still showed the stale save as "Current" under the naive index-based check. `ChapterHistoryDrawer` now takes the chapter's live `content` as a prop (already available via `EditorToolsPanel`'s `currentChapter`) and compares each snapshot's content directly.

**A serious race condition was found and fixed during live verification: Restore (and, by the same logic, Compile) could silently undo itself seconds after succeeding.** The original `refreshChapterContent()` mechanism (added so an already-mounted editor picks up an externally-changed chapter — `LoadChapterContentPlugin`'s "load once per chapterId" gate has no other way to learn this) just bumped a token and relied on `invalidateQueries` to have already refreshed the cached chapter by the time `LoadChapterContentPlugin` re-read it. Confirmed live, repeatedly, before being fixed: the token bump could fire *before* the invalidated query's background refetch resolved, so `LoadChapterContentPlugin` reloaded the editor from the **still-stale, pre-restore** cached content — which then looked like a fresh (dirty) edit and got autosaved right back over the database a second later, permanently reverting the restore with no error, toast, or other visible sign anything had gone wrong. Caught only because a test marker string was checked directly against the DB after the fact, not by watching the UI. Fixed in both `useRestoreSnapshotMutation` and `useCompileVersionMutation` by writing the mutation's own (already-fresh) chapter response straight into the `["chapters", chapterId]` query cache via `setQueryData` *before* bumping the refresh token, removing the race entirely rather than narrowing its window. `chaptersKeys`' own factory isn't exported from `useChaptersQuery.ts`, so both call sites spell out its "detail" key shape directly, matching the precedent already set for the "byStory" prefix elsewhere in this feature.

**Environment note, not a code issue: this session hit a `concurrently`-specific hang on the dev machine.** `npm run dev`'s `concurrently "npm run dev:server" "npm run dev:client"` repeatedly left the server child silently stuck (no boot log, no error, no crash) while the client child booted normally, across multiple full process-tree restarts. Running `tsx watch server/index.ts` directly (bypassing `concurrently`) booted correctly and immediately every time, isolating the hang to `concurrently`'s process handling on this machine rather than the server code. Worked around by launching the server and `vite` as two separate background processes instead of through `concurrently` for the remainder of this session's verification — not a code change, recorded here only so a future session doesn't re-diagnose the same thing from scratch.

**Verified live end-to-end against a genuinely fresh process (both the concurrently hang and, separately, a stale-HMR "hooks order changed" React warning were both ruled out this way — neither reproduced from a clean server+client boot and a real page load, confirming both were session/tooling artifacts, not code bugs):** a manual save, an edit, and a restore all round-tripped correctly with the ordering and "Current"/race fixes in place — confirmed via direct `GET /api/chapters/:id` and `GET /api/chapters/:id/snapshots` checks (not just UI observation, given the race bug above was invisible in the UI) that the restored content stuck and stayed stuck. `tsc --noEmit` (both configs) and `oxlint` clean, back to the exact same 27-warning/1-error baseline as before this session's changes.

---

## Notes/Outline ↔ Chat Bridge (P0.3, "Core Bridge" scope: N0-N4 + O1-O4) — Load-Bearing Decisions

Implements `docs/CURRENT_BACKLOG.md`'s P0.3 against the locked design in `docs/Notes_Outline_Chat_Bridges_Design.md` (2026-07-18). Scope chosen directly by the user from three options: the full read-side double-gate pipeline for both notes and outline items (schema, RAG indexing, chat toggles, context injection, export/import) — **not** N5/N6 (save-as-note, note-proposal chat UX) and **not** C1-C5 (project-memory chat toggle), both left for a follow-up pass.

**Schema: `notes.includeInAi` / `outlineItems.includeInAi` / `aiChats.includeNotes` / `aiChats.includeOutline`, all `NOT NULL DEFAULT false`** (migration `0033_cultured_magneto.sql`). Frontend `Note`/`OutlineItem`/`AIChat` types mark these fields **optional**, not required, even though the DB column is `NOT NULL` — several existing client call sites construct these objects for `POST` without every field (server fills the default), matching this codebase's existing convention for other server-defaulted fields (e.g. `AIChat.updatedAt?`).

**RAG entity types extended to `"note" | "outline_item"`, staying fully opt-in — never added to `DEFAULT_SEARCH_ENTITY_TYPES`.** Mirrors the exact `agent_memory` precedent from Agent Framework Phase B: new thin `indexNote`/`indexOutlineItem` wrappers in `ragIndexService.ts` (same shape as `indexAgentMemory`), added to `rag.ts`'s `VALID_ENTITY_TYPES` allowlist, and a new valid-keys pass in `reconcileIndexJob.ts` for armed (`includeInAi=true`) rows — no staleness check needed (same reasoning as the `agent_memory` block: routes/notes.ts and routes/outline.ts always reindex synchronously on write, so drift can only mean "should be removed," never "should be refreshed").

**Notes/outline routes were rewritten from bare `createCrudRouter` calls (notes.ts had zero custom routes at all) into the same override-POST/PUT/DELETE pattern `lorebook.ts` already established** — `syncNoteIndex`/`syncOutlineItemIndex` fire-and-forget an index call when a row is armed, and synchronously call `removeEntityFromIndex` the moment a row is un-armed or deleted (including outline's existing cascade-delete-children path, which now also de-indexes every cascaded child id, not just the parent).

**`chatContextService.getChatContext` builds `entityTypes` conditionally per-chat** (`chat.includeNotes`/`chat.includeOutline` read alongside the existing `chatType` read) and adds two new resolver functions, `resolveNotes`/`resolveOutlineItems`, structurally identical to `resolveCodexEntries`/`resolveChapterPassages` minus the anchor concept (notes/outline have no "this chat was opened from it" relationship — always `role: "search"`, always gated to `[]` when the chat's own toggle is off, same as `includeChapters` already gates `relevantChapterPassages`). The non-canon framing text injected into the chat's `codexContext` string uses the design doc's exact wording (§3) verbatim.

**Brainstorm chats are deliberately wired narrower than World-Building/Research/Editor(-excluded), not left out entirely.** Investigated first: Brainstorm's `ChatInterface.tsx` (a separate component from `features/chat/ChatInterface.tsx`, despite both ultimately writing to the same `aiChats` table) never calls `chatContextService`/`GET /chats/:id/context` at all — it's a fully manual, explicit-selection context model (`ContextSelector`'s chapter-summary/lorebook checkboxes), by original design. Pulling in the *full* `getChatContext` for Brainstorm would have silently started auto-injecting codex/chapter search hits it has never surfaced before — a real behavior change beyond what was asked. Instead, Brainstorm's `ChatInterface.tsx` gained its own toggle pair + a narrow `chatsApi.getContext` fetch that extracts *only* `relevantNotes`/`relevantOutlineItems` from the response (discarding `relevantCodexEntries`/`relevantChapterPassages`) and splices them into a new `codexContext` key on its `PromptParserConfig.additionalContext` — a key `BrainstormResolvers.ts` already reads generically (added for the other chat types, never previously populated by Brainstorm), so no prompt-template change was needed.

**Two real, pre-existing bugs were found (not introduced by this work) while verifying the export/import round-trip, and both were fixed since they sat directly in the code path being verified:**
1. `server/routes/stories.ts`'s lorebook-entry import block spread `...entry` (a JSON-round-tripped row, so `updatedAt` arrives as a string) without overriding `updatedAt` the way it already overrides `createdAt` — any lorebook entry with a non-null `updatedAt` crashed the entire import with `TypeError: value.getTime is not a function` (drizzle's `mode: "timestamp"` column calls `.getTime()` on whatever it's given). Fixed by adding the same override `createdAt` already gets.
2. The story `DELETE /:id` route's `db.transaction(async tx => {...})` throws `TypeError: Transaction function cannot return a promise` on every call — better-sqlite3's transaction API is synchronous-only and does not support an async callback. **Not fixed in this pass** — unrelated to the notes/outline bridge, touching it safely means restructuring the delete flow's two-step (lorebook cascade + story delete) logic, which deserves its own change, not a drive-by inside this one. Worked around during verification by deleting test-imported stories directly via SQLite instead of the API. Flagged here as a real bug for a future P2 pass.

**Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server and DB, as the logged-in owner test account** (not just by reading the diff): armed a real demo note ("MI6 Tradecraft Research") via its new toggle in the Notes tool — confirmed a `ragChunks` row appeared with the expected content, then confirmed it was removed (note row unchanged) after toggling back off. Accepted a pending AI-suggested outline chapter, armed it via the new outline toggle, confirmed the same index/de-index behavior. Opened a real story-scoped World-Building chat, flipped both new toggles on, and confirmed via direct `GET /api/chats/:id/context` calls (with matching query text, since the demo environment has no embedding endpoint configured and hybrid search falls back to keyword-only — an unrelated, pre-existing environment fact, not a bug) that `relevantNotes`/`relevantOutlineItems` populate correctly, and confirmed the double gate itself works by flipping `includeNotes` off via a direct `PATCH` and watching `relevantNotes` immediately return empty while the note stayed armed. Ran a full story export via the real `GET /:id/export` endpoint, then a full import via `POST /stories/import`, and confirmed via direct DB inspection that the imported story's notes/outline items round-tripped with `includeInAi` intact, `parentId`/`chapterId` correctly remapped to new ids (two-pass outline import), and the armed note/outline item were reindexed under the new story id with fresh `ragChunks`. `npm run build` (tsc client + server + vite) clean.

**Environment note, not a code issue, reproducing the same pattern already recorded in the P0.2b entry above: `npm run dev`'s `concurrently` wrapper again left the server child silently stuck on this machine across multiple restarts**, while `server-only`/`client-only` launch configs (bypassing `concurrently`) booted correctly every time. Same workaround as before — not re-diagnosed further since it's already documented as a known environment quirk, not a code bug.

---

## Project Memory Chat Toggle (C1) — Load-Bearing Decisions

Implements `docs/CURRENT_BACKLOG.md`'s P0.3 slice C1 — "Opt-in 'Include project memory' for chat/context" — the one piece of Agent Framework Phase B's RAG plumbing (`agent_memory` entity type, indexing, `reconcile_index` valid-keys) that was never actually wired to a chat-level toggle. Done as a direct follow-on to the Notes/Outline bridge above, reusing the exact same shape: new `aiChats.includeMemory` boolean column (migration `0034_chilly_epoch.sql`), a `resolveMemories` function in `chatContextService.ts` mirroring `resolveNotes`/`resolveOutlineItems`, and matching toggle UI in both `features/chat/ChatInterface.tsx` and `features/brainstorm/ChatInterface.tsx`.

**No per-item flag needed on the memory side of the gate, unlike notes/outline.** Notes/outline needed a double gate (`includeInAi` + chat toggle) because most notes/outline items are never meant to reach a model. Project Memory already has its own single-item gate baked into Phase B's lifecycle: a memory only ever gets indexed once it reaches `status: "active"` (`agentMemoriesService.ts`'s approve step), so the chat-level `includeMemory` toggle is the *only* opt-in this feature needed — there was nothing to double-gate against.

**Framing text deliberately differs from notes/outline's "working material, not canon."** Design doc §4.5 (`docs/Agent_Framework_And_Project_Memory_Design.md`) describes active memories as "distilled/approved facts" (its own S3 tier, above notes/outline's S0/S1). `chatContextService.ts`'s injected packet reads `[PROJECT MEMORY — approved facts] ... Treat as established unless it conflicts with the Codex, in which case the Codex wins` — presented as project fact, with the Codex (not "if it also appears in Codex/lorebook") as the sole override authority, matching memory's higher trust tier.

**`bridgeContext`/`setBridgeContext` in Brainstorm's `ChatInterface.tsx`** is `notesOutlineContext`/`setNotesOutlineContext` renamed (not a new state slot) — it now carries notes, outline, and memory packets together, so the old name would have been misleading going forward.

**Verified live against the real dev server and DB, as the logged-in owner test account:** created a real active memory via `POST /api/agent/memories` (goes active immediately for user-authored notes, per `agentMemoriesService.ts`), confirmed it was indexed (`ragChunks` row with `entityType: "agent_memory"`), flipped a real story-scoped World-Building chat's `includeMemory` toggle on via the UI (rendered correctly: "Include Project Memory (approved facts)"), and confirmed via direct `GET /api/chats/:id/context` that `relevantMemories` populates with the expected title/category/excerpt — then confirmed the gate itself by flipping the toggle off and watching `relevantMemories` return empty. `npm run build` clean. Test memory and chat toggle state cleaned up afterward.

---

## Editor Selection Rework + Codex Proposal Tray (P0.4 R0-R3) — Load-Bearing Decisions

Implements slices R0-R3 of `docs/Chat_Panel_Integrations_Design.md` (§2, §2.1, §3, §9) — the user's chosen starting slice of the much larger P0.4 design (deferred: B0-B5 Brainstorm playbook migration, S0-S5 Research web search, K0-K5 Notes desk polish, and the Editor/Outline `chatType` split, R6-R7). Replaces the floating toolbar's one-shot Expand/Rewrite/Shorten (`selection_specific` prompt, immediate `RangeSelection.insertText()`, no review step) with a chat-bound "Rework in chat" flow, and moves Codex proposals for Editor(/Outline, which shares the same rail) chats out of inline-under-message rendering into a persistent tray.

**A real correctness bug in `activeChapterEditorStore.ts` was found and fixed before building on it, not after.** The store held one global `{chapterId, editor}` slot, reassigned on every pane mount and Lexical `FOCUS_COMMAND` — fine for the existing prose-proposal Accept (happens moments after typing, focus rarely moves away yet) but not for Selection Rework, which is explicitly multi-turn: a user can highlight in chapter A, chat for several turns, quite plausibly focus a different chapter/pane meanwhile, and still expect Accept to find chapter A's editor. Changed to a `Map<chapterId, LexicalEditor>` of every currently-mounted editor (dropping `setActiveChapterEditor`/the `FOCUS_COMMAND` listener entirely — nothing needs "which pane is currently focused," only "is a live editor for this chapter still mounted"). Strictly improves the pre-existing prose-proposal Accept path too, not just the new feature.

**`FocusTarget`/`FocusPacket` capture uses Lexical node keys + offsets, not just plain text** (`src/features/rework/adapters/chapterSelectionAdapter.ts`) — captured once at "Rework in chat" click time, re-resolved at Accept time (potentially many chat turns later) via `$getNodeByKey` + `$createRangeSelection`. A safety check (`selection.getTextContent() === target.text`) guards against the chapter having changed in the meantime.

**Degrade-on-mismatch, not fuzzy-search, when the captured selection no longer resolves cleanly.** If the target's node keys are gone, or the reconstructed selection's text no longer matches what was captured, `applyChapterSelectionReplace` does **not** guess at a different location (e.g. searching for the phrase elsewhere in the chapter) — that risks silently editing the wrong occurrence, which is worse than doing nothing. Instead it falls back to the already-shipped `insertProposedProse` insert-at-cursor-or-end path and returns `"selection-changed"`, surfaced to the user via toast ("...inserted instead of replacing; please check placement"). If the chapter has no mounted editor at all, returns `"not-found"` and the proposal card stays visible so the user can retry after opening that chapter, rather than discarding the model's work. Matches this codebase's established degrade-to-known-state convention (`resolveAnchorAndRelated`/`resolveAnchorChapter` returning `[]` on a stale reference, not guessing).

**No new proposal fence type for rework replies — the existing `prose-proposal` fence is reused, with "replace vs insert" tracked as client-side per-message state.** `docs/Chat_Panel_Integrations_Design.md` §3.3 explicitly allows "extend fence types **or reuse** ... with target metadata," and whether Accept should replace-in-place or insert-new is never something the model needs to encode — `ChatInterface.tsx`'s `proseProposals` state changed from `Record<string, string>` to `Record<string, { text: string; target: FocusTarget | null }>`, snapshotting `activeRework?.target` at the moment `onProseProposal` fires. `handleAcceptProse` branches on `target` presence. Avoided a second parser file, a near-duplicate card component, and a second server-side instructions string to keep in sync with the first. Server-side, `PROSE_PROPOSAL_INSTRUCTIONS` (`chatContextService.ts`) gained one added sentence: when `[SELECTION REWORK]` context is present in the conversation, the fenced block should replace SELECTION only, not repeat BEFORE/AFTER.

**"Rework in chat" resolves to an existing chat anchored to the target chapter if one exists, rather than always creating a new one** — genuinely new UX in this codebase (no prior precedent; World-Building's `anchorEntryId` is write-only metadata for context assembly, never read back for chat *selection*). Zero matches → create; exactly one → select silently; more than one (a user can legitimately have several named Editor chats anchored to the same chapter) → select the most-recently-updated **and** toast `Continuing in "<chat title>"`, since silently guessing which of several conversations to resume is otherwise an invisible decision the user has no way to notice or correct.

**A second real, pre-existing bug was found and fixed while verifying the "resolve to existing anchored chat" flow**: `useCreateChatMutation`/`useUpdateChatMutation` (`src/features/chat/hooks/useChatQuery.ts`) invalidated `chatKeys.byStory(storyId)` — which resolves to the *type-suffixed* key `["chats","story",storyId,"all"]` — on every chat create/update. React Query's `invalidateQueries` only matches queries whose key **starts with** the given key, so this never actually touched any type-specific list query (`["chats","story",storyId,"editor"]`, `"worldbuilding"`, etc. — every real caller in the app). Confirmed live: creating a chat via the new Rework flow correctly selected/displayed it in the main pane (that half doesn't depend on the cache, it uses the mutation's own returned object) but the chat never appeared in the sidebar `ChatList` until a full page reload. This bug predates Rework — it would have equally affected the pre-existing "New Chat" button — but Rework's find-or-create logic depends on the list being fresh for its own "does an anchored chat already exist" check, so it was fixed here rather than left for a future session. Fixed by invalidating the shorter `["chats","story",storyId]` prefix instead, which matches every typed sub-list for that story.

**Environment limitation, not a code issue: could not complete a live end-to-end Accept (real LLM reply → replace selection) in this dev environment.** Confirmed via direct network trace that the generate request reached `POST /api/ai/grok-session/generate` successfully and returned `200 OK`, but the response body was `data: [DONE]` with zero content tokens — the configured SuperGrok session provider isn't actually producing output in this dev environment right now (same category of gap already documented elsewhere this session as "RAG search: embedding unavailable... Connection error" — this machine's AI backend connectivity, not this feature's code). Every other piece of the flow was verified live and worked correctly: selection capture and `ReworkCard` preview (exact captured text shown, correct truncation labels), find-or-create chat binding (both branches — zero-match create and one-match reuse — exercised live against the real DB), the request reaching the server with correct context (confirmed via the `/context` network call), Codex tray rendering (status tabs, empty state), and the "Clear rework" control. `applyChapterSelectionReplace`'s Accept-time logic itself is a direct, carefully-reviewed adaptation of the exact `RangeSelection.insertText()` pattern already proven live elsewhere in this codebase (`FloatingTextFormatToolbarPlugin`'s existing `handleGenerateWithPrompt`/`handleHumanize`), not new/unproven Lexical usage. `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean throughout.

**A hot-reload gotcha hit again, worth remembering for future sessions**: after adding the `includeMemory` column and reading it in `chatRepository.ts`/`chats.ts`, the already-running `tsx watch` server kept returning chat objects with `includeMemory` silently absent (not `false` — genuinely missing from the response) for several requests, because `tsx watch`'s hot-reload hadn't yet picked up the `schema.ts` change and `db.select().from(schema.aiChats)` was still working from the stale in-memory column list. A full server restart (stop + fresh `preview_start`) fixed it immediately. Same root cause as the earlier import-fix false-negative in the Notes/Outline bridge entry above — if a just-added column/field silently doesn't show up in a response, restart the dev server fully before assuming the code is wrong.

---

## P0.3 Remaining Slices (N5/N6, C2-C5) — Load-Bearing Decisions

Implements every remaining slice of `docs/CURRENT_BACKLOG.md`'s P0.3 "continuity glue": N5 (save message/selection as note), N6 (AI `note-proposal` accept/reject card), C2 ("suggest memories from this scan" button), C3 (scanner reads active Project Memory for contradiction checks), C4 (per-story unattended scan schedule, default off), and C5 (Codex auto-compile from manuscript). Picked up in one session per explicit user direction ("all of P0.3 remaining, cheapest/most contained first").

**N5/N6 — both note-proposal and prose-proposal stay ephemeral, never persisted server-side.** `parseNoteProposals.ts` mirrors `parseProseProposal.ts` exactly (single fenced block per reply, client-only React state until Accept/Reject) rather than the heavier `codexPendingChanges`-backed pattern — a Story Note is a single lightweight insert, not a multi-stage review workflow, so there was nothing to gain from a durable pending row. `NoteFormDialog.tsx` (the Notes tool's own "New Note" title/type picker) is reused as-is for N5's manual save flow instead of a second bespoke dialog. **N6's `NOTE_PROPOSAL_INSTRUCTIONS` is only ever added to `WORLDBUILDING_FRAMING`, never `PROSE_PROPOSAL_INSTRUCTIONS`** — Editor chats stay canon-only in both directions (read AND write), matching the existing acceptance criterion. **Brainstorm gets N5 (manual save) but not N6 (AI propose)** — its `useMessageGeneration` hook doesn't parse any fenced block at all (unlike `useChatMessageGeneration`), and wiring fence-parsing into a second, structurally different generation hook was out of scope for what N6 asked for.

**C2 needed zero new server code.** `POST /api/agent/jobs` was already a fully generic manual-enqueue endpoint (jobType/storyId/entityId/payload), and `distill_memory` already accepted an arbitrary scanId as `entityId` — so "Suggest memories from this scan" is purely a `useSuggestMemoriesMutation` wrapper + a button on each completed scan row in `RagScannerPanel.tsx`'s scan history. No dedup concerns beyond what `agentJobsRepository`'s existing (jobType, storyId, entityId, status) index already provides.

**C3 is a per-scan opt-in flag (`payload.includeMemory` on the `agentJobs` row), not a persisted per-story setting** — deliberately mirrors C1's chat-level toggle rather than C4's persisted story flag, since arming memory-awareness for one scan should never silently change another. `ragScanner.ts` gained a second system prompt constant (`SCANNER_SYSTEM_PROMPT_WITH_MEMORY`) instead of always interpolating a possibly-empty Project Memory section into one prompt, so a scan that didn't opt in reads byte-for-byte identical to before this change — a real regression-avoidance concern given the Scanner's prompt is already tuned. `RagScanEvidence.source` gained a third value (`"memory"`) alongside the existing `"chapter"`/`"codex"`. Active memories are read via `listMemories({storyId, status: "active"})` (every active row, no top-K search) rather than through `ragIndexService.search`, since a scan already processes the whole chapter in one LLM call — there's no "top-K for a conversational turn" concept to reuse here, unlike `chatContextService.ts`'s `resolveMemories`.

**C4 uses a single fixed daily cadence (`stories.unattendedScanEnabled`, no interval column) rather than a configurable schedule.** The backlog only ever asked for "toggle (default OFF)," not a scheduling UI; a fixed cadence matching `prune_history`'s existing daily cadence avoids inventing a config surface nothing requested. `jobRunner.ts`'s `runScheduleTick` now reads `stories.unattendedScanEnabled` per story and calls the exact same `maybeEnqueuePeriodic` dedup/cadence helper `reconcile_index` already uses — no new scheduling primitive. Opted-in stories' auto-enqueued scans never set `includeMemory` (defaults false) — C3 stays a manual, explicit choice per scan, never silently folded into the unattended path.

**C5 (biggest lift) shapes as a manual-trigger job through the *existing* Codex approve/reject pipeline, per the backlog's own explicit recommendation** — not a new auto-compile pipeline, not new-entry creation, and not a silent background job. `codexCompileJob.ts`'s `runSuggestCodexUpdatesJob`: (1) name-substring-matches chapter text against codex-enabled entries visible to the story (same level/scopeId scoping precedent as `ragScanner.ts`'s `resolveEntityIdsByName` — no embeddings pass needed for a job this narrow), (2) sends one combined LLM call with the chapter text + each candidate's current wardrobe/appearance/wounds/items, (3) for each entry with a genuine detected change, calls the *exact same* `codexService.proposeChange(entryId, {proposedState}, "ai", sourceRef)` that chat proposals use — `sourceType: "ai"` already existed in `CodexPendingSourceType` (`"chat" | "ai"`) but nothing had ever produced an `"ai"`-sourced row until this job.

**Deliberately never touches `customFields` or entry description/tags — only the four concrete-state arrays.** `customFields` is a user-defined extension point (arbitrary key/value pairs the user themselves set up), not something manuscript text should silently reshape; leaving it out of the model's write surface avoids a categorically different (and much harder to validate) kind of "hallucinated user intent." Per-category null-means-unchanged (never an empty array) so the model omitting a category the chapter didn't address can't be misread as "this character now has zero wardrobe."

**New review surface was required — this was the real gap, not just "call proposeChange."** The only existing pending-change UI (`CodexProposalTray.tsx`) is scoped to one chat's `sourceRef` prefix (`"chat:{chatId}..."`); a job-sourced proposal's `sourceRef` (`"job:{jobId}:chapter:{chapterId}"`) would never match any chat's tray and would have been invisible to the user despite the server-side `GET /api/codex/:entryId/pending` endpoint and `getPendingChangesForEntry` repository function *already existing* with zero frontend consumer. Added `CodexPendingChangesPanel.tsx` next to `CodexHistoryPanel.tsx` in the Lorebook entry editor — entry-scoped, source-agnostic (shows both chat- and job-sourced pending changes), Approve/Reject only (no Edit-before-approve here, unlike the chat tray, since there's no chat to revise the proposal through). Approving one of these produces the exact same `"ai_suggestion"`-labeled snapshot `CodexHistoryPanel.tsx` already knew how to render (`codexService.ts`'s `pendingSourceToCodexSource` already mapped `"ai" → "ai_suggestion"` before this job ever existed to use it).

**New `codex_compile` feature key added to the per-feature-endpoint system** (`src/types/aiSettings.ts`), per CLAUDE.md's standing "per-feature endpoint selection required" constraint — this can run on a different model/machine than the writing model, same as `rag_scanner`/`agent_memory_distill`.

**`suggest_codex_updates` follows the exact same "never auto-enqueued" precedent as `distill_memory`/`rag_scan_story`** — added to `AgentJobType` with the same doc-comment convention, manual `POST /api/agent/jobs` only, absent from `jobRunner.ts`'s schedule tick.

**Verification: typecheck clean throughout** (`tsc --noEmit` both client and server tsconfigs, run after each slice). **Live UI verification via the Browser pane** confirmed the C3 "Include Project Memory" switch and C5 "Suggest Codex updates from this chapter" button both render correctly in the RAG Scanner drawer, and `GET /api/stories` confirmed the new `unattendedScanEnabled: false` column round-trips correctly (migration `0035_odd_nextwave.sql` ran cleanly on server startup).

**A real environment/process-isolation gotcha, worth remembering:** this dev machine already had another chat session's `npm run dev` bound to ports 3001/5173 for this same working directory. Clicking "Suggest Codex updates" through the Browser pane produced a **400 `jobType must be one of: reconcile_index, rag_scan_chapter, rag_scan_story, prune_history, distill_memory`** — the *other* session's long-running server hadn't restarted since before `suggest_codex_updates` was added to `routes/agentJobs.ts`'s `JOB_TYPES` allowlist, so its in-memory validation list was stale (the client-side Vite HMR bundle *did* pick up the new button correctly, since only the server process was stale). **This is not a code defect** — confirmed by directly invoking `runSuggestCodexUpdatesJob` in an isolated one-off `tsx` script (bypassing the shared job queue entirely): it correctly found the codex-enabled candidate entry (`Elena Cross`, manually enabled + given an initial wardrobe entry for this test), matched her name in the chapter text, and reached `buildClientForFeature("codex_compile")`'s LLM call, failing there with `Connection error` — the same well-documented "no reachable AI provider in this dev sandbox" limitation noted in the C1/R0-R3 entries above, not a new gap. A related artifact: enqueuing the job via the shared dev server produced a job row whose stored `error` read `"handler is not a function"` rather than `"Connection error"` — this came from a **second, stale job-runner process** (the other session's server) racing to claim the same `agentJobs` row from the shared SQLite file, whose own in-memory `HANDLERS` map (built before this session's `jobRunner.ts` edit) had no `suggest_codex_updates` entry. Both stale-server artifacts resolve once that other session's `npm run dev` is restarted — **the user's other running dev-server session needs a restart to pick up every schema/route/job-type change from this session** (migration 0035, `suggest_codex_updates`/`codex_compile` registration, and the C2/C3/C4 payload/query-param changes to the existing `rag_scan_*`/`distill_memory` job types).

---

## P0.4 R4/R5/R7/R8 — Lorebook Rework → WB, Dedicated Outline Chat, Outline Row Rework, WB Handoff — Load-Bearing Decisions

Implements the next slice of `docs/CURRENT_BACKLOG.md`'s P0.4 after R0-R3: R4 (Lorebook field rework → World-Building chat), R5 (Outline gets its own dedicated chat, retiring the bulk "Generate with AI" button), R8 (outline row rework + "Open in WB" lore-suggestion handoff), and — found necessary mid-plan, not originally scoped as its own slice this session — R7 (splitting `chatType`/`promptType` "outline" out from "editor"). Full design: `docs/Chat_Panel_Integrations_Design.md` §1/§3/§4 (locked 2026-07-18, unimplemented until now).

**Correction to the backlog's own framing, found via research before implementation:** `docs/CURRENT_BACKLOG.md` says R6-R7 are "deliberately deferred — neither R4/R5/R8 strictly require it." That's wrong for R7's core deliverable. `OutlinePage.tsx` was mounting `EditorChatRail` directly with `chatType="editor"` — Outline had no chat identity of its own, it was literally the Editor's rail relabeled. A "dedicated Outline chat" (R5's own wording) is impossible without a real `outline` chatType, so R7's split happened as part of R5 here — not scope creep, the only way to deliver what R5 already promised. R6 (auto-insert/auto-accept toggle UI) stayed out, per the user's own bundle choice — every new proposal path defaults to manual accept, no new toggle switches added.

**R4 shipped narrower than a literal reading of the host matrix — description-field sub-span rework only, not per-row structured-Codex-field (wardrobe/wounds/items/appearance) rework.** The structured fields live three components deep (`CodexStateEditor.tsx` → `StateListBox`/`LabeledFieldsBox`, plus a parallel `NaturalStateFields.tsx` tree for Natural View) with no `entryId`/`storyId` threaded to any of them today; wiring a rework trigger into every row would have meant new props through ~6 components for comparatively low value (these are short single-line values a user can usually just retype faster than a rework round-trip). Cut deliberately, flagged as a fast-follow if wanted, not silently dropped.

**WB Codex proposals moved from inline `ProposalCard` (Approve/Reject only) to the same `CodexProposalTray` Editor chats already use (Approve/Edit/Reject).** `WorldBuildingChatPanel` never had a tray before this — every WB proposal rendered under its message in the thread. Since a rework turn *does* have a chat to revise a proposal through (unlike the entry-scoped `CodexPendingChangesPanel`, which is Approve/Reject-only for the opposite reason — "no chat to revise through"), reusing the tray gives WB rework the same edit-before-approve Editor rework already has, for free — a deliberate simplification unifying all three proposal-producing chat types (`editor`/`worldbuilding`/`outline`) on one review surface (`ChatInterface.tsx`'s `usesCodexTray` flag), rather than maintaining two divergent UIs for the same underlying `codexPendingChanges` row.

**No new "apply" adapter exists for R4, on purpose.** Unlike the chapter-selection case (`applyChapterSelectionReplace`, which re-resolves a live Lexical selection and calls `insertText`), Accept for a lorebook rework is just the *existing*, unmodified `codex-proposal` approve flow — the model is instructed (via a new `[LOREBOOK FIELD REWORK]` block in `ChatInterface.tsx`'s `reworkContext`) to reply with a `modify_entry` proposal whose `proposedDescription` is the *complete* new field value (before + replacement + after reassembled by the model itself), not a fragment. `lorebookFieldAdapter.ts` therefore only has a capture half (`captureDescriptionSelection`, native `textarea.selectionStart`/`selectionEnd` — no Lexical, no node keys, since the description field is a plain `<Textarea>`), no apply half.

**`FocusTarget` generalized from a single-member union to four members** (`chapter-selection` unchanged, plus `lorebook-field`, `lorebook-structured-field` — unused this pass but added since the type already anticipated it, cheap to include now — and `outline-item`). Introduced a narrower `ChapterSelectionTarget` alias (`Extract<FocusTarget, {kind: "chapter-selection"}>`) for the two call sites (`applyChapterSelectionReplace`, `ChatInterface.tsx`'s `proseProposals` state) that only ever handle the chapter case and need the narrow shape, not the full union, to type-check.

**`pendingReworkStore.ts`'s `PendingReworkRequest` generalized from a chapter-only `chapterId` field to `panel: "editor"|"worldbuilding"|"outline"` + `anchorId: string`.** Each rail's consumption effect now filters on its own `panel` before consuming, so `EditorChatRail`/`WorldBuildingChatPanel`/`OutlineChatRail` can all subscribe to the same store without stepping on each other's requests — verified live: reworking a description field in a WB-anchored `useEffect` never fires the Editor rail's effect, confirmed via the network trace showing only the WB chat's `POST /api/chats` fired, not a second Editor-chat create.

**`ChatInterface.tsx`'s single `isEditorChat` boolean split into four flags** (`isEditorChat`, `isOutlineChat`, `showContextSelector`, `forceStructuredContextOff`, `usesCodexTray`) since Outline agrees with Editor on some axes (no manual context selector/toggles — always-on structured context instead) but disagrees on others (still shows Notes/Memory switches, unlike Editor). The one Outline-specific UI suppression: the "Include Outline" switch is hidden *only* for `promptType === "outline"` chats (redundant — that chat already gets the full outline tree unconditionally) while Notes/Memory switches stay visible there — verified live, the toggle row for the auto-created Outline Chat showed exactly "Include Notes" + "Include Project Memory", no "Include Outline".

**`ReworkCard.tsx`'s trailing hint text was hardcoded chapter-specific** ("...replace only the highlighted selection in the chapter") — a real bug caught live during R4 verification (the WB rework card was telling the user something false about how Accept works there). Fixed by adding an optional `hostHint` prop (defaulting to the original text, so Editor's existing caller needs no change) and a new `reworkHostHint` `useMemo` in `ChatInterface.tsx` that switches on `activeRework.target.kind` — verified live for both the `lorebook-field` and `outline-item` cases, each rendering its own correct hint text.

**Outline chat's server-side context (`chatContextService.ts`) adds two new always-on reads, gated on `chatType === "outline"` only** (never behind the pre-existing `includeOutline` toggle, which stays the *other* chat types' opt-in RAG-search path, now confirmed still empty for Outline's own `relevantOutlineItems` field in a live context fetch): `resolveFullOutlineTree` (every non-rejected `outlineItems` row, unranked) and `resolveWrittenChapterSummaries` (`chapters.title`/`chapters.summary` — a real, distinct field from any linked outlineItem's own summary, confirmed via `server/db/schema.ts`, not a subset of the tree read). Verified live via a direct `GET /api/chats/:id/context` inspection: `outlineTree` correctly reconstructed the full chapter/scene hierarchy with real ids/parentIds/order, `writtenChapters` returned the 3 actual prose chapters' own (different) summaries, and the `systemPrompt` field contained all four fence instructions (`outline-proposal`, `codex-proposal`, `note-proposal`, `lore-suggestion`) concatenated correctly.

**Outline proposal fences split by durability, not treated uniformly:** `outline-proposal` `"create"` is parsed and immediately inserted as a `status: "pending", source: "ai_suggested"` row — the *exact* mechanism the now-retired bulk-Generate button used — so the existing tree Accept/Reject badges (`OutlineChapterCard.tsx`/`OutlineSceneRow.tsx`, unmodified) handle it with zero new UI, confirmed unaffected live (11 pre-existing pending demo rows rendered with their "AI Suggested" badges exactly as before). `"edit"`/`"reorder"`/`"delete"` (acting on an already-confirmed row) stay ephemeral instead — a new `OutlineProposalCard.tsx`, same posture as `ProseProposalCard`/`NoteProposalCard` (not persisted until Accept, which then calls the existing `PUT`/`PATCH /reorder`/`DELETE` endpoints directly). This avoids a parallel `outlineItemPendingChanges` table; the design doc's own §3.4 table explicitly sanctions "accept-in-place with undo" as an alternative to a durable pending row for Outline specifically.

**Lore-suggestion → WB handoff uses the *existing* same-tab cross-tool navigation pattern (`StoryContext.pendingLorebookEntryId`, previously used only by the Relationships graph's "Open entry"), not the URL-query-param mechanism the plan originally assumed.** Research turned up that `useWorkspaceDeepLink.ts` wipes all query params (`setSearchParams({}, {replace:true})`) on mount as a side effect of consuming `?story=&tool=`, which would race and silently drop any `?seedName=...` params appended alongside it. `StoryContext` gained a sibling field, `pendingLorebookSeed: {name, category, blurb} | null` + setter, consumed once by `LorebookPage.tsx` (mirroring its existing `pendingLorebookEntryId` effect exactly) to open `CreateEntryDialog` pre-filled — reusing the *existing* `DocumentImportDraft` shape/prop (previously only fed by the AI document-import flow) rather than inventing a second prefill mechanism. `CreateEntryDialog`/`LorebookEntryEditor` needed no new plumbing beyond threading the already-defined `draftValues` prop one level further than before.

**`outlineGenerator.ts` (the retired bulk-Generate button's backend) was deleted outright, not left dead.** Along with it: `POST /outline/generate` route, `useGenerateOutlineMutation`, `outlineApi.generate`, the `OutlineGenerationResult` type, and the now-orphaned `outline_generation` per-feature-endpoint key (`src/types/aiSettings.ts` — nothing calls `buildClientForFeature("outline_generation")` anymore since chat-driven structure proposals go through the model already selected in the Outline chat's own UI, not a fixed background-job endpoint slot). The WB template catalogue's `"outline"` template slug was also removed (`WORLD_BUILDING_TEMPLATES`) per the design doc's explicit §1 instruction — pre-existing WB chats with that now-unmatched `templateSlug` degrade gracefully (`buildSystemPrompt` just falls back to the framing text with no template hint), same pattern already established for other stale-reference cases in this codebase; confirmed live, an old "Story Outline" WB chat from a prior session still opened and worked normally.

**Infra fix required regardless of scope, found before it could bite:** `server/db/seedSystemPrompts.ts` skipped seeding *entirely* if any system prompt row existed at all — so a brand-new `"outline"` promptType entry in `src/data/systemPrompts.ts` would never reach this (or any existing user's) dev DB. Changed to a per-`promptType` diff-and-insert-only-what's-missing. Verified live on this session's own server restart: boot log read exactly `"Seeding 1 missing system prompt(s)..." → "Successfully seeded 1 system prompt(s)"` — the pre-existing 8 prompt types were correctly left untouched, only the new one was added.

---

## P0.4 B0-B4 — Brainstorm Hub — Load-Bearing Decisions

Implements `docs/Chat_Panel_Integrations_Design.md` §5's B0 (migrate Brainstorm onto the shared `aiChats`/`chatContextService`/`ChatInterface` stack, own `chatType: "brainstorm"`), B1 (composer blurb + Guided setup button + Light/Standard/Grill-me style dropdown + empty-story CTA), B2 (playbook depth control), B3 (depth-adaptive propose → synopsis/overview note/opt-in memory/handoffs), and B4 (durable tray: overview proposals + handoffs + slot checklist, Open/Send/Accept-doesn't-clear, only Mark done does). B5 (WB/Outline domain playbooks) and the S/K/R6 tracks stay out of scope, per backlog. Plan locked with user via `EnterPlanMode` before implementation (`C:\Users\Reuben\.claude\plans\zazzy-wishing-cray.md`).

**B2's depth control confirmed prompt-driven with the user before implementation, not a scripted ask/capture/confirm state machine.** Light/Standard/Grill-me are three system-prompt hint variants (`chatContextService.ts`'s `STYLE_HINTS`) that shape how thoroughly the model interviews the user in ordinary multi-turn chat — no server-tracked interview FSM, no per-turn slot-fill bookkeeping. The *only* persisted "slot" concept is the fixed 5-entry `BRAINSTORM_SLOTS` known/unknown checklist (`brainstormSlots` table) — a much smaller, simpler build than a real wizard engine, and closer to what the design doc's actual wording ("playbook chooses depth; user confirms writes") asks for than an invented rigid state machine would have been.

**Brainstorm's `chatContextService.ts` context assembly reuses existing resolvers wherever the shape already matched, rather than duplicating them.** `chapterSummaries` reuses `resolveWrittenChapterSummaries(storyId)` verbatim (same function Outline's always-on `writtenChapters` already uses), just toggle-gated (`includeChapterSummaries`) instead of `chatType==="outline"`-gated. Brainstorm is the **first chat type where lorebook search is opt-in rather than always-on** — every other non-Editor chat type has always searched `lorebook_entry` unconditionally (`DEFAULT_SEARCH_ENTITY_TYPES`); `getChatContext`'s `entityTypes` computation now filters `lorebook_entry` back out for `chatType==="brainstorm"` unless `chat.includeLorebook` is true, a new branch that leaves every other chat type's behavior byte-for-byte unchanged (verified live: toggling "Include Lorebook" off/on on a Brainstorm chat visibly changed `relevantCodexEntries` in a direct `GET .../context` fetch, while a parallel WB chat's own context fetch was unaffected).

**One fence type covers all three of B3's "Overview SoT" write targets (synopsis/note/memory), not three separate fences.** `overview-proposal` is discriminated by `proposalType: "synopsis"|"note"|"memory"` — simpler for the model to reason about than three near-identical fence formats, and lets a single `brainstormChecklistService.createChecklistItem` call persist any of the three uniformly. The `"memory"` branch is only even mentioned in the system-prompt instructions when the chat's own `includeMemory` toggle is on (reuses the *existing* C1 toggle as the gate — no new memory-specific opt-in state needed). Handoffs use a second fence (`handoff-packet`) wrapping an array (mirrors `lore-suggestion`'s shape) so a single reply can propose handoffs to more than one destination at once (e.g. Outline + WB from the same Grill-me turn) — `overview-proposal` stays singular per reply, matching every other single-fence convention in this codebase.

**B4's tray needed a genuinely new table, not a reuse of `codexPendingChanges`.** That table's `pending→approved→rejected` status model *resolves* the row on approve/reject — B4 explicitly requires the opposite (Open/Send/Accept perform the real side effect but leave the item in the Active queue; only an explicit "Mark done" checkbox moves it out). New `brainstormChecklist` table (`kind: 'overview_proposal'|'handoff'`, `status: 'pending'|'opened'|'done'|'dismissed'`) backs both tray sections that need this lifecycle. The third tray section (slot checklist) is a genuinely simpler model with no lifecycle at all — just a `known|unknown` flip — so it lives in its own tiny `brainstormSlots` table rather than being folded into the checklist table's `kind` enum.

**Fence-to-checklist persistence happens immediately on parse, not on user Accept — a real posture difference from every other proposal type in this codebase.** Prose/note/outline-edit proposals stay ephemeral client-side state until the user acts; `overview-proposal`/`handoff-packet` are POSTed to `brainstormChecklistService.createChecklistItem` the instant `useChatMessageGeneration`'s strip chain parses them (`ChatInterface.tsx`'s `onOverviewProposal`/`onHandoffPackets` callbacks), so B4's tray is durable across a page reload from the moment the model proposes something — the model's proposal *is* the checklist row; Accept/Open/Send then perform the actual downstream write (synopsis update, note create, memory create, or cross-tool navigation) and flip status to `"opened"`.

**Handoff destinations split into two genuinely different mechanisms, reusing existing precedent wherever one already fit.** WB reuses `StoryContext.pendingLorebookSeed` completely unchanged (the exact mechanism R8 built for Outline's "Open in WB" lore-suggestion) — a `handoff-packet` with `destination: "worldbuilding"` just maps `seedName`/`seedCategory`/`summary` onto that field's existing `{name, category, blurb}` shape. Outline and Research have no such structured pre-fill target, so they get a new, generalized sibling field, `StoryContext.pendingChatComposerSeed: {tool, text} | null` (same one-shot, non-localStorage-persisted posture as `pendingLorebookSeed`), consumed by a new tiny effect in `OutlineChatRail.tsx` and `ResearchTool.tsx` that feeds a new lightweight `ChatInterface` prop, `initialComposerText` (deliberately kept separate from the existing `initialRework` prop — no `FocusTarget`/packet involved, it just seeds the composer's `input` state). Notes has no chat rail of its own yet (K1, out of scope) — "Send" on a Notes handoff skips navigation entirely and calls `useCreateNoteMutation` directly, the same call `ChatInterface.tsx`'s pre-existing N5 "Save message as note" already makes.

**A real, previously-undiscovered bug in `POST /api/notes` was found and fixed while verifying the Notes handoff live, not introduced by this work.** `notesApi.create`'s own client-side type signature (`Omit<Note, "id"|"createdAt"|"updatedAt">`) promises the caller never has to supply `updatedAt` — but `server/routes/notes.ts`'s create handler only ever overrode `createdAt`, never `updatedAt`, despite `notes.updatedAt` being a `NOT NULL` column with no default. Every note-creation path in the app (`useCreateNoteMutation`, used by the Notes tool's own "New Note" button, `NoteList.tsx`, and `ChatInterface.tsx`'s pre-existing N5 manual-save and N6 note-proposal-accept flows) was silently broken, failing with a 500 `NOT NULL constraint failed: notes.updatedAt` — confirmed live via network trace when the Brainstorm Notes handoff's "Send" button first failed. Fixed by setting `updatedAt: now` alongside the existing `createdAt: now` override; the fix is a two-line change with no behavior change for any caller (every caller already omitted `updatedAt` from its request body, matching what the type signature always promised). Verified live after the fix: both a direct `POST /api/notes` call and the real "Send" button click produced a `201 Created` note that round-tripped through `GET /api/notes/story/:id`.

**No new server-side allowlist/validation surface needed for the new `chatType`.** Confirmed (same finding as Outline's R5/R7 entry above) that `server/routes/chats.ts` has no runtime `chatType` allowlist at all — only the TS `ChatType` union gates it, and `createGenericChat` already accepts any `ChatType` value. Zero chat-CRUD route changes needed for `"brainstorm"` itself; the only `chats.ts` change was adding `includeLorebook`/`includeChapterSummaries`/`brainstormStyle` to the `PATCH /:chatId` meta-fields allowlist, mirroring `includeNotes`/`includeOutline`/`includeMemory` exactly.

**`server/routes/brainstorm.ts` was gutted and repurposed entirely, not left dead alongside a new file.** Its previous contents (`createCrudRouter` over `aiChats`) became fully redundant once Brainstorm chats ride `/api/chats` like every other type — same "zero route changes needed for chat CRUD" fact Outline's migration already established. The `/api/brainstorm` mount point was kept and its router rewritten to serve the two genuinely new resources instead: `GET/POST /checklist`, `PATCH /checklist/:id`, `GET /slots`, `PATCH /slots/:slotKey`. On the client, `src/features/brainstorm/components/{ChatInterface,ChatList}.tsx`, `hooks/useBrainstormQuery.ts`, and `hooks/useMessageGeneration.ts` were deleted outright (superseded by the shared stack); `hooks/useMessageEditing.ts` was also deleted after confirming — via a repo-wide grep — it had zero remaining consumers once the old `ChatInterface.tsx` that was its only caller was gone. The genuinely shared UI primitives the migrated stack still imports (`ChatMessageList.tsx`, `ContextSelector.tsx`, `MessageInputArea.tsx`, `AssistantMessageContent.tsx`, `MarkdownRenderer.tsx`, `ThinkingBlock.tsx`, `useChatMessages.ts`, `useContextSelection.ts`) were left untouched in place, despite living in a folder now named after a feature they're no longer specific to — consistent with the precedent already set when the shared `features/chat/ChatInterface.tsx` started importing them for Outline/WB/Editor before this session.

**Empty-story CTA (B1) required one small, deliberately narrow API addition, not a new dialog.** `CreateStoryDialog.tsx` gained two optional props — `trigger?: ReactNode` (overrides its default "Create New Story" button, so `StoriesTool.tsx`'s empty-state "Start in Brainstorm" button can reuse the exact same form/mutation) and `onCreated?: (story) => void` (fired alongside the existing close+reset, since creating a story never auto-navigated anywhere before this — confirmed by reading `CreateStoryDialog`'s prior `onSuccess`, which only closed the dialog and reset the form). Every other `CreateStoryDialog` call site is unaffected (both props are optional, no existing usage passes them).

**Verification: typecheck clean throughout** (`tsc --noEmit`, both client and server tsconfigs, plus a full `npm run db:generate`/`db:migrate` cycle producing `0036_true_boomerang.sql` with exactly the intended 3 `aiChats` columns + 2 new tables, no unrelated diff). **Full live verification via the Browser pane** against a clean server restart (confirmed no stale dev-server process was already bound to 3001/5173 before starting, avoiding the multi-session landmine documented in the 2026-07-20 session-state memory): auto-create-on-mount, all 5 toggle switches (Notes/Outline/Memory/Lorebook/Chapter Summaries) rendering and persisting across a `PATCH`+reload round-trip, Guided Setup prefilling the composer per style, a direct `GET .../context` inspection confirming `chapterSummaries`/`priorSetupSlots`/`handoffStatus` populate correctly and the assembled `systemPrompt` contains the new fence instructions verbatim, manually-inserted checklist rows (standing in for live model output, since — same well-documented limitation as every P0.3/P0.4 session before this one — this dev sandbox's configured AI provider doesn't reliably return content) rendering correctly in the tray, Accept on an overview proposal writing the story synopsis **and** flipping the tagged slot to "known" **and** staying in the Active tab under an "Opened" badge, Mark done correctly moving an item to the Done tab and nowhere else, and all four handoff destinations exercised end-to-end through their real UI buttons: WB (`CreateEntryDialog` opened pre-filled with "Marcus Webb" — confirmed via input `.value` inspection, since Radix's `position:fixed` dialog overlay makes `offsetParent` an unreliable visibility check), Outline (composer prefilled with the handoff's `detail` text after a real tool-switch), Research (same), and Notes (a real note created in the database, confirmed via `GET /api/notes/story/:id`, after first hitting and fixing the `updatedAt` bug above). All test data (chat, checklist rows, duplicate notes, synopsis change, slot flip) was cleaned up from the demo story after verification.

**Verification: `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean.** Live verification required restarting another chat session's dev server bound to the same ports/working directory (same landmine as the C5 entry above) — the OS processes were confirmed as plain `tsx server/index.ts`/`vite.js` for this exact repo before being stopped, with explicit user confirmation of the restart. Live-verified end to end: R4's description-field rework (selection capture → `ReworkCard` preview → WB chat find-or-create → real `POST /api/ai/grok-session/generate` reaching the server, failing only at the same documented grok.com anti-bot 403 every prior session has hit, not a new gap) and Codex tray rendering; R5's Outline chat auto-create/auto-select-on-mount, the "Include Outline" toggle suppression, the full `outlineTree`/`writtenChapters` context payload, and the "Generate with AI" button's removal; R8's row-level rework binding to the already-open Outline chat with the corrected hint text. Regression-checked: the Editor tool's own `EditorChatRail` still shows a separate "Editor Chats" list with zero cross-contamination from the new Outline chat, confirming the R7 split didn't merge the two pools. Not exercised live (environment-blocked, not a code gap): a real model-generated `outline-proposal`/`lore-suggestion` fence and the resulting "Open in WB" handoff — no AI provider is reachable in this dev sandbox, the same limitation documented in every session since R0-R3.
