## Agent Framework Phase C — Superseded Tab, Pin Semantics, Writer-Pref Browser (P1.1) — Load-Bearing Decisions

First P1 (post-Phase-B) work, scoped à la carte from `docs/CURRENT_BACKLOG.md`'s P1.1 list. Deliberately **not** touched this pass: soft job concurrency and migrating fully off `ragScans` dual-write — both are explicitly conditional in the backlog's own wording ("only if daily pain" / "only when consumers are ready") and neither condition holds today.

**Superseded tab is genuinely read-only, not just an unfiltered fourth status.** `ProjectMemoryPanel.tsx` gained a fourth `TABS` entry backed by the status filter `listMemories` already supported (no backend change needed). `ProjectMemoryList.tsx`'s `MemoryCard` narrowed its action visibility: Pin only renders for `status === "active"` (pinning a dead/pending row has no effect once P1.1's chat-context change below is a status-active-only path — see next point), Edit only for `pending`/`active` (editing a rejected or superseded row makes no sense; the service layer's `applyContentEdit` already throws on both, this just stops the UI from offering a button that would 500), Delete unchanged (any non-pending status can still be permanently removed).

**Pin semantics is a real behavior change, not just a UI affordance.** Previously `pinned` was pure decoration — toggleable in the UI but never read by anything downstream. `chatContextService.ts`'s `resolveMemories` now takes the chat's `storyId` and, alongside the existing ranked-hybridSearch path (`role: "search"`), runs a second parallel query for `status: "active" AND pinned: true` rows for that story, dedupes against the search hits by id, and prepends them (`role: "pinned"`) — **unbounded by `RELEVANT_ENTRIES_LIMIT`**, deliberately: the limit exists to cap noisy ranked results, not to throttle a small number of facts the writer explicitly flagged as "never let this drop out of context." `ChatContextMemoryExcerpt.role` gained `"pinned"` as a second literal (`src/types/worldbuilding.ts`); `ChatInterface.tsx`'s prompt renderer appends `", pinned"` to a pinned row's category parenthetical so the model has some signal it's a standing fact, not just topically ranked for this turn. `ProjectMemoryList.tsx` also gained a stable pinned-first sort (`byPinnedFirst`) within whichever status tab is showing — reorders only across the pinned/unpinned boundary, never within either group, since the query already orders by `createdAt desc`.

**Cross-project writer_pref memories previously had no creation path and no browse surface at all.** `agentMemoriesService.ts`'s own comment already documented that global (`storyId: null`) memories are never indexed for search — by design, since `hybridSearch` is always story-scoped — but nothing in the actual UI ever let a user *create* one: `ProjectMemoryPanel.tsx`'s dialog always passed the current story's id. Fixed by:
- Extracting the create-note dialog out of `ProjectMemoryPanel.tsx` into a shared `NewMemoryNoteDialog.tsx` (`storyId: string | null`, `defaultCategory` prop) — both the story-scoped panel and the new global card use the identical component, just parameterized.
- A new `global` boolean on `ListMemoriesParams` (`agentMemoriesRepository.ts`) that filters `isNull(storyId)` instead of the usual `eq(storyId, x)` — mutually exclusive with `storyId` in practice, threaded through the route (`?global=true`), client (`agentMemoriesApi.list`), and hook (`useProjectMemoriesQuery`) unchanged in shape otherwise.
- A new `WriterPrefsCard.tsx` (`src/features/agent-memory/components/`), mounted in `SettingsPage.tsx` alongside `RecentJobsCard` — Settings is the natural home since writer_pref notes aren't tied to any one story. Two tabs only (Pending/Active, not all four) since nothing produces story-scoped-style Rejected/Superseded rows for global memories yet (no `distill_memory` job runs against global scope). New notes default to `category: "writer_pref"` (the intended cross-project use) but the dialog's category picker still allows any value.

**A real pre-existing TS build error was found in `folderService.ts`, unrelated to this work — flagged via `spawn_task`, not fixed inline.** `npm run build` failed on `server/services/folderService.ts(43,15)` (`parentId` implicitly `any`, circular inference) both before and after this session's edits (confirmed via `git status --short` showing no local changes to that file) — pre-existing on `fork/main`, out of scope for a P1.1 memory-panel pass. Verified this session's own changes are clean by running `tsc --noEmit` (client) and `tsc --project server/tsconfig.json --noEmit | grep -v folderService.ts` (server) separately, plus a standalone `vite build`, all clean.

**Environment note, not a code bug: this session hit the documented `tsx watch` dev-server hang landmine again** (see [[session_state_2026-07-21_pending_edge_review]]) — `npm run dev`'s combined config sat silent with nothing on port 3001, resolved by switching to the pre-existing `server-only` + `client-only` `.claude/launch.json` configs, which booted normally. Separately, this session also hit a **Radix Dialog close-animation stall specific to the automated Browser pane**: after a successful create/submit, the `New Memory Note` dialog's `data-state` attribute flipped to `"closed"` but the content stayed fully rendered/interactive (`display: grid`, `pointer-events: auto`, `opacity: 1`) for several tool-call round-trips, causing an initial Pin click to silently land on the still-visible dialog instead of the list underneath. A full page reload cleared it every time, and a subsequent Pin click on the same button worked correctly (200 on the `PATCH`) — logged here as an automation-environment quirk (likely a missing/instant CSS transition never firing an `animationend` Radix's `Presence` is waiting on in this headless context), not a regression to chase further, matching the `computer{action:"screenshot"}` timeout already logged as an environment limitation in the H0-H7 entry above.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` (client) clean; `npx tsc --project server/tsconfig.json --noEmit` clean except the pre-existing, unrelated `folderService.ts` error above; `npx vite build` clean. **Full live verification via the Browser pane** (`server-only`/`client-only` launch configs): created a global `writer_pref` note via `WriterPrefsCard` on the Settings page, confirmed it listed under Active with the `?global=true` query param round-tripping correctly; pinned it, confirmed the `PATCH` succeeded and the pinned icon persisted across a reload; created a story-scoped `project_note` memory, then a second with the same `memoryKey`, confirmed the first flipped to `superseded` and appeared read-only (Delete only, no Pin/Edit) under the new Superseded tab; deleted all three test rows afterward via the same UI (each confirmed by its `DELETE` request).

## Lorebook Relationship Graph — G1.5+ (AI-Suggested Edges, Persisted Layout, RAG Reindex-on-Edge-Change), Load-Bearing Decisions

Closes out the three remaining P1.2 items (`docs/CURRENT_BACKLOG.md`): AI edge suggestions, saved canvas layout, and reindexing lorebook text when relationships change so RAG can see them. All three built in one pass since they touch the same files.

**AI-suggested edges (`graph_suggest_edges`) follows the exact `distill_memory`/`suggest_codex_updates` precedent — manual-trigger only, pending-row output only.** New `AgentJobType`/`FeatureKey` member (`graph_suggest_edges`, feature label "Relationship Graph (Suggest Edges)"), handler in `server/services/jobs/graphSuggestEdgesJob.ts`, registered in `jobRunner.ts`'s `HANDLERS` map and `agentJobs.ts`'s `JOB_TYPES` allowlist — never added to the schedule tick. Scope is story-wide rather than chapter-scoped (unlike `suggest_codex_updates`) since there's no natural "what changed" filter for static entry descriptions — entries are read alphabetically and capped at `MAX_ENTRIES = 60` for prompt-size/cost, a deliberate simplification flagged in the job file's own comment rather than a relevance heuristic. A new `storyGraphService.proposeAiSuggestedEdge` (distinct from the existing `createEdge`) mirrors its validation but always writes `status: "pending", source: "ai_suggested"` and — unlike `createEdge`'s throw-on-conflict behavior — silently skips (returns `null`) when an active edge of that exact type already exists, so one hallucinated/already-true candidate doesn't abort a whole batch. Candidates are deduped against both active and pending edges (by `fromId|toId|edgeType` key) before ever reaching the LLM call's "already recorded" context block, and again after parsing, so re-running the job doesn't spam duplicate pending proposals. Trigger: an owner-only "Suggest relationships" button in `StoryGraphCanvas.tsx`'s toolbar (`useSuggestGraphEdgesMutation`, same fire-and-forget-enqueue-no-polling shape as `useSuggestCodexUpdatesMutation`) — output lands in the already-shipped Pending tab (P1.2's earlier pass) completely unchanged; approve/reject don't discriminate by `source` at all, so no review-UI work was needed here.

**Persisted layout is Full-graph-only by design, not an oversight.** New `storyGraphLayout` table (`storyId`, `nodeId`, `x`, `y` as `real`, unique on `(storyId, nodeId)`, migration `0043_eminent_squadron_supreme.sql`) stores one row per dragged node. Ego view's `layoutEgo` is a BFS-ring layout relative to whichever entry is centered — not a stable absolute position across different centers — so it deliberately never reads or writes this table; only `viewMode === "full"` merges `useGraphLayoutQuery`'s saved positions over `layoutGrid`'s computed fallback (`positions?.get(n.id) ?? computedPositions.get(n.id) ?? {x:0,y:0}` in `StoryGraphCanvas.tsx`'s layout effect) and wires `onNodeDragStop` to persist. Upsert is a plain select-then-insert-or-update (`storyGraphRepository.upsertLayoutPosition`) rather than a drizzle `onConflictDoUpdate` — no upsert precedent existed elsewhere in this codebase, and call volume is one row per drag, so the extra round-trip is a non-issue. `useSaveLayoutPositionMutation` deliberately skips both a toast (would fire on every drag-stop, pure noise) and the broad `storyGraph.all` invalidation the other mutations use (would refetch/re-layout the graph query mid-drag, fighting the user) — only that story's own layout query is invalidated. A "Reset layout" button (owner-only, only rendered when `savedPositions.size > 0`) clears the whole table for the story via `DELETE /stories/:storyId/graph/layout`.

**RAG reindex-on-edge-change required making `buildLorebookEntryText` async, not just adding a fire-and-forget call.** The obvious-looking approach — leave `buildLorebookEntryText` (the sync entry-only text builder) alone and only append relationship text inside `indexLorebookEntry` — would have desynced `reconcileIndexJob.ts`'s own staleness check, which calls `buildLorebookEntryText` directly and compares a content hash against what's actually stored to decide whether to reindex (its own comment: "recomputes indexable text the exact same way indexLorebookEntry does and can't drift"). Left as sync-entry-only text, reconcile's periodic pass would see a hash mismatch against the richer combined text `indexLorebookEntry` actually wrote, "fix" it by reindexing back to entry-only text, and silently erase every relationship line on the next 15-minute tick. Instead `buildLorebookEntryText` itself became `async`, gained a `buildRelationshipText(entryId)` internal helper (queries `storyGraphEdges` where `status = "active"` and either endpoint matches, resolves the other endpoint's name, formats `"{fromName} — {label|typeLabel} — {toName}"` per line — the same literal sentence indexed on both endpoints, deliberately not rephrased per perspective since that would need reverse-semantic label mapping that doesn't exist), and both call sites (`indexLorebookEntry`, `reconcileIndexJob.ts`) now `await` it. Only `active` edges are ever included — `pending`/`rejected` edges must not leak into indexed "fact" text.

**Reindex triggers on every active-state transition, not just create.** `storyGraphService.ts`'s `reindexEdgeEntries(fromId, toId)` (fire-and-forget via `attemptPromise`, same posture as `server/routes/lorebook.ts`'s own on-write calls) fires from: `createEdge` when `status === "active"`; `updateEdge` when the resulting row is active; `approveEdge` (pending → active, unconditionally); `deleteEdge` (only if the edge being removed was active — fetches it first to check); and `migrateStoryRelationships` (once per touched entry at the end of the loop, not per-edge, to avoid redundant reindex calls during a bulk migration). `rejectEdge` and `proposeAiSuggestedEdge`'s pending-only writes deliberately do **not** reindex — a pending/rejected edge was never in the indexed text to begin with.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. Live-verified in the Browser pane (`server-only`/`client-only` launch configs) against the demo story: confirmed `GET .../graph/layout` empty initially; `PUT .../graph/layout/lore-elena-cross` with `{x:321,y:654}` returned 200, `GET` reflected it, and a full page reload showed the node's actual DOM `transform: translate(321px, 654px)` in Full-graph view (not the grid default) — proving the merge-over-fallback logic works, not just the API; clicked "Reset layout," confirmed the table cleared and the button itself disappeared once `savedPositions.size` hit 0. Enqueued `graph_suggest_edges` directly via `POST /api/agent/jobs`: job correctly went `queued → running → failed` after 3 attempts with `"Connection error."` — this dev sandbox's local model endpoint (`localhost:1234`) isn't reachable here, the same "no reachable AI provider" limitation every P0.3/P0.4/P1 session before this one has hit (see e.g. the R0-R3 and S0-S5 entries above) — confirmed no partial/orphaned pending edges were left behind by the failed run. For reindexing, created a real active edge with a unique label, confirmed via `POST /api/rag/search` that both endpoints' indexed chunks gained a `"Relationships:"` block containing that exact edge sentence and were keyword-searchable by the label; deleted the edge and confirmed the same search now returned zero results, proving the delete path reindexes too. Test edge cleaned up afterward; the fetched `useIsOwner`-gated buttons (Suggest relationships, Reset layout) were also confirmed rendering correctly via `read_page`.

## Transfer Log + Settings IA — S0, T0-T3, Load-Bearing Decisions

Ships `docs/Transfer_Log_And_Settings_IA_Design.md` in full: Settings reorganized into sub-nav headings (S0), a new `deskTransfers` send-journal table (T0), every known cross-desk seed path instrumented to write `proposed`/`opened` rows (T1), and a Logs → Transfers UI with story picker, search, Open origin, and Re-seed destination (T2). This entry is T3.

**Every writer lives in a client event handler, so the row-create path is a client POST, not an internal service call — the opposite of most "log" mechanisms in this codebase.** `indexLorebookEntry`/`proposeChange`/etc. are called directly by server-side route handlers because the triggering action (an HTTP request) already originates server-side. Here the triggering action — a tray's "Open" button, a fence-parse callback in `ChatInterface.tsx`, a "Send to Notes chat" click — is a React event handler with no server round-trip of its own. `POST /api/stories/:storyId/transfers` (`deskTransfers.ts`) exists purely so these client actions have somewhere to report to; `deskTransfersApi.log(...).catch(() => {})` is fire-and-forget at every call site, matching the design's "a transfer-log write failing must never block the real seed dispatch it's describing."

**Scoping call: only genuinely cross-desk sends are logged, not every use of the generic seed mechanisms.** The design doc's Job description is explicit — "desk→desk seeds (what left chat A for desk B)" — so three same-desk uses of `pendingChatComposerSeed`/`pendingLorebookSeed`/checklist mechanisms found in the inventory pass are deliberately **not** instrumented: `note_split` (Notes "Accept all" stays entirely within Notes), the Notes "Import dump" seed (`notes → notes`), and `overview_proposal`'s `synopsis`/`memory` sub-types (write directly into `stories.synopsis`/Project Memory — neither is a "desk" in the `WorkspaceTool` sense, only the `note` sub-type creates a genuine cross-desk artifact). This is why `DeskTransferKind` has no `note_split` member despite `brainstormChecklist.kind` having one, and why `ChatInterface.tsx`'s `onOverviewProposal` instrumentation checks `proposalType === "note"` (and, defensively, that the originating chat isn't itself Notes — Notes' own prompt never emits `"note"` overview proposals, but the parser is shared/defensive, so a same-desk log would otherwise be possible in principle).

**A sixth `DeskTransferKind`, `highlight_to_notes`, was added beyond the design doc's own enumerated mechanisms.** The inventory turned up two genuine desk→desk seeds that don't fit any of `shuttle`/`shuttle_return`/`handoff`/`overview_proposal`/`lore_suggestion`: chat-bubble text selection → "Send to Notes chat" (`ChatInterface.tsx`'s `handleSendSelectionToNotesChat`, any non-Editor chat type → notes) and chapter-prose selection → "Send to Notes chat" (`SaveSelectionAsNoteButton.tsx`, editor → notes). Both are single-step actions with no separate persisted "propose" moment (unlike the tray-backed mechanisms), so both `proposed` and `opened` events log together at the one click, same posture as `lore_suggestion`'s single-step "Open in WB."

**`fromChatTitleSnapshot` required threading new props through five existing tray/card components that had no reason to know their own chat's title before.** `ShuttleTray.tsx` (used by `EditorChatRail.tsx`/`OutlineChatRail.tsx`/`LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`'s WB panel), `BrainstormChecklistTray.tsx` (`BrainstormTool.tsx`), `NotesChecklistTray.tsx` (`NotesChatRail.tsx`), and `LoreSuggestionCard.tsx`/`OutlineProposalTray.tsx` (`OutlineChatRail.tsx`) all previously received only `chatId`, never the full chat object — every one of them gained a `fromChatTitleSnapshot: string` (and `ShuttleTray` also `storyId`/`fromDesk`) prop threaded down from whichever rail already holds the full `AIChat` object in scope. `ShuttleTray`'s Chat-Shuttle "return" path (`ResearchTool.tsx`'s `handleSendBriefBack`) instead fetches the **origin** chat via `chatsApi.getById` at log time, since `activeShuttleContext` only ever carried an id, never the chat's own chatType/title.

**"Re-seed destination" required routing via `useNavigate` (react-router), not `window.location.assign`, because the pending-seed fields are in-memory-only.** `StoryContext.tsx`'s `pendingChatComposerSeed`/`pendingLorebookSeed` have no localStorage persistence effect (only `currentStoryId`/`currentChapterId`/`currentTool` do) — a hard navigation (`window.location.assign("/")`) would unmount the entire React tree and lose the seed the instant before Workspace could remount and consume it. Since `StoryProvider` wraps the whole app in `main.tsx` (including the `/settings` route, not just `/`), a client-side `navigate("/")` keeps the same React tree/context alive across the route change, so the seed set from `TransfersLogCard.tsx` survives to be consumed by whichever rail mounts under `Workspace`. This only works because Settings already sits inside the same top-level `StoryProvider` — a page rendered by a fully separate provider tree would need a different mechanism (e.g. reviving `useWorkspaceDeepLink`'s query-param approach).

**"Open origin" is scoped to story+desk navigation only — selecting the specific origin chat within that desk is out, per decision #10's own "missing chats best-effort" allowance.** Every chat rail (`EditorChatRail`, `OutlineChatRail`, `NotesChatRail`, etc.) manages its own `selectedChat` as local `useState`, not something a different page can reach into and set. Building a generic "select this chat" cross-rail mechanism would mean touching all six rails for one button's convenience. Instead, `handleOpenOrigin` (`TransfersLogCard.tsx`) verifies the origin chat still exists via `chatsApi.getById` (toasting "That origin chat no longer exists" and stopping if not — the design's explicit "toast if gone" requirement), then navigates to the right story+desk and names the chat to look for in a toast. A future pass could wire real chat auto-selection if this proves annoying in practice.

**Settings IA uses Radix Tabs in vertical orientation for the sub-nav, not a new sidebar component.** `SettingsPage.tsx`'s `Tabs`/`TabsList`/`TabsTrigger` (already used elsewhere in the app) needed no new primitive — `orientation="vertical"` on the `Tabs` root plus `flex-col`/`justify-start` utility classes on `TabsList`/`TabsTrigger` produced the left-column sub-nav the design doc calls for, keeping keyboard nav (arrow keys move between vertical tabs) for free. `WriterPrefsCard` (not mentioned in the design doc's own live-audit table, added since) was placed under **Writing tools** alongside TTS/Humanizer/Grammar — a judgment call, not a locked decision — since it's a writing-style preference surface, not a Logs or Data concern. `ThemeToggle` was deep-linked (rendered again inside a new Appearance tab) rather than moved — the existing Sidebar/TopBar/MainLayout instances are untouched, so this is purely additive.

**Environment note:** the `server-only`/`client-only` dev processes were already running from the prior (P1.2) session and predated every schema/route change made this session — a stale server silently 404s new routes as HTML (the dev server's SPA fallback), which looks like a JSON-parse error client-side (`SyntaxError: Unexpected token '<'`) rather than a clean 404. Restarting `server-only` picked up the new `deskTransfers` table/migration and all route changes cleanly. Worth remembering for any future session that reuses a long-lived dev server across schema changes.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. Live-verified in the Browser pane: Settings' 7 tabs (Appearance/Providers & keys/Local/Feature routing/Writing tools/Logs/Data) all render their expected cards with no console errors; Logs → Transfers shows the story picker (defaulting to the demo story), "No transfers in the last 30 days" empty state, and — after posting a real `shuttle`/`proposed` row directly via the API — the row rendered with correct kind/event badges, desk labels, timestamp, and truncated subject. "Re-seed destination" confirmed end-to-end: clicking it navigated (client-side, no reload) to the Research tool and the composer textarea's actual `.value` (not just its placeholder) contained the row's `subject` + `(Context: ...)` crumb, proving the in-memory seed survived the route change. "Open origin" against a deliberately fake `fromChatId` correctly toasted "That origin chat no longer exists" and did not navigate. Test transfer row cleaned up directly from the SQLite file afterward (no delete route exists by design — the log is append-only + auto-pruned).

## Context / Token Meter — M0-M5, Load-Bearing Decisions

Ships `docs/Context_Token_Meter_Design.md` in full for its stated v1 scope: Local settings (n_ctx override, best-effort server-metadata fetch, soft-warn toggle), a hybrid char-heuristic estimate pipeline reusing the chat's own real context assembly, a composer chip with an expandable budget-slice breakdown, real post-turn usage capture for the Local provider, and a soft-warn confirm-before-send. This entry is M5.

**The estimate pipeline reuses `ChatContext` (the same object `chatsApi.getContext` already fetches for system-prompt building) rather than a second divergent prompt builder — exactly the tradeoff the design doc invites.** `ChatInterface.tsx`'s pre-existing context-fetch effect built a flattened `codexContext` string and discarded the raw object; this pass added one line (`setRawChatContext(context)`) to also expose it as state. `useContextEstimate.ts` derives budget slices (System/Lore/Memory/Notes/Outline/Research/History/Draft) directly from that object's own fields (`relevantCodexEntries`, `relevantMemories`, `outlineTree`, etc.), each run through `estimateTokens()` (chars/4, the design doc's own suggested heuristic — non-goal #3 explicitly waives "perfect tokenizer coverage"). This is a genuine **approximation**, not a byte-identical replica of the final assembled prompt string (whitespace/section-header formatting differs, and it doesn't include the model's own chat-template overhead) — acceptable per the design doc's explicit "v1 may approximate ... if full dry-assemble is expensive" allowance. There is no "refine on send with a real tokenizer count" in this pass — the chip is always labeled "(est.)", which is simply the design doc's own documented fallback state ("if refine fails, keep heuristic and mark UI as est.") made permanent for v1 rather than a sometimes-state.

**Real usage capture (M3) is scoped to the Local provider only — every other provider stays in the design's own "usage not available → hidden" branch, not a corner cut.** Design decision #2 explicitly allows this: "Local always; other providers when usage is available; else hide." Capturing real usage from OpenAI/OpenRouter/Grok would mean threading a structured `usage` field through `wrapOpenAIStream`'s stream-to-plain-text collapse (`streamUtils.ts`) for each of those providers' SDK-based paths, a materially larger change than Local's raw-fetch path. Local's request already goes through `processStreamedResponse` directly (no `wrapOpenAIStream` wrapping — see `AIService.generate`'s existing local/grok-session early-return branch), so adding `stream_options: {include_usage: true}` to `LocalAIProvider.generate()`'s JSON body and teaching `processStreamedResponse` to recognize a final usage-only chunk (`choices: []`, populated `usage`) was a contained, two-file change. `useStreamingGeneration.ts`'s `processStream` return type changed from `Promise<string>` to `Promise<{text, usage}>` — its only other caller, `useSceneBeatGeneration.ts`, never destructured the return value (reads `streamedText` from hook state instead), so no other call site needed touching.

**`ChatMessage.usage` is optional and persisted through the exact same `appendMessage` call every chat message already goes through** — `server/services/chatService.ts`'s `appendMessage` gained a fourth optional parameter, spread onto the message object only when present (`...(usage ? { usage } : {})`), so every existing caller (every other chat type, every other provider) is unaffected. `POST /:chatId/messages` and `chatsApi.appendMessage` both widened to accept it optionally. The per-message badge (`ChatMessageList.tsx`) renders only when `message.usage` exists, so non-Local assistant messages are visually unchanged.

**Best-effort server-metadata fetch for n_ctx (design decision #5) reads a short list of plausible field names rather than one guaranteed field, because generic OpenAI-compatible `/v1/models` has no such field in spec.** `LocalAIProvider.fetchModels()` now checks `loaded_context_length`/`max_context_length`/`context_length`/`n_ctx` on each model object (in that order) before falling back to the previous hardcoded `16384` — LM Studio and some other local runners add extra fields beyond the OpenAI spec onto this same endpoint's response; a plain llama.cpp/vLLM server without such extensions just falls through to the old hardcoded value, same as before this pass. `contextWindowOverride` (new `aiSettings` column) always wins when set, satisfying "user override wins" independent of what fetch produces.

**Soft-warn (M4) reuses the existing `ConfirmDialog` component** (`src/components/ui/ConfirmDialog.tsx`, already used by `SettingsPage.tsx`'s demo-data deletion) rather than a bespoke dialog — `handleSubmit` in `ChatInterface.tsx` was split into a thin threshold-check wrapper and the original logic renamed `doSend`; the check only fires when `selectedModel?.provider === "local"` (matching M2/M3's Local-only usage-capable framing) and `aiSettings.softWarnNearLimit` is on (default false). Confirming calls `doSend()` unchanged; cancelling just closes the dialog and leaves the draft intact — never a hard block on the estimate alone (non-goal #2), matching the acceptance criteria exactly.

**Environment notes, both real automation-environment quirks encountered and resolved, not app bugs:**
- Mid-session, `SettingsPage.tsx`'s tab-switching stopped responding to any click (verified via a `document.addEventListener('click', ..., true)` counter that stayed at 0 for `computer`-tool clicks, and confirmed via `data-state` inspection that Radix's tab state genuinely wasn't changing even for a raw `element.click()`) — after many hours and many Vite HMR hot-updates in one `client-only` dev-server process. A full restart of the `client-only` process (not just a page reload) fixed it immediately. Distinct from the previously-documented stale-server-404-as-HTML landmine (P0.4/Transfer-Log sessions) — this one is client-side HMR state corruption, not a stale server.
- The already-documented Radix Dialog close-animation stall (`data-state="closed"` but still `display: grid`, first logged in the P1.1 session) recurred for the soft-warn `ConfirmDialog` after clicking Cancel — cosmetic only, confirmed by checking the actually-relevant behavior (composer draft text untouched, no message sent) rather than the dialog's lingering visibility.
- A live composer-chip reactivity check that initially looked like a bug (typing more text didn't visibly move the rounded "X.Xk" total) turned out to be correct behavior at insufficient scale — confirmed via the React fiber's `memoizedProps.estimate` that the Draft slice **was** updating (40 tokens for a ~160-char addition against a ~1800-token total, an invisible rounding delta) before a much larger addition (11,200 chars) produced a clearly visible jump (1.8k → 4.6k). Worth remembering before assuming a UI number "isn't updating" — check the underlying prop value before concluding the pipeline is broken.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean throughout every slice. Live-verified in the Browser pane against the demo story's Editor chat with the local default model selected: chip rendered `1.8k / 16.4k tokens (est.) · 89% left`; expand popover showed real slice values (`System 747`, `Lore 1.0k`, matching the chat's actual anchored-chapter + synopsis context); live-typed/injected draft text correctly moved both the Draft slice and the total (confirmed via direct React fiber prop inspection, then via a large 11,200-char addition producing a clearly visible `4.6k / 16.4k · 72% left`); Local settings tab's new Context/Token Meter card correctly showed the resolved default model's context length (`16,384`) as the override field's placeholder, and the soft-warn toggle/threshold round-tripped through a real `PUT /api/ai/settings/:id` and reload; lowering the threshold to 20% and sending a ~29%-of-window draft correctly opened the soft-warn confirm with the right percentage in its message, and Cancel correctly left the draft untouched with nothing sent. All test chats (`New Chat` entries created for this verification pass) cleaned up afterward via the API; settings reverted to defaults (`softWarnNearLimit: false`, `softWarnThreshold: 0.9`).

---

## Chat Model Routing + Chat Chrome Density — MR0-MR2, CC0, CL0, Load-Bearing Decisions

Ships `docs/Chat_Model_Routing_And_Chrome_Design.md` in full for its locked v1 scope: a sticky global Cloud|Local default for chat model resolution, a compact per-chat mode toggle with a filtered/grouped model menu, a collapsed-by-default "Context & memory" chrome disclosure, and single-line chat list rows.

**MR0/MR1 — routing logic lives in one pure, framework-free module (`src/features/ai/utils/resolveChatDefaultModel.ts`), not inline in the hook.** `resolveChatDefaultModel` (M5/M6 — initial resolution for a new/opened chat: keep `lastUsedModelId` if it's still in the model catalogue, else fall back to the mode default) and `resolveModelForModeSwitch` (M7 — the segmented-control toggle: keep the current model only if its own provider already matches the new mode, else jump to that mode's default) are the two entry points; `computeModeDefaultModelId` and `isProviderConfigured`/`getDefaultModelIdForProvider` are shared internals. A provider counts as "configured" only when it has **both** a key/URL **and** a chosen default model — mirrors `server/services/aiClientFactory.ts`'s `clientFromGlobalSettings` check exactly, reimplemented client-side since that function isn't reachable from the browser bundle. M3's "last-used cloud provider if still configured" is interpreted **per-chat** (the chat's own current/former model's provider), not as a new piece of global state — there is no server-side concept of "the last cloud provider used anywhere," and adding one wasn't worth it for a Lean-locked v1 with no separate primary-provider dropdown.

**New `aiSettings.preferredMode` column (`text`, enum `cloud|local`, default `cloud`, migration `0046_solid_quasimodo.sql`).** `GET /ai/settings`'s first-ever-row bootstrap path returns a plain JS object rather than a DB round-trip, so it needed an explicit `preferredMode: "cloud"` added alongside the existing hardcoded fields (`server/routes/ai.ts`) — otherwise a fresh install's very first settings fetch would omit the field entirely (undefined) even though the DB row itself carries the schema default from the second fetch onward. Settings UI: a small "Chat default routing" card at the top of the Providers & keys tab (not a new Settings section) — a two-tab Cloud|Local control wired straight to a new `useUpdatePreferredModeMutation`.

**MR2 — `useChatSystemPrompt` gained a `chatId` parameter and two new `useEffect`s, because switching chats within an already-mounted rail doesn't remount the hook.** `ChatInterface` is never given a `key={selectedChat.id}` (confirmed by checking every call site — `EditorChatRail.tsx`/`OutlineChatRail.tsx`/`NotesChatRail.tsx`/`BrainstormTool.tsx`/`ResearchTool.tsx`/`LorebookEntryEditor.tsx` all wrap it only in an `ErrorBoundary` keyed by `resetKeys`, which resets error state, not component identity), so the hook's local `selectedModelId` state needed its own chat-identity-keyed reset effect — this was a latent gap in the pre-existing code (switching chats would have silently carried over the previous chat's in-memory model selection until a manual re-pick) that MR2's own auto-resolve requirement (M5/M6) forced into the open; fixing it was necessary to implement M5/M6 correctly, not a scope-creep bugfix. The second effect does the actual M5/M6 auto-resolve-and-persist once models/settings are loaded and nothing valid is selected. Both are marked `eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` (matching this repo's existing convention, e.g. `BrainstormTool.tsx`/`ResearchTool.tsx`) since they're deliberately keyed narrower than their full closure.

**`ChatSystemPromptControl.tsx` rewrite**: a `Tabs`-based Cloud|Local segmented pill (reusing the exact `raycast-pill-active` styling Research's own Story|Global toggle already established, not a new visual pattern) sits beside the model `Select`, which now filters to the active mode and groups cloud models by provider via `SelectGroup`/`SelectLabel` (both newly exported from `src/components/ui/select.tsx` — Radix already provided them, they just weren't re-exported yet). "Edit system prompt" (previously a permanent labeled button) moved into a `DropdownMenu` behind a bare "⋯" icon button, per C5. "Full catalogue under More…" (the design doc's own v1-optional line) was **not** built — filtered-by-mode is the only view.

**CC0 — the two existing toggle-group `<div>`s in `ChatInterface.tsx` (Notes/Outline/Memory/Lorebook/web-search/chapter-summaries, and auto-insert/auto-accept/auto-shuttle) are wrapped in one `Collapsible`, unmodified internally.** Every toggle's own render condition (which chat types see it) was left exactly as-is; a parallel `armedContextLabels` array mirrors those same conditions read-only to build the collapsed-state summary chip (C3), so the chip can never show a toggle that isn't actually rendered underneath. Expand/collapse state is a new global localStorage flag (`useContextMemoryExpanded.ts`, `useSyncExternalStore` pattern copied from the existing `useShowModelThinking.ts`) rather than per-chat — matches C6's primary recommendation, collapsed by default (C2). The wrapping `{(!isEditorChat || usesCodexTray) && (...)}` guard (unchanged from the two groups' own prior combined visibility) means the disclosure itself never renders for a chat type that would have nothing inside it.

**CL0 — `ChatListItem.tsx` row rewrite**: one flex row (`py-1.5 px-2`) instead of a two-row `flex-col` stack; a new `formatShortTimestamp` (time-of-day if today, `"Mon D"` this year, `"Mon D, YYYY"` otherwise) replaces the old always-full `toLocaleDateString() + toLocaleTimeString()` pair. Edit/delete buttons shrank to `h-6 w-6`/`h-3.5 w-3.5` icons, still hover-only (`opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100`) plus a new `group-focus-within:opacity-100` for keyboard access. Applies to all five rails that share `ChatList`/`ChatListItem` (Editor, Outline, Notes, World-Building, Brainstorm) with no per-rail changes — Research has no chat list at all, per its own single-chat-identity design. Per-chat folder-depth indent (noted as absent during exploration) was left alone — out of this design doc's own locked scope (L4 only asks to "keep tree indent," i.e. the folder header's own indent, not add new per-leaf indent).

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. Live-verified in the Browser pane against the demo story's Editor chat: new chat auto-selected `grok-4.3` (Cloud, via the grok-oauth fallback since `grokKey` was a placeholder test value with no `defaultGrokModel` set — confirming the "configured = key **and** default model" check works as designed, not just "key present"); toggling the segmented control to Local switched to the resolved local default (`for-her-darkside-31b-v1.3`) and back to Cloud returned to `grok-4.3`; the model dropdown showed cloud models grouped by provider label (SuperGrok/Grok OAuth/Grok) and local-only models under Local; the "Context & memory" disclosure rendered collapsed by default, showed exactly the two toggles Editor chats get, and displayed an `Auto-accept Codex` summary chip when collapsed with that toggle on; `PUT /api/ai/settings/:id` round-tripped `preferredMode` through the new Settings card (confirmed via direct response inspection) and was reverted to `cloud` afterward; `ChatListItem` DOM confirmed rendering with the new compact classes and a resolved short timestamp. One environment quirk hit and resolved, not an app bug (same category as the P1.1/M0-M5 sessions' own HMR notes): this sandbox's `preview_start`-managed dev server process didn't bring its Express half up on port 3001 (`tsx watch` silently produced no output, matching the previously-documented "tsx watch dev-server hang" landmine) — running the server directly in a separate background shell against the same Vite frontend worked around it for this verification pass. All test chats created during verification were deleted afterward via the API.

## Scene Beat Removal — SB1-SB8 in full, Load-Bearing Decisions

Ships `docs/Scene_Beat_Removal_Design.md` in full, including SB6 (drop the `sceneBeats` table) — see its own note below for why that step specifically needed a live confirmation from the user before running, unlike the rest of this pass. Removes the inline "Scene Beat" AI-generation feature (`Alt+S`, `SceneBeatNode`, its whole `nodes/scene-beat/` component tree, `/api/scenebeats`) while leaving **Concrete Beats** (`BeatMarkNode`, `concreteBeats` table, the Beats panel) completely untouched — these are two different, easily-confused systems that only ever shared one file (`nodes/index.ts`'s node-registration array); untangling which of the many `sceneBeat*`/`scenebeat*`-named symbols were feature-specific vs. quietly generic was most of this pass's actual work (see the "naming trap" note below).

**SB1 — a shared content-migration function, not a bespoke one-off.** `server/services/sceneBeatContentMigration.ts`'s `migrateSceneBeatNodesInContent(contentJson, commandsById)` is pure (no DB access) — it walks a chapter's Lexical JSON and rewrites any `scene-beat` node into a plain paragraph containing that beat's own `command` text (empty paragraph if the id has no match), never a silently-dropped blank node, matching the design's own "failure mode to avoid: chapter-open crash." A second function, `migrateSceneBeatChapterContent()`, wraps this into a boot-time idempotent sweep (same posture as `seedSystemPrompts.ts`'s existing `patchStaleSystemPrompts()`) over `chapters`/`chapterVersions`/`chapterSnapshots`, prefiltered by a cheap `LIKE '%"scene-beat"%'` so it's a no-op query on every boot after the first successful migration anywhere. Called from `server/index.ts`'s `initializeDatabase()`, right after `patchStaleSystemPrompts()`. Live-verified against the real dev DB with a synthetic scratch story/chapter/scene-beat row (inserted, migrated, confirmed converted, cleaned up) — the actual dev DB had zero real scene-beat content to migrate (`chapters`/`chapterVersions`/`chapterSnapshots`/`sceneBeats` all queried directly, all zero), so this path is defensive/forward-looking for any other real install rather than something this session's own data needed.

**The "naming trap" — most of the research time went into telling apart truly Scene-Beat-only symbols from confusingly-named-but-actually-generic ones sharing the same `scenebeat`/`sceneBeat` substring, before deleting anything.** Concretely:
- `PromptParserConfig.scenebeat` / `PromptContext.scenebeat` / `SceneBeatResolver` / the literal `{{scenebeat}}` template variable — **kept, unchanged.** `ChatInterface.tsx`'s `createPromptConfig` populates `scenebeat: input.trim()` for **every** chat type (Editor/WB/Outline/Brainstorm/Research/Notes), and `BrainstormResolvers.ts`'s own `UserInputResolver` reads the identical field — this is the generic "current composer/instruction text" carrier, just named after its historical origin.
- `PromptParserConfig.sceneBeatContext` / `.sceneBeatMatchedEntries` / `.matchedEntries` (bare), and their resolvers `SceneBeatContextResolver` / `SceneBeatMatchedEntriesResolver` (`LorebookResolvers.ts`) — **removed.** Confirmed via grep that these three fields were populated *only* by code inside the now-deleted `nodes/scene-beat/` tree (`ContextToggles.tsx`, `GenerationControls.tsx`, `useLorebookMatching.ts`, `sceneBeatPromptService.ts`) — nothing else in the app ever set them.
- `{{additional_scenebeat_context}}` / `{{scenebeat_context}}`-adjacent special-combination handling in `promptParser.ts`'s `parseSpecialCombination`/`getAdditionalContextFormatted` — **left alone.** Despite the name, it operates on `context.chapterMatchedEntries` (the generic "Matched Tags" feature, populated by `LorebookTagPlugin`/`useChapterMatching`, still live) and `context.additionalContext.selectedItems` (the generic manual-lorebook-selection path, still reachable from the legacy/general `ContextSelector` chat flow) — genuinely unrelated to the deleted component despite the variable name.
- `SlashCommandPlugin` was deleted in full (not just its one command emptied) — its `SLASH_COMMANDS` array had exactly one entry (Scene Beat) with an explicit `// Add more slash commands here as needed` that nothing had ever used, so an empty "/" menu would have been dead UI, not a preserved extension point.

**SB7 — `promptType: "scene_beat"` rows are recategorized to `"other"` via a new boot-time migration, not deleted, matching the design's "do not hard-delete user prompt rows."** `seedSystemPrompts.ts` gained `migrateSceneBeatPromptType()` (same idempotent-UPDATE shape as `patchStaleSystemPrompts()`), called from `server/index.ts` right after the SB1 sweep. This was necessary, not optional: `seedSystemPrompts()` only ever *inserts* missing promptTypes, never updates existing rows, so simply deleting the `"scene-beat-system"` entry from `src/data/systemPrompts.ts`'s seed array would have left that real, already-seeded system row (`isSystem: true`) permanently stuck at `promptType: "scene_beat"` — confirmed live: the dev DB had exactly this row before the fix, and the boot migration correctly flipped it to `"other"` (verified via direct sqlite query, `0` rows left at `scene_beat` afterward).

**SB5 — story/series/admin import all reuse `migrateSceneBeatNodesInContent`, not just "ignore the sceneBeats key" as the design's own wording literally suggests.** An old export/backup (from before this removal shipped) carries its own `sceneBeats` array alongside chapter content with real `scene-beat` nodes — once there's no live table to look up command text from, that embedded array is the *only* remaining source of it, so all three import routes (`stories.ts`, `series.ts`, `admin.ts`) build a `Map<id, command>` from `storyData.sceneBeats ?? []` and run it through the migration function on each chapter's content before insert, then simply never write to a `sceneBeats` table. Silently discarding the key instead (the design doc's literal phrasing) would have reintroduced the exact "chapter-open crash" failure mode for anyone restoring an old backup.

**SB6 (drop the `sceneBeats` table) needed an explicit user confirmation before running — the one genuinely irreversible step in this whole pass.** Drizzle's schema migrations run unconditionally, first, inside `runMigrations()` — before any JS boot logic (including SB1's now-retired content sweep) ever gets a chance to run. That means a DROP TABLE migration shipped in the *same* release as the content-sweep would delete the table on an install's very first boot after upgrading, before that install's own chapters/versions/snapshots had ever been swept — safe only for a database already confirmed to have zero real scene-beat content. This session's own dev DB was directly verified to have exactly that (`chapters`/`chapterVersions`/`chapterSnapshots`/`sceneBeats` all queried, all zero real scene-beat content), but CLAUDE.md's own "Docker-first... Tailscale... work PC" language implies at least the possibility of another deployment this session couldn't see — so rather than assume, the user was asked directly (`AskUserQuestion`) whether they had another database with real Scene Beat content; they confirmed they didn't and to proceed. Migration `0047_misty_tempest.sql` (`DROP TABLE sceneBeats`) applied and verified live (`sqlite_master` query confirms the table is gone). SB1's boot-time sweep function (`migrateSceneBeatChapterContent`) was retired along with it — it queried the now-dropped table for command text, so keeping it around post-drop would just throw at every boot; `sceneBeatContentMigration.ts` now exports only the pure `migrateSceneBeatNodesInContent` conversion function, still used by the story/series/admin import paths (which get their command-text lookup from the import payload's own embedded `sceneBeats` array, never the DB).

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean throughout, both before and after SB6. Live-verified in the Browser pane against the demo story's chapter "The Drop" (real content, not a scratch chapter): chapter loaded with no crash after the full removal; `Insert` toolbar dropdown menu items confirmed via DOM query to be exactly `["Horizontal Rule", "Page Break"]` (Scene Beat gone); `Alt+S` confirmed via direct DOM diff to insert nothing (editor child count and `.scene-beat-node` presence both unchanged before/after); the Concrete Beats panel confirmed still opening with its normal description text, unaffected. SB1's migration sweep (before its own retirement) and SB7's prompt-type migration were both exercised against the real dev DB directly (not just synthetically), and the SB6 drop itself was confirmed via a direct `sqlite_master` query post-boot. A stale-Vite-HMR-state artifact (this session's dev server had been running continuously since before most of these files were deleted/changed) surfaced as `does not provide an export named 'SceneBeat...Resolver'` console errors after editing — resolved by a full dev-server process restart, not a page reload; same category of environment quirk documented in the M0-M5 and Chat-Model-Routing sessions' own DECISIONS.md entries, not an app bug.

## Local In-Process Embeddings — IE0-IE6, Load-Bearing Decisions

Ships `docs/Local_Embeddings_Design.md` in full: a new `"local-inprocess"` provider for the `"embedding"` feature only, running `nomic-ai/nomic-embed-text-v1.5` directly inside the Node server via `@huggingface/transformers`, with zero external endpoint dependency.

**One correction made to the backlog's original framing before building, not after.** The backlog proposal described this as an "ONNX/WASM runtime — no native bindings, no GPU required." In Node, `@huggingface/transformers` actually runs on `onnxruntime-node`, which ships **prebuilt native `.node` binaries** — same risk class as `sqlite-vec`'s prebuilt binary (no compile step required, unlike `canvas` which compiles from source), but a native binding nonetheless. Confirmed via research this needs `libgomp1` installed at runtime on Debian-slim (OpenMP dependency of the native binary) — added to both Dockerfile stages alongside the existing `canvas`/`sqlite-vec` native-dependency handling.

**Model weights are baked into the Docker image at build time, not downloaded at first run — the same "no runtime network dependency" posture this project already takes for its native binaries.** `server/scripts/prefetchEmbeddingModel.mjs` runs once in the builder stage (the one deliberate network fetch, same category as `npm ci` needing registry access), forcing `@huggingface/transformers`'s own file-system cache (`env.cacheDir`) to populate a fixed `.embedding-model-cache` directory, which is then `COPY --from=builder`'d into the runtime stage unchanged. Runtime code (`localEmbeddingService.ts`) sets `env.allowRemoteModels = false` unconditionally, so the container can never reach huggingface.co regardless of how it was built. A new `verify-local-embeddings.mjs` (mirrors the existing `verify-sqlite-vec.mjs` pattern) runs in both Docker stages, actually embedding a test string against the copied-across cache — proving the cache is genuinely usable, not just present.

**Dimension match (768, same as `EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS`) meant zero `vec_chunks` schema migration was needed** — confirmed by inspecting the model repo's actual published ONNX file list (`onnx/model_quantized.onnx` et al.) before committing to `dtype: "q8"` (the v4 API's replacement for the old `quantized: true` boolean — a real API-shape difference from what the backlog note assumed, caught by reading the installed package's own type declarations rather than guessing from memory of older `transformers.js` versions).

**`embedTexts()` branches to the new backend before ever calling `buildClientForFeature`/`OpenAI`, not by teaching `aiClientFactory.ts`'s provider switch to handle it.** `local-inprocess` has no HTTP client at all, so shoehorning it into `clientFromEndpoint`'s OpenAI-shaped return type would have meant either a fake client object or a special-cased return type across every other caller of `buildClientForFeature`. Instead, `embeddingService.ts`'s `embedTexts()` checks `getFeatureEndpoints().embedding?.provider` first and returns `embedTextsLocally()` directly when it's `"local-inprocess"`; `aiClientFactory.ts`'s switch still gets a defensive throw case (keeps it exhaustive, catches any future misuse). Scope to the embedding feature only is enforced twice — client-side (`FeatureEndpointsCard.tsx` only lists the option on the Embeddings row) and server-side (`routes/admin.ts`'s two `PUT` routes reject the provider for any other feature key) — since a UI-only restriction would leave a direct API call able to silently break chat/scanner/etc.

**Closed a real correctness gap that this feature would otherwise ship with: mixing embedding spaces in one vector index.** Switching the embedding backend/model doesn't change `vec_chunks`' dimension (both are 768), so nothing would have forced old LM-Studio-embedded chunks to get re-embedded — they'd sit alongside new local-embedded chunks in the same KNN search, where cosine distances between two different models' vector spaces are meaningless numbers, not just "slightly worse" ones. `reconcileIndexJob.ts`'s `entityNeedsReindex` gained a model-mismatch check (a chunk's stored `embeddingModel` not matching the currently-configured one now counts as drift, same tier as the existing null-embedding check) via a new `resolveActiveEmbeddingModel()` helper in `embeddingService.ts`. This was extended to **all five** embeddable entity types, not just lorebook/chapter — `agent_memory`/`note`/`outline_item` chunks were previously only ever checked for *orphan* removal, never staleness, a pre-existing gap independent of this feature (closed here because leaving it would have meant three of five entity types silently never migrating off a stale model). `buildNoteText`/`buildOutlineItemText` were relocated from `routes/notes.ts`/`routes/outline.ts` into `ragIndexService.ts` (exported, alongside the already-exported `buildLorebookEntryText`) rather than having `reconcileIndexJob.ts` import from `routes/` — the services→routes import direction would have been a real layering inversion for no benefit, since `buildMemoryText` was already sitting in a service file (`agentMemoriesService.ts`) with no such issue.

**"Rebuild everything" reuses the existing `reconcile_index` job type with `job.storyId === null` meaning "loop every story," rather than a new job type** — mirrors `prune_history`'s already-established "some jobs are global" precedent (`agentJobs.storyId` was already nullable for exactly this, per its own schema comment). `runReconcileIndexJob` now branches on `job.storyId`: single-story behavior is completely unchanged when a storyId is given; when omitted, it loops `db.select().from(schema.stories)` and sums per-story counts into one aggregate result. No new entries needed in `AgentJobType`, `jobRunner.ts`'s `HANDLERS`, or `agentJobs.ts`'s `JOB_TYPES` — `POST /api/agent/jobs` already treats a missing `storyId` as `null`. Trigger UI is a single "Rebuild embedding index" button + `ConfirmDialog` next to `FeatureEndpointsCard.tsx`'s embedding row (the first global, non-story-scoped job trigger button in the app — every prior job-trigger button, e.g. `RagScannerPanel.tsx`'s scan buttons, is story-scoped), reusing the existing `agentJobsApi.enqueue`/toast/Recent-Jobs-view pattern with no new progress UI.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. `server/scripts/prefetchEmbeddingModel.mjs` and `verify-local-embeddings.mjs` both run successfully against a real local prefetch (confirmed a 768-dim vector from a real cache-only pipeline load, and separately confirmed the exact `env.allowRemoteModels = false` code path resolves purely from cache with zero network calls). Live-verified end to end in the Browser pane against the demo story: saved the Embeddings feature endpoint to "Local (in-process)" (confirmed via direct sqlite read of `aiSettings.featureEndpoints`), clicked "Rebuild embedding index," confirmed the resulting `reconcile_index` job (`storyId: null`) completed with `{"storiesChecked":1,"lorebookEntries":{"checked":18,"reindexed":18},"chapters":{"checked":3,"reindexed":3},"notes":{"checked":1,"reindexed":1},"outlineItems":{"checked":1,"reindexed":1}}`, and confirmed via direct DB queries that every resulting chunk carries `embeddingModel: "nomic-embed-text-v1.5 (local)"` with a real 3072-byte (768 × float32) vector in `vec_chunks` — not a placeholder. A real `hybridSearch()` query against the freshly-local-embedded index (`"Berlin surveillance operation"`) returned topically correct, semantically-matched results with `matchedBy: "both"`/`"vector"`. One real environment landmine hit and resolved during this same pass, not an app bug: a stale dev-server process from an earlier point in this session (started ~3.5 hours prior, holding port 3001 with pre-fix code) was still the one actually listening, making the first save attempt fail with the old provider-validation error message — resolved by killing every stray `node` process and starting one fresh instance; the DB state itself was untouched by the stale process's failed attempt.

**Docker build verified end to end once the user started Docker Desktop, same session, later.** `docker build` succeeded cleanly through both stages — `prefetchEmbeddingModel.mjs` downloaded real weights in the builder stage, and `verify-local-embeddings.mjs` passed in **both** stages (builder, and again in the runtime `--omit=dev` stage against the `COPY --from=builder`'d cache). The strongest test: `docker run --rm --network none --entrypoint node <image> server/scripts/verify-local-embeddings.mjs` — with the container's networking fully disabled, the embedding pipeline still loaded and produced a real 768-dim vector purely from the baked-in cache, proving the "no runtime network access" design decision holds even under an adversarial test, not just "the code sets a flag." A full container boot (`docker run` with the real `entrypoint`/`CMD`, fresh ephemeral data volume) also confirmed clean migrations + a 200 from `/api/auth/status`. Image size: 3.34GB (includes the baked embedding-model cache plus the existing build toolchain left installed for `canvas`/`better-sqlite3`, same as before this feature). Test image and container were removed after verification — no leftover artifacts.

## Lexical Editor Deepen — LE0-LE3, Load-Bearing Decisions

Ships `docs/Lexical_Editor_Design.md`'s plugin-add axis lock in full: `lexical`/`@lexical/*` bumped 0.39.0 → 0.48.0 in lockstep, `ListPlugin`/`CheckListPlugin` mounted on the main Editor's rich-text path, lists/checklists smoke-tested, and all three export paths spot-checked and fixed for list content. Tables stay locked-C (nodes only, no `TablePlugin`, no insert UX) — untouched.

**The version bump surfaced two real breaks, both pre-existing latent risk rather than anything introduced by this pass.** `@lexical/react/LexicalContextMenuPlugin` was removed upstream between 0.39 and 0.48 in favor of `LexicalNodeContextMenuPlugin` — a fully different declarative API (`items: NodeContextMenuOption[]` with `$onSelect`/`$showOn` instead of the old render-prop `menuRenderFn`/`onSelectOption`/manual portal). `ContextMenuPlugin/index.tsx` was rewritten against the new API, verified against Lexical's own current playground source (fetched live) to confirm this exact shape is upstream's own sanctioned replacement, not a guess. New matching CSS (`.lexical-context-menu`/`-item`/`-separator`) since the new component only exposes `className`/`itemClassName`/`separatorClassName` props and renders its own internal DOM structure (no more `.typeahead-popover ul li`). This plugin is gated behind `shouldUseLexicalContextMenu` (`appSettings.ts`, default `false` — a pre-existing, unrelated dev-only toggle with no product Settings UI), so it had to be flipped on temporarily in a local edit, verified live via a dispatched `contextmenu` event confirming the exact expected item set (`Copy`/`Cut`/`Paste`/`Paste as Plain Text`/`Delete Node`, with `Remove Link` + its separator correctly hidden via `$showOn` outside a link), then reverted — `git diff` confirms zero net change to that file's default.

**The second break was a genuine runtime crash in chapter load, not a compile error, and briefly cost real (recovered) demo data.** `FloatingLinkEditor.tsx` passed `editor.registerUpdateListener(({ editorState }) => editorState.read(() => $updateLinkEditor()))` — `$updateLinkEditor()` returns `true` on two of its paths (vestigial, unused by any caller). Lexical 0.48 added a feature where an update-listener's return value is treated as a cleanup function to invoke before the listener's next call; returning `true` made the *next* editor update crash with `TypeError: unregister is not a function` inside Lexical's own `triggerListeners`, since `true` isn't callable. This broke `LoadChapterContentPlugin`'s `editor.setEditorState()` call outright — chapters silently failed to load their content (word count 0, placeholder text) with a caught-but-swallowed error. One-line fix: wrap the listener in a block body so nothing is returned. While this bug was live, repeated dev-server reloads during testing caused the failed load's empty-recovery state to autosave over the demo chapter ("The Drop")'s real content twice — both recovered via the app's own `chapterSnapshots` restore endpoint (P0.2b), confirmed byte-for-byte back to original via direct API check. No data loss; flagged here because it's a concrete demonstration of why that restore path exists, and because any other pre-0.48-mount editor session with `FloatingLinkEditorPlugin` active would have hit this same crash on the very next keystroke after loading Lexical 0.48 — this was not an edge case.

**LE1 (`ListPlugin`+`CheckListPlugin`) is a pure mount — no new toolbar/command code, since the toolbar already dispatched `INSERT_UNORDERED_LIST_COMMAND`/`INSERT_ORDERED_LIST_COMMAND`/`INSERT_CHECK_LIST_COMMAND` (`ToolbarPlugin/utils.ts`) and `ListNode`/`ListItemNode` were already registered — only the plugins that make those commands actually do something were missing.** Verified live: toolbar-driven Bulleted/Numbered/Check List all produce correct `<ul>/<ol>/<li>` structure (checklist items get `role="checkbox"`/`aria-checked`/the checklist theme class — CheckListPlugin's own signature, not something plain `ListPlugin` would render), and the toolbar's own block-type button correctly reflects list state round-trip. **Enter/Tab keyboard behavior inside lists (LE2's other half) could not be verified through this session's browser-automation tool** — a direct test showed the tool's synthesized `Return` keypress has no effect even in a plain paragraph with no list involved (cursor doesn't advance, no new paragraph node created), confirming this is an automation-environment limitation rather than an app or plugin defect. Not chased further, since Enter/Tab-in-lists is well-trodden, unmodified upstream Lexical behavior, not custom SN code.

**LE3's export spot-check found a real, previously-unreachable bug across all three export paths, fixed as part of this pass rather than just reported.** `convertLexicalToHtml.ts`, `convertLexicalToMarkdown.ts`, and `lexicalUtils.ts`'s `extractPlainTextFromLexical` (shared by the PDF exporter) each hand-roll their own minimal Lexical-JSON walker and none of the three had ever special-cased `list`/`listitem` node types — every list fell through to the generic "recurse into children, no wrapping/spacing" branch, meaning list item text would concatenate with **zero separator** (e.g. two items "Bullet one"/"Bullet two" export as the single run-on word `Bullet oneBullet two`). This gap was always latent in the code but functionally unreachable before this pass — without `ListPlugin` mounted, a user could never toolbar/type their way into creating a list node in the editor UI, so it never mattered. LE1 makes list creation a normal editing action, so shipping LE1 without this fix would have meant a newly-reachable, silent content-corrupting bug on every export. Fixed all three: HTML now emits real `<ul>/<ol>/<li>` (checklist items get a `☑ `/`☐ ` prefix, since a static export has no interactive checkbox to render); Markdown emits GFM-style `-`/`1.`/`- [x]` syntax; the shared plain-text extractor (PDF's only path) now gives each list item its own line and a blank line after the whole list block, stopping the word-fusion even though PDF's own `jsPDF.splitTextToSize` doesn't render dedicated bullet/number glyphs (a pre-existing PDF-exporter simplicity, not something this pass's scope covers — the fix's job was to stop data corruption, not add rich PDF list rendering). `types.ts`'s `SerializedLexicalNode` gained `listType`/`checked` fields to type these branches without `any`. Verified live against all three real converter functions (imported directly via Vite's dev-server ES module URLs, not reimplemented/mocked) run against a chapter seeded via direct API `PUT` with a bullet list, numbered list, and mixed-checked checklist, sandwiched between two plain paragraphs — confirmed correct output for all three formats, then the scratch chapter was deleted.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean throughout every step. Live-verified in the Browser pane: chapter load/save round-trip, typing/autosave, undo/redo, the rewritten context menu (temporarily re-enabled), toolbar-driven list/numbered-list/checklist creation and toolbar state round-trip, and all three export converters against real seeded list content. Two disposable scratch chapters were created and deleted for editor-UI and export testing respectively, keeping the demo story's own three chapters untouched aside from the two recovered incidents noted above. Also hit the pre-existing `tsx watch` dev-server-hang landmine (documented from prior sessions) — worked around identically, running the server directly instead of through `concurrently`.

## Lexical Editor — CodeHighlight/TreeView/Collab-Residue (T2 quick decisions), Load-Bearing Decisions

Grills and closes three of the six "not locked yet" T2 axes from `docs/Lexical_Editor_Design.md` — the three the user flagged as short stance calls rather than real design work, in the same session that also closed out P1.4 and shipped RAG Scanner inline highlighting.

**CodeHighlight: locked to leave as-is, no build work.** `CodeNode`/`CodeHighlightNode` stay registered in `PlaygroundNodes` for load-compat only — same posture as the Tables lock (option C): kept only so any old chapter JSON containing a code block still loads, no toolbar insert action, no syntax-highlighting polish. This is a fiction-manuscript editor; code blocks aren't a real writer-facing need.

**TreeView: found to be fully dead before any decision was even made, then removed.** Investigation showed `showTreeView` (`appSettings.ts`, defaulted `true`, no UI ever exposed to change it) never actually mounted Lexical's real `<TreeViewPlugin>` debug component anywhere in this fork — it only ever toggled one CSS class (`.tree-view` → `border-radius: 0` on `.editor-container`) purely as a cosmetic side effect. Since it defaulted `true` with no toggle, that "zero border-radius" state had been the *permanent* visual state all along, not a debug-mode indicator. Removed the setting from `appSettings.ts`, its destructure/className usage in `Editor.tsx`, and the now-orphaned CSS rule in `index.css` — this **restores the editor container's normal 0.5rem rounded bottom corners**, a real (if minor) visible change, called out and confirmed live via `getComputedStyle` before/after rather than assumed.

**Collab residue: removed entirely, not parked.** Traced the full dead path before touching anything: `isCollab` (`appSettings.ts`) only ever changed one placeholder string ("Enter some collaborative rich text...") and was never `true` in practice; the one real `CollaborationPlugin` mount (`ImageComponent.tsx`'s caption editor) was gated on `isCollabActive` from Lexical's own `useCollaborationContext()` — which nothing in this app's component tree ever sets to `true` (no top-level `CollaborationPlugin` exists anywhere to activate it), so that branch was unreachable, not just unused. Removed: `collaboration.ts` (the `y-websocket` provider factory) entirely; the `CollaborationPlugin`/`isCollabActive`/`createWebsocketProvider` imports and the dead ternary branch in `ImageComponent.tsx` (now unconditionally renders `HistoryPlugin`, matching the always-taken path); `isCollab` from `appSettings.ts`/`Editor.tsx`; and the `yjs`/`y-websocket` npm dependencies (confirmed no other file in `src/` referenced either package before removing). This is explicitly finishing what the plugin-add axis already decided (multiplayer Collaboration was SKIP'd there, P3 in the lock table) — not a new product decision, just removing the scaffolding that decision left behind. Real-time collab was never a `@lexical/yjs` direct dependency either, so nothing else needed touching.

**A concrete, measurable win came out of the collab-residue removal, not just code hygiene**: the `ImageComponent` production chunk shrank from ~128 KB to ~8 KB (confirmed via two `npm run build` runs, before and after) — the `yjs`/`y-websocket` libraries had been getting bundled into that async chunk the whole time despite being unreachable at runtime.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean both before committing to the plan and after implementing. `npm install` after the `package.json` edit removed exactly 2 packages (`yjs`, `y-websocket`) with no cascading removal of shared transitive deps. Live-verified in the Browser pane against the real dev DB: editor loads/saves/renders correctly, `.editor-container`'s computed `border-bottom-*-radius` confirmed at `8px` (0.5rem, the un-square default) post-change, zero console errors on a fresh hard navigation (an initial stale-HMR `ReferenceError: showTreeView is not defined` was traced to a leftover Vite dev-server module cache from before a full server restart — confirmed via source grep that no `showTreeView` reference existed anywhere in `Editor.tsx`, and the error did not reproduce after restarting the dev server).
