## Lexical Editor — List "Done" Bar (T2), Load-Bearing Decisions

Closes the "explicit list 'done' bar (nested lists, MD round-trip guarantees, checklist export rules)" T2 axis from `docs/Lexical_Editor_Design.md`, grilled in the same session as the CodeHighlight/TreeView/collab-residue axis above.

**Nested-list creation needed no build work, but wasn't just assumed — it was traced through upstream Lexical source and then live-verified.** `TabIndentationPlugin`'s underlying `registerTabIndentation` (`@lexical/extension`) only dispatches `INDENT_CONTENT_COMMAND` when the selection's anchor is at true block-start (`$indentOverTab`'s check #2); anywhere else in the line, Tab inserts a literal tab character (`INSERT_TAB_COMMAND`) — this is upstream Lexical's own sanctioned Word/Docs-style convention, not something this app implements or could easily change. Once `INDENT_CONTENT_COMMAND` fires, `@lexical/list` doesn't need its own handler for it: `ListItemNode.setIndent()` is itself overridden to call `$handleIndent`/`$handleOutdent` (`formatList.ts`), which perform real structural nesting (wrapping into a child `ListNode`), not a cosmetic margin change. Live-verified in the Browser pane on a disposable scratch chapter (created via direct API call, deleted after): toolbar-created a bulleted list, pressed Enter for a second item, pressed Tab at the item's true start (confirmed via DOM inspection after each step, since this session's browser-automation `Home`/arrow-key presses proved unreliable for cursor repositioning — worked around by relying on Enter always placing the cursor at a fresh empty item's start rather than fighting flaky cursor navigation) — produced a real nested `<ul><li>…<ul><li>Nested item</li></ul></li></ul>` structure.

**Two real, previously-unreported bugs were found and fixed, both reachable today since LE1 made list creation a normal editing action:**
- **Markdown export nesting was syntactically invalid.** `convertLexicalToMarkdown.ts`'s `list` branch built each item's line by mapping *all* of a `listitem`'s children (including a nested `list` node, when present) through the same `processNode` and joining with `""` — a nested list's own rendered lines got concatenated directly onto the parent item's line with no newline or indentation (e.g. `- Item 1- Sub item 1a` as one run-on line). Fixed with a dedicated `renderList(node, depth)` helper, walked directly rather than through the generic `processNode` dispatcher so depth can be tracked without threading a `depth` parameter through every unrelated node type — 2-space GFM indentation per nesting level, each nested list's items on their own lines.
- **PDF/plain-text export silently dropped checklist state.** `extractPlainTextFromLexical` (`lexicalUtils.ts`) never referenced a `listitem`'s `checked` field anywhere — a checked vs. unchecked item exported identically. Fixed by prefixing `☑ `/`☐ `, mirroring `convertLexicalToHtml.ts`'s own existing glyph convention (that converter already handled this correctly) rather than inventing a new one.

**Markdown chapter import was explicitly scoped out, not silently skipped** — confirmed via user decision after investigation showed no Markdown-file-to-Lexical-content path exists anywhere in this app today. `$convertFromMarkdownString`/`$convertToMarkdownString` (`@lexical/markdown`) are only used for live-typing shortcuts (`MarkdownShortcutPlugin`) and table-cell content rebuilding — never for importing a `.md` file into chapter content. `documentImportService.ts` does accept `.md` files, but only for Lorebook entries, and it feeds raw text to an LLM for structured field extraction, discarding list markup into prose rather than preserving Lexical list nodes. Since nothing round-trips today, the axis's original "MD round-trip guarantees" framing was moot without first building an import leg — not built, since there's no current requester, consistent with this project's standing bias against building speculative features.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. Both export-converter fixes verified against **real editor-created content**, not just hand-crafted test JSON — the same disposable scratch chapter used for the nested-list creation check was also run through all three converters (`convertLexicalToMarkdown`, `convertLexicalToHtml`, `extractPlainTextFromLexical`) via Vite's dev-server ES module transform (same technique the LE0-LE3 pass established), confirming correct nested/indented Markdown, correct nested `<ul>` HTML (already working pre-fix), and correct plain-text flattening. Scratch chapter deleted after.

---

## Lexical Editor — Toolbar/Mobile/Selection/Rework Polish (T2), Load-Bearing Decisions

Closes a scoped subset of the broadest, least-defined remaining T2 axis from `docs/Lexical_Editor_Design.md`: "Toolbar / mobile / selection / rework polish." Rather than attempt the whole vague axis, findings from investigation were brought back to the user for an explicit scope decision — touch support and the upgrade-breakage/compat-matrix documentation were deliberately excluded, not silently skipped.

**Toolbar overflow — a real, previously-diagnosed-but-never-implemented bug, not new scope.** `docs/plan-mobile-styling-issue-58.md` (pre-dating the move to `src/components/story-editor/`, file paths stale) already diagnosed this exact problem: `.toolbar`'s plain `overflow-x: auto` row lets the trailing `ml-auto` cluster (word count, Focus session, Maximize) scroll out of reach entirely on narrow viewports, and proposed collapsing into an overflow menu. That fix was apparently never built — the CSS was still exactly the bare scrollable row the doc complains about. Rather than build the doc's proposed dynamic collapsing-overflow-menu (real new component, real design-decision surface: which items get hidden first, in what priority), the minimal targeted fix was chosen: `position: sticky; right: 0` on the trailing cluster in `ToolbarPlugin/index.tsx`, so the cluster (Maximize above all — the single most-needed-to-reach action) is always visible regardless of scroll position, verified via `getComputedStyle`/`getBoundingClientRect` at `scrollLeft: 0` (the worst case for a non-sticky element) at a 375px viewport, and confirmed clickable.

**Link-insert invalid-URL-default — a standing, previously-unactioned `TODO`.** `utils/url.ts`'s `validateUrl` carried `// TODO Fix UI for link insertion; it should never default to an invalid URL such as https://.` Investigation traced the actual reachable path: `FloatingLinkEditor.tsx`'s edit-mode input defaulted to the literal string `"https://"` (both on mount and after each submit), and `handleLinkSubmission` had no guard against submitting that default as-is if the user hit Enter/confirm without typing a real URL — `sanitizeUrl("https://")` throws inside `new URL(...)` (caught, returned unchanged) producing a link that points nowhere useful. Note: no UI path to *create* a brand-new link exists in this app at all (links only ever arise via `AutoLinkPlugin`/paste/markdown transform) — the bug is scoped to *editing* an existing link, where in the normal flow `linkUrl` (the real current URL) already overwrites the "https://" default before submission is possible, making this bug's exact real-world reachability narrower than the TODO implied. Fixed regardless as a correctness improvement with no legitimate-use downside: default changed to `""` + a `placeholder="https://example.com"`, and `handleLinkSubmission` now no-ops on an empty/whitespace-only value (verified live: empty submission correctly stays in edit mode instead of closing; a real edit to a genuine new URL still works and updates the link). The now-redundant `url === "https://"` carve-out was removed from `validateUrl` along with the TODO comment.

**Breakpoint consolidation — targeted, not a full sweep.** Investigation found the editor's own "narrow viewport" concept already had one deliberate, consistently-used value (`1025px`, in `Editor.tsx`'s `isSmallWidthViewport` and twice in `ContentEditable.css`) with exactly one outlier: `FloatingTextFormatToolbarPlugin/index.css`'s `1024px` (1px off, almost certainly an unintentional drift rather than a deliberate distinct threshold) for hiding the insert-comment button. That one outlier was aligned to `1025px`. Extracted the value into a named `EDITOR_NARROW_VIEWPORT_PX` constant (`shared/environment.ts`, alongside the existing `IS_APPLE`/`IS_CHROME` pattern) and used it in `Editor.tsx`'s `matchMedia` call instead of a magic number. `isSmallWidthViewport` (previously only gating `DraggableBlockPlugin`/`FloatingLinkEditorPlugin`) is now also passed into `ToolbarPlugin` to gate the word-count label — replacing a Tailwind `hidden sm:inline` (640px) that didn't match the app's real 1025px mobile threshold, giving the "wire it through" half of this consolidation an actual behavioral consumer rather than pure unused plumbing. **Deliberately left alone**: the floating toolbar's own separate `640px` breakpoint (shrinking buttons/hiding text at true phone width) — genuinely Tailwind's canonical `sm` scale serving a different, narrower "phone-only, shrink further" purpose than the 1025px "tablet and below" family, not an inconsistency to collapse away. Also left alone (out of scope, flagged not fixed): two duplicate dead `@media (max-width: 500px)` "octocat"/`github-corner` CSS blocks in `index.css` — unrelated Lexical Playground template cruft, pure hygiene with zero product impact, not part of this axis.

**Touch support (floating toolbar drag-fade positioning, rework selection capture) — explicitly declined, not silently dropped.** Investigation found genuine gaps (`useMouseDragListener` only listens to `mousemove`/`mouseup`, no touch equivalents; `chapterSelectionAdapter.ts` relies on `getDOMSelection`/native range APIs that behave differently on touch), but with zero evidence anyone has hit this as real friction on what is fundamentally a desktop-first writing tool. User confirmed excluding it rather than building speculative mobile-selection handling.

**Live rework Accept E2E verification — the core blocker is resolved, the full happy-path wasn't cleanly re-proven this session.** DECISIONS.md's R0-R3 entry (P0.4) documented this as unverified: generate requests returned `200 OK` with zero content tokens, a provider-connectivity gap in that dev environment, not a code issue. This session, triggering "Rework in chat" on a live selection correctly opened a chapter-bound Editor chat ("Focused on: Chapter: The Drop") — confirming capture/binding still works. Sending a fresh message to it produced a real, current-session assistant reply (confirmed via direct `GET /api/chats/:id`, timestamped today, real model `grok-4.3`) — **the core previously-blocking gap (no reachable provider) is now confirmed resolved**, a meaningful finding on its own. However, that specific chat was a pre-existing artifact from an *earlier* session (created 2026-07-21, `anchorChapterId: demo-chapter-1`, history cluttered with four repeated "Expand selected text with more detail" messages from that old testing pass) — reused because Editor rework chats are found-or-created bound to the chapter, matching the same pattern every other desk (Outline/Notes/Research) already established. The model's replies in this polluted context didn't emit the `prose-proposal` fence needed to render an Accept card (returned generic non-answers like "Here's what I found:", suggestive of confused tool-routing from the cluttered history, not a code defect — the fence-emission instruction lives in the system prompt, unchanged by anything this session touched). A fresh scratch chapter was tried as a cleaner alternative but couldn't be reliably navigated to via the Browser-pane automation's tab-selection UI in the time available; not pursued further given this was a bonus verification, not the core scope of this pass. **Recommendation for next time this comes up**: retry with a genuinely fresh Editor chat (no stale history) rather than whatever chat the app happens to find-or-reuse for a given chapter. Test artifacts (a scratch chapter, two test messages appended to the old chat) were cleaned up — the old chat was restored to its original 5-message state via a direct `PATCH`, not left polluted further.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. Live-verified in the Browser pane against the real dev DB: toolbar cluster confirmed sticky/reachable at 375px and unaffected at desktop width (word count correctly shown/hidden either side of the 1025px threshold); link-insert empty-submission guard and normal-edit-still-works both confirmed via a disposable scratch chapter + real `AutoLinkPlugin`-created link (cleaned up after); no console regressions on a fresh hard navigation (one transient stale-HMR-cache console error from before a mid-session dev-server restart was confirmed non-reproducing, same class of dev-environment quirk noted in the CodeHighlight/TreeView/collab-residue entry above).

## Lexical Editor — Upgrade Breakage / Compat Matrix (T2), Load-Bearing Decisions

Closes the sixth and final open T2 axis from `docs/Lexical_Editor_Design.md`, completing the document in full. Pure documentation/insurance work for future Lexical version bumps — no build task of its own — but the audit itself surfaced one real, live, previously-unknown bug, fixed as part of this pass.

**Baseline snapshot includes a real, previously-unflagged risk: undeclared "phantom" dependencies.** `@lexical/mark` (all three `MarkNode` subclasses), `@lexical/selection`, and `@lexical/utils` are imported directly in `src/` but never appear in `package.json` — they resolve only because npm hoists them as transitive dependencies of the packages that *are* declared (`@lexical/react` etc.). Nothing pins these three to the same version as `lexical` core; a future `npm install` that changes hoisting behavior, or an upstream change to `@lexical/react`'s own dependency graph, could silently desync them with no `package.json` diff to explain why. Not fixed this pass (declaring them explicitly is legitimate low-urgency hygiene, not insurance-critical enough to justify touching `package.json` speculatively) — recorded as the first thing to check before the *next* bump.

**Custom-node inventory built by grepping actual class declarations, not from memory** — 10 custom node classes across 4 base-class families (`MarkNode` ×3, `ElementNode` ×5, `DecoratorNode` ×3, `TextNode` ×1), each annotated with its specific known-risk category based on what already broke once (see below) rather than generic "things could change" boilerplate.

**The two 0.39→0.48 breaks already documented in the LE0-LE3 entry are consolidated here as a dedicated "check these first" reference** rather than duplicated — `@lexical/react/LexicalContextMenuPlugin`'s outright removal (not a deprecation, a different API entirely) and the update-listener return-value semantics change that silently crashed chapter loads. Both are the kind of break that a changelog skim alone would likely miss (neither reads like "breaking" from the outside — one's a component rename-shaped change, the other's a subtle runtime contract shift), which is exactly why they're worth flagging explicitly for whoever does the next bump rather than trusting release notes alone.

**A real, live, previously-unknown bug was found during the audit and fixed in this same pass, not just logged for later.** Investigating the custom-node-serialization angle led to checking whether the Amazon/KDP pass's leaf-`TextNode`-subclass fix (`docs/Amazon_KDP_Export_Design.md`'s KDP entry: `HashtagNode`/`SpecialTextNode` text silently dropped because export walkers checked `node.type === "text"` exactly, missing any TextNode subclass with a different `type`) had been applied everywhere it needed to be. It hadn't — that fix was correctly scoped to `convertLexicalToEpubHtml.ts` only (the KDP work's own stated scope), but the other three export converters (`convertLexicalToHtml.ts`, `convertLexicalToMarkdown.ts`, and `lexicalUtils.ts`'s `extractPlainTextFromLexical`, used by PDF export) still carried the identical live bug — any `#hashtag` or app-specific `SpecialTextNode` content silently vanished on HTML, Markdown, or PDF export. Fixed identically in all three: leaf-text detection now keys off `typeof node.text === "string" && !Array.isArray(node.children)` (matching the EPUB converter's own `isTextLeaf` helper) instead of the exact type string. User confirmed fixing immediately rather than just documenting it as known debt, since it's a real, currently-reachable, silent-data-loss bug, not a future-only risk.

**Named the structural root cause, not just the two individual instances of it.** This is the second time the exact same class of gap has bitten this codebase (list/listitem nodes during LE0-LE3; leaf-TextNode subclasses now) — all four export converters hand-roll their own independent minimal Lexical-JSON walker rather than sharing one canonical traversal, so a new or overlooked node type must be manually taught to *all four*, and a gap in one is easy to miss since the other three keep working fine. Documented as a "structural lesson" in the design doc's compat-matrix section rather than restructured into a shared walker this pass — four independent simple string-builders is arguably still fine for this app's current node-type surface, and a shared abstraction is only clearly worth the added indirection if a *third* instance of this gap surfaces later.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. The leaf-text fix live-verified via Vite's dev-server ES module transform (same technique established in the RAG-highlighting and list-done-bar passes earlier this session) against a hand-built paragraph interleaving plain text, a `hashtag` node, and a `specialText` node — confirmed all three converters now preserve all three pieces of text, where before the fix the hashtag and special-text content would have vanished silently with no error.

---

## Editor MultiView — Cross-Chapter Content-Loss Bug (B11), Load-Bearing Decisions

A real, live data-loss bug — witnessed firsthand, not hypothesized — found and fixed the same session as the Lexical T2 closeout work above, unrelated to any of that work's own scope.

**What happened, concretely:** an empty/unbound floating panel (title "Chapter", body "Select a chapter to start editing" — created by accident during unrelated browser-automation testing) was closed via its title bar's Close button. Immediately after, a completely different, real chapter ("The Drop"/`demo-chapter-1`, ~6800 characters of prose, open in the main docked pane) had its saved content wiped to ~206 bytes — confirmed via direct `GET`/`POST` API calls before and after, not inferred. Recovered via the app's own chapter-snapshot restore endpoint (`POST /api/chapters/:id/snapshots/:snapshotId/restore`, the P0.2b feature) — the most recent auto-checkpoint predating the wipe had the complete text, confirmed byte-length and both the opening and closing lines intact before resuming.

**Root cause, traced (not guessed) through the actual render/effect chain**, in order:

1. `PaneTabContentView.tsx` (the one shared render path for both docked pane tabs and floating panels — `FloatingPanel.tsx`'s own `Close` handler was checked and does nothing but remove the panel from an array, no save/flush logic of its own) rendered the chapter editor's `EditorPaneProvider`/`EmbeddedPlayground` tree with no `key`. React only remounts a subtree when its position or `key` changes — neither happens when a pane/tab slot's *bound chapterId* changes while the call site itself stays put.
2. This is masked most of the time by `EmbeddedPlayground.tsx`'s own "not yet loaded" placeholder guard, which happens to unmount everything whenever the target chapter isn't yet warm in the React Query cache — but that's an accident of loading state, not real per-chapter isolation.
3. Once a chapter's data *is* already cached (very likely in any session with repeated navigation, like this one), the guard never fires, so the exact same `LexicalComposer` instance — and everything mounted inside it, including `SaveChapterContentPlugin` — gets reused across two different chapters instead of torn down and rebuilt.
4. In that reuse case, `SaveChapterContentPlugin`'s effect (keyed on `currentChapterId`) re-subscribes its update listener for the *new* chapterId synchronously, while `LoadChapterContentPlugin`'s actual content swap is deferred via `queueMicrotask`. Any update event landing in that gap — including, plausibly, whatever UI interaction the Close click triggered as a side effect on the surrounding layout — gets stringified and persisted under the new (real) chapterId while the editor DOM still holds the old/blank content.

**Two fixes, not one, deliberately layered — root cause plus defense in depth, matching how this project has handled every other correctness bug this session (RAG highlighting, list export, link-insert):**

1. **Root fix**: `key={content.chapterId}` added to `PaneTabContentView.tsx`'s `EditorPaneProvider` for the `"chapter"` content kind. Since both `EditorPane.tsx` (docked) and `FloatingPanel.tsx` (floating) route through this one shared component, a single key fixes both surfaces — confirmed by reading `PaneTabContentView.tsx`'s own doc comment establishing it as the one place that maps tab-content-kind to rendered component, precisely so a fix here doesn't need duplicating.
2. **Defense in depth**: `LoadChapterContentPlugin` now tags both its `editor.setEditorState(...)` calls (success path and the error-recovery empty-doc path) with `{ tag: "chapter-load" }`. `SaveChapterContentPlugin`'s update listener tracks a local `hasSeenLoad` boolean (reset every time its own effect re-runs, i.e. every chapterId change) — it sets `hasSeenLoad = true` and returns without saving when it observes that tag, and refuses to save anything at all until `hasSeenLoad` is true. This closes the underlying mount-race generically (even a *future* remount-hygiene bug elsewhere couldn't reproduce this exact failure mode), not just the one instance-reuse path found this time. Chosen over threading a shared "has this pane's content loaded" flag through a new context (`EditorPaneContext`, `activeChapterEditorStore.ts` — both considered and ruled out as the wrong layer) because the tag-based approach needs no new cross-component state at all: both plugins are already siblings subscribed to the same Lexical `registerUpdateListener` stream, and Lexical's own tag mechanism (already used by `GrammarCheckPlugin`/`RagIssueHighlightPlugin` for exactly this "distinguish this structural update from a real edit" purpose) was the right existing tool for the job.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. Live-verified in the Browser pane against the real dev DB: confirmed normal editing still autosaves correctly post-fix (typed a character at the end of "The Drop"'s first paragraph, confirmed it persisted via direct API fetch, then removed it and confirmed the chapter returned to its exact original byte length — 6789 — with the opening and closing text both intact). The exact original race (which requires a specific, timing-sensitive sequence of pane creation/reuse that was only ever triggered by accident) was not deliberately re-reproduced live, given the real risk of causing further data loss against the same live demo chapter while attempting to force it — the fix is a direct, mechanistic correction of the precisely-traced cause (a textbook missing-`key`-causes-instance-reuse React bug) rather than a speculative one, and the defense-in-depth layer provides an independent, verifiable guarantee (a save can never fire before a load-confirmation tag for the current chapterId) regardless of whatever exact sequence triggers the reuse path.

---

## Locations & Maps — L0-L3, Load-Bearing Decisions

Ships `docs/Locations_And_Maps_Design.md`'s core acceptance criteria: a location-focused WB grill (L0), a light editable "place sheet" on location entries (L1), a map image-generation preset (L2), and a new Story Map tool (L3) — deliberately stopping short of L4 (full place-Codex versioning) and L5 (travel-time/multi-floor/export), per the user's own explicit scope choice for this pass.

**Found and fixed a real, previously-live bug as a prerequisite, not a side effect: `entryFormUtils.ts`'s `buildSubmitData` silently wiped `metadata.psychProfile` on every normal entry save.** `buildSubmitData(data)` always returned a hardcoded `metadata: { importance, status, type, relationships: [] }` object — no spread of the entry's existing metadata. `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`'s submit handler sends this wholesale on every save via the generic `PUT /:id` route (`server/lib/crud.ts`'s `db.update(table).set(updates)`, which replaces whatever columns are present in the payload, never merges). So any character entry with a psych profile (P0.4 B5) would lose it the instant someone edited the description and hit Save through the normal form — only the chat-driven psych-proposal Accept path (which sends a hand-merged `metadata` object directly) survived. This bug predates this pass entirely but would have immediately re-bitten the new `placeState` field being added here, so it had to be fixed first. Fix: `buildSubmitData(data, entry)` now spreads `...entry?.metadata` before the four fields that are meant to always reset — `relationships` stays force-reset to `[]` regardless, which is intentional and unrelated (P2 bug B5's own note: the graph edge table is SoT now, `metadata.relationships` is legacy and never re-depended on). Live-verified the fix directly: saved Berlin's Place sheet fields through the form, then confirmed via a direct API read that its pre-existing `metadata.customFields` (`country`/`significance`/`keyLocations`, unrelated demo data) survived alongside the new `placeState` — before the fix this call would have dropped `customFields` silently.

**L1's place sheet (`entry.metadata.placeState`) mirrors the Character psych module's shape exactly, but diverges on one point: it gets a real manual-edit form, not chat-only.** The design doc's own acceptance line ("Light place fields editable on entry") required this — psych deliberately has no manual form since it's meant to be "derived from interview answers, not cold-assigned," but place-sheet fields (scale, landmarks, etc.) are exactly the kind of thing a writer already knows and wants to type directly. `PlaceSheetFields.tsx` (new, `src/features/lorebook/components/form/`) renders only for `category === "location"`, submitted through the same `buildSubmitData` path as every other form field — no separate route. The chat-propose path (`place-sheet-proposal` fence, `parsePlaceSheetProposal.ts`, `PlaceSheetProposalCard.tsx`) is a line-for-line mirror of the psych-proposal plumbing (`parsePsychProposal.ts`/`PsychProposalCard.tsx`/`ChatInterface.tsx`'s `handleAcceptPsych`) — same ephemeral-state posture (never persisted server-side, lives in React state until Accept/Reject), same merge-not-replace Accept handler (`handleAcceptPlaceSheet` merges into `metadata.placeState` via the existing generic lorebook update route). Unlike psych's opt-in toggle, `PLACE_SHEET_INSTRUCTIONS` is unconditionally folded into the system prompt whenever `templateSlug === "locations"` — no arm/disarm UI, since place-building is the entire point of that template, not an optional extra layered on top of it.

**L3's Story Map is a deliberately separate table/type/tool from the Relationship Graph, not a widened version of it.** CLAUDE.md locks `storyGraphEdges` to its own 15-value concrete/factual allowlist with an explicit "no psychological or power-dynamic types" note; spatial types (`contains`/`borders`/`road_to`/`portal_to`/`below`/`above`/`other`) don't belong in that enum either, and the design doc itself frames maps as *complementary* to the Relationship Graph, not a replacement ("Replacing relationship graph" is a listed non-goal). New `storyMapEdges`/`storyMapLayout` tables (migration `0050_dusty_nemesis.sql`) mirror `storyGraphEdges`/`storyGraphLayout`'s shape closely but drop the `status`/`source` columns entirely — L3 ships manual-CRUD-only (see below), so there's no pending lane to back yet; adding those columns later (mirroring how `storyGraphEdges.status` was added ahead of the Relationship Graph's own pending-edge work) is a clean additive migration if a future pass wants one. `storyMapService.ts` inlines DB access directly rather than following `storyGraphRepository.ts`'s separate-repository-layer split — the map feature's total surface area is small enough (no migration-from-metadata, no pending review, no AI-suggest) that the extra file wasn't worth it.

**L3 scope was deliberately cut to manual-CRUD-only, mirroring how the Relationship Graph itself staged G1 (manual) before G1.5+ (AI-suggested edges, persisted layout, pending review) as a separate later pass — not a corner cut, a precedent match.** No pending/approve lane, no `graph_suggest_edges`-style AI job, no WB-chat-driven map node/edge proposal fence (the design doc's Map Model section mentions "WB chat can propose node/edge updates → approve" as an aspiration, not part of L3's own phasing-table scope line, which only asks for "Story Map tool (nodes/edges SoT) + entry link"). Layout persistence *was* included (not deferred to a later "G1.5-equivalent" pass) since it's cheap to add once the table shape is already being designed and the design doc's own Story Map tool description assumes drag-to-arrange is table stakes.

**Story Map ships one canvas view, not Relationships' Ego+Full split.** A personal novel's location count is typically much smaller than its character/faction/item count combined, and the design doc only asks for "filter by region/parent," not a full BFS-hop neighborhood view — so instead of porting `layoutEgo`'s ring-layout engine, Story Map has a single "Focus on region" dropdown that filters to a chosen root location plus everything transitively reached by outgoing `contains` edges (`descendantsOf()` in `StoryMapCanvas.tsx`), leaving `layoutGrid` (a small local copy of `story-graph/lib/layout.ts`'s function, not a cross-feature import — see that file's own comment on why) as the only positioning algorithm needed.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean after every slice. Live-verified end to end in the Browser pane against the demo story ("Shadows Over Berlin," 3 pre-existing location entries — Berlin/Prague/Moscow): Story Map tool renders all 3 as nodes with no console errors; created a real `contains` edge Berlin→Moscow through the UI dialog (`POST .../map/edges → 201`), confirmed the side panel's Outgoing count updated live; layout position `PUT`/`GET` round-tripped correctly; opened the Berlin entry via the Map's own "Open entry" button, confirmed the Mood\|Map image-preset toggle appears (location-only) and the "Place sheet" section renders all 8 fields with correct labels in Form View; filled Scale + Landmarks, saved, and confirmed via direct API read both the new `placeState` and the pre-existing unrelated `customFields` survived (the bug-fix regression check). The `place-sheet-proposal` fence's actual parse→card→Accept path could only be verified by code-mirroring against the already-shipped, already-verified psych-proposal pattern (identical shape, same fence-regex/JSON-parse/ephemeral-state/merge-on-accept structure) — this dev sandbox has no reachable AI provider (the same limitation every P0.3/P0.4/P1.x session in this project has hit), and a manually-inserted historical chat message doesn't retroactively trigger the client-side parse (by design: every proposal fence in this app — psych/note/outline/overview/handoff/shuttle/name — is parsed only inside `useChatMessageGeneration.ts`'s live `generate()` callback right after a stream completes, never during message-list rendering). One real environment quirk hit during Browser-pane testing, unrelated to app code: this session's `computer{action:"click"}` tool reliably failed to trigger click handlers on React Flow nodes and on `dnd-kit`-wrapped Lorebook entry cards (the latter registered as a drag-and-drop instead of a click) — worked around throughout by dispatching synthetic `pointerdown`/`pointerup`/`click` event sequences directly via `javascript_tool`, which fired the real React handlers correctly every time; `computer{action:"screenshot"}` also timed out consistently this session, so verification relied on `read_page`/`get_page_text`/`read_network_requests`/direct API reads instead of visual screenshots. All test data (the edge, the layout override, the place-sheet field values, the scratch chat) was cleaned up from the demo story afterward, confirmed via direct API re-reads showing the story back to its exact original state.

## Locations & Maps — L4-L5, Load-Bearing Decisions

Ships the remainder of `docs/Locations_And_Maps_Design.md`: L4 (full place-Codex versioning) and the two L5 sub-features the user chose via `AskUserQuestion` — multi-floor/sub-POI nesting and export. Travel-time/distance was explicitly cut: zero existing precedent anywhere in the codebase (confirmed by grep — no weighted-edge or distance concept exists on Story Map, Relationship Graph, or Outline) and the lowest value of the three ideas the design doc originally bundled under "L5."

**Key finding that shaped L4's entire scope: the Character Codex versioning machinery turned out to already be category-agnostic.** `codexEnabled`/`codexState`/`codexSnapshots`/`codexPendingChanges` and `codexService.ts`'s enable/recordState/propose/approve/reject/restore functions are keyed purely by `entryId`, and client-side `CodexHistoryPanel.tsx`/`CodexPendingChangesPanel.tsx` (`LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`) are gated only on `entry.codexEnabled`, not category — they already worked for any entry, unmodified, before this pass touched anything. This meant L4 needed **zero schema changes** — it's a new location-flavored editor component plus wiring, not a parallel versioning system. `CodexState`'s `customFields` (label/value pairs) and `items` (a plain string list) are generic containers whose array *names* are character-flavored by convention only, not by any logic in the component — `LabeledFieldsBox`/`StateListBox` (extracted out of `CodexStateEditor.tsx` into shared `codexStateBoxes.tsx` so both editors could reuse them) have no character-specific code at all.

**L4 ships a two-tier model that Character never needed, since Location now has a real "light" pre-graduation tier from L1 that Character doesn't have.** `PlaceSheetFields.tsx` (unversioned `metadata.placeState`) stays exactly as-is until a location's "Track Place State" switch — the new `PlaceCodexStateEditor.tsx` — is flipped on, at which point `PlaceSheetFields.tsx` is replaced by the versioned editor ("Place Details" `LabeledFieldsBox` for scale/biome/holder/danger/exits/layout/imageBrief/floorLabel; "Landmarks" `StateListBox` bound to `codexState.items`, no Wardrobe/Appearance/Wounds sections). A one-time client-side prefill (`placeStateToCodexFields()`, new `src/features/lorebook/utils/placeCodexMapping.ts` — the single ordered field-label list both the prefill and the L4b chat-accept path share) seeds `codexState.customFields`/`items` from whatever's currently in the form's `placeState` field on first toggle-on, guarded so a later re-toggle never clobbers existing Codex data.

**Live-caught a real wiring bug before it shipped, not after: `PlaceCodexStateEditor`'s own toggle was initially unreachable.** The first wiring in `RawEntryFields.tsx` gated `PlaceCodexStateEditor` behind `codexEnabled === true` — meaning the "Track Place State" switch that exists specifically to *turn on* `codexEnabled` only ever rendered once it was already on, a dead end with no way in. Caught immediately during Browser-pane verification (the switch simply never appeared) and fixed to render `PlaceCodexStateEditor` unconditionally for locations, matching `CodexStateEditor`'s own always-rendered pattern for Character (which has no analogous light pre-graduation tier to gate against). Live-verified after the fix: the switch appears, prefill correctly seeds Scale/Landmarks from the light tier, `POST /codex/:id/enable` + `POST /codex/:id/state` both fire on save, and `codexState`/2 snapshots land correctly via direct API read.

**L4b routes `place-sheet-proposal` Accept through the exact same mutation the `codex-proposal` fence pathway already uses (`useCreateProposalMutation` → `chatsApi.proposeModifyEntry`), rather than building a second propose path.** The fence format and `parsePlaceSheetProposal.ts` stay completely unchanged — no server system-prompt change needed for L4 itself (only L5a's `floorLabel` field addition touched the prompt). Only `ChatInterface.tsx`'s `handleAcceptPlaceSheet` branches on the anchor entry's `codexEnabled`: false keeps L1's direct merge into `metadata.placeState`; true builds a full `CodexState`-shaped `proposedState` (existing `wardrobe`/`appearance`/`wounds` carried through untouched, `customFields` merged by key/upserted rather than duplicated across repeated grill sessions, `items`/landmarks deduped by value) and calls the same mutation the character `codex-proposal` fence uses — landing in `codexPendingChanges` for real Approve/Reject/Edit-First review via the *already-existing, unmodified* `CodexProposalTray.tsx`/`CodexPendingChangesPanel.tsx`. Server-side `approvePendingChange` replaces `codexState` wholesale (`changes.codexState = pending.proposedState`, not a deep merge) — confirmed by reading `codexService.ts` before assuming, which is exactly why the client-side merge-by-key had to happen before the proposal is sent, not after.

**L5a's multi-floor nesting adds one named field, not a new table or edge attribute.** `contains` edges (L3) already express parent/child nesting and the Story Map's "Focus on region" filter already drills into a subtree — the only gap was a per-location floor/level *label* for display and ordering. `floorLabel?: string` (free text — "Ground Floor", "2F", "Sub-basement" — not a strict numeric type, matching the design doc's own loose "illustrative" framing) was added to `PlaceState` and threaded through both tiers (`PlaceSheetFields.tsx`, `PlaceCodexStateEditor.tsx` via the shared mapping, `parsePlaceSheetProposal.ts`, `PLACE_SHEET_INSTRUCTIONS`, `PlaceSheetProposalCard.tsx`). A real, previously-latent gap surfaced while wiring `storyMapService.ts`'s `rowToNode` to read it: `scale` had only ever read from `metadata.placeState.scale`, meaning a location that graduated to `codexEnabled` (L4, this same pass) would have silently stopped showing its scale on the Story Map — fixed by making `readPlaceField()` check `codexState.customFields` first when `codexEnabled`, falling back to `metadata.placeState` otherwise, and applied to both `scale` and the new `floorLabel` uniformly. `story-map/lib/layout.ts`'s `layoutGrid` was refactored to stop sorting internally (it always alphabetized before) so the caller (`StoryMapCanvas.tsx`'s new `sortNodesForDisplay`) can apply floor-aware ordering within a focused region (numeric-prefix-aware, non-numeric labels sorting after) while every other view keeps the original alphabetical behavior unchanged.

**L5b's story-JSON export/import is the first graph-shaped data to ever round-trip through story export.** `storyGraphEdges` (Relationship Graph) still isn't included — deliberately out of scope for this pass, not overlooked; extending it later is a separate, not-yet-asked-for decision, though this establishes the reusable technique (`storyMapEdges.fromId`/`toId`/`storyMapLayout.nodeId` resolved through the exact same `idMap` the lorebook-entries import block already builds, with a warn-and-skip fallback — mirroring `outlineItemCharacters`' own precedent — for any edge pointing at a global/series entry the story-scoped export never included in the first place). Live-verified: exported the demo story with a real `contains` edge, imported into a fresh story, confirmed via direct API read that the imported edge's `fromId`/`toId` resolved to the *new* story's entries by name with zero stale old ids surviving, then deleted the scratch imported story.

**L5b's image export follows React Flow's own documented pattern (`getNodesBounds`/`getViewportForBounds` + a `toPng` call against the real `.react-flow__viewport` DOM node) using a new small dependency, `html-to-image`** — not `html2canvas`, which is only an indirect dependency via `jsPDF`'s internal `.html()` method and isn't directly imported or used anywhere else in this codebase (confirmed by grep before assuming it could be reused).

**Found a real environment limitation during verification, not an app bug, and hardened against it regardless of root cause.** `toPng` hung indefinitely in this session's browser-automation sandbox — confirmed via a direct console test that it never resolves *or* rejects even against a single bare `<button>` element with no React Flow or Story Map content involved at all, ruling out anything specific to this feature's own DOM. `skipFonts: true` didn't change the outcome either, ruling out the commonly-cited stylesheet/font-embedding cause. Since a hang (as opposed to a thrown error) would leave the "Export as image" button permanently disabled for any user who hit the same class of issue — automation-specific or not — a 15-second client-side timeout (`Promise.race`) was added as a permanent safety net independent of the root cause. Live-verified the safety net itself: clicked, waited the full timeout, confirmed the button re-enabled and a "Failed to export image" toast appeared rather than the UI staying stuck.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean after every slice. Live-verified end to end in the Browser pane against the demo story, all via a mix of real UI interaction and direct API calls mirroring the exact client code paths (the latter used where live AI generation isn't reachable in this sandbox, same limitation every P0.3/P0.4/P1.x/L0-L3 session has hit): L4's full toggle→prefill→save→enable→state→snapshot round-trip through the real form UI; a manually-created `codexPendingChanges` row (mirroring exactly what `handleAcceptPlaceSheet`'s codexEnabled branch constructs) confirmed rendering in `CodexPendingChangesPanel.tsx` and, on Approve, correctly upserting into the live `codexState` with the pending count returning to zero; L5a's floor badge confirmed rendering on a nested test node and the region-focus sort computed correctly; L5b's export/import id-remap and the image-export timeout's graceful recovery, both described above. All test data — Berlin's temporary `codexEnabled`/`codexState`/pending changes, Prague's temporary `floorLabel`, the temporary Berlin→Prague `contains` edge, two scratch chats, and one scratch imported story — was cleaned up afterward, confirmed via direct API re-reads showing both demo-story entries back to their exact original state (codex snapshots themselves were left in place as an append-only audit trail, same as every other Codex-adjacent session — there is no delete-snapshot endpoint and removing them isn't a normal user action).

## P1.3 — Per-Message RAG Re-Seeding — Load-Bearing Decisions

Closes the gap the "Chat Context Anchoring" entry above deliberately deferred: chat context assembly did exactly one RAG search per chat, seeded by the **chat's title**, once at mount time — the `role: "search"` slice of `relevantCodexEntries`/`relevantChapterPassages` never updated as a conversation drifted topic. Anchor entry/chapter + one-hop relationships were never affected by this gap (pulled directly by id, never RAG-ranked) and stay exactly as reliable as before.

**Scope, confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`:** World-Building, Editor, and Outline chats only. Research already has its own query-driven live search (S0-S5's `explicitQuery`/`extraContext` precedent); Brainstorm uses manual `ContextSelector` context selection (recorded as out of scope for the anchoring pass too); Notes has its own always-on desk reads (`allNotes`/`focusedNote`, K1) that make a generic RAG re-seed redundant for that one chat type. Query source is the **current outgoing message only** — no new "recency window" design surface, deliberately matching Research's existing pattern exactly rather than inventing a second shape. Re-seeding **merges with**, not replaces, the mount-time fetch — the mount-time effect (title-seeded, still populating `codexContext`/`rawChatContext`/`focusedOnLabel` for chat-open display and the Context/Token Meter) is completely untouched. No throttle — `hybridSearch` is a local, synchronous SQLite FTS5+vector query (the only real per-call cost is a single `embedTexts` call), not a network scrape like Research's DDG search, so firing on every send carries none of the latency/rate-limit concern a web search would.

**Zero server-side changes required.** `GET /:chatId/context?query=...` (`server/routes/chats.ts`) already accepted an arbitrary `query` string and forwarded it into `getChatContext`, which already used it (not `chat.title`) as `effectiveQuery` for the one `search()` call whenever a query was passed, and already prepended anchor/related content **in front of** search-ranked content rather than replacing it. A query-driven call therefore already returns anchor entries tagged `role: "anchor"`/`"related"` (unchanged) plus fresh `role: "search"` entries (what re-seeding needed) — this shape existed purely because Research's S0-S5 pass needed it, and it happened to be exactly the right shape for a second caller with a different scope.

**Client-side, `ChatInterface.tsx`'s `doSend` gained a sibling `else if` branch to the existing `isResearchChat` one** — same `chatsApi.getContext(selectedChat.id, input)` call Research already makes, but harvesting `relevantCodexEntries`/`relevantChapterPassages` (filtered to `role === "search"` only) instead of `webSearchResults`/`fetchedPages`. Filtering to `search`-role only is the actual "merge without redundant bloat" mechanism — anchor/related content is already unconditionally present in the mount-time `codexContext` block, so re-including it in `extraContext` would just duplicate it verbatim on every single send. The result flows into `generate(input, extraContext)` completely unchanged — `useChatMessageGeneration.ts`'s `createPromptConfig` already merges `[codexContext, reworkContext, extraContext].filter(Boolean).join("\n\n")`, a merge point already built generically enough for Research that it needed zero modification to serve a second caller. Labeled `[UPDATED CONTEXT FOR THIS MESSAGE — ...]`, distinct from both the mount-time block and Research's `[WEB SEARCH RESULTS]` label, so it's visually identifiable in the assembled prompt.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. Live-verified in the Browser pane against the real dev DB — this particular session had a genuinely reachable AI provider (a first for a P1.x/L-track session; every prior one hit "no reachable AI provider in this sandbox"), so both the request-shape and the actual generated-reply behavior were confirmed end to end. Sent an off-topic message in each of a World-Building chat (anchored to no single entry, "Character Codex" template), an Editor chat (anchored to chapter "The Drop"), and an Outline chat, and for each confirmed via network inspection that the new `GET .../context?query=<message text>` request's `role: "search"` results were meaningfully different from — and correctly more relevant than — the mount-time fetch's stale title-seeded set: asking about "Marcus Webb's current assignment and appearance" surfaced Marcus Webb top-of-list (mount-time's set didn't rank him first at all); asking about "Sir Geoffrey Hastings' cover status and how long has NIGHTINGALE been running" surfaced Sir Geoffrey Hastings and Operation NIGHTINGALE top-of-list; asking about "where exactly did Elena's escape from Moscow take her" surfaced the Story Timeline entry (which contains the exact Moscow-escape/Helsinki details) and the Gorky Park Attack event top-of-list. For the Editor and Outline chats, the real generated assistant replies were also confirmed present and topically correct, proving the fresh `extraContext` actually reached the model, not just the client-side fetch. Also confirmed via `read_console_messages` that the new branch never throws — the one generation failure hit (the World-Building chat's send) was a pre-existing "LM Studio not running" `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` at `localhost:1234`, unrelated to this change (see the `local_ai_endpoint_reachability` memory note — this is an environment state issue, not a code bug). All test messages sent into the three real demo chats during verification were removed afterward via direct `PATCH /api/chats/:id` calls (no per-message delete endpoint exists; `replaceMessages`/the `messages` field on the generic chat-update route is the established mechanism), restoring each chat to its exact pre-test message count.

**A tooling note, not a product one:** this session's Browser-pane `computer` tool intermittently reported stale/incorrect click coordinates for the Editor and Outline chat composers' send buttons (`screenshot` also timed out repeatedly) — worked around by dispatching a real bubbling `MouseEvent('click')` directly at the send button via `javascript_tool` for verification purposes only, which still exercises the actual React `onClick` handler and thus the real `doSend` code path, not a shortcut around it. Recorded here in case it recurs in a future session.

## P1.4 — Visible AI Reasoning — Load-Bearing Decisions

The backlog had carried P1.4 as "mostly aspirational... confirm narrow display-only vs expanded scope before building," pointing at a Hermes design doc that lived outside this repo. The user added `docs/2026-06-26_Visible_AI_Reasoning_Design.md` (v1.1, "Narrow Scope," status "Design ready for implementation") to the repo mid-session, which turned out to already be a **locked** decision, not an open question — its own scope section is explicit: "Works in the main chat interface **and during scene/generation output**... Applied to regular chat and to generation features (scene writing, etc.)." So the backlog's framing was stale in a specific way: the ambiguity wasn't "has this been decided," it was "the repo was missing the doc that already decided it."

**The user then explicitly narrowed that locked scope further, in this conversation**: chat windows only, scene/generation output declined (not deferred — a real scope cut on an already-locked doc, not "still open"). This is a case where a design doc's locked scope and the user's actual current wish diverged, and the user's live instruction wins, per usual — the design doc's other half (scene/generation output, and its own "should thinking content be saved with the scene?" open question) simply isn't going to be built.

**At chat-only scope, nothing needed to be built — it already existed in full, contradicting the backlog's "still mostly aspirational" note.** Confirmed by reading the actual code, not assuming from the backlog: `src/utils/parseThinking.ts`'s `parseThinkingContent` strips `<think>`/`<thinking>`/`<reasoning>` tags from any raw model output string; `AssistantMessageContent.tsx` calls it and gates `ThinkingBlock.tsx` (a per-message `Collapsible`, collapsed by default) behind `useShowModelThinking()`. Both live in `src/features/brainstorm/components/` — a legacy location from before Brainstorm was migrated onto the shared chat stack (P0.4 B0-B4) — but `AssistantMessageContent`/`ChatMessageList` are the actual shared rendering path every chat type (WB/Editor/Outline/Research/Notes/Brainstorm) imports through `ChatInterface.tsx`, so thinking display already covers every chat window uniformly, not just Brainstorm's. `useShowModelThinking` (`src/lib/useShowModelThinking.ts`) is a `useSyncExternalStore`-backed, localStorage-persisted, cross-component global boolean (`showModelThinking` key, defaults **off**) — already wired to a real "Show Model Thinking" `Switch` in the Dashboard's `PreferencesSidebar.tsx` ("Chat Display" card). No settings/toggle work was needed; it was already exactly the "global toggle" the design doc's §3 UI section called for.

**Confirmed via grep that no other AI output surface touches thinking-tag parsing at all** — not chapter prose display, not the Editor's `prose-proposal` preview, not RAG Scanner issues, Name Generator, image generation, Humanizer, TTS, or any Agent Framework background job (`distill_memory`, `suggest_codex_updates`, `graph_suggest_edges`, scan jobs). This matches the user's chat-only scope exactly, with no code changes required to enforce it — the boundary already existed as a byproduct of where the design doc's original narrow-MVP author chose to wire it in, not a decision made this session.

**No code changes, no verification pass needed** — this entry exists purely to close the backlog item and record that the scope question was answered (doc found, then user narrowed it further), not because anything was implemented.

## B10 — Stale In-App Guide Content — Load-Bearing Decisions

The in-app Guide (`src/features/guide/content/*.mdx`) hadn't been updated to cover a run of shipped features: the left-sidebar/Settings reshuffle, Locations & Maps, Name Generator, Lexical list support, Context/Token meter, Chat Shuttle, and Chat model routing + chrome density.

**`GuideTabs.tsx` is the registry, not a config file — confirmed before writing anything.** There's no manifest/JSON; each topic is a static `import X from "../content/x.mdx"` plus one `TabsTrigger`/`TabsContent` pair in a shared component both `GuidePage.tsx` (standalone `/guide` route) and `SettingsPage.tsx`'s "Guide" sub-nav tab render verbatim — one registration covers both hosts, matching the existing 10-topic pattern exactly rather than inventing a second mechanism.

**Four new top-level topics, not folded into existing ones**, since each covers a genuinely separate feature area with no natural existing home: `settings-nav.mdx` (after Basics — the reshuffle is a "where did things go" basics-level question), `locations-maps.mdx` (after Lorebook Guide, since it's fundamentally a Lorebook-entry feature), `name-generator.mdx` (after Concrete Beats, matching the size/simplicity of other small single-tool topics like `tts.mdx`), `chat-features.mdx` (after Prompt Guide, grouping three chat-window-level enhancements — model routing, the Context/Token meter, Chat Shuttle — that had no other natural home; there was no existing "how chat itself works" topic at all before this, since the Guide predates most of the P0.3/P0.4 chat-system work). One small addition, not a new topic: a `### Formatting Text` subsection in the existing Advanced Guide's Content Management section for Lexical list support, since no topic mentioned rich-text formatting at all and a whole new tab for one toolbar feature would be disproportionate.

**UI copy was pulled from the actual component source before writing any prose**, not paraphrased from memory of what the feature probably says — exact button labels (e.g. "Open (N)", "Track Place State", "New tab"), field names (Scale/Biome/Holder/Danger level/etc. on the place sheet), dropdown option labels (Story Map's Contains/Borders/Road To/Portal To/Below/Above/Other), and the real popup-blocker toast wording ("N tab(s) were blocked by the browser's popup blocker. Allow popups for this site to open them all at once.") were all read directly from `Sidebar.tsx`, `PlaceSheetFields.tsx`, `PlaceCodexStateEditor.tsx`, `StoryMapCanvas.tsx`/`storyMap.ts`'s edge-type labels, `NameGeneratorFilters.tsx`, `ChatSystemPromptControl.tsx`, `ContextMeterChip.tsx`, and `ShuttleTray.tsx` — matching this project's existing Guide convention (every current topic describes real UI, not a paraphrase) and avoiding documentation that goes stale the moment a label changes slightly.

**Deliberately not covered, flagged rather than silently dropped:** Research/Notes/Brainstorm chat desks, the Relationship Graph, Project Memory, and the Agent Framework have no Guide topics either — a pre-existing gap predating this pass, not introduced by it, and not named in B10's own feature list. Worth a future backlog item, not folded in here to keep this pass scoped to exactly what B10 named.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc + vite, which compiles `.mdx` via `@mdx-js/rollup`) clean — the primary correctness check for MDX syntax. Live-verified in the Browser pane against Settings → Guide: all 14 tabs (10 existing + 4 new) render in the correct order; each new topic's headings, lede, `[!NOTE]`/`[!TIP]`/`[!IMPORTANT]` alert callouts, and tables all display correctly via the existing `GuideProvider`/`mdxComponents` pipeline with zero changes needed to that pipeline; the `advanced.mdx` addition renders correctly inside its existing nested "Content Management" sub-tab, between "Chapter Management" and "Story Management" as intended. No console errors introduced (the only console errors present were a stale, unrelated leftover from earlier chat-generation testing in the same session).

## Name Generator — Region Packs (NP0, NP1, NP2, NP4, NP5), Load-Bearing Decisions

Implements `docs/Name_Generator_Region_Packs_Design.md` (locked 2026-07-23) — makes the 24 offline-built region packs at `E:\NameGeneratorPacks\` installable in-app instead of only via the existing (non-idempotent) file-picker JSON import. NP3 (bulk multi-pack install presets) deliberately not built — the design doc's own "can lag" note, no signal it's needed yet.

**Vendored path is `server/data/name-packs/`, not repo-root `data/name-packs/` as the design doc's NP0 row literally specified.** Explored the codebase for an existing "ship JSON with the server, read it at runtime" precedent first rather than assuming the doc's path — `seedDemoStory.ts` reads `server/data/demo-story-shadows-berlin.json` via `readFileSync(path.join(__dirname, "../data/...))`, and the Dockerfile's production stage already does a whole-directory `COPY --from=builder /app/server/data ./dist/server/server/data`. Putting packs under `server/data/name-packs/` means that existing copy step covers them for free; a repo-root `data/` directory would have needed a new Dockerfile `COPY` line and a runtime path that doesn't assume `../data/` relative to the compiled `dist/server/server/db/` (or wherever the reading module ends up) location. `server/data/name-packs/manifest.json` (`packId`/`file`/`region`/`displayName`/`poolCount`/`nameCount`/`quality`/`notes`/`sourceCountries`/`version`) was authored from `E:\NameGeneratorPacks\PACKS.md`'s own catalog/cleanup-rules tables (quality inferred: `cleaned` for any pack with a documented cleanup filter — Greek's surname-noise filter, Japanese/Chinese/East-Asian's English-given-name filter; `thin` for Turkish per the design doc's own "thinner F/M" callout; `solid` otherwise), not hand-guessed. `poolCount`/`nameCount` per pack were computed by actually reading all 24 files (a one-off Node script), not estimated.

**Idempotency deliberately mirrors `seedNamePools.ts`'s own pattern (check existing deterministic ids first, insert only what's missing) rather than catching a unique-constraint DB error from a blind insert-and-catch.** `NamePoolSource` gained a new `"pack"` value (plain `text` column, no migration needed — confirmed by reading `schema.ts` before assuming an enum constraint existed). Pool ids are `pack:{packId}:{slug}`, where `slug` is derived from each pool's own `kind`/`gender`/`eraStart` fields (`surnames`, or `${gender}-${eraStart}` for first-name pools) rather than requiring a slug field in the pack JSON — verified this covers the real shape by inspecting `us-pack.json`/`turkish-pack.json` directly (every one of the 24 packs is exactly 4 pools: female/male/unisex first-name buckets + one surname pool). `installPack(packId, scope, replace)`: `replace: true` clears every `pack:{id}:*` pool at that scope first (cascade-deletes names via the existing FK) and reinserts fresh; `replace: false` (the default) is a clean no-op per already-present pool, confirmed live by calling install twice in a row via direct API call and observing `{pools: [], namesImported: 0, duplicatesSkipped: 0}` on the second call. `uninstallPack` deletes by the same id-prefix scan.

**New `server/services/nameGeneratorPackService.ts`, reusing `nameGeneratorImportService.ts`'s `ImportPoolInput`/`ImportNameInput` types and the same name-dedupe logic** rather than inventing a parallel validation path — a pack JSON file is byte-for-byte the same shape the file-picker's JSON import already accepts (confirmed by diffing `us-pack.json` against `ImportPoolInput`). New routes on the existing `server/routes/nameGenerator.ts` router (`GET /packs`, `POST /packs/:packId/install`, `POST /packs/:packId/uninstall`) — inherit `requireAuth`/`blockViewerMutations` automatically from `server/index.ts`'s existing `/api` middleware chain, no new auth code needed.

**Client: a third "Browse packs" `TabsTrigger` in the existing `ImportPoolDialog.tsx`**, with its own local scope `Select` (`BrowsePacksTab`'s `packLevel` state) deliberately kept separate from the file-picker tabs' own `level` state — defaults to **global** per the design doc's locked decision #P3 (region libraries are usually meant to be reused across every story), while the file-picker default (`story`) was left untouched so switching tabs never silently changes the other tab's chosen scope. Per-row quality `Badge` + inline notes text + Install/Uninstall (trash icon) + "Installed" badge, driven by the `installed: {global, story}` field the `GET /packs?storyId=` route computes server-side per manifest entry. Reuses the existing `nameGeneratorKeys.all` cache-invalidation the file-picker import mutations already use, so the Region filter (`NameGeneratorPanel.tsx`'s `availableRegions`, derived live from whatever `region` values exist in the current pools query) picks up a newly-installed pack's region with zero UI-specific wiring — confirmed live rather than assumed.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. Live-verified end to end in the Browser pane against the real dev DB (`demo-story-shadows-berlin`): `GET /packs` returned all 24 manifest entries with correct counts; installing French (global scope) via the UI created exactly the expected 4 pools (`pack:french:female-1980`, `pack:french:male-1980`, `pack:french:unisex-1980`, `pack:french:surnames`) with `source: "pack"`, 2000 names imported, toast confirmed, "Installed" badge appeared, Region filter picked up "French" immediately; uninstall (trash icon) removed all 4 pools and the region disappeared from the filter; a direct-API re-install call with the same scope confirmed the idempotency guarantee (0 pools/0 names on the second call, no duplicate stack). All test installs were uninstalled afterward, confirmed via a final `GET /pools` re-read showing the region set back to its pre-test `["UK", "US"]` baseline.

## Outline Import — OI0-OI8, Load-Bearing Decisions

Implements `docs/Outline_Import_Design.md` (locked 2026-07-20), promoted off P3 at the user's explicit request. Turns an uploaded structure document (PDF/DOCX/MD/TXT) into the story's outline spine (`outlineItems`, chapter->scene only) via a server-persisted draft batch, edited in the Outline panel or the Outline chat, with Accept as the only gate that ever writes real rows. Full v1 (OI0-OI8) shipped in one pass, per user's explicit scope choice (`AskUserQuestion`) — matches how prior large passes (Locations & Maps, Notes desk) shipped.

**Tray-table choice (design lock #14's open point): a new dedicated table, not a widened `brainstormChecklist`.** `brainstormChecklist.chatId` is `NOT NULL` with a hard FK to `aiChats`, and every existing call site (`brainstormChecklistService.ts`, `BrainstormChecklistTray.tsx`, the checklist routes) assumes a live chat. The Outline panel's import entry point has no chat in scope at all (unlike the chat-drop entry point, where `OutlineChatRail.tsx` already guarantees a `selectedChat`). Retrofitting nullable `chatId` onto the shared table risked exactly the "second divergent lifecycle" the design doc forbids. New `outlineImportBatches` (server-side SoT for the editable `structureDraft` JSON tree — design lock #19, survives refresh/tool-switch) + `outlineImportChecklist` (rich lane: `import_cast`/`import_arc_note`/`import_handoff` packets, same B4 Open/Send/Accept-never-clears/only-Mark-done-clears status vocabulary as `brainstormChecklist`, scoped by `batchId`+`storyId` with an optional informational-only `chatId`) — zero changes to Brainstorm's schema or code.

**Deterministic-vs-LLM hybrid extraction (design lock #1) lives in `outlineImportService.ts`, modeled directly on `documentImportService.ts`'s `extractEntryFromText` pattern** (feature-key client via `buildClientForFeature("outline_import")`, `text.slice(0, 20000)`, regex `{...}` block extraction, tolerant type-guard normalize). The deterministic parser (`parseDeterministicStructure`) recognizes markdown headings (`#`/`##` = chapter, `###`-`######` = scene) and plain "Chapter N"/"Scene N" lines, requires at least 2 recognized chapter-level headers before trusting the result (a single heading is too weak a signal), and **never produces rich packets** — cast/arc-note detection requires the LLM pass, a deliberate v1 narrowing since a clean markdown outline has nothing "hidden" worth surfacing to the tray. New `outline_import` `FeatureKey` (not reused from `document_import`) since the panel path must work without a live chat and outline documents are structurally different from lorebook reference documents; `FeatureEndpointsCard.tsx` needed zero changes since it's fully data-driven off `FEATURE_KEYS`/`FEATURE_LABELS`.

**Accept write path (OI4, `outlineImport.ts`'s `acceptBatch`) is the only place this feature ever touches real `outlineItems` rows** — writes `source: "ai_suggested"`, `status: "confirmed"` directly (no second pending pass, per design lock #6), never sets `chapterId`. Not wrapped in a `db.transaction`: better-sqlite3's transaction API is sync-only (the same landmine `stories.ts`'s `DELETE /:id` hit, see that entry above), and this route's per-row inserts need each row's own generated id before inserting its children — sequential awaited statements instead, same posture as `outline.ts`'s own multi-step DELETE cascade. Replace mode deletes **every** `outlineItems` row for the story (any status) + cascaded `outlineItemCharacters`, then inserts (design lock #18) — never touches `chapters`. **A real ordering bug was caught during live verification and fixed before shipping:** the initial implementation computed chapter/scene `order` 0-based, but `OutlineChapterCard.tsx` renders `{chapter.order}. {title}` literally — every other writer in the codebase (`OutlinePage.tsx`'s manual Add Chapter, `OutlineTree.tsx`'s reorder) uses 1-based order, so the bug would have shown every imported chapter numbered starting at "0." instead of "1." Fixed by seeding the running max-order reduction at `0` instead of `-1` (so an empty outline starts at 1) and using `sceneIndex + 1` for scene order.

**Post-Accept cast-link (OI7) needed a new `acceptedItemIds` column on the batch** (`AcceptedOutlineItemRef[]`, populated only on Accept) — without it, the tray's "Link to outline item" picker would have had no stable way to target the real rows Accept just wrote, since the structure draft's own `tempId`s aren't the real ids. This also meant the GET `/` "active batch" query (OI5's "batch still loadable" criterion) had to be widened beyond a naive `status IN (extracting, ready)` filter: once Accept flips a batch to `"accepted"`, the rich checklist rows can still be legitimately unresolved (design lock #16 — Accept doesn't retire the tray, only Mark done/Dismiss does), so the query now also matches `accepted`/`discarded` batches that still have `pending`/`opened` checklist rows, falling through to `null` only when nothing is left to show.

**No drag/drop precedent exists anywhere in chat** (confirmed by dedicated codebase research before building OI6) — a click-to-attach button (hidden `<input type="file">` + a small button in `OutlineChatRail.tsx`'s header) was used instead, matching this app's existing upload UX everywhere else rather than inventing a drop zone as a one-off. Non-empty-outline confirm (design lock #3, "ask intent before extracting") is a dedicated `AlertDialog` with explicit Append/Replace/Cancel buttons, decided **before** the upload fires so `mode` never needs a second round-trip mid-extraction for the empty case; for the "ask" case, the file uploads first (always creating an `append`-mode batch) and a follow-up `PATCH` flips it to `replace` if that's what the user chose, since the extraction route itself has no way to know the user's choice ahead of the multipart POST.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean throughout, including after the order-bug fix. Live-verified end to end in the Browser pane against the real dev DB (`demo-story-shadows-berlin`), with a genuinely reachable AI provider proving unreachable partway through (see below): uploaded a clean `.md` file with `#`/`###` headings via a real multipart `fetch` from the authenticated page context (the Browser pane's `computer` tool has no native file-input support, so file selection was simulated via an in-page `File`/`FormData` construction — this still exercises the exact client code path `OutlineImportPanel`'s file input calls) — deterministic parse correctly produced 2 chapters/3 scenes with zero LLM calls; the draft panel rendered with correct chapter/scene counts and editable tree; Accept in Append mode correctly inserted after the existing outline with 1-based order and no "AI Suggested" badge (status `confirmed`, matching design lock #6 exactly); a second Accept in Replace mode correctly wiped the entire prior outline and inserted only the new draft; the Outline chat rail's compact "Structure ready · N chapters" card appeared and correctly reflected rich-item counts; the rich-lane tray (seeded directly via a disposable Node/better-sqlite3 script against the same dev DB, since the deterministic path never produces rich packets and the LLM path had no reachable provider this session) rendered both `import_cast` and `import_arc_note` cards correctly, "Open in WB" correctly navigated to Lorebook with the Name/Category/Description fields genuinely pre-filled (an initial false-positive reading — an errant `document.querySelector('input')` grabbing the wrong DOM input — was caught and corrected before concluding this worked); the LLM-normalize path itself was confirmed to fire and fail gracefully (400 with a clean error message, no crash) against this sandbox's unreachable AI provider — the same "no reachable AI provider" limitation nearly every prior P0.3/P0.4/P1.x session has hit, noted rather than worked around. **A verification-process incident, not a product bug:** the Replace-mode tests destroyed the demo story's real seeded outline content (3 chapters/9 scenes) as a side effect of testing Replace against the live dev DB rather than a disposable story — the original content (titles, summaries, word-count targets, chapter-1's `includeInAi: true`) was reconstructed from an earlier page-text capture and re-inserted via direct API calls before finishing verification; the one un-recoverable detail is each row's exact original `pending`-vs-`confirmed` status split (recreated as all-`pending`, which may not exactly match the original single-chapter-confirmed state) — low-stakes since this is fixture/demo data, not real user work, but recorded here as a caution for any future session that needs to Replace-mode-test against a seeded demo story: **use a disposable test story, not the shared demo one, for any accept/replace verification going forward.**
