## Character Guided Playbook Packs — PP0-PP5, Load-Bearing Decisions

Implements `docs/Character_Guided_Playbook_Packs_Design.md` (mechanics locked 2026-07-19), promoted off P3 at user request. Ships PP0-PP5 (schema, seeded shipped shells, a dedicated Playbooks panel + import, WB Character arm toggle, resolve-ladder context injection, story/global export-import) with **placeholder shipped-pack bodies** — PP6 (real interview-question prose) stays explicitly deferred until the user locks that content, per the design doc's own "mechanics must ship without waiting for final prose" allowance.

**Reworked away from the design doc's original "Notes-backed" storage premise before writing any code**, after live research found it didn't fit reality: `notes.storyId` is `NOT NULL` (no representable global scope), `notes` has no `metadata`/`folderId` column, and the Notes desk has no folder-tree UI at all (Lorebook is the only place with that engine). Raised this to the user directly rather than forcing the doc's assumption; a follow-up conversation revealed their actual mental model — packs are a distinct object ("separate sets of .md files with lists of interview questions"), not a note subtype, and they explicitly want the full 3-tier story→global→shipped ladder since packs get reused across stories. Landed on: a **brand-new dedicated `playbookPacks` table** (not notes-backed, deviating from the doc's lock with the user's explicit sign-off) with `storyId` nullable from day one — no `NOT NULL`-relaxation risk since this is a fresh `CREATE TABLE`, not an `ALTER` on an existing one — mirroring `agentMemories.storyId`'s own nullable-global convention. A **dedicated "Playbooks" panel/tool**, not a Notes-desk folder — its own sidebar tool (`PlaybookPacksTool.tsx`) for the in-story view and a Settings card (`PlaybookPacksPanel storyId={null}`, next to `WriterPrefsCard`) for the global-scope view, both backed by the same `PlaybookPacksPanel` component.

**Copy-on-edit (design doc §11's recommended lean)**: shipped rows (`packScope: "shipped"`) are never directly editable or deletable (`updatePlaybookPack`/`deletePlaybookPack` both check-and-reject); "editing" a shipped or global pack calls `copyPackForEdit`, which creates a new row at the target scope with `sourcePackId` tracing back to the original — the original stays pristine. "Reset to shipped/global" is just deleting the override row, since the ladder falls through automatically.

**Resolve ladder (`resolvePlaybookPackService.ts`'s `resolvePlaybookPack`) is three sequential exact-match queries (story → global → shipped), not a single `OR`-based query** — `agentMemoriesRepository.ts`'s own `listMemories` was checked first as a precedent and turned out not to have a ready-made "story-exact, else global" fallback query to copy (its `global`/`storyId` params are mutually exclusive, not a ladder), so this was written fresh. Missing pack at every tier resolves to `null`, not an error (design doc: "soft success").

**Context injection is a brand-new pattern in this codebase, not a copy of an existing one.** Research into the psych-module/place-sheet precedents (the two existing "read structured character/location data into context" features) found neither actually reads its own `metadata.psychProfile`/`metadata.placeState` back into the prompt at all — they're pure system-prompt *instructions* telling the model to derive fields from conversation and emit a proposal fence. Playbook packs are the first feature to directly resolve-and-inject a document body into context (design doc §5: "direct inject... not hope hybrid search finds the note") — modeled instead on `resolveMemories`'s DB-read-then-Promise.all shape, added as a new `resolvePlaybookPackContext` in `chatContextService.ts`, gated on `chat.usePlaybookPack === true && chat.templateSlug === "character_codex"` (v1 Character-only scope). The `[PLAYBOOK PACK — interview curriculum, not story canon]` block(s) are assembled client-side in `ChatInterface.tsx`'s existing `sections.push(...)` pattern (same as the Notes/Memory blocks), joined into the mount-time `codexContext` — not the per-message `extraContext` path, since a pack is stable per-chat like Notes/Memory, not query-dependent like RAG/web search. `usePlaybookPack` was added to the context-fetch effect's dependency array to avoid repeating the exact stale-context bug class B5 hit once already for `wbStyle`/`includePsychModule`.

**`GuidedSetupControl.tsx`'s `extraToggle` (a single optional slot, B5's original shape) was generalized to `extraToggles` (an array)** — Character chats now need two toggles (psych module + playbook pack arm) under Guided Setup, and the old shape only supported one. The only call site (`LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`) was updated to pass both; Guided Setup's click handler now also unconditionally arms `usePlaybookPack` for Character chats (design doc §3's click sequence — arm always fires, unlike the psych nudge which is Grill-only), and a small "Open Playbooks" link was added next to the toggle row (`useStoryContext().setCurrentTool("playbooks")`).

**`aiChats.usePlaybookPack` column threading required touching four places beyond the schema**, all mirroring `includePsychModule`'s existing shape exactly: `src/types/story.ts`'s `AIChat` interface, `chatsApi.update`'s inline body type in `client.ts` (not `Partial<AIChat>` — the route's PATCH body is its own explicit whitelist type, not the domain type), `server/routes/chats.ts`'s PATCH handler (destructure + type + `metaFields` assignment, three separate spots), and `chatRepository.ts`'s `UpdateChatMetaFields`/`updateMeta`. `toChat`'s `...row` spread meant no read-side change was needed — the column flows through automatically once written.

**Import (`.md`/`.txt` only) reuses the plain-text branch of `documentImportService.ts`'s `extractTextFromFile`** rather than adding a second file-reading path, with a minimal hand-rolled frontmatter parser (`parsePackFrontmatter`, plain regex over a leading `---` block) instead of a new YAML dependency, since only three flat string fields need to round-trip. The import dialog always sends `playbookKey`/`style`/`packScope` as explicit form-field fallbacks (not just relying on frontmatter) — an early draft of the dialog only offered them via optional frontmatter and would have 400'd on every plain file with no frontmatter, caught and fixed before shipping.

**Global-pack backup/portability got its own `/playbook-packs/global/export`/`import` pair**, mirroring the existing Lorebook global-export precedent (`type: "global-lorebook"` marker) with its own `type: "global-playbook-packs"` marker — per the design doc's explicit "do not leave story export as the only backup for globals" requirement. Story export/import only ever carries `packScope: "story"` rows (added to `stories.ts`'s existing `notes`/`orgFolders`-shaped export/import blocks, same spread-verbatim-with-fresh-id pattern) — global/shipped packs are deliberately excluded from story JSON, same posture as global Lorebook entries.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. Migration `0053_hesitant_microbe.sql` applied cleanly; boot log confirmed the shipped-pack seed ran (4 shells: `character_codex`/light/standard/grill + `character_psych`/any). Live-verified end to end in the Browser pane against the real dev DB (`demo-story-shadows-berlin`): Settings' Playbook Packs card and the story's Playbooks sidebar tool both rendered the 4 shipped shells; "Copy to global to edit" created a real global override row (confirmed via a toast and the row moving into the Global section); editing that override's body persisted and re-rendered correctly; the WB Character chat's "Psych module" and "Use playbook pack" toggles both rendered and, once flipped on, correctly PATCHed `aiChats` (confirmed via direct API read) and correctly reflected in `GET /chats/:id/context`'s new `playbookPack` field — `concrete` resolved the shipped `character_codex`/`standard` pack for the chat's current `wbStyle`; `psych` correctly stayed `null` on this particular pre-existing chat because it has no `anchorEntryId` (a genuine pre-existing B5 gate — psych needs an anchor to attach a proposal to — not a bug introduced here, confirmed by inspecting the chat row directly). All test state (the global pack copy, the two toggle flips) was cleaned up afterward via direct API calls, restoring the demo chat to its original values.

## Character Guided Playbook Packs — PP6 (Starter Content), Load-Bearing Decisions

Closes out the one deferred piece of PP0-PP5 above: real interview-question prose for the four shipped packs, replacing the placeholder shell body (`"_This pack is a shell..._"`) that had shipped since PP1. Design doc §8 was explicitly left "designing" pending human content decisions — this pass made those decisions with the user directly (`AskUserQuestion`) before writing anything.

**Source material was three user-supplied files** (`Light/Normal/Extreme character creation questions.md`, general-purpose questionnaires not written for this app) mapped directly onto the app's three Guided-setup style tiers: Light→`light`, Normal→`standard`, Extreme→`grill`. Adapted rather than reused verbatim — reorganized into the app's own section-heading convention, and each tier's closing "Sexuality" section was expanded into "Sexuality & Power Dynamics" (grill's gets the deepest treatment, including explicit follow-up-pressure prompts consistent with that tier's own "push past the first answer" framing) per the user's confirmed choice to bake erotic/power-dynamic-aware angles directly into the concrete-coverage packs, rather than keeping them genre-neutral.

**`character_psych` had no source material at all** (none of the three files covered MBTI/Enneagram) — written fresh as a narrative-interview-voice cue sheet per the user's confirmed preference (an interviewer's script with framing/transitions, not a bullet quiz), covering the four MBTI axes, Enneagram type/wing/instinct, and freeform-blurb prompts, closing with an explicit instruction to land the answers in the psych profile's blurb field. Reiterates the existing psych-module boundary inline (`psych-proposal` only, never `codex-proposal`, never scanner-enforced) since this pack is the one place a model reads instructions about where its answers should and shouldn't go.

**Content lives in a new `src/data/playbookPackContent.ts`** (one exported string constant per key+style), imported into `playbookPackService.ts`'s `SHIPPED_PACKS` array — mirrors `src/data/systemPrompts.ts`'s existing "inline data module imported by the seed function" precedent rather than reading markdown files from disk at boot (design doc §8's own alternative suggestion), since this project already has that exact pattern for shipped content and a second mechanism wasn't warranted for four short strings.

**Boot-time seed upgrade, not just future-inserts:** `seedShippedPlaybookPacks()` previously only inserted rows genuinely missing from the DB — any dev/prod instance that had already booted once under PP1 would keep its placeholder-shell rows forever, since nothing re-checked already-seeded content. Added a second pass that detects a shipped row still holding the exact old placeholder string and updates it to the new real body. Safe specifically because copy-on-edit (PP0-PP5's own design) never mutates a shipped row in place — a user's real edit always forks to a new global/story row instead — so "body === old placeholder" can only mean "never touched since PP1 seeded it," never "user wrote something that happens to match."

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit -p server/tsconfig.json` clean. Live-verified against the real dev DB using the `server-only`/`client-only` launch configs (not plain `npm run dev`) per the known `tsx watch`-under-`concurrently` boot gotcha (`dev_environment_gotchas` memory) — the combined-process preview genuinely never brought the Express half up this session, confirming the memory's fix still applies. Boot log confirmed `"Upgraded 4 shipped playbook pack(s) from placeholder shell to real content"`; a direct authenticated `GET /api/playbook-packs/` confirmed all four rows now carry real bodies (1394/2425/4672/2286 bytes for light/standard/grill/psych respectively) instead of the ~250-byte placeholder.

## Per-Message Action Bar — P1.5 MB1-MB4, Load-Bearing Decisions

Closes out P1.5 (MB0 — Copy — had already shipped 2026-07-25). Adds Delete (both roles), Edit (both roles, previously assistant-only UI that was never actually wired up), and Regenerate (assistant only) to `ChatMessageList.tsx`'s per-message hover toolbar, wired through `ChatInterface.tsx` — the one shared component every chat type (WB/Editor/Outline/Research/Notes/Brainstorm) renders through, so this shipped once for all six.

**MB1 (originally scoped as new dedicated single-message server routes/helpers) turned out to be unnecessary and was dropped rather than built for its own sake.** Messages live as one JSON array on the `aiChats` row (no per-message table), and the generic `PATCH /:chatId {messages}` full-array-replace already existed end to end (`chatsApi.update` → `chats.ts`'s PATCH handler → `chatService.replaceMessages` → `chatRepository.updateChatMessages`) — it was already how the (unwired) assistant-only edit UI was originally meant to save. Delete/Edit/Regenerate all just build the next array client-side (filter out a message, splice-and-update one, or slice-and-truncate) and PATCH it back through that one existing endpoint. No new routes, no new `chatRepository` helpers, no new migration — building `deleteMessage`/`updateMessageContent`/`truncateMessagesAfter` server primitives as originally staged would have been pure duplication of a primitive the full-replace endpoint already provides for a single-row-JSON-array chat model this small.

**MB3's user-vs-assistant edit asymmetry was locked exactly as the backlog's own recommendation:** assistant edits rewrite `content` in place (plus `originalContent`/`editedAt`/`editedBy`/`edited` metadata, preserving the *first* `originalContent` across repeat edits rather than overwriting it) with no cascade, since an assistant reply never fed back into the model's own context. User edits do the same field update but also truncate every message after the edited one (`selectedChat.messages.slice(0, idx)` + the edited message) — once the user's own turn changes, whatever the model replied with next was responding to text that no longer exists, so leaving it in place would be actively misleading. No auto-resend after a user edit — the truncated chat just sits ready for the user to send something new, avoiding the ambiguity of silently firing a second generation the user didn't explicitly ask for.

**MB4 (Regenerate) reruns a full fresh send rather than replaying the original turn's context.** Deletes the target assistant reply and searches backward for the nearest preceding user message (skipping over any other assistant messages in between, e.g. after a Delete rearranged the sequence); truncates from that user message onward, persists, then calls the *existing* `generate()` (from `useChatMessageGeneration.ts`) with that message's own text. `doSend`'s inline extraContext computation (Research web-search / WB-Editor-Outline per-message RAG re-seed, P1.3) was extracted into a standalone `computeExtraContext(text)` so Regenerate gets the same fresh-context treatment a real new send would, rather than reusing whatever was current at the original send time — consistent with P1.3's own "context should reflect what's being asked now, not the mount-time snapshot" reasoning.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` (client) clean throughout. Live-verified in the Browser pane against the real dev DB, using a disposable scratch chat created and deleted via direct `fetch` calls (see incident note below for why direct-API scratch data was used for the rest of the pass): MB2 confirmed a plain Delete removes exactly the targeted message with zero cascade to its neighbors; MB3 confirmed both the in-place assistant-edit path (content updated, `edited: true`, message before/after untouched) and the user-edit-with-truncate path (content updated, every later message gone); MB4 confirmed against a real reachable cloud provider this session — truncated correctly past an intervening assistant message to the right prior user message, then produced a genuine fresh assistant reply. The Cancel path of the new delete `ConfirmDialog` was also confirmed to leave messages untouched (caught a stale-`ref` false-positive mid-testing where a second click accidentally opened the same dialog again rather than doing nothing, resolved by re-querying the DOM directly instead of trusting a stale accessibility-tree ref).

**Incident during testing, not a product bug:** MB3's edit-truncate behavior was first verified against a live, non-disposable World-Building chat (`Character Codex`, Nana Usmanov, dated Jul 25) rather than scratch data. Editing that chat's first (user) message correctly truncated the ~20 messages of real prior conversation that followed it, per the designed behavior — the feature worked exactly as intended, but against the wrong target. That chat isn't part of the static demo-seed fixture (`server/data/demo-story-shadows-berlin.json`), so it isn't re-seedable, and this project has no automated DB backup (`docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` documents only a manual `cp data/storynexus.db data/storynexus.db.backup`, not run recently enough to help here). User was informed directly and confirmed (2026-07-27) to leave the truncated chat as-is rather than attempt a WAL-level recovery. Same lesson Outline Import's own verification note already recorded: **any test that can mutate or truncate real conversation/outline history belongs on a disposable scratch chat/story, never on existing demo or seeded data** — this is the second time in this project's history that lesson had to be relearned live instead of applied up front.

## Name Generator — NP3 Bulk Install Presets, Load-Bearing Decisions

Closes out the region-packs track (`docs/Name_Generator_Region_Packs_Design.md`) in full — NP0/NP1/NP2/NP4/NP5 shipped 2026-07-24, NP3 (deliberately deferred at the time as genuinely optional) shipped 2026-07-27.

**Presets are a client-side grouping constant (`NAME_PACK_PRESETS` in `ImportPoolDialog.tsx`), not a manifest/schema concept.** 5 curated groups (European/MENA/Asian/African/Anglo-Americas) partition all 24 vendored packIds with zero overlap — every packId belongs to exactly one preset, so "installed" checks per preset never double-count. Grouping lives entirely in the client because there's no other consumer that would need it server-side (the manifest's per-pack `installed: {global, story}` shape already gives everything a preset button needs to compute its own state).

**Bulk install reuses the existing single-pack install endpoint N times rather than adding a new bulk server route** — same reasoning as P1.5's MB1 (see that entry above): a new `useInstallPresetMutation` hook calls `nameGeneratorApi.installPack` once per packId via `Promise.allSettled` (not `Promise.all` — one pack failing shouldn't lose the results of the others, and the existing install endpoint is already idempotent per-pack) and aggregates one summary toast (new pools/names, already-installed count, failed count) instead of firing N individual toasts from N separate mutation instances. Query invalidation also happens exactly once after the whole batch, not once per pack.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` (client) clean. Live-verified in the Browser pane against the real dev DB: the "Browse packs" tab correctly renders all 5 preset buttons with their pack counts; clicking "MENA (4)" fired exactly 4 `POST .../install` calls (arabic/mena/turkish/israeli) followed by one `GET /packs` refetch, and the button flipped to a disabled "MENA — installed" state; the aggregate toast read "16 new pool(s), 7008 name(s)" (4 packs × 4 pools each, matching the manifest's own per-pack pool counts). Test installs uninstalled afterward via direct API calls.

## Local Generation: Silent Empty-Reply Bug + Max Output Tokens Override, Load-Bearing Decisions

Found while investigating a user report that the Name Generator's `name-proposal` fence (see the P1.5-adjacent "Per-Message Action Bar" entry above and `docs/Name_Generator_Design.md` NG6) "never returns a list of names to the chat." Root cause turned out to be unrelated to name proposals specifically — it silently affects **any** message in a chat with a large enough system prompt on a reasoning-capable local model.

**Root cause, confirmed live against the user's actual LM Studio endpoint (`localhost:1234`, model `artemis-31b-v1h-i1-gguf`):** this is a reasoning model — its completions response carries a separate `reasoning_content` field alongside `content` (the app has never read or merged this field; `streamUtils.ts` only ever reads `delta.content`). The WB Character chat's real system prompt (persona + Codex rules + every proposal-fence instruction, including `NAME_PROPOSAL_INSTRUCTIONS`) measured **~20,500 characters**. Against `AIService`'s previous hardcoded `maxTokens: 2048` — a budget shared between the model's internal reasoning and its visible reply — the model spent its entire budget reasoning about that large prompt and hit `finish_reason: "length"` with `content: ""`, never producing a word of visible reply. Reproduced 3 times live in the user's own Nana Usmanov WB chat: three real sends, zero assistant replies, zero errors shown anywhere.

**The silent half of this was the more serious bug, independent of the token-budget root cause**: `useChatMessageGeneration.ts`'s `generate()` did `if (!fullResponse) return;` with no toast, no error, nothing — a turn could fail for any reason (this one, a flaky provider, a timeout) and the user would see literal silence with no indication anything went wrong. Fixed with a `toast.error` explaining the likely cause (reasoning budget exhaustion) and pointing at the new setting below — this fires for *any* empty-content failure, not just the reasoning-model case, so it's a real correctness fix independent of the specific root cause found here.

**Two mitigations, both requested (user chose "both raise the default and add a real override" over just the toast alone):**
- `AIService.DEFAULT_MAX_TOKENS` raised from 2048 → 4096 (module constant, was previously an inline default parameter value — changed to a static class field since a parameter default can't reference `this.settings`).
- New `aiSettings.localMaxOutputTokens` column (nullable, mirrors `contextWindowOverride`'s existing "null = use default" convention exactly) — Settings → Local's `ContextMeterSettingsCard.tsx` gained a new "Max output tokens (Local)" field (own mutation, `useUpdateLocalMaxOutputTokensMutation`, kept separate from `useUpdateContextMeterSettingsMutation` since this is an output-generation-budget concept, not a context/token-*metering* concept, despite living in the same settings card for now — no new card needed since Local is already this card's whole scope). `AIService.generate()`'s `maxTokens` parameter changed from a hardcoded default to optional, resolved as `explicit-arg ?? (local ? settings.localMaxOutputTokens : undefined) ?? DEFAULT_MAX_TOKENS` — an explicit override always wins, mirroring `contextWindowOverride`'s "user override wins" doctrine (M0-M5 Context/Token Meter entry above). No new server route needed — `PUT /api/ai/settings/:id` is already a fully generic field-spread with no per-field whitelist, confirmed by reading the route before assuming a new one was needed.

**Brainstorm gained `NAME_PROPOSAL_INSTRUCTIONS` too** (was previously only WB/Editor/Outline, per `docs/Name_Generator_Design.md`'s own locked scope — `parseNameProposal.ts`'s client-side comment claiming "Editor/WB/Outline/Brainstorm" was stale/wrong until this change, now accurate) — user explicitly asked for this alongside the bug investigation.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` (client) and `npx tsc --project server/tsconfig.json --noEmit` (server) both clean. Migration `0054_quiet_grey_gargoyle.sql` (`ALTER TABLE aiSettings ADD localMaxOutputTokens integer`) applied cleanly, confirmed via a live `GET /api/ai/settings` showing the new field. Live-verified the actual fix end to end against the real dev DB and the user's real local model: set `localMaxOutputTokens: 8000` via direct API call, resent the exact same name-request message that had silently failed 3 times in a row at the old 2048 default, and this time got a real assistant reply plus a rendered `NameProposalCard` ("Name Generator / First name suggestions — RU"). That specific attempt returned zero matching names because the model proposed `region: "RU"` which doesn't exactly match the installed "Slavic" pool's region label — a separate, minor prompt-wording/region-matching nuance, not the bug under investigation, left unfixed this pass.

---

## Name Generator: Region-Matching Guidance (Dynamic Regions Addendum), Load-Bearing Decisions

Follow-on to the "Local Generation: Silent Empty-Reply Bug" entry above — once that fix let the model actually reach content, its `name-proposal` fence still returned zero names because `region: "RU"` doesn't match the installed `"Slavic"` pool's region label.

**Confirmed the actual constraint before designing a fix:** `nameGeneratorRepository.ts`'s `listPoolsForScope` matches region by exact, case-sensitive string equality (`eq(schema.namePools.region, filter.region)`) — no fuzzy/synonym matching exists anywhere in the generate path. Renaming the "Slavic" pack to "Russian" (one of the two options the user raised) was rejected: that pack is RU-forward *plus* Polish/Czech names, so the rename would be inaccurate, and critically **a manifest rename only affects future installs** — the region string is copied into each `namePools` row at install time, so already-installed pools would keep the old label regardless, silently reintroducing the same mismatch for existing stories. A synonym/alias table (the user's other suggestion) was also passed over: several of the 24 combo packs (MENA = Arabic+TR+IR, East Asian = JP+CN+HK+TW+SG) have no single obviously-correct alias mapping, so a hand-maintained alias table would just be guessing in a different place.

**Fix: tell the model the real installed region values instead of letting it guess one.** New `resolveAvailableNameRegions(storyId)` in `chatContextService.ts` reuses `listPoolsForScope(storyId, {})` (same global∪story∪series scope resolution `generate()` itself uses — not a new query) and returns the distinct sorted region set. A new `nameRegionsAddendum(regions)` renders a short "these are the only valid region values, case-sensitive, omit region if none fit" note, appended only to the four chat types whose framing already includes `NAME_PROPOSAL_INSTRUCTIONS` (editor/outline/worldbuilding/brainstorm — never research/notes, which never had it). `buildSystemPrompt` gained an `availableNameRegions: string[] = []` parameter computed once and threaded to all 5 return sites via a single `regionsAddendum` local, rather than repeating the resolve call per-branch. Skips the query entirely for chat types that never propose names, and renders as `""` (no addendum) when a story has zero pools installed, so this doesn't inflate every chat's prompt length — a deliberate concern here specifically because the previous entry in this file just fixed a bug caused by prompts being too long for a reasoning model's token budget.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --project server/tsconfig.json --noEmit` and `npx tsc --noEmit` (client) both clean. Live-verified against the real dev DB: `GET /chats/:id/context` confirmed the addendum text lists the story's actual live pool regions (`Arabic, Slavic, UK, US` at time of testing); resent the same "8 female Russian first names" request that had returned zero matches under `region: "RU"` before this fix, and the model this time proposed `region: "Slavic"` (the exact installed value) and got back 8 real names with working Use/Codex actions.

---

## Name Proposal Card: Compact Chat Display + Fence-Only Reply Fix, Load-Bearing Decisions

Follow-on to the two entries above, after live-verifying the region-guidance fix worked and getting direct user feedback that `NameProposalCard`'s results were too dense to read in a chat bubble — each row carried a tier badge, pool-name text, and three labeled buttons, and `full_name` pairs doubled that (two tier badges, two pool names).

**New `CompactNameChip`, local to `NameProposalCard.tsx` (not exported/shared)** — deliberately not a change to `GeneratedNameRow`/`GeneratedNamePairRow`, which stay exactly as they are for the main Name Generator panel (full width, tier/pool info genuinely useful there). The chat card needed a different presentation, not a shared one: just the name plus four icon-only actions (Favorite/Copy/Use/Codex, `h-6 w-6` icon buttons with `title` tooltips instead of labeled buttons), no tier badge, no pool-name text — that attribution is dropped entirely for this surface. Rendered in a `flex flex-wrap` container instead of `space-y-2` stacked full-width rows, so chips can sit side by side when width allows. `full_name` pairs collapse to the same chip (`"First Last"` as one name, no separate first/last tier badges) rather than a wider pair-specific variant.

**Found and fixed a second, unrelated real bug while stress-testing the compact display with a `full_name` request:** when a reply is *entirely* a fenced block with no conversational wrapper around it (this model did exactly that once web ran out of things to say), every parser strips its own content and `cleanedContent` comes out as an empty string — `chatsApi.appendMessage`'s server route rejects empty content outright, throwing and crashing the whole turn, silently dropping the just-parsed proposal along with it (the append call happens before any of the `onXProposal` callbacks fire). Fixed with a plain fallback: `const finalContent = cleanedContent.trim() || "Here's what I found:";` — any non-empty placeholder is fine since the real content is the proposal card rendered below it, not the bubble text. This is a general fix, not name-proposal-specific: the same crash-and-lose-the-proposal failure mode was already possible for codex-proposal/note-proposal/outline-proposal/etc. any time a reply was fence-only, just not yet hit until this session's testing produced one.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Live-verified against the real dev DB and the user's real local model: a `full_name` request ("8 full female Slavic names") first hit the empty-content crash (confirmed via console: `Error during generation: Error: content is required`, thrown from the exact `cleanedContent` empty-string path), then after the fallback fix, the same request succeeded end to end — 8 real name pairs (e.g. "Monika Pavlova", "Marina Orlova") rendered as compact single-line chips with working Favorite/Copy/Use/Codex icon buttons, confirmed via a zoomed screenshot showing the new tight layout next to the much taller previous row design.

## Amazon/KDP Kindle-Ready EPUB Export — KDP1-KDP4, Load-Bearing Decisions

Implements `docs/Amazon_KDP_Export_Design.md` (T3, locked 2026-07-28). Job: make the existing "Download as EPUB" action produce a Kindle-ready file — no new export path, no multi-profile system.

**KDP0 gap audit found the pre-existing EPUB path was worse than the design assumed**: `server/services/epubGenerator.ts`'s hand-rolled converter only handled `text`/`linebreak`/`paragraph`/`heading` (no bold/italic/underline, no lists, no images, no quotes); its cover page embedded the story synopsis directly in the EPUB body (violates the locked "no synopsis in body" rule); chapter titles were forced to `Chapter {order}: {title}` (violates "no forced prefix" rule); and its `.chapter`/`.chapter-title`/`.chapter-content` CSS classes were dead — never actually applied to the generated markup.

**KDP1 — single Lexical→HTML SoT (`src/utils/export/convertLexicalToEpubHtml.ts`).** Deliberately a pure string-builder, not a port of the richer client `convertLexicalToHtml.ts` — that file uses real `document.createElement` DOM APIs and cannot run server-side (no jsdom/linkedom dependency in the project), so building a new DOM-free converter was the only option that could satisfy "one shared mapping used by both client and server" without adding a new dependency. **Found and fixed a real, previously-unnoticed correctness bug shared by both pre-existing HTML converters (client and server)**: they gated text-node handling on `node.type === "text"` exactly, but several registered Lexical node types are `TextNode` subclasses with a different `type` string and no `children` array — `HashtagNode` (`@lexical/hashtag`) and this app's own `SpecialTextNode` (extends `TextNode`). Under the old logic these leaf nodes have no `children` to recurse into, so they silently returned `""` — their text was dropped entirely, not just unformatted. Fixed by keying leaf-text detection off "has a `text` string and no `children` array" rather than the exact `type` value. Only the new EPUB converter got this fix — `convertLexicalToHtml.ts`/`convertLexicalToMarkdown.ts` (html/markdown/pdf export) were left untouched, since the design only requires unifying the EPUB path.

**Scene-break detection (new, no prior code anywhere did this):** a paragraph counts as a break when empty or its trimmed text is exactly `***`, `* * *`, or `---`; consecutive break signals collapse to one glyph. **Judgment call, not explicitly in the design doc**: a paragraph-level Lexical `horizontalrule` node (the editor's own toolbar-insertable horizontal rule) is treated identically to a detected scene break, since it's clearly the same authoring gesture the design's textual triggers are meant to catch — documented here per the design doc's own allowance to note nuanced implementer calls.

**Real bug caught during live verification, fixed before shipping**: the first implementation treated "paragraph has no text" as sufficient to call it a scene break — but a paragraph containing only an image (no caption text) also has no text, so it was misdetected as a blank scene-break paragraph and the image was silently dropped from the export entirely. Fixed by distinguishing "genuinely empty" (every child is a blank text leaf or a linebreak) from "has non-text content" (an image or anything else) — only the former triggers the break; a paragraph holding an image is never treated as a break, regardless of its text content.

**KDP2 — package assembly (`epubGenerator.ts` rewritten).** Front matter is now title + generated `© {createdAt year} {author}. All rights reserved.` copyright line only — synopsis removed from the body entirely (stays as OPF `description` metadata, unchanged). Chapter title now falls back to `Chapter {order}` with no forced prefix when a real title exists. Switched `prependChapterTitles` to `false` and render the chapter heading manually inside each chapter's own content — this replaced the previous design where the library's own default auto-injected an unstyled `<h1>` per chapter (including an empty `<h1></h1>` on the front-matter chapter, since its `title` was `""`) with a single explicit `<h1 class="chapter-title">` we fully control, matching the "chapter title, no forced prefix" rule cleanly. CSS rewritten to actually apply to the generated markup (previously-dead classes fixed), paragraph margin removed (trade typography has no inter-paragraph gap), and `font-family` dropped entirely so the Kindle reader's own default typeface wins (previously declared a system-font stack).

**Real image-embedding bug found and fixed as a prerequisite for "images pass through" to be true at all**: chapter images inserted via drag/drop/paste (`DragDropPastePlugin`) are stored as base64 `data:` URIs directly in the Lexical JSON, but `epub-gen-memory`'s image resolution fetches every `<img src>` via `node-fetch@2`, which does not support `data:` URLs (only `file://`/`http(s)`) — any chapter with a pasted image would have silently broken EPUB generation. Fixed by scanning each chapter's generated HTML for `data:` URIs, writing each one to a temp file (`fs/promises.mkdtemp`, cleaned up in a `finally` after `epub(...)` resolves or rejects) and rewriting `src` to a `pathToFileURL(...).href` the library's Node fetch path can actually read. Not named in the design doc explicitly, but this is a bug fix required to make the doc's own "images pass through" lock true, not scope creep.

**KDP3 — soft preflight.** No existing preflight/dry-run pattern existed anywhere in the codebase. Implemented in `DownloadMenu.tsx` (the single UI entry point for both story- and chapter-level EPUB downloads — a chapter's "epub" format already just delegates to the full-story export) rather than inside `exportStoryAsEpub.ts`/`downloadStory.ts`, since those are plain async utility functions with no component state to host a dialog; `DownloadMenu` already had everything needed (`id`/`type`) to fetch the story + chapters and compute warnings before the real download call. New `epubPreflight.ts` checks: no chapters, all-empty chapters, missing title/author, very short total word count, and large embedded `data:` images (byte-length heuristic on the base64 payload). Reuses the existing `ConfirmDialog` primitive — warnings render as a single joined sentence with an "Export anyway" confirm action; zero warnings skips the dialog entirely and exports immediately, so the common case is unchanged. Never a hard block — there is no path that actually prevents the download.

**KDP4.** Added an "Exporting for Kindle" subsection to the existing Advanced Guide (`src/features/guide/content/advanced.mdx`, same placement precedent as the LE3 pass's "Formatting Text" section) rather than a new Guide tab, per the design doc's own suggestion. Relabeled the download menu item to "Download as EPUB (Kindle)".

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client + tsc server + vite) clean throughout. Live-verified against the real dev DB via a disposable scratch story (created and deleted via direct API calls, per this project's own "use scratch data" lesson): posted a chapter with hand-built Lexical JSON covering bold/italic text, a `***`-only scene-break paragraph, a blockquote, a bullet list, and a `data:` URI test image, plus a second untitled chapter; fetched the generated EPUB, decoded and unzipped it, and confirmed the title/copyright front page (no synopsis text), correct chapter titles (`Chapter One: The Beginning` verbatim, `Chapter 2` fallback on the untitled one, no forced "Chapter N:" prefix), bold/italic/blockquote/list markup all present, the scene-break paragraph rendering as centered `* * *` with the following paragraph correctly marked no-indent, and the test image embedded as a real 68-byte PNG binary resource inside `OEBPS/images/` (not a broken `data:` reference) — this pass is what caught the scene-break/image bug above. Also live-verified the preflight modal end to end through the actual Stories page UI (not just the API): clicking "Download as EPUB (Kindle)" on a story with zero chapters showed "Before you export... This story has no chapters yet," and "Export anyway" completed the download (confirmed via the "Story downloaded as EPUB" toast); clicking through the same menu's HTML and PDF options on the content-bearing scratch story produced their own success toasts with no console errors, confirming those unmodified export paths still work. Both scratch stories deleted after verification.

---

## Story Labyrinth Brand & Type — BT0-BT2, Load-Bearing Decisions

Implements `docs/SL_Brand_Type_Implement_Design.md` (locked 2026-08-04). Job: self-host the brand type stack for app chrome, promote pinned icon/wordmark assets into `public/`, and show the real wordmark in the TopBar — without touching manuscript prose fonts, T1 chrome tokens, or running any product rename (SL0+ stays separate).

**BT0 — fonts + Tailwind.** `tailwind.config.js` gained `theme.extend.fontFamily.{sans,mono,display}` (Inter / JetBrains Mono / Cormorant Garamond, each with the same system-stack fallback chain the design doc specified). Self-hosted woff2 under `public/fonts/{inter,jetbrains-mono,cormorant-garamond}/`: Inter 400/500/600 and JetBrains Mono 400/500 fetched from `cdn.jsdelivr.net/fontsource` (the actual static woff2 files, not a runtime CDN dependency — matches T5's "self-host" lock, just sourced the binary bytes over the network once during this build pass rather than converting the vendored TTFs by hand); Cormorant Garamond 600/700 copied directly from the already-vendored `docs/design-mocks/brand/fonts/CormorantGaramond/*.woff2` (no conversion needed, that kit already ships woff2). `@font-face` rules added to `src/index.css`, `body` gained `font-sans`. **Found a real font-inheritance gap the design doc's own T4 lock would otherwise have silently broken**: nothing in the editor previously set an explicit `font-family` on the manuscript canvas — it inherited whatever `body` used, so switching `body` to Inter would have forced Inter onto the Lexical prose too. Fixed by adding an explicit `font-family: ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif` reset to `.ContentEditable__root`/`.ContentEditable__placeholder` (`src/components/story-editor/ui/ContentEditable.css`) — scoped to the actual prose canvas, not the whole `.editor-shell` wrapper, so the editor's own toolbar buttons still pick up the app-wide Inter chrome font like every other button in the app.

**BT1 — asset promotion.** Copied from `docs/design-mocks/brand/exports/` into `public/icons/` (dark-ember `icon-{512,192,180}.png`, `favicon-{32,16}.png`) and `public/brand/` (wordmark PNGs, see below). `index.html`'s favicon/apple-touch-icon links switched from the old upstream `icons/icon.svg` to the new PNG ladder (SVG link removed entirely rather than left as a second competing favicon — browsers don't have a consistent tie-break rule between an SVG and PNG favicon link, so leaving both risked the old mark winning in some browsers). `manifest.json` gained the 192/512 PNGs alongside the existing SVG entry; name strings deliberately left as "Story Nexus" per T11 (rename is SL0+, not this pass). No `favicon.ico` built — no ICO-encoder available in this environment and the doc explicitly allows skipping it ("skip if painful"); PNG favicon links cover all evergreen browsers.

**Wordmark transparency — real deviation from the design doc's asset pipeline, but the intended output.** T8 calls for exporting the transparent wordmark PNGs from `SL_wordmark_live_dark3.psd`/`_light2.psd` by hiding the Background layer in Photoshop. This environment has no Photoshop, no working `psd-tools`/Pillow (pip has no network route to PyPI — `SSLCertVerificationError` on every index host tried), and `sharp` (already a project dependency, confirmed available) cannot decode PSD at all (`Input file contains unsupported image format`). Per the design doc's own explicit fallback clause ("If Claude cannot drive Photoshop: leave a short note in PR... do not block BT0 on PSD export"), this was **not** treated as a hard blocker. Instead: confirmed both pinned flat exports (`SL_wordmark_pinned_dark.png` / `_light.png`, the literal JPEGs `SL_wordmark_live_dark3.jpg`/`_light2.jpg` were promoted from) have a **flat, uniform background color** (measured via a `sharp` raw-buffer read: dark = `rgb(8,8,10)` at all four corners and across a sampled strip; light = `rgb(240,233,217)`, same uniformity) — meaning a distance-based chroma key against that single sampled color produces a result functionally equivalent to a real alpha-channel PSD export, since there's no gradient/photo background to fight. Wrote a one-off script (not committed — scratch tooling only) that: computes per-pixel Euclidean distance from the sampled background color, maps distance below 10 to alpha 0 / above 60 to alpha 255 with a linear ramp between (avoids a hard-edged cutout), trims the fully-transparent margin, and re-pads 24px. Output verified by compositing onto a saturated, maximally-different test color (`rgb(60,60,200)`) — no dark/light halo visible at any edge, confirming the key threshold was clean. Saved as `docs/design-mocks/brand/exports/SL_wordmark_{dark3,light2}_transparent_keyed.png` (kept alongside the PSDs as the working artifact) and copied into `public/brand/wordmark-{dark,light}.png`. **If Hermes/the user later does a real Photoshop alpha export from the pinned PSDs, it's a drop-in replacement at the same `public/brand/` paths** — no code change needed, this was purely an asset-generation shortcut, not an architectural one.

**BT2 — TopBar wordmark + theme-aware asset selection.** `TopBar.tsx`'s plain "Story Nexus" text replaced with `<img>` at the two `public/brand/` paths, `h-6 w-auto` sized. **Theme-awareness needed more than a light/dark binary** — this app has 18 theme ids (`src/lib/theme-provider.tsx`), most named for their palette (Midnight, Sand, Ember, Mid Stone, ...) rather than "light"/"dark", so a naive `theme === "dark"` check would have shown the wrong wordmark on 16 of them. Classified every theme by its own `--background` HSL lightness value read directly from `src/index.css` (threshold at 50%): `dark/midnight/midnight-graphite/sand/graphite/forest/dark-parchment/abyss/matrix/ember` are dark-family (lightness 2%-40%); `light/mist/bone/sepia/mid-stone/mid-slate/mid-sage` are light-family (70%-100%) — notably **Graphite (40%) and the three `mid-*` themes (70-72%) sort opposite to what their names might suggest** (Graphite dark, "Mid" themes light), confirmed correct by the lightness numbers rather than guessing from the label. Exported as `isDarkThemeId(theme)` from `theme-provider.tsx` (handles `"system"` by re-checking `matchMedia` at call time) rather than duplicating the set in `TopBar.tsx`, so any future consumer needing the same classification reuses it instead of re-deriving. Settings' own header (a separate, simpler layout with no TopBar) was left untouched — it never showed the app name/wordmark before this pass either, out of scope.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. Live-verified in the Browser pane against the real dev DB (hit the known `tsx watch`-under-`concurrently` dev-server startup flake documented in this session's own memory — server produced zero output and every `/api/*` call ECONNREFUSED'd; switched to the `.claude/launch.json` `server-only`+`client-only` two-process workaround, which booted cleanly): confirmed via `document.fonts`/`getComputedStyle` that `body`/sidebar labels compute to `Inter, ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif` while `.ContentEditable__root` still computes to the old `ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif` stack (Inter absent) — the T4 lock holds under real render, not just code inspection. Wordmark confirmed switching `/brand/wordmark-dark.png` to `/brand/wordmark-light.png` on a Dark to Light theme change, and separately confirmed the dark wordmark renders correctly (no wrong-asset flash) against **Graphite** specifically — the one theme in the dark-family set whose name could plausibly mislead a naive implementation, so this was the one non-obvious case worth checking live rather than trusting the lightness math alone. `favicon-32.png` confirmed as the live `<link rel="icon">` href. No console errors during any of the above.

**Not done this pass (BT3/BT4, explicitly optional per the design doc):** no brand color CSS tokens added, no About-page blurb — neither had an obvious restrained call site to justify adding without drive-by scope creep.

---

## Story Labyrinth Brand & Type — BT3-BT4 (Optional Slices), Load-Bearing Decisions

Follow-on to the BT0-BT2 entry above. Both slices are explicitly optional per `docs/SL_Brand_Type_Implement_Design.md` ("done when: tokens exist; zero or 1-2 restrained call sites" / "if an About/Help surface exists") — picked up on direct user request.

**BT3 — brand color tokens.** `--brand-crimson: 350 55% 45%` and `--brand-ember: 32 90% 55%` added once to the true `:root` block in `src/index.css` (not per-theme) — deliberately global and theme-independent, since these are logo-identity colors, not part of any theme pack's palette. Placing them in `:root` rather than duplicating across all 18 `.theme-name` blocks works because CSS custom properties cascade: `:root` and `.dark`/`.midnight`/etc. all resolve to the same `<html>` element, and none of those theme blocks redefine `--brand-crimson`/`--brand-ember`, so there's no override to worry about — one declaration site, available everywhere. **One restrained call site** (the doc's own suggested ceiling): the TopBar wordmark image (`TopBar.tsx`) gets a hover-only ember glow (`hover:[filter:drop-shadow(0_0_6px_hsl(var(--brand-ember)/0.55))]`, transitioned) — matches the design kit's own guidance that ember accents belong on "maze glow, warm accent" contexts, restrained Raycast-strength, not body text. No menu labels, buttons, or primary color repainted.

**Verification gap, documented rather than silently left unclear:** live-verified in the Browser pane that both tokens resolve correctly (`getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue('--brand-ember')` → `"32 90% 55%"`) and that the exact compiled CSS rule for the hover utility is syntactically correct and uniquely matched (confirmed via `document.styleSheets` inspection — no conflicting `filter` rule exists). However, simulating `:hover` through this Browser pane's automation and then reading `getComputedStyle(...).filter` returned a transparent no-op (`drop-shadow(rgba(0,0,0,0) 0px 0px 0px)`) even though `element.matches(':hover')` correctly reported `true` — while applying the identical `filter` declaration inline (bypassing the `:hover` pseudo-class entirely) resolved correctly to the real orange glow (`drop-shadow(rgba(244, 147, 37, 0.55) 0px 0px 6px)`). This isolates the discrepancy to the automation harness's synthetic-hover-plus-computed-style path specifically, not the CSS itself — consistent with this project's own documented Browser-pane automation quirks (`dev_environment_gotchas` memory, items 5-6, Radix animation/HMR edge cases specific to this sandbox). A real mouse hover in an actual browser will trigger genuine `:hover` and pick up the verified-correct rule. Flagging this rather than claiming full end-to-end hover verification, since the automation's own signal was inconclusive on this one point even though every other angle (token values, rule uniqueness, declaration correctness) checked out.

**BT4 — About/credit blurb.** No dedicated About/Help page exists in this app. The closest qualifying surface per the doc's own conditional ("if an About/Help surface exists") is the Guide page's welcome header (`src/features/guide/pages/GuidePage.tsx`) — an existing `CardHeader`/`CardTitle`/`CardDescription` block literally titled "Welcome to The Story Nexus." Added one small `<p>` line beneath the existing description: "A personal, freeware fork — evolving toward *Story Labyrinth*" — with "Story Labyrinth" in `font-display` (Cormorant Garamond 600), muted/small text, no change to the existing title/description strings themselves (avoids the doc's explicit "no drive-by string replace across app" constraint — this is a new addition, not a rename of what's already there). Live-verified: the span computes to `"Cormorant Garamond", Georgia, serif` at weight 600, scoped to just that phrase.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. Live-verified in the Browser pane against the real dev DB (session persisted from the prior BT0-BT2 pass, Graphite theme still active from that testing): confirmed `--brand-crimson`/`--brand-ember` present with correct values on `document.documentElement`; confirmed the Guide page's new credit line renders with the correct text and font; confirmed via direct stylesheet inspection that the hover-glow rule compiles to exactly the intended `filter` declaration with no conflicting rule (the one point not confirmed via live simulated hover, per the gap noted above).

## Story Labyrinth Rename — SL0-SL3, Load-Bearing Decisions

User explicitly promoted the rename ("since we are rebranding this whole app, we need to find and replace all 'The Story Nexus' or 'Story Nexus' references and change them to Story Labyrinth"), executing `docs/SL_Brand_And_Type.md` §8's SL0-SL3 minimum on top of the same-day BT0-BT4 brand/type pass. Ran a full-repo reconnaissance grep first (all tracked files, case-insensitive `story nexus`/`storynexus` family) before touching anything, specifically to separate two categories that a blind find-replace would have conflated: **this fork's own product identity** (rename target) vs. **genuine credit to the upstream `JonSilver/TheStoryNexus` project** (must stay accurate — CLAUDE.md's own standing rule is "always credit; never pretend to be upstream").

**What changed (SL1 — package/app identity):** `package.json` (`name`: `thestorynexus` → `storylabyrinth`, `displayName` → `Story Labyrinth`) and both mirrored root-name entries in `package-lock.json`; `index.html` `<title>` and `apple-mobile-web-app-title`; `public/manifest.json` `name`/`short_name` (superseding the BT1 pass's own T11-sanctioned "may stay Story Nexus until SL1" allowance — that allowance's trigger condition is exactly what just happened). README.md's title and Overview paragraph — but **not** its "The Road to Here" section, which is Jon Silver's own first-person retrospective ("I wasn't happy with the IndexedDB-based database... so I rewrote..."); renaming "Story Nexus" inside his own quoted history would have put words in his mouth for a name he never used, so that section is untouched verbatim. Added one new line instead: an italicized fork/credit note right under the H1, linking the real upstream repo (`https://github.com/JonSilver/TheStoryNexus`).

**What changed (SL2 — UI/Guide strings):** `LoginPage.tsx`'s "Story Nexus is password-protected" string; `useDocumentTitle.ts`'s tab-title template (`Story Labyrinth - ${toolLabel}`) and its own comment; `toolLabels.ts`'s comment; `GuidePage.tsx`'s H1 and welcome `CardTitle` (both now render "Story Labyrinth" in `font-display`/Cormorant Garamond, consistent with BT3/BT4's established pattern); and prose across 5 Guide `.mdx` files (basics/advanced/lorebook/prompts/tts). `advanced.mdx` needed a surgical touch, not a blanket replace: it has both a "Story Nexus" prose mention (renamed) and a `[GitHub Issues](https://github.com/JonSilver/TheStoryNexus/issues)` link a few lines later (left untouched — that's a real, currently-valid support link, not a naming reference).

**GuidePage.tsx's own credit line superseded the BT4 pass's wording** — BT4 (same day, earlier) added "A personal, freeware fork — evolving toward *Story Labyrinth*" on the theory the rename hadn't shipped yet. Now that it has, that phrasing was stale on arrival; replaced with "A personal, freeware fork of **The Story Nexus**" as a real `<a>` link to the upstream repo (verified live: `href="https://github.com/JonSilver/TheStoryNexus"`, `target="_blank"`) — the credit direction flipped from "becoming X" to "descended from Y," which is the more honest framing once the rename is real.

**What changed (SL3 — doc headers):** `CLAUDE.md`/`DECISIONS.md`/`docs/CURRENT_BACKLOG.md` title headers and `CLAUDE.md`'s `**Project:**` line (its own upstream-credit lines — "Create a purpose-built fork of JonSilver/TheStoryNexus," "Base: JonSilver/TheStoryNexus" — deliberately left alone, same upstream-vs-ours distinction); the repeated `**Project:** Story Nexus Fork` header line across 9 individual design docs (`Chat_Panel_Integrations_Design.md`, `Character_Guided_Playbook_Packs_Design.md`, `Locations_And_Maps_Design.md`, `Notes_Outline_Chat_Bridges_Design.md`, `Thin_Story_Graph_And_Lorebook_Visualization.md`, `Agent_Framework_And_Project_Memory_Design.md`, `Chat_Shuttle_Design.md`, `Outline_Import_Design.md`, plus one body-prose mention in `Thin_Story_Graph_And_Lorebook_Visualization.md`) — four of these have a literal `(E:\StoryNexus-Fork)` path suffix that stayed unchanged, since that's the actual on-disk directory path, not a product-name string (the directory itself was not renamed — renaming it would break every relative reference in this session and any other tooling pointed at that path). Also renamed the two forward-looking P3 backlog lines that referenced the app by old name (`Personal_Agent_Memory → Story Nexus bridge`, `Neo4j / Mem0 / Letta inside Story Nexus`) and two still-open planning docs' body prose (`mobile-responsive-refactor-plan.md`, `workspace-design-plan.md`).

**Explicitly left untouched, and why (not an oversight):**
- **DB filename/path** (`storynexus.db` — `drizzle.config.ts`, `server/db/client.ts`, `server/scripts/exportNamePack.mjs`, all Docker files, `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`) — this is `docs/SL_Brand_And_Type.md` §8's own **SL5**, explicitly flagged "separate day." Renaming the default path constant would make the running app look for a file that doesn't exist on its next restart — the existing data would still be sitting on disk under the old filename, effectively invisible to the app until someone manually renamed or symlinked it. Real data-continuity risk for zero cosmetic benefit in a backend default.
- **Docker image/service/container names, `.github/workflows/docker-publish.yml`'s `jonsilver/storynexus` image tag, `.env.production.example`/`.env.tailscale.example`, `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`'s infra commands** — SL5/publish-identity territory. The Docker Hub image `jonsilver/storynexus` is literally upstream's published image (not something this fork has republished under its own name), and the compose/tailscale service names must match whatever's actually running — renaming these in docs without also actually standing up a new published image/service would make the deployment instructions describe infrastructure that doesn't exist.
- **Session cookie name** (`storynexus_session`, `server/middleware/auth.ts`) **and localStorage key prefixes** (`storyNexus.sidebar`, `storyNexus.session.*`, `storyNexus.preferredLayout.*`) — functional identifiers, not display strings. Renaming either has a real (if small) side effect on anyone with an existing session or saved UI state: a cookie-name change silently logs the user out once; a storage-key change silently resets sidebar-collapse state and any in-progress session summaries. Neither SL1-SL3 named these, and a "pure rename" request shouldn't carry a surprise logout/state-reset as a side effect.
- **`.git/config`'s remote URL** (`https://github.com/JonSilver/TheStoryNexus.git`) — this is `docs/SL_Brand_And_Type.md` §8's **SL6** ("own GitHub remote"), a real infrastructure/hosting decision (does this fork get its own GitHub repo?) that's the user's to make, not a string-replace target, and per this session's own standing git-safety rules, git remote config is never touched without explicit instruction.
- **`UPSTREAM_CLAUDE.md`** — this *is* the upstream project's own original CLAUDE.md, kept verbatim for reference. Every "Story Nexus" in it describes the actual upstream project's own identity from its own maintainer's perspective; renaming it would falsify a historical/reference document.
- **Historical and generated artifacts**: `docs/archived/*`, `docs/design-mocks/*.html` (frozen design-exploration mockups whose `<title>`/`<h1>` literally record what they were called *at the time*), `.planning/codebase/*` (tool-generated codebase snapshots, not hand-authored), `docs/issue-64-research.md` (a frozen research-investigation snapshot quoting the old Guide text as it existed when that investigation ran), and `docs/plan-mobile-styling-issue-58.md`'s one mention (a planning note about literal on-screen text that has since been superseded by the BT2 wordmark-image change anyway). Every `DECISIONS.md`/`docs/CURRENT_BACKLOG.md`/`docs/SL_Brand_Type_Implement_Design.md` entry describing *pre-rename* state (e.g. "TopBar showed plain text 'Story Nexus'") was likewise left as-is — those are accurate historical records of what was true when written, same treatment as every other point-in-time DECISIONS.md entry in this file.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. Live-verified in the Browser pane against the real dev DB: browser tab title read "Story Labyrinth" and updated per-tool (`Story Labyrinth - Editor - ...`); `/manifest.json` fetched live and confirmed `name`/`short_name` both `"Story Labyrinth"`; the standalone Guide page (reached via the tablet-width Guide button, since the desktop layout only exposes Guide navigation through Settings' sub-tab) showed "Story Labyrinth Guide" / "Welcome to Story Labyrinth" with a real, correctly-targeted upstream credit link; Settings' embedded Guide tab confirmed the five edited `.mdx` files render the new copy end to end (Basics guide fully re-read, all five renamed sentences present, no leftover "Story Nexus" text in rendered output).

## Story Labyrinth Rename — SL5 (DB Filename / Docker / Publish Identity), Load-Bearing Decisions

Picked up where SL0-SL3 (above) deliberately left off. Two of the four SL5 pieces carry real functional risk, so each was confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion` before touching anything, rather than assumed:

- **DB filename** (`storynexus.db` → `story-labyrinth.db`) — user chose "rename with auto-migrate" over leaving the filename alone or a manual-move approach. New default lives in `server/db/client.ts`/`drizzle.config.ts`/`server/index.ts`/`server/scripts/exportNamePack.mjs`/`entrypoint.sh`/`Dockerfile`. A one-time migration in `server/db/client.ts` runs on every boot: if the new default path doesn't exist yet, it checks the *same directory* for a `storynexus.db` and renames it (plus `-wal`/`-shm`/`-journal` sidecars) before opening the connection. **Deliberately not gated on `DATABASE_PATH` being unset** — Docker's own `Dockerfile` sets `DATABASE_PATH` explicitly to the new default, so "env var unset" isn't a reliable signal of "first run with the old default" in that environment; checking by directory instead covers both bare `npm start` and Docker without a second code path. Verified in an isolated sandbox (not the live shared dev DB another session had open) by fabricating legacy `storynexus.db(+wal/shm)` files and running the exact migration logic standalone: first run renamed all three files and preserved content, second run was a correct no-op (idempotent). `npm run build` clean.
- **Docker Hub namespace** — user confirmed they plan to actually publish this fork's image (Docker Hub + Unraid + local build), under their own namespace `bloodgrv`. Renamed `jonsilver/storynexus` → `bloodgrv/story-labyrinth` everywhere it appears: `.github/workflows/docker-publish.yml`'s `DOCKER_IMAGE` env, all four compose files' `image:`/`container_name:`/service-key/`DATABASE_PATH`, `.env.production.example`/`.env.tailscale.example`, and README.md's Docker Hub link/pull instructions/version-pinning examples/release-tag examples. Compose service keys themselves also renamed (`storynexus:` → `story-labyrinth:`) since they're purely local to each compose file (no cross-service DNS dependency on that name — `languagetool`'s only reference to the app service name was in a comment).
- **Tailscale hostname default** (`TS_HOSTNAME=storynexus` → `story-labyrinth`) — user confirmed rename; this only changes `.env.tailscale.example`'s *template* default, not any already-deployed `.env` file, so it can't silently break an existing live Tailscale setup.
- **Session cookie** (`storynexus_session` → `story_labyrinth_session`, `server/middleware/auth.ts`) **and localStorage key prefixes** (`storyNexus.sidebar`/`storyNexus.session.*`/`storyNexus.preferredLayout.*` → `storyLabyrinth.*`, `WorkspaceContext.tsx`/`sessionStorage.ts`) — both renamed as part of this pass. Both are self-healing, one-time side effects (a stale cookie means one extra login; a stale storage key means sidebar-collapse/session-summary state resets once) rather than data loss, so no migration shim was added for either.

**Left untouched, same as SL0-SL3's own list:** `.git/config`'s remote (SL6, user's own infra decision), Hermes-side references (SL4, outside this repo), `UPSTREAM_CLAUDE.md` and genuine `JonSilver/TheStoryNexus` credit links (Guide page/mdx), and historical/generated artifacts (`docs/archived/*`, `.planning/codebase/*`). No Unraid template exists in this repo yet — the user mentioned publishing to Unraid as a future step, not a file to create in this pass.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. DB migration logic verified standalone in an isolated sandbox directory (not the live shared dev DB, which another session had open at the time) rather than live in the Browser pane, since exercising it for real would have required restarting a server against the real `data/` folder — deferred to the user's own next server/container restart, which will apply the one-time rename safely per the verified logic above.

## Theme Roster Cleanup — Removed Mist/Graphite/Dark, Reworked Sepia, Load-Bearing Decisions

User request: remove the Mist, Graphite, and Dark themes outright, and rework Sepia — the shipped version wasn't a real sepia look, just a light theme with an orange tint (very high contrast: background L92%/foreground L15%, a ~77-point lightness swing, closer to "white paper, near-black ink with a tint" than genuine muted aged-paper sepia).

**Removed `.mist`/`.graphite`/`.dark` CSS blocks from `src/index.css`** and their `THEME_OPTIONS`/`Theme` union entries in `src/lib/theme-provider.tsx`. Minimal blast radius confirmed by grep before touching anything — no component branches on these ids by string comparison anywhere in `src/`.

**Removing the literal "Dark" theme id uncovered a real pre-existing coupling that would otherwise have silently regressed:** Tailwind's `darkMode: ["class"]` config keys every `dark:` variant utility in the app (19 files — `GrammarIssuePopover`/`RagIssuePopover` severity badges, `prose-invert` on the Notes/Chapter-notes editors, red alert boxes in Settings, several `border-gray-300 dark:border-gray-700` cards) off one specific, literal class name: `dark`. `ThemeProvider`'s own effect only ever added the *one* concrete theme's own class name to `<html>` (e.g. `midnight`, `abyss`, `sand`) — meaning **`dark:` variants were previously only ever live when the user had the literal "Dark" theme selected**, and silently dead for every other dark-family theme (Midnight, Abyss, Ember, Sand, Forest, Dark Parchment, Eclipse) despite those being equally dark. Simply deleting "Dark" without addressing this would have made `dark:` variants dead for 100% of themes, a straight regression from "sometimes works."

**Fix (also closes the pre-existing gap, not just avoids a new one):** `ThemeProvider` now toggles an auxiliary literal `"dark"` class on `<html>` independently of the specific palette class, driven by the already-existing `DARK_THEME_IDS` set (`src/lib/theme-provider.tsx`, previously built for the BT2 wordmark-selection logic — reused here rather than re-deriving). Both the theme-select effect and the `system`-preference-change effect call `root.classList.toggle("dark", DARK_THEME_IDS.has(appliedTheme))` right after adding the palette's own class. Live-verified: selecting Midnight now yields `<html class="midnight dark">`; selecting Light yields `<html class="light">` (no `dark`) — so `dark:` utilities now correctly fire for every dark-family theme, not just one.

**`system` preference resolution and the app's own default theme both hardcoded the literal `"dark"` id** (`getSystemTheme()`'s dark branch; `main.tsx`'s `defaultTheme="dark"`). Both now resolve to **Midnight** instead — the closest existing analog to what "Dark" was (near-black, cool-toned, `--background: 240 15% 4%` vs the old Dark's `193 100% 7%`). This is a judgment call, not a locked spec; if Reuben would rather System-dark/first-run default to a different dark theme (Abyss, Ember, Sand, Eclipse), it's a one-line change in both spots.

**Migration for stale `localStorage` values:** anyone (in practice, just this dev session) with `"dark"`, `"mist"`, or `"graphite"` already stored under the `app-theme` key would otherwise silently get no matching CSS block on next load (the app would fall through to `:root`'s own default Light-ish values with no theme class at all, and no error). Added `REMOVED_THEME_MIGRATIONS`/`resolveStoredTheme()` — a one-time remap (`dark`/`graphite` → `midnight`, `mist` → `light`) applied when reading the stored value; the corrected value is written back to `localStorage` automatically via the existing persist-on-change effect, so it's truly one-time. Concretely mattered this session: the active dev browser's theme was `"graphite"` at the moment this shipped.

**Sepia rework** — new values, all warmer and lower-contrast than before: `--background: 38 32% 87%` (soft warm tan, was `35 38% 92%`), `--foreground: 22 38% 22%` (warm sepia-ink brown, was `25 35% 15%` — near-black). The lightness swing between background and foreground dropped from ~77 points to ~65, and every other token (card/popover/secondary/muted/accent/destructive/sidebar/border) was re-derived around the same warmer, more muted hue family rather than just scaling the two endpoints — `--destructive` in particular moved from a fairly saturated `5 60% 40%` red to a more muted brick-red `8 55% 42%` to fit the same "aged photograph" mood instead of clashing with it. `--accent-glow-strength` dropped slightly (0.25 → 0.2) to match the calmer overall palette.

**A real automation-environment artifact hit during verification, worth flagging so it isn't mistaken for a CSS bug later:** the Browser pane's screenshot capture rendered the new Sepia background as a strongly-saturated yellow — visually alarming, and not what the intended HSL value should produce. Cross-checked via `getComputedStyle` (`--background` correctly read back as `38 32% 87%`) and a canvas `getImageData` pixel readback (confirmed exact `rgb(232, 225, 211)`, the correct pale warm cream) — both ground-truth checks confirmed the actual rendered pixel is correct and the screenshot tool's own image encoding/color handling is what's misrepresenting it in this sandbox, not the app. Recommend a real visual check in an actual browser rather than trusting this tool's screenshots for future subtle-color-difference work.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean — `ThemeToggle.tsx`'s `Record<Theme, ...>` icon map catching the type change confirmed no leftover references to the removed ids anywhere. Live-verified in the Browser pane against the real dev DB: theme dropdown now lists exactly 14 palettes + System (Mist/Graphite/Dark absent); switching Light → Midnight → Light confirmed the auxiliary `dark` class toggles correctly; Sepia's new computed background/foreground values confirmed via both `getComputedStyle` and a canvas pixel readback.

## Sepia Theme: Tailwind `sepia` Class Collision + Illegible Tag Chips, Load-Bearing Decisions

Two real bugs surfaced once the reworked Sepia palette (previous entry) made them visible in practice — user-reported: "why do the pictures become sepia toned? same with the logo" and "the lore book tags are unreadable."

**Bug 1 — Tailwind class-name collision applied a real photographic filter to the whole page.** Our custom theme system names its CSS class after the theme id (`.sepia { --background: ...; }` in `src/index.css`), but Tailwind ships a built-in filter utility literally named `sepia` (`.sepia{--tw-sepia: sepia(100%); filter: ... var(--tw-sepia) ...}`). Both rules matched the exact same `<html class="sepia">` element — our rule contributed CSS custom properties (harmless), Tailwind's contributed an actual `filter: sepia(100%)` declaration our rule never set, so nothing overrode it. A `filter` on an ancestor element visually post-processes everything painted inside it, so every image, the TopBar wordmark, and technically all rendered pixels were being run through a real sepia photo filter any time the theme was active — confirmed by grepping the compiled CSS output and finding both `.sepia{...}` rules sitting back to back. This was always present in principle from whenever the theme was first named "Sepia," but only became obviously visible once real character portrait images and the wordmark existed to visibly show a color-cast (a plain color-token palette doesn't visibly reveal a `filter: sepia()` the same way a photo does).

**Fix:** renamed the theme's internal id from `"sepia"` to `"theme-sepia"` throughout — `src/lib/theme-provider.tsx` (`Theme` union, `THEME_OPTIONS` entry — user-facing label stays exactly "Sepia", only the internal id/class changed), `src/index.css` (`.sepia` → `.theme-sepia` selector), `src/components/ThemeToggle.tsx` (`THEME_ICONS` map key). Added `sepia: "theme-sepia"` to the existing `REMOVED_THEME_MIGRATIONS` map (added in the prior theme-cleanup pass for the dark/mist/graphite removals) so anyone with the old literal `"sepia"` already in `localStorage` self-heals on next load instead of silently re-triggering the same collision forever. No other current theme id collides with a Tailwind utility name (checked against Tailwind's own filter-family utilities — blur/brightness/contrast/grayscale/hue-rotate/invert/saturate/sepia/drop-shadow — since that's the exact class of bug that bit here); worth keeping in mind if a future theme name is ever picked from a common English word.

**Bug 2 — Lorebook entry tag chips illegible (light text on a near-invisible tint).** `LorebookEntryCard.tsx`/`LorebookEntryRow.tsx` render each entry's free-form tags as `<Badge variant="secondary" className="bg-primary/10 ...">` — overriding the badge's background to a 10%-opacity tint of `--primary`, but never overriding its text color, so it silently fell back to whatever `variant="secondary"` implies: `text-secondary-foreground`. This was invisible before this session's Sepia rework because the old `--secondary-foreground` happened to be a dark color; the deliberate `--secondary`/`--secondary-foreground` change earlier this session (making `--secondary` the caramel accent with light near-white text, for the sidebar active-nav highlight) flipped `--secondary-foreground` to near-white — which, paired with a background that's still ~90% the page's own color (only a 10% tint), produced near-white-on-near-page-color: functionally invisible text. This was a real regression introduced by the earlier `--secondary-foreground` change, not a new problem with the tag markup itself, but the tag markup's own reliance on an unrelated variant's text color for an intentionally-custom background was the latent defect that let it happen.

**Fix:** both call sites now explicitly set `text-primary` alongside the existing `bg-primary/10`, so the chip's text and background are always derived from the same token pair regardless of what `--secondary-foreground` happens to be in any theme — `className="bg-primary/10 text-primary ..."`. Checked for the same `variant="secondary"` + custom `bg-*` override pattern elsewhere in the codebase (grep) — these two were the only instances.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. Live-verified in the Browser pane against the real dev DB: compiled CSS confirmed exactly one `.theme-sepia{...}` rule (our tokens) and zero remaining bare `.sepia{filter:...}` rule; `getComputedStyle` confirmed `filter: none` on `<html>`, `<body>`, the TopBar wordmark `<img>`, and every Lorebook entry portrait `<img>` while Sepia was active; simulating a stale `localStorage` value of the old literal `"sepia"` and reloading confirmed the migration correctly re-resolves to `"theme-sepia"` with no filter reapplied; a real Lorebook entry's "supporting character" tag chip confirmed rendering as `rgb(114, 76, 49)` text (dark brown, matches `--primary`) on a `rgba(114, 76, 49, 0.1)` background — legible, not the previous near-white-on-near-transparent.

## SL Monogram in Sidebar Rail, Load-Bearing Decisions

User request: add the SL monogram logo above "Stories" at the top of the left rail. User supplied the source image inline in chat across a few iterations (a plain white-background PNG, then a color variant) before confirming the actual file was already saved to disk at `docs/design-mocks/brand/exports/menu maze.png` — a transparent PNG matching the final color variant (brown "S", crimson "L", gold circular-maze backdrop), 2048×2048 with generous transparent margin.

**Promoted and trimmed**, not used as-is — the source has a large transparent border unsuitable for a compact rail icon. Used `sharp` to `trim()` the transparent margin (yielding a 1200×933 tight crop) and resized to two fixed rail sizes: `public/brand/sl-monogram.png` (96px) and `sl-monogram@2x.png` (192px, for high-DPI — not yet wired to a `srcset`, kept for a future pass rather than left unused on disk).

**Wired into `Sidebar.tsx`** — a new `<div className="flex justify-center pt-3 pb-1">` sits directly above the existing `<nav>` tool list inside the desktop `<aside>` (the `hidden md:flex` rail; the separate `md:hidden` mobile bottom bar was left untouched — "top of the left rail" only applies where a rail exists). Sized `h-10` expanded / `h-7` collapsed via the same `collapsed` boolean the rest of the sidebar already branches on. Unlike `BrandMark.tsx` (the wordmark component from the login/Stories/Series work), this monogram is **not** theme-aware — it's a fixed-color mark (brown/crimson/gold) rather than a flat wordmark with dedicated dark/light exports, so it uses one asset for every theme. Confirmed acceptable by live-checking it against a dark theme (Midnight) at both sidebar widths — the gold maze linework and crimson "L" retain good contrast against a near-black rail; a lighter theme wasn't separately spot-checked this pass, worth a look if it ever reads poorly there.
