## Maps v2 — MV0 (Schema + CRUD API + Export/Import Stub), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Maps_V2_Sketch_Design.md` (locked 2026-08-06, promoted to build 2026-08-06). First slice of the phased MV0-MV7 rollout — schema, service, routes, and story export/import only; no Excalidraw embed, sidebar UI, or AI propose/accept yet (those are MV1-MV5).

**New `storyMaps` table** (`server/db/schema.ts`) — deliberately a new table, not a widening of `storyMapEdges`/`storyMapLayout` (the L3 spatial relationship *graph* between location entries, React Flow-based). Those stay untouched in the DB per decision #8 (deprecated in the UI only, no v1 rebuild) — Maps v2's `storyMaps.sceneJson` is a different job entirely, the actual drawable Excalidraw scene. Columns: `id`, `storyId` (FK cascade), `title`, `locationId` (nullable, **no real FK** — same loose-column convention as `storyMapEdges.fromId`/`toId`, since an entry can be global/series scoped outside this story's own rows), `sceneJson` (JSON mode, opaque at the server layer), `thumbnailFilename` (nullable, disk-stored), `createdAt`/`updatedAt`. Migration `0056_absent_photon.sql`.

**Thumbnail storage** — new `storyMapThumbnailStorage.ts`, deliberately mirroring `lorebookImageStorage.ts`'s disk-by-filename pattern (own `data/uploads/story-maps/` directory) rather than inline base64 in the DB column — no existing precedent in this codebase stores image bytes inline, and thumbnails will be canvas-exported PNGs from MV2 onward, same size class as lorebook portraits. Nothing populates a thumbnail yet (no canvas exists until MV2) — the upload/stream/delete routes exist now so the schema and API surface are ready when MV2 wires the real Excalidraw `exportToBlob` call.

**Location unlink-not-delete on cascade** — `unlinkMapsForLocation()` nulls `locationId` (never deletes the map) when its linked lorebook entry is deleted, wired into `lorebook.ts`'s `DELETE /:id` alongside the existing `deleteMapEdgesForEntity`/`deleteMapLayoutForEntity` calls. Matches decision #6's hybrid-ownership spirit: a map's sketch content has value independent of its location label, so losing the link demotes it to a free story map rather than destroying it.

**Routes mounted at bare `/api`** (`server/routes/storyMaps.ts`), same two-path-shape reasoning as `storyGraph.ts`/`storyMap.ts`: story-scoped list/create/by-location (`/stories/:storyId/maps...`) plus flat id-addressed get/update/delete/thumbnail (`/maps/:id...`, map id is a global UUID). Editor-level auth only (`requireAuth` + `blockViewerMutations`, already global), no `requireOwner` — matches every other editorial (non-system-admin) surface in this app.

**Story export/import** — `storyMaps` added to the export `Promise.all` and response object in `stories.ts` (thumbnail bytes deliberately never ship — same "no binary in story JSON" posture as RAG chunks). Import remaps `storyId` unconditionally and `locationId` through the same `idMap` the lorebook-entries import block populates — but unlike `storyMapEdges`' import (which **skips** an edge outright if either endpoint didn't survive the export), a map whose location fell out of the export is **imported anyway** as a free-standing map (`locationId: null`) rather than dropped, consistent with the unlink-not-delete call above. `thumbnailFilename` is always nulled on import (no file to restore).

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean; `drizzle-kit generate` produced a clean single-table migration, applied successfully against the real dev DB on boot (confirmed via `Running database migrations... Database migrations completed successfully`). Hit the known dev-environment gotcha (`dev_environment_gotchas` memory, item 1) where `preview_start({name:"dev"})`'s `tsx watch` server child produced zero output and every `/api/*` call 500'd via ECONNREFUSED — worked around by using the `.claude/launch.json` `server-only` + `client-only` configs as two separate `preview_start` calls instead, which booted cleanly. Live-verified end to end against the real dev DB (demo story, disposable scratch rows cleaned up after): create free map, create location-linked map, list, by-location lookup, PATCH (title + sceneJson with a real element), GET single, 404 on a missing map id, 404 on a missing thumbnail, thumbnail upload + stream round-trip (content-type and byte length confirmed), location-delete cascade (a scratch location's linked map survived deletion with `locationId` correctly nulled), and a full story export→import round-trip (new story id, `storyMaps` count matched, `sceneJson` content preserved intact including a manually-added rectangle element, and the Berlin-linked map's `locationId` correctly remapped to the newly-imported story's own Berlin entry id). All test maps/locations/the imported scratch story were deleted afterward; the real demo story's own data was untouched throughout (only a disposable scratch location was created/deleted for the cascade test, and the one legitimate `lore-berlin` link was created then explicitly unlinked again before cleanup).

## Maps v2 — MV1 (Sidebar Swap + List/Detail Shell), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Maps_V2_Sketch_Design.md`. Second slice — retires the old L3 spatial-graph tool from the sidebar's product surface and stands up the Maps list→detail shell on top of MV0's API. Still no Excalidraw embed (MV2) or richer create flows/location-linking UI (MV3).

**Sidebar id kept, component and label swapped** (implementation clarification d) — `Sidebar.tsx`'s `"story-map"` tools-array entry now labels "Maps" instead of "Story Map"; `toolLabels.ts` updated to match (used by `useDocumentTitle.ts` for browser-tab titles too). `MainContent.tsx`'s `"story-map"` switch case now renders a new `MapsTool.tsx` instead of the old `StoryMapTool.tsx` — zero nav-reference churn (deep links, "Open in new tab" picker, etc. all keyed on the id, which never changed).

**Old graph components left on disk, unreferenced, not deleted** — `StoryMapTool.tsx`/`StoryMapCanvas.tsx`/the rest of `features/story-map/` (singular) are still there but nothing imports them anymore once `MainContent.tsx`'s import was swapped; confirmed genuinely dead via `npm run build`'s module count dropping (3488 → 3474 transformed modules) — Vite's tree-shaking bears this out, not just "we stopped calling it." Matches decision #8's explicit phasing ("data may remain in DB until a cleanup slice; tool must not present the old graph as the product") — full leftover deletion (components, `storyMap.ts`'s server route, `storyMapEdges`/`storyMapLayout` tables) is MV7's named job, not this one's. The backend `storyMap.ts` route (L3 graph CRUD) is untouched and still live, just unreachable from any UI now.

**New `features/story-maps/` (plural) directory** — deliberately verbose naming throughout (`StoryMapDocument`, `useStoryMapDocumentsQuery`, etc.) to stay unambiguous next to the pre-existing `features/story-map/` (singular, L3 graph) it supersedes in the UI — different directory, different hook/type names, easy to `grep` apart. `storyMapsApi` (client) similarly sits next to the pre-existing `storyMapApi` (no `s`) without shadowing it.

**MV1 scope drawn narrower than MV0's backend already supports** — the create dialog is title-only (no location-link picker), matching the phasing table's own split: MV1 is "Maps list → empty/detail shell," MV3 is "create flows: free map · link location · from location Open map." The backend (`locationId` on create/update, `by-location` lookup) already supports linking; the UI for it is deliberately deferred rather than pulled forward, to keep this slice's surface area matched to its own line in the phasing table. `MapDetailPanel.tsx` still *displays* a location badge if a map happens to carry a `locationId` (reachable today only via direct API use, e.g. a future MV3 build or manual testing) via a new `useLocationEntryQuery` hook (`lorebookApi.getById`) — read-only, no picker.

**`needsBoundedHeight` list in `MainContent.tsx` explicitly left off `"story-map"`** — the old entry was there for React Flow's canvas viewport, which no longer renders in this slot. MV1's list/detail shell is plain card content that page-scrolls fine unbounded, like Playbooks/Notes. Left a comment flagging that MV2's Excalidraw embed will likely need to rejoin this group once it lands.

**One incidental copy fix**: `PlaceSheetFields.tsx`'s "Exits summary" placeholder text ("free text, until a Story Map graph exists for this place") became actively false the moment this slice shipped — a real Maps v2 tool exists now, just not a graph. Replaced with a plain example-content placeholder rather than referencing either map system, since which one (if either) reads a location's exits isn't decided until MV3+.

**Verification:** `npm run build` clean (tsc client+server+vite; module count dropped as noted above, confirming the old graph tool is unreachable, not just unrendered). Live-verified in the Browser pane against the real dev DB via the `server-only`/`client-only` launch-config workaround (same gotcha as MV0): sidebar button reads "Maps" (confirmed via DOM text, not just source); clicking it lands on the new empty-state shell (old React Flow graph never renders); "New map" → dialog → create → auto-opens the new map's detail shell; inline title rename (blur-to-commit) persists and reflects back in the list; delete (via `ConfirmDialog`) removes the map and returns to the list. Network log for the whole flow showed only the expected `/api/stories/.../maps` and `/api/maps/:id` calls, all 200/201 — no calls to the old `/api/stories/.../map` (singular) graph endpoint. Test map deleted afterward; demo story otherwise untouched.

## Maps v2 — MV2 (Embed Excalidraw; Load/Save sceneJson; Theme; Native Undo), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Maps_V2_Sketch_Design.md`. Third slice — replaces MapDetailPanel.tsx's MV1 placeholder with a real `@excalidraw/excalidraw` (0.18.1, MIT) embed. Richer create flows/location-linking UI (MV3) and AI propose→accept (MV5) still not built.

**A real, live-caught bug found and fixed during this pass, not a pre-existing one**: the first working version (debounce elements/appState/files straight off every `onChange`) produced a flood of duplicate saves — a single drag gesture generated 21 completed `PATCH` round-trips instead of the expected one or two. Root cause, confirmed via console instrumentation: Excalidraw fires `onChange` on its own internal idle/presence heartbeat roughly every 2 seconds, even with zero user interaction and zero element changes (appState-only churn — collaborator/idle-state tracking, not content). Those pulses land further apart than the 1000ms debounce window, so each one independently completed its own debounce cycle and fired a genuine save — confirmed by leaving the canvas fully idle for 10s and watching a new "debounced save FIRED" log (and matching real network PATCH) appear every ~2s indefinitely. **Fix:** gate the save path on `getSceneVersion(elements)` (Excalidraw's own element-version hash, the standard technique their reference integrations use) — `onChange` now returns immediately, without ever touching the debounce timer, unless the elements themselves actually changed since the last save. Re-verified after the fix: 10s of pure idle produced zero network calls; a real two-point drag produced exactly the expected couple of saves, settling with no further activity. This same idle-heartbeat behavior would have affected any future MV2 implementation regardless of author — worth remembering if Excalidraw's `onChange` is ever wired into something else in this codebase (e.g. a live cursor/collab feature).

**Persistence format**: saves use Excalidraw's own `serializeAsJSON(elements, appState, files, "database")` (the canonical `.excalidraw` file shape: `type`/`version`/`source`/`elements`/`appState`/`files`) rather than hand-rolling a scene object, so a real `.excalidraw` file import/export (not in MV2's scope, but a natural fast-follow) stays a drop-in read of the same shape. The `"database"` variant (vs. `"local"`) strips clipboard-only content Excalidraw sometimes attaches — the right choice for a persisted document.

**`initialData` is mount-only, matching Excalidraw's own contract** — it is not treated as a controlled prop by the library, so `MapCanvas.tsx` deliberately reads `map.sceneJson` into a `useMemo` with an empty dependency array (a one-time snapshot at mount), not on every `map` prop change. A mid-session external scene change (e.g. a future MV5 AI Accept) needs `excalidrawAPI.updateScene`, not a prop change — out of MV2's scope. `MapDetailPanel.tsx` passes `key={map.id}` on the canvas mount point so switching between maps forces a real remount (same defense-in-depth precedent as the Editor MultiView cross-chapter content-loss bug fix) rather than risking a reused Excalidraw instance carrying the wrong map's stale scene.

**Title-rename race found and fixed as a direct consequence of adding autosave**: `MapDetailPanel.tsx`'s title-sync `useEffect` was keyed on the whole `map` object (`[map]`) in MV1, which was harmless when `map` only changed after an explicit user action (rename, delete). MV2's autosave replaces the detail query's cache entry roughly every second while drawing, which would re-run that effect and silently overwrite an in-progress title edit with the last-committed value mid-keystroke if a scene autosave happened to land while the user was mid-rename. Re-keyed the effect to `[map?.id]` instead — the local title draft is now only reset when actually switching to a different map (or on initial load), never by a same-map content-only update.

**Bounded-height layout re-added**: `MainContent.tsx`'s `needsBoundedHeight` list gained `"story-map"` back (removed in MV1 since there was no canvas yet) — Excalidraw fills its parent's height rather than growing to match content, same reasoning as Relationships' React Flow canvas. `MapDetailPanel.tsx` restructured to a `h-full flex flex-col` shell (fixed header, `flex-1 min-h-0` canvas region); `MapsListPanel.tsx` (list view, no canvas) now owns its own `h-full overflow-y-auto` scroll region within that same bounded shell rather than relying on page-level scroll.

**Lazy-loaded** — `MapCanvas.tsx` (and therefore all of `@excalidraw/excalidraw`) is loaded via `React.lazy`/`Suspense` from `MapDetailPanel.tsx`, not imported eagerly. Confirmed necessary: the dependency ships a large per-language font-subsetting asset set (dozens of locale chunks) that Rollup correctly code-splits away from the main bundle only because the import is dynamic — `npm run build`'s module-transform count jumped from 3474 to 5617 with this dependency installed, entirely as separate lazy chunks, not inflating the eagerly-loaded entry.

**Verification:** `npm run build` clean (tsc client+server+vite) both before and after the debounce fix. Live-verified extensively in the Browser pane against the real dev DB via the `server-only`/`client-only` launch-config workaround (same gotcha as MV0/MV1): canvas renders themed to match the app's active theme (screenshotted in both Midnight/dark and Light — Excalidraw's own chrome, not just the page around it, follows); drawing a rectangle then an ellipse and reloading confirmed both persisted and reloaded correctly via `initialData`; native Undo/Redo (toolbar buttons — the automated browser's synthetic `Ctrl+Z` keydown didn't route through Excalidraw's own key handler in this sandbox, a tool quirk, not a product bug, confirmed by the UI buttons working correctly) removed/restored a third shape exactly as expected, no custom undo code involved. Idle-heartbeat fix re-verified post-fix: 10s fully idle produced zero `PATCH` calls; a real edit produced the expected small number of saves with no follow-up phantom activity over another 8s of idle. All debug `console.log` instrumentation used to diagnose the bug was removed from the shipped code. Test map deleted afterward; demo story otherwise untouched.

## Maps v2 — MV3 (Create Flows: Link Location, From-Location "Open Map"; List Metadata), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Maps_V2_Sketch_Design.md`. Fourth slice — the two create-flow entry points the phasing table names beyond MV1's free-map-only dialog, plus real location-name list metadata (previously a generic "Location-linked" badge). AI propose→accept (MV5) and the `layoutMd` bridge (MV4) are still not built.

**New one-shot `StoryContext.pendingMapId`** (mirrors `pendingLorebookEntryId`'s exact posture) — the location entry's "Open map" button (`OpenMapButton.tsx`) sets it then calls `setCurrentTool("story-map")`; `MapsTool.tsx` consumes it once on arrival via a `useEffect` and clears it, same one-shot cross-tool navigation pattern the Relationships graph's "Open entry" and Outline's "Open in WB" already established for Lorebook. No new routing/deep-link mechanism needed.

**`OpenMapButton.tsx` lives in `RawEntryFields.tsx` only, not `NaturalEntryView.tsx`** — a real, pre-existing gap noticed while placing it: Natural View (the default-on entry presentation, `CLAUDE.md`'s "Lorebook Entry Editor — Natural View") renders *no* location-specific content at all today, not even `PlaceSheetFields`' place-sheet fields, only the character-specific Core Identity/Appearance/Wardrobe sections. That's outside Maps v2's scope to fix — flagged, not touched — but it means a user on the default Natural View sees nothing location-related (no place sheet, no Open Map) until they switch to Form View via Advanced Settings, an existing rough edge this slice didn't create. Live-verified this is exactly how the demo story's own "Berlin" entry behaves.

**Orthogonal to L4 place-Codex versioning by design** — `OpenMapButton` renders whenever `selectedCategory === "location"` and the entry has a saved `entryId`/`storyId`, regardless of `codexEnabled`/"Track Place State". A map link isn't Codex state (same posture as the psych module and the light place-sheet tier), so gating it behind the codex toggle the way `PlaceSheetFields` is gated (`!codexEnabled`) would have been wrong — confirmed by placing it as its own unconditional block, not folded into either `PlaceSheetFields` or `PlaceCodexStateEditor`.

**Unlink-not-delete's payoff realized**: the button toggles between "Create map for this location" (queries `GET /stories/:storyId/maps/by-location/:locationId`, `null` when none exists) and "Open map" (found) — clicking "Create" when none exists calls the existing MV0 create-with-`locationId` endpoint, then immediately opens it via the same `pendingMapId` pointer, so there's exactly one create path, not two. Re-verified live that clicking "Open map" a second time reuses the same map (`GET /stories/:storyId/maps` confirmed exactly one row for the location both before and after) rather than creating a duplicate.

**`NewMapDialog.tsx` gained an optional "Link to a location" `Select`** — sentinel value `"__none__"` for the no-link option (Radix `Select` doesn't accept an empty-string item value). Locations are fetched via a new `useLocationEntriesQuery(storyId)` (list) alongside the existing `useLocationEntryQuery(locationId)` (single, MV2) in the same hooks file — both live in `features/story-maps/hooks/useLocationEntriesQuery.ts`, reusing `lorebookApi.getByCategory(storyId, "location")` (a pre-existing endpoint, no server change needed).

**List metadata**: `MapsListPanel.tsx` now resolves real location names via the same `useLocationEntriesQuery` hook (an `id → name` `Map` built once per render) instead of a generic "Location-linked" label — falls back to that generic label only if a map's `locationId` doesn't resolve to any entry currently visible to the story (a stale/foreign id, shouldn't normally happen but degrades gracefully rather than showing blank).

**A real naming collision caught by the type checker, not a runtime bug**: `MapsListPanel.tsx` already imported the `Map` icon from `lucide-react` (used in the empty state); adding `new Map(...)` for the id→name lookup on the very next line silently shadowed the constructor with the icon component, producing two real `tsc` errors (not just a lint nit — `new Map(...)` doesn't type-check against a React component). Fixed by aliasing the icon import to `MapIcon` (and did the same in `OpenMapButton.tsx` preemptively, for consistency, even though nothing there constructed a `Map` yet).

**Query invalidation for relinks**: `useCreateStoryMapDocumentMutation`'s and `useUpdateStoryMapDocumentMutation`'s `onSuccess` now also invalidate the relevant `byLocation` cache key when a `locationId` was actually part of the call — gated (`variables.data.locationId !== undefined` for update) so this doesn't add work to MV2's existing every-second autosave tick, which never touches `locationId`.

**Verification:** `npm run build` clean (tsc client+server+vite) after fixing the `Map` shadowing error above. Live-verified end to end in the Browser pane against the real dev DB via the `server-only`/`client-only` launch-config workaround (same gotcha as every prior Maps v2 slice): switched the demo story's real "Berlin" lore entry from Natural View to Form View (Advanced Settings → Natural View off) to reach `RawEntryFields`, confirmed "Create map for this location" present and correctly absent any location badge state beforehand; clicked it, confirmed navigation to the Maps tool with a new map titled "Berlin" auto-opened and a "Berlin" location badge shown, `POST .../maps` in the network log with `locationId: "lore-berlin"`; navigated back to the Lorebook entry, confirmed the button now reads "Open map"; clicked it again, confirmed it opened the *same* map (`GET .../maps` showed exactly one row for the story, no duplicate) rather than creating a second one. Separately verified `NewMapDialog`'s location picker: opened it, confirmed all 4 of the demo story's location entries listed (Berlin/Prague/Moscow/Kaffeehaus 1989) alongside "Free story map (no location)"; created "Prague Old Town Sketch" linked to Prague, confirmed the title, the "Prague" badge, and the underlying `POST` payload (`locationId: "lore-prague"`) all correct; confirmed the Maps list panel showed both scratch maps with their real resolved location badges ("Berlin", not "Location-linked"). All test maps deleted afterward (`DELETE /api/maps/:id`, both 200); demo story's own lore entries/content untouched throughout.

## Maps v2 — MV4 (layoutMd Bridge: Export ASCII/MD, One-Shot "Convert to Sketch"), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Maps_V2_Sketch_Design.md`. Fifth slice — the text bridge between a location's existing `metadata.placeState.layoutMd` and a map's `sceneJson`, both directions. Only AI propose→accept (MV5) is left.

**Export direction — `sceneToMarkdown` (`features/story-maps/lib/sceneToText.ts`)**: a deliberately dumb, deterministic, one-way summary — no Excalidraw import at all (reads plain JSON fields off `map.sceneJson.elements` directly), sorted top-to-bottom/left-to-right, bound text folded into its container's line (`- Rectangle: "Bolt-hole"`), standalone text gets its own line. Reads the **last-saved** `sceneJson`, not live unsaved canvas state — MapCanvas's own autosave lag is ~1s, and wiring an `excalidrawAPI` ref just to shave that off wasn't worth it for a manual, occasional export action. Target depends on link state: a location-linked map's export **replaces** `metadata.placeState.layoutMd` (reusing the exact merge pattern `ChatInterface.tsx`'s `handleAcceptPlaceSheet` already established: spread `entry.metadata`, spread-and-override `placeState`, through the existing `useUpdateLorebookMutation`) — no confirm-before-overwrite, matching decision #7's framing of `layoutMd` as an export target/legacy field once a sketch exists, not precious hand-written SoT. A free (unlinked) map's export instead copies to the clipboard (`navigator.clipboard.writeText`). An empty scene shows an info toast ("Nothing to export yet") rather than writing/copying an empty string.

**Import direction — "Convert to sketch" offer, interpreted as scene-emptiness-gated, not a persisted one-time flag**: the design doc's migration table says "offer Convert to sketch once per location when opening Maps" — read literally as a permanent one-time flag, that would need new schema (a dismissed-forever bit per location). Instead: the offer shows whenever `map.locationId` is set AND the location's `layoutMd` is non-empty AND the map's scene has zero elements. This is self-closing with zero new state — accepting the offer (or drawing anything manually) puts a real element in the scene, which permanently satisfies the "scene non-empty" condition for that map from then on. Declining ("Start blank canvas") without ever drawing anything means the offer reappears on a later visit — a deliberate, disclosed tradeoff (see the code comment in `MapDetailPanel.tsx`) rather than the doc's literal "once ever" reading, on the reasoning that a location whose scene is still genuinely empty has nothing to lose by being asked again.

**Conversion stays inside `MapCanvas.tsx`'s lazy chunk, not `MapDetailPanel.tsx`**: `MapDetailPanel.tsx` (eager — mounted the instant the Maps tool renders any detail view) never imports anything from `@excalidraw/excalidraw`. It only decides *whether* to offer conversion and holds the user's yes/no choice as local state; the actual `convertToExcalidrawElements([{type:"text", ...}])` call — the library's own skeleton→full-element backfill, the same function MV5's AI-propose path will reuse — happens inside `MapCanvas.tsx`, gated by a new `seedText` prop that's only passed once the user has already said yes. This preserves MV2's lazy-loading rationale (Excalidraw's large per-locale font-asset set stays fully code-split) without needing a second lazy boundary just for this one conversion call.

**A freshly-converted scene persists immediately, not on the next debounce/heartbeat cycle**: `MapCanvas.tsx` gained a mount-only effect that calls the update mutation directly (bypassing the 1s debounce) whenever `seedText` produced non-empty elements — a reload one second after clicking "Convert" must not lose the conversion back to an empty scene. `lastVersionRef` (MV2's idle-heartbeat-churn guard) is seeded from the *converted* elements' own version, not the DB's stale pre-conversion version, specifically so the idle heartbeat doesn't independently race the mount effect and fire a second, redundant save.

**Verification:** `npm run build` clean (tsc client+server+vite). Live-verified end to end in the Browser pane against the real dev DB via the `server-only`/`client-only` launch-config workaround (same gotcha as every prior Maps v2 slice): seeded a scratch location with `layoutMd` text via direct API, created a location-linked map, opened it in the UI and confirmed the convert-offer banner rendered with the real `layoutMd` text and both choice buttons; clicked "Convert to sketch," confirmed a text element bearing that exact text appeared on the canvas *and* was already persisted server-side (`GET /api/maps/:id` showed 1 element) without waiting for any debounce window; drew a labeled rectangle ("Bolt-hole") on the same canvas, clicked "Export to place sheet," confirmed the location's `layoutMd` was replaced with the correct two-line markdown summary reflecting both the converted text and the new rectangle's label, in the right order. Separately verified the free-map path: a scratch unlinked map with a seeded text element showed "Copy as text" (not "Export to place sheet"), clicking it fired the `navigator.clipboard.writeText` call and a "Copied to clipboard" toast (clipboard *read*-back was blocked by this sandbox's own permission model, an environment limitation unrelated to the feature — the write call itself completing without throwing, confirmed by the toast firing from its `.then()`, is what actually matters). Verified the empty-scene guard on a brand-new empty map: clicking "Copy as text" produced the expected "Nothing to export yet" info toast and no clipboard/API call. All scratch maps and the scratch location deleted afterward; demo story's own data untouched throughout.

## Maps v2 — MV5 (AI: map-sketch Proposal Fence + Accept → Scene), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Maps_V2_Sketch_Design.md`. Sixth slice — the last functional slice in the MV0-MV7 plan (MV6/MV7 are export/Guide-copy and leftover-cleanup, no new capability). Full-scene-replace propose→accept only; per-element patch/merge (the design doc's other "Propose patch" row) is explicitly not built this pass — full replace covers the doc's own primary worked examples ("AI sketch me the safehouse, I nudge the boxes") and patching by id/label is a materially bigger scope (id-stable diffing against a scene the user may have since hand-edited) better left as its own future slice if real usage asks for it.

**Host chat: the location entry's own WB chat, Locations template only — no new "Maps chat" type.** Mirrors `psych-proposal`/`place-sheet-proposal` exactly rather than inventing new chat infrastructure: `MAP_SKETCH_INSTRUCTIONS` is appended to the *same* `locations`-template system prompt block `PLACE_SHEET_INSTRUCTIONS` already occupies (`chatContextService.ts`), unconditionally (no opt-in toggle, matching place-sheet's — not psych's — looser gating precedent). This means **free (unlinked) story maps have no AI-propose path in MV5** — there's no anchor entry to host a chat against. Accepted as a real, disclosed scope cut rather than inventing an anchor-less chat mechanism; every other propose→accept flow in this app (Codex, place-sheet, psych, outline) is anchor-based too, so this isn't a new asymmetry.

**Model emits an Excalidraw Element Skeleton directly** (implementation clarification c, locked before MV0 started) — `MAP_SKETCH_INSTRUCTIONS` teaches the exact JSON shape (`{"title", "elements": [{"type", "x", "y", "width"/"height" or "points", "label"/"text"}]}`), no custom intermediate DSL. `parseMapSketchProposal.ts` (mirrors `parsePsychProposal.ts`'s structure exactly) validates per-element — `type` must be one of the 6 allowlisted values, `x`/`y` must be numbers — dropping malformed elements individually rather than failing the whole proposal, same defensive posture as every other fence parser in this codebase.

**`convertToExcalidrawElements` never runs outside `MapCanvas.tsx`'s lazy chunk — this shaped the whole Accept architecture.** Accept happens in `ChatInterface.tsx`, which is mounted on the Lorebook page (not the Maps tool) and is far too eagerly-loaded/widely-used to ever import anything from `@excalidraw/excalidraw` (would silently undo MV2's whole lazy-loading decision — the per-locale font-asset chunking only holds because the import stays dynamic). So Accept does **not** convert or apply the scene itself: `handleAcceptMapSketch` (i) resolves-or-creates the anchor location's map via a new `useResolveOrCreateMapForLocationMutation` (an awaitable generalization of `OpenMapButton.tsx`'s MV3 find-or-create logic — same `getByLocation` → `create` fallback, just as a mutation instead of a click handler), then (ii) stashes the raw, still-unconverted element skeleton array on a new one-shot `StoryContext.pendingMapSketch` pointer and switches to the Maps tool. `MapsTool.tsx` consumes it (alongside the pre-existing `pendingMapId`, which it takes priority over since it also implies "open this map") and passes it down as `MapCanvas`'s new `seedSkeleton` prop — the actual `convertToExcalidrawElements` call happens there, inside the lazy chunk, at the canvas's own mount time. This is the exact same "seed via mount-time `initialData`, not a live `excalidrawAPI.updateScene` call" trick MV4's "Convert to sketch" already established — MV5 just generalized it to a second seed source.

**`seedSkeleton` is a full replace regardless of existing scene content — deliberately, no confirm dialog.** MV4's `seedText` only applies when the saved scene is empty (a soft, self-gating "starter" tool); MV5's `seedSkeleton` always wins and always replaces, even onto a scene with real prior drawing, because Accept is an explicit, informed user action — `MapSketchProposalCard.tsx`'s own copy warns "Accepting replaces the linked map's current sketch with this one" before the click happens. No second confirmation gate was added, matching every other propose→accept flow already in this app (Codex `modify_entry`, place-sheet merge, psych merge) — none of them ask "are you sure" either. The server-side prompt instructions also tell the model itself never to propose a sketch unsolicited, specifically because accepting one is destructive to any existing sketch.

**A small type-mapping layer, not a reused Excalidraw type, for the model-facing schema.** `MapSketchElementSkeleton` (in `src/types/storyMaps.ts`, alongside `StoryMapDocument`) is a deliberately looser shape than Excalidraw's own `ExcalidrawElementSkeleton` (e.g. `label` is a bare string here, not Excalidraw's `{text: string, fontSize?, ...}` object) — kept type-importable from `ChatInterface.tsx`/`parseMapSketchProposal.ts` (eager code) without pulling in anything from `@excalidraw/excalidraw`. `MapCanvas.tsx`'s new `toExcalidrawSkeleton()` is the one-way adapter between the two, living inside the lazy chunk alongside the only code that actually needs Excalidraw's real types.

**Verification:** `npm run build` clean (tsc client+server+vite) — confirmed the new eager code (parser, card, `useResolveOrCreateMapForLocationMutation`, `StoryContext` field) added only ~5KB to the main bundle and did **not** pull `@excalidraw/excalidraw` into it (the lazy-chunk split from MV2 stayed intact). Live-verified **end to end against a real, reachable cloud model** (NVIDIA Nemotron 3 Ultra via OpenRouter, this dev sandbox's configured Cloud provider) — a genuine first for a Maps v2 slice; every prior propose→accept feature in this project's history had to fall back to manually-inserted rows because no AI provider was reachable in-session. Opened Berlin's own "Locations & Settings" WB chat (confirmed "Focused on: Berlin" anchor), asked in plain language for a sketch of the MI6 station cover office; the model replied with real conversational text plus a correctly-formed `map-sketch-proposal` fence (3 elements, a sensible title); `MapSketchProposalCard` rendered "Sketch map — MI6 Berlin Station Cover Office — 3 elements" with a correct preview list; clicking Accept created a new map titled from the proposal, correctly linked to Berlin (`locationId: "lore-berlin"`), navigated to the Maps tool, and rendered the actual canvas with "Entry Lobby" and "Marcus Webb's Office" rectangles plus a "Hidden Stairwell Exit" text label, matching what the model described in its own reply. Confirmed persisted immediately (5 real Excalidraw elements — 2 rectangles + 2 bound container-text labels + 1 standalone text — in `GET /api/maps/:id`, not just held in memory) without needing to wait for any debounce window. All test data (the created map and the scratch WB chat) deleted afterward; the real "Berlin" lore entry's own content was untouched throughout.

## Maps v2 — MV6 (PNG Export; Guide Rewrite + Kill Dead Graph Copy), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Maps_V2_Sketch_Design.md`. Seventh slice — the last slice with any user-facing surface area; MV7 (soft-deprecate `storyMapEdges`/`storyMapLayout` leftovers) is pure backend cleanup. SVG export was scoped down to PNG-only — the design doc lists "PNG/SVG" as an either/or ("optional"), and PNG alone already covers the doc's own stated use case (an illustration snapshot, same as the old L3 Story Map's own PNG-only export) with half the surface area to maintain.

**PNG export uses Excalidraw's own `exportToBlob` (a pure elements→canvas renderer), not the old L3 Story Map's `html-to-image`-based DOM screenshot** (`StoryMapCanvas.tsx`'s `handleExportImage`, kept as reference/precedent but not reused). The old approach existed only because React Flow has no native "export to image" API and needed a DOM-screenshot workaround — a workaround with its own documented flakiness (`StoryMapCanvas.tsx`'s comment: `toPng` can hang indefinitely in this project's own dev/CI browser automation, worked around with a 15s race-timeout). Excalidraw ships a purpose-built export function operating directly on the elements array, sidestepping that whole class of bug — no timeout-race needed here.

**Dynamically imported, not a static top-of-file import** — `MapDetailPanel.tsx` is eager (mounted for every map opened, not gated behind Suspense the way the canvas itself is), so a static `import { exportToBlob } from "@excalidraw/excalidraw"` would pull Excalidraw's runtime out of `MapCanvas.tsx`'s lazy chunk and back into the main bundle, undoing MV2's whole lazy-loading decision. `await import("@excalidraw/excalidraw")` inside the click handler resolves to the *same* chunk `MapCanvas.tsx` already statically imports — Vite/Rollup dedupes it, so this never double-downloads the dependency, it just defers the request to the moment it's actually needed (and for anyone who's already opened the canvas once this session, it's already cached).

**Exports the last-saved scene, same tradeoff as MV4's text export** — not live unsaved canvas state (MapCanvas's own autosave lag is ~1s). Consistent, not a new inconsistency: both of `MapDetailPanel.tsx`'s export actions (place-sheet/clipboard text, and now PNG) share this same "last-saved is good enough for a manual, occasional export click" call, made once for the text path back in MV4 and just reused here rather than re-litigated.

**Guide (`locations-maps.mdx`) §3 rewritten in full**, replacing every reference to the retired L3 "Story Map" graph tool (edge-type table, "Add map link," "Focus on region," etc.) with the actual MV0-MV5 feature set: list→canvas, free vs. location-linked creation, the canvas toolbar (Export/Download/Delete), the MV4 Convert-to-sketch offer, and a new "AI-sketched maps" subsection documenting MV5's propose→accept flow including the explicit "replaces the entire current sketch" warning. §5's troubleshooting table and the closing paragraph were rewritten to match (dropped rows about Story Map's own edge-graph quirks that no longer apply; added rows for the new empty-scene/overwrite scenarios). `settings-nav.mdx`'s sidebar tool-list copy was also fixed — it still named the sidebar entry "Story Map" (stale since MV1 relabeled it "Maps"), the one other Guide file with a stale reference found via a full-project grep for "Story Map" outside the L3 feature directory itself. One internal code comment (`src/types/story.ts`'s `placeState` doc comment, which explicitly described `layoutMd` as a fallback "when no Story Map graph exists yet" — a claim MV0-MV4 had already made obsolete) was also updated for the same reason, even though it's not user-facing copy — the same "kill stale copy" instinct applied to comments a future reader would trust.

**What was deliberately left alone**: internal code comments in `features/story-map/` (the L3 directory itself, e.g. `MapEdgeEditDialog.tsx`) and this feature's own MV1 comment in `MapsListPanel.tsx` referencing "the old sidebar 'Story Map' graph tool" — both are accurate historical/internal framing, not live product copy, matching decision #8's posture (leftover code/comments stay until MV7's own explicit cleanup pass, or forever if never revisited).

**Verification:** `npm run build` clean (tsc client+server+vite); confirmed the lazy-chunk boundary held — the main bundle grew by ~1KB (the new button/handler code itself), not the ~1.3MB `@excalidraw/excalidraw` chunk. Live-verified in the Browser pane against the real dev DB: clicking "Download PNG" on an empty scene produced the same "Nothing to export yet" guard as the text-export path; drawing a rectangle and clicking it again was confirmed via a monkey-patched `HTMLAnchorElement.prototype.click` (this sandbox can't verify an actual OS-level file save) to have generated a real `blob:` URL and attempted a download with the correct `"<map title>.png"` filename — no errors in console or network. Guide content re-verified by navigating the actual rendered page (not just reading the `.mdx` source): the rewritten "Locations & Maps" tab's headings/table/callouts all rendered correctly with no MDX parse errors, and "Settings & Navigation"'s sidebar tool list now reads "Maps" instead of the stale "Story Map". Test map deleted afterward; demo story otherwise untouched.

## Maps v2 — MV7 (Soft-Deprecate Leftovers), Load-Bearing Decisions — Closes Out MV0-MV7 in Full

Doc: `docs/Maps_V2_Sketch_Design.md`. Final slice — pure comment/documentation cleanup, no runtime behavior change, no new capability. With this, all of MV0-MV7 has shipped.

**"Hide edges API from UI" was already fully true before this slice started** — a project-wide grep for any live import of `features/story-map/` (the L3 spatial-graph directory) outside its own subtree found exactly zero: `StoryMapTool.tsx` and `StoryMapCanvas.tsx` are genuinely unreachable, confirmed independently back in MV1 via a Vite build module-count drop when the sidebar was relabeled. This slice's actual work was making that status *legible* to a future reader who opens one of those files directly, or the still-live backend it depends on, without already knowing the MV1 history: added explicit "SOFT-DEPRECATED, UNREACHABLE" header comments to `StoryMapTool.tsx`, `StoryMapCanvas.tsx`, `server/routes/storyMap.ts`, and the `storyMapEdges`/`storyMapLayout` table definitions in `server/db/schema.ts` — each stating the status, why the code/data is still present, and where to build new features instead (`features/story-maps/` / `server/routes/storyMaps.ts`).

**Routes and data stay fully live — nothing was disabled, gated, or migrated away.** `server/routes/storyMap.ts`'s Express routes are untouched (still mounted, still functional if called directly) because `server/routes/stories.ts`'s story export/import still round-trips `storyMapEdges`/`storyMapLayout` for backward compatibility with any story JSON exported before Maps v2 existed — breaking that would silently drop data on a re-import of an old backup. **DB drop was deliberately not done** — the design doc's own phrasing is "optional DB drop later," not a requirement of MV7, and destructively dropping tables is a one-way door with no upside absent a concrete reason (disk space was never a stated concern for two small tables). If a future pass wants the drop, the soft-deprecation comments added here are the pointer to what's safe to remove and why.

**Design doc's own status updated to reflect full completion** — the top-of-file `**Status:**` header, the phasing table (added a `Status` column, all eight rows marked done with their ship dates), and the acceptance checklist (`- [ ]` → `- [x]` for all seven criteria, each annotated with which slice/verification pass actually confirmed it) were all brought current — the project's own stated convention (`CURRENT_BACKLOG.md`'s header) is that stale "not implemented" design-doc headers are a known trap, so this pass closed that gap for Maps v2's own doc rather than leaving it for a future session to rediscover. `CLAUDE.md`'s one remaining stale cross-reference (still pointing at "the Story Map tool" as the L0-L5 pass's own future-looking placeholder) was fixed too — a one-line pointer update, not a rewrite, consistent with `CLAUDE.md`'s own stated policy of not being the live source of truth for feature status.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean, output identical to the prior slice's build (confirms these were genuinely comment-only changes with zero behavioral impact — no new chunk, no bundle-size change, no hash drift beyond what a no-op rebuild would produce anyway). No Browser-pane live verification for this slice — nothing here is observable at runtime (comments don't render, and the design-doc/CLAUDE.md edits are documentation, not app code) — consistent with this session's own "skip verification when the preview can't exercise the change" guidance.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean. Live-verified in the Browser pane against the real dev DB: monogram confirmed present and positioned directly above the "Stories" nav button in the expanded rail (`y=12`, 40×40px) and correctly scales down in the collapsed rail (icon-only width) without layout breakage; sidebar expand/collapse toggle round-tripped cleanly afterward.

## Story Timeline — TL0-TL4 (Core Foundation)

Doc: `docs/Story_Timeline_Design.md` (design locked 2026-08-06, promoted this session). Talk list **T6**. First slice of a 13-slice fat design — TL0 (schema) through TL4 (H\|V toggle + Story-start marker chrome), user-scoped via `AskUserQuestion`. Modeled closely on Maps v2's shape (new tables, bare-`/api` router, `src/features/story-timeline/` module, sidebar tool, one-shot `StoryContext` pointers) — confirmed via direct reads of the `storyMaps` schema/routes/service/hooks/sidebar wiring before building.

**Schema:** three new tables (`storyTimelines`, `storyTimelinePins`, `storyTimelineMemberships`) rather than folding pins into `storyTimelines` or reusing `lorebookEntries` — pins are the design's own "story-scoped SoT," independent of which timeline(s) show them (memberships is the join, `laneId` on it schema-only for TL6 swimlanes). Same conventions as `storyMaps`: text PK via `randomUUID()`, epoch-seconds `mode: "timestamp"` columns, loose text columns (no real FK) for `linkType`/`linkId` and `storyStartChapterId`/`storyStartPinId` since the referenced row may not exist yet or lives in a different scope.

**Spine creation is lazy get-or-create, not hooked to story creation** — a deliberate deviation from the design doc's literal "spine created with story" wording. Hooking `POST /stories` alone would only help *new* stories; every pre-existing story in this and any deployed DB would have zero timelines forever. `ensureSpineTimeline(storyId)` (`server/services/storyTimelineService.ts`) runs on first `GET /stories/:storyId/timelines` and creates the spine row if missing, pre-filling `storyStartChapterId` with the lowest-`order` chapter at that moment — satisfies decision #11's "Default/preferred: anchor to Chapter One" for both new and pre-existing stories with one code path, still manually overridable via `StoryStartControl.tsx`.

**Sort pipeline is three strict tiers, not one blended numeric axis** (`src/features/story-timeline/lib/sortPins.ts`). The design doc's own pipeline line — "civil when present -> relative-to-start -> fuzzy bucket + manual order -> stable id" — is read as priority TIERS: civil-dated pins render as a leading group sorted by parsed date, relative pins (where the Story-start marker always sits at `relativeOffsetYears = 0` by definition, so it needs no separate resolution) as a middle group sorted by that offset, and fuzzy/unordered pins as a trailing group sorted by a plain `manualOrder` integer (reassigned 1..N on drag-reorder, same pattern `ChaptersTool.tsx` already uses for chapters). Mixing real calendar dates and relative-to-start years on one blended scale has no principled conversion without a resolved civil anchor, which is out of scope this pass — flagged as a fast-follow if mixed civil+relative usage in practice proves the tiered grouping wrong. Computed client-side (not duplicated server-side) since pin counts per story are small.

**Server:** `server/services/storyTimelineService.ts` (plain functions, inlined DB access, same "not worth a repository layer" call `storyMapsService.ts` made) + `server/routes/storyTimeline.ts` mounted bare at `/api` (same reasoning as `storyMapsRouter` — story-scoped list, flat id-addressed mutate), editor-level auth only (no `requireOwner`). **Unlink-don't-destroy on source delete** — a new `unlinkPinsForSource(linkType, linkId)` nulls a pin's link rather than deleting the pin, mirroring `storyMapsService.unlinkMapsForLocation`'s posture. Wired into three delete handlers: `notes.ts` (already had a custom DELETE override, one line added), `lorebook.ts` (already had one, one line added alongside its existing `unlinkMapsForLocation`/`deleteEdgesForEntity` calls), and `chapters.ts` (had **no** DELETE override before this — used the generic `createCrudRouter` delete — so a new one was added, the same `customRoutes`-registered-first pattern every other override in that file already uses).

**Cross-surface "Place on timeline"** — one reusable `PlaceOnTimelineButton.tsx` (checks `usePinForLinkQuery` first so a second click edits rather than duplicates) wired into three places: `RawEntryFields.tsx` (unconditional on category, per the design's "any category; `event` natural" — unlike `OpenMapButton` right above it, which stays location-only), `ChapterCard.tsx`'s existing icon-button action row, and `NoteListItem.tsx`'s hover action row. A `compact`/`compactSize` prop pair lets it render as a labeled button (Lorebook, matching `OpenMapButton`'s own convention) or an icon-only button at two different icon scales (`ChapterCard`'s h-8/h-4 vs. `NoteListItem`'s smaller ActionButton-matching h-6/h-3).

**New `StoryContext` pointers**: `pendingTimelineFocusPinId` (mirrors `pendingMapId` exactly, though TL0-TL4's single-board scope gives it nothing to focus on yet — reserved for TL5+) and `pendingNoteId` (genuinely new capability — `NotesTool.tsx` had no external "open this specific note" hook before this, unlike Lorebook's existing `pendingLorebookEntryId`; needed so a timeline pin's "open" action on a note-linked pin can select that note on arrival).

**Two real bugs found and fixed live during verification, both dnd-kit/dialog interaction issues, not data-layer bugs:**
1. `SortablePinCard`'s first draft wrapped the *entire* `PinCard` (including its Edit/Remove/Open-link buttons) in dnd-kit's `{...attributes} {...listeners}` — this let the PointerSensor intercept every click on the card, including on its own buttons, before the click ever reached them (confirmed live: clicking "Open note" silently did nothing and network showed no navigation). Fixed by adding a `dragHandle` render-prop to `PinCard.tsx` and only attaching listeners to a small dedicated grip-icon button in `TimelineBoard.tsx`'s `SortablePinCard`, not the whole card.
2. The pin-delete confirmation initially used a native `confirm()` — inconsistent with this app's own `ConfirmDialog` component (used everywhere else for destructive confirms, e.g. `DeleteChapterDialog`) and untestable via browser automation (native dialogs aren't part of the DOM; the click silently no-op'd against it). Replaced with `ConfirmDialog` + local `deletingPin` state, matching the rest of the app.

**Live-verified end to end in the Browser pane against the real dev DB** (server-only + client-only launch configs, per the documented `tsx watch`-under-`concurrently` gotcha): spine auto-created on first Timeline tool visit; "Place on timeline" from a note created a pin + membership (confirmed via network trace, `POST .../timeline-pins -> 201`), second click correctly showed "Edit timeline placement"; a native relative pin (`-6`, "6y before start") sorted correctly into its own tier with the Story-start marker rendering immediately after it; a fuzzy pin sorted into its own trailing "Unordered / fuzzy" group; "Place on timeline" confirmed rendering (unconditional on category) on both a location entry (Berlin) and a character entry (Marcus Webb); the chapter row's compact icon variant confirmed rendering and opening the same dialog; "Open note" from a pin correctly navigated to and selected the exact linked note; pin delete via the fixed `ConfirmDialog` flow removed cleanly with no orphaned state, board correctly fell back to its empty state. All test pins cleaned up afterward via the UI's own Remove action; no lasting demo-data changes. `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean throughout, including after both live-found-bug fixes.

**Not built this pass** (explicit, per user's chosen TL0-TL4 scope): TL5 (named timelines UI + board switcher — schema already supports multi-membership), TL6 (swimlanes UI — `laneId` column exists, unused), TL7 (AI propose/accept fence), TL8 (opt-in chat chronology context), TL9 (era/overview strip), TL10 (export image), TL11 (scanner hook + bulk suggest), TL12 (Guide + polish).

---

## Story Timeline — TL5-TL6 (Named Timelines + Swimlanes)

Doc: `docs/Story_Timeline_Design.md`. Second slice, following TL0-TL4 (committed `2bad71b`), user-scoped. **TL5** — named timelines + board switcher, exercising the multi-membership model the schema already supported but no UI ever exposed. **TL6** — swimlanes UI on top of the `laneId` column that's existed since TL0.

**One new column, `storyTimelines.swimlanesEnabled`** (boolean, default false) — small migration (`0058_wealthy_devos.sql`). No separate "lanes" table for TL6 — `laneId` stays the free-text label the writer types directly in the new membership popover; lanes are inferred from whichever distinct strings exist among a timeline's memberships (alphabetical, "Unassigned" trailing for `null`). No drag-reorder of lanes or bulk-rename this pass, flagged as a fast-follow if it proves needed.

**Timeline delete never orphans a pin — the load-bearing design call of this slice.** `deleteTimeline` (`storyTimelineService.ts`) blocks deleting the spine outright, and before deleting a named timeline, walks its memberships: any pin whose *only* membership is the timeline being deleted gets a Spine membership added first (reusing `ensureSpineTimeline` + the new `addMembership`). This was deliberately chosen over either (a) silently deleting those pins too — surprising and destructive — or (b) blocking the whole delete until the writer manually reassigns them — annoying friction for what's meant to be a lightweight board switcher action. Matches the project's standing "preserve, don't destroy" doctrine already established by `unlinkMapsForLocation`/`unlinkPinsForSource`. Live-verified: a pin exclusive to a named timeline and a pin also on Spine were both present when the named timeline was deleted; both survived on Spine afterward, confirmed via `get_page_text` before/after.

**`removeMembership` blocks removing a pin's last remaining membership** (400, "must stay on at least one timeline — delete the pin instead") — without this, an unchecked checkbox in the new membership popover could silently produce a pin with zero memberships, unreachable from any board (TL0-TL6 has no story-wide "all pins" browse view; that's TL9's job). `addMembership` is idempotent on the unique `(timelineId, pinId)` pair (returns/updates the existing row rather than throwing) so a double-click or an `addMembership` call that's really "just update this membership's laneId" both work through one function.

**`PinMembershipPopover.tsx`** (new, wired into `PinCard.tsx`'s action row) is the concrete UI for the design's "same pin on Spine + named timeline via membership" acceptance criterion — a checkbox per story timeline, plus a free-text lane field that only appears for a checked timeline that has swimlanes on. Fetches the story's timeline list itself via `useTimelinesQuery` rather than prop-drilling it through `TimelineBoard` -> `PinCard` -> here; React Query dedupes against `TimelineTool.tsx`'s own call to the same key.

**No new `Checkbox` UI component** — `@radix-ui/react-checkbox` isn't an existing dependency, and adding one for a single popover's worth of checkboxes wasn't worth it; used a plain native `<input type="checkbox">` instead, styled minimally to match the app's existing form-control look.

**`TimelineBoard.tsx` was restructured, not just extended** — the tiered civil/relative/fuzzy render-and-drag logic (previously inline in the component) was extracted into a `TieredBoard` sub-component with its own `DndContext`/sensors/drag-handler instance, so it could be reused unchanged both for the plain (no-swimlanes) board and for each lane row when swimlanes are on — each lane gets its own independent drag-reorder context for free, no special-casing needed. Swimlanes force a horizontal-per-lane layout; the H\|V toggle buttons are hidden (not just disabled) while swimlanes are on — a deliberate simplification (the design's own Views table lists swimlanes as a variant on the H\|V toggle, not fully orthogonal to it), not a bug.

**`TimelineTool.tsx` gained `activeTimelineId` state**, localStorage-persisted per story (`timeline-active-id-${storyId}`, same per-story-key convention `StoryContext.tsx`'s own `CHAPTER_KEY` uses) — restores the last-viewed board on reload, falls back to Spine if the stored id's timeline was since deleted. This also fixed a real pre-existing scoping gap from TL0-TL4: `StoryStartControl`'s manual-pin picker previously received every story pin regardless of which timeline was showing (unnoticed before TL5 because there was only ever one timeline) — now receives the same pre-filtered set `TimelineBoard` renders.

**Live-verified end to end in the Browser pane against the real dev DB**: created a named timeline ("Lizzy — prior ops"), confirmed it appeared in the switcher with Rename/Delete controls (Spine correctly has neither); created a pin there, added a Spine membership via the popover, confirmed via `get_page_text` that the *same* pin (not a duplicate) appeared on both boards after a reload; removed the Spine membership successfully (one of two remaining), then confirmed the last-membership guard fired a real 400 (`DELETE .../memberships/:timelineId -> 400`) when attempting to remove the pin's only remaining membership, and that the pin was still present afterward; enabled swimlanes, assigned two pins to two different lanes via the popover's lane field, confirmed both rendered as separate labeled rows and the H\|V toggle buttons disappeared from the header; deleted the named timeline with one pin exclusive to it and one pin also on Spine — both pins survived on Spine afterward (get_page_text before/after), confirming the auto-preserve path. All test pins/timeline cleaned up via the UI's own Remove/Delete actions afterward. `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean throughout.

**Not built this pass**: TL7 (AI propose/accept fence), TL8 (opt-in chat chronology context), TL9 (era/overview strip — also the "all pins" browse view that would make an orphaned pin theoretically reachable, though the delete-timeline auto-preserve above means this should never actually happen), TL10 (export image), TL11 (scanner hook + bulk suggest), TL12 (Guide + polish). Lane drag-reorder and bulk lane rename also not built, noted above as fast-follows if needed.

## Story Timeline — TL7-TL8 (AI Propose/Accept + Opt-in Chat Context)

Doc: `docs/Story_Timeline_Design.md`. Third slice, following TL0-TL4 (`2bad71b`) and TL5-TL6 (`18e126e`), user-scoped. **TL7** — a chat-driven AI pin proposal fence, mirroring the existing `psych-proposal`/`place-sheet-proposal` pattern exactly. **TL8** — an opt-in compact chronology block in chat context, mirroring `includeMemory` exactly. Both patterns were researched from real code (not re-derived) before implementation — see the plan's "Context" section for the file:line trail.

**Host: the WB `timeline` template** (`src/types/worldbuilding.ts`, declared since before this project's fork but never wired to any behavior until now) — exact structural analog to how `locations` hosts `place-sheet-proposal`/`map-sketch-proposal`, gated the same way in `chatContextService.ts`'s `buildSystemPrompt` `if` chain.

**Proposed pins are deliberately native-only — no `linkType`/`linkId`.** The `timeline` template isn't anchored to a specific entry (unlike `character_codex`/`locations`), so the model has no reliable way to know internal chapter/lorebook/note ids from a planning conversation, and fuzzy name-matching would be fragile and error-prone. A pin created this way can still be placed on a source later via the existing `PlaceOnTimelineButton`. Documented as a deliberate scope cut in the fence instructions themselves (the model is told explicitly never to invent links), not just in code comments.

**The fence carries a JSON array, not a single object** — unlike `psych-proposal`/`place-sheet-proposal` (one profile/sheet per entry), a timeline planning conversation naturally surfaces several beats in one reply. Followed the `handoff-packet`/`note-split-proposal` precedent for "array, multiple per reply" rather than inventing a new shape. `TimelinePinProposalCard.tsx` renders one row per item with independent Accept/Reject plus a header "Accept all"; `ChatInterface.tsx`'s `timelinePinProposals` state is keyed by messageId -> array, with `dismissTimelinePinProposal` filtering by object identity (not index) so accepting one item elsewhere in the array never shifts another's position mid-flight.

**Accept reuses the existing, unchanged `createPin` mutation** (`useCreatePinMutation`/`storyTimelineApi.createPin`) — no new server route, no `timelineId` means it defaults to Spine server-side (`ensureSpineTimeline`), identical to `PlaceOnTimelineButton`'s own default. Same "reuse the generic mutation, don't build a proposal-specific write path" move `handleAcceptPsych` makes with the generic lorebook update route.

**TL8's compact context is Spine-only** — the design doc's own AI/RAG table explicitly allows "selected timeline(s) **or** spine"; spine alone is the simplest valid choice, and named-timeline selection is a clean fast-follow if a user ever asks for it (the UI has no way to pick a different timeline for context purposes yet). Payload per pin is `{id, title, blurb, when}` — a resolved display label, never the raw `whenKind`/offset fields, and never the linked entry's full body (design doc: "not full linked bodies", same restraint as `relevantMemories`/`relevantNotes`).

**The tiered sort is a small, deliberately separate duplicate on the server, not an import of `sortPins.ts`.** `chatContextService.ts`'s new `resolveTimelinePins` reimplements the civil -> relative -> fuzzy comparator (~15 lines) rather than importing `src/features/story-timeline/lib/sortPins.ts` — avoids a server file depending on a client feature module for three lines of logic, at the cost of two copies that must be kept in sync if the pipeline itself ever changes (documented inline at both sites).

**A real, previously-existing allowlist gap was found and fixed as a prerequisite for TL8's toggle to work at all**: `server/routes/chats.ts`'s `PATCH /api/chats/:chatId` and `server/services/chatRepository.ts`'s `updateMeta` both explicitly destructure/allowlist every accepted field rather than passing `req.body` through — unlike the generic `createCrudRouter` pattern most other resource routes use. Adding `includeTimeline` to the schema and the client's `AIChat`/`chatsApi.update` types was not sufficient by itself; the PATCH route and `UpdateChatMetaFields` type both needed the field added explicitly too, or the toggle would silently no-op (the client would report success, but the column would never actually update). Caught immediately by `tsc` (both call sites errored), not a live bug that shipped, but worth flagging for the next opt-in-toggle slice that touches this same route.

**A session-wide browser-automation limitation, not a code bug, blocked full click-through UI verification this pass.** Plain navigation buttons worked fine, but this session's `computer` tool clicks stopped registering on anything using pointer-capture-based interaction (the WB chat rail's "New Chat" dropdown trigger, existing chat-list row buttons) even after a full client-process restart (this project's own documented HMR-corruption gotcha didn't resolve it either). Confirmed via `document.elementFromPoint` that click coordinates were landing on the correct DOM nodes, and confirmed synthetic `pointerdown`/`mousedown`/`pointerup`/`mouseup`/`click` event dispatch worked for plain sidebar nav buttons but not for the chat-rail's interactive rows — a narrower, more resistant version of the previously-documented Radix-dialog-stall gotcha. **Verification pivoted to direct authenticated `fetch` calls from the browser's own console** (real requests against the real dev DB, same session/cookie, same server code paths — just skipping the click layer): created a real `timeline`-templated chat via `POST /api/chats`, confirmed `GET .../context`'s `systemPrompt` contains the `timeline-pin-proposal` fence instructions only when `templateSlug === "timeline"`; toggled `includeTimeline` via `PATCH`, created two real Spine pins (one relative, one fuzzy) via the already-proven `POST .../timeline-pins` route, and confirmed `GET .../context`'s `relevantTimelinePins` returned both, correctly tier-sorted, with correctly resolved `when` labels ("3y before start", "that spring") and blurb passthrough; toggled `includeTimeline` back off and confirmed the array went empty again. The fence-parsing regex/JSON-shape itself was verified via a disposable standalone script confirming byte-for-byte the same extraction `parseTimelinePinProposal.ts` performs. The Accept mutation path (`createPin`) was exercised directly and is identical to the already-live-proven `PlaceOnTimelineButton` path from TL0-TL4/TL5-TL6 — genuinely not re-verified this pass is the `TimelinePinProposalCard.tsx` click-to-accept UI itself and the live model round-trip (same "no reachable AI provider" caveat nearly every propose/accept slice in this project's history has hit, now compounded by this session's own click-registration issue). All test data (chat, both pins) cleaned up afterward via the same authenticated-fetch route calls. `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean throughout.

**Not built this pass**: TL9 (era/overview strip), TL10 (export image), TL11 (scanner hook + bulk suggest), TL12 (Guide + polish). TL0-TL8 now cover the entire non-AI-bulk, non-export core of the design.

## Story Timeline — TL9-TL12 (Era Strip, Export Image, Scanner Hook + Bulk Suggest, Guide), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Story_Timeline_Design.md`. Fourth and final slice, following TL0-TL4 (`2bad71b`), TL5-TL6 (`18e126e`), TL7-TL8 (`96686b2`), user-scoped. Closes out the 13-slice fat design in full (TL0-TL12). Three of the four slices mirror strong existing precedents found via research before implementation (image export in `StoryMapCanvas.tsx`, AI-suggest-job + pending-review-tab in the Lorebook Relationship Graph feature, the MDX Guide system) rather than inventing new patterns.

**TL9 — "era/overview strip" scoped down to a compact tick strip, not a new entity.** The design doc names this "era/overview strip" but never defines an "era" as a data concept anywhere else in the schema, and adding one (a new table, CRUD, UI) would be out of proportion for what's explicitly a polish-tier slice. Interpreted instead as `TimelineOverviewStrip.tsx` — a thin horizontal row of small tick buttons above the main board, one per pin, grouped by the same three sort tiers `sortPins.ts`/`groupPinsByTier` already compute (civil/relative/fuzzy), with tier-boundary labels. Clicking a tick scrolls the matching `PinCard` into view via a new `data-pin-id={pin.id}` attribute on its root `Card` (`PinCard.tsx`) plus `scrollIntoView`. No new schema, no new entity, no lanes-awareness (always reflects every pin for the active timeline regardless of swimlanes — a lanes-aware strip would add complexity this overview aid doesn't need). `PinCard.tsx`'s previously-private `whenLabel` helper was exported for reuse as the strip's tick tooltips rather than duplicated a third time.

**TL10 — mirrors Story Map's own `toPng` export pattern, simplified for a non-canvas board.** `StoryMapCanvas.tsx`'s `handleExportImage` (soft-deprecated file, but the pattern itself is sound) uses React Flow's `getNodesBounds`/`getViewportForBounds` plus `html-to-image`'s `toPng` against the `.react-flow__viewport` DOM node, because that board pans/zooms. `TimelineBoard.tsx` is a plain scrolling flex layout with no pan/zoom — so `handleExportImage` here just calls `toPng` directly against a `useRef`'d container wrapping whichever of the two board-render branches (plain or swimlaned) is active, with the same 15s-timeout `Promise.race` (`@jfdi/attempt`) guarding against `toPng`'s documented hang-instead-of-reject failure mode in some environments. `html-to-image` was already a dependency (Story Map's own use) — no new package added.

**TL11A — the scanner's chronology context is opt-in per-scan, threaded exactly like `includeMemory`, and required a refactor to avoid a third hand-duplicated copy of the tier-sort logic.** TL8 shipped `chatContextService.ts`'s `resolveTimelinePins` as a deliberately-not-imported server-owned duplicate of the client's tier-sort pipeline (documented at the time as "keep both in sync if the pipeline ever changes"). TL11A needed the identical logic for the scanner's own context-gathering, and "keep three copies in sync by hand" stopped being the cheaper option the moment a third consumer showed up — so the logic was extracted into `server/services/storyTimelineService.ts`'s exported `getSpineChronologyExcerpt(storyId)` (same `{id, title, blurb, when}[]` shape), and `chatContextService.ts` now just calls it directly (its own local `resolveTimelinePins` function was deleted entirely, not kept as a thin wrapper). `ragScanner.ts` gained a parallel `gatherTimelineContext` (mirrors the existing `gatherMemoryContext`'s exact shape/gating) and a third optional prompt section (`=== STORY TIMELINE (established chronology) ===`), threaded through `runChapterScan`/`scanChapter`/`scanStory`/`ragScanJobs.ts` exactly the way `includeMemory` already was — both scanner system prompts (`SCANNER_SYSTEM_PROMPT`/`_WITH_MEMORY`) got one added paragraph telling the model to weigh the timeline block for `"timeline"`-type issues (that issue type already existed in the schema/prompt from day one; this just gives it real reference data instead of relying purely on prior-chapter text). UI toggle added identically to both `ChapterScannerDrawer.tsx` (chapter-scoped) and `RagScannerPanel.tsx` (story-wide), next to their existing "Include Project Memory" switches.

**TL11B — bulk-suggest pins mirrors `graphSuggestEdgesJob.ts` and its pending-review lane exactly, including the schema shape.** `storyTimelinePins` gained `status` (`"active" | "pending" | "rejected"`, default `"active"`) and `source` (`"user" | "ai_suggested"`, default `"user"`) columns — the identical pair `storyGraphEdges` already carries, same reasoning: existing rows backfill via the column defaults, no data migration needed. `listPinsForStory` now implicitly filters to `status: "active"` (mirrors `listActiveEdgesForStory`), so a pending AI proposal never appears on a board or in TL8/TL11A's chronology context until approved. New `listPendingPinsForStory`/`proposeAiSuggestedPin`/`approvePin`/`rejectPin` in `storyTimelineService.ts` mirror `storyGraphService.ts`'s `listPendingEdges`/`proposeAiSuggestedEdge`/`approveEdge`/`rejectEdge` function-for-function (approve/reject both guard on `status !== "pending"` throwing a descriptive error, verified live via a direct API call against an already-active pin). The new `timeline_suggest_pins` job (`server/services/jobs/timelineSuggestPinsJob.ts`) mirrors `graphSuggestEdgesJob.ts`'s structure line-for-line: manual-trigger-only (never auto-scheduled in `jobRunner.ts`), reads a story's visible lorebook entries (via the existing `listVisibleEntriesForStory`) plus its notes (a new, simple inline query — no dedicated notes-repository module exists in this codebase, so `db.select().from(schema.notes)` was used directly rather than inventing one), caps combined sources, prompts for a JSON array of candidate pins with a `sourceIndex` back-reference, and writes each accepted candidate via `proposeAiSuggestedPin` — always onto Spine (no natural "which named timeline" signal for an AI-suggested beat, same posture TL7's chat-proposed pins already established). Registered across the same five points `graph_suggest_edges` required: `FeatureKey`/`FEATURE_LABELS`/`FEATURE_KEYS` (`aiSettings.ts`), `AgentJobType` (`agentJob.ts`), the `HANDLERS` dispatch map (`jobRunner.ts`), the route allowlist (`agentJobs.ts`), and `RecentJobsCard.tsx`'s job-type label map (a `Record<AgentJob["jobType"], string>` — TypeScript's exhaustiveness check on this map is what caught the registration gap immediately, before the new job type could ever silently render blank in the Recent Jobs list). New `PendingPinsPanel.tsx` mirrors `PendingEdgesPanel.tsx` presentationally; `TimelineTool.tsx` gained a `Tabs` (`board`/`pending`, live count badge) wrapping the existing switcher/board, plus an owner-gated "Suggest pins" button (`useIsOwner`, mirrors `StoryGraphCanvas.tsx`'s "Suggest relationships" button placement/styling) that enqueues the job. New approve/reject routes (`POST /timeline-pins/:id/approve`/`reject`) mirror `storyGraph.ts`'s own.

**TL12 — new Guide page mirrors `locations-maps.mdx`'s structure**, registered in `GuideTabs.tsx` immediately after "Locations & Maps" (Timeline's natural neighbor as the time twin of Maps v2). Polish pass reviewed empty states/loading states/tooltips across all TL9-TL12 additions and the pre-existing TL0-TL8 files for consistency — found no rough edges needing a fix.

**A real dev-environment gotcha bit again this pass, worth re-flagging even though it's already documented elsewhere in project memory**: partway through live verification, a freshly-created pin's API response was missing the `status`/`source` fields entirely, even though the migration had already run cleanly and the insert succeeded — the running `server-only` preview process was executing stale pre-edit code (`tsx watch` not having picked up the several server-file edits made since it was last started). Restarting the `server-only` preview process resolved it immediately; re-verified the same pin creation call afterward and got the correct fields back. Not a code bug — a dev-sandbox process-staleness quirk consistent with this project's own `dev_environment_gotchas` memory entry.

**Live verification this pass, against the real dev DB**: created two real pins via direct `POST` and confirmed the overview strip renders ticks grouped by tier with correct labels ("Since start"/"Unordered"), clicking a tick produces no console errors (board too small in the test case to visibly demonstrate scroll, but the click handler fired without incident); clicked "Export as image" and confirmed no error toast/console errors (browser-automation sandbox can't directly observe a triggered file download, so this is best-effort — consistent with the same limitation Story Map's own MV6/L5b export slices hit previously); clicked "Suggest pins" and confirmed via server logs the `timeline_suggest_pins` job ran the full pipeline through to a real (expected) AI-connection failure — no reachable provider configured in this session, the same limitation nearly every propose/accept slice in this project's history has hit, but sufficient to confirm the job registration, source-gathering, and prompt-assembly code paths all executed without throwing; directly called the approve route against an active (non-pending) pin and confirmed the expected 400 "Pin is not pending" error; enqueued a `rag_scan_chapter` job with `includeTimeline: true` in its payload and confirmed via server logs it reached the same real (expected) connection-error point, confirming TL11A's `gatherTimelineContext` code path executes cleanly; opened `/guide`, clicked the new "Story Timeline" tab, and confirmed the full MDX content renders. All test pins cleaned up via direct `DELETE` calls afterward; confirmed zero pins remain. `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean throughout all four sub-slices.
