## Notes Desk Org + Browse UI — T7 (NO0-NO6), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Notes_Org_Browse_Design.md` (design locked 2026-08-07, all axes 1-10 pre-decided by the user's own grill before this build). Brings the Notes desk up to Lorebook-shaped parity — sticky Browse + closable note tabs, Cards|List, cosmetic folders (B9 engine extended with a third `kind`), thin tags, smart piles, title+body search — without touching AI gates, RAG, or the double-gate doctrine. All ten locked-decision axes were followed as specified; no scope renegotiation was needed mid-build.

**Folders engine extended to a third `kind: "notes"` rather than duplicating it.** `orgFolders.kind` was already a bare `text` column (no DB-level enum) supporting `"lorebook" | "chat"` — widening every TypeScript union (`folderService.ts`'s `FolderKind`, `types/folders.ts`, `foldersClient.ts`, `useFoldersQuery.ts`, the `routes/folders.ts` GET guard) to admit `"notes"` needed zero schema/migration changes on `orgFolders` itself. `validateSameBucket` (the "a folder's parent must share the same scope/category/chatType" guard) already worked correctly for `kind: "notes"` with **no code change** — neither its lorebook nor its chat branch fires for a third kind, and the base kind+scopeId check already covers notes' whole scoping model (just `scopeId = storyId`, no level/category/chatType concept). `createFolder`'s validation likewise needed no new branch. Only `deleteFolder`'s leaf-reparent step (previously a binary `if (kind === "lorebook") ... else ...` that implicitly assumed "not lorebook = chat") needed a real third branch added, and `deleteFoldersForStoryScope`'s cleanup query needed a third `or(...)` arm — the only two places in `folderService.ts` that hard-assumed exactly two kinds existed.

**`resolveNotesFolderId` mirrors `resolveChatFolderId`, not `resolveLorebookFolderId`** — a note's own folder-matching is simpler than either precedent (`folder.kind === "notes" && folder.scopeId === note.storyId`, no category/chatType/level to also check), closer to chat's shape than lorebook's category-aware one. Wired into `routes/notes.ts`'s PUT override with the same `attemptPromise` -> 400-on-mismatch pattern `lorebook.ts`'s PUT already established, rather than lorebook's dual-trigger-on-category-change design (notes have no category-equivalent field that could drag a stale folderId along).

**Each folder-tree consumer builds its own presentational tree, per the established Lorebook-vs-Chat precedent — not a third copy-paste-free abstraction.** Investigated whether `LorebookFolderSidebar.tsx`/`LorebookFolderTreeRow.tsx` could be generalized for reuse; found they're tightly typed to Lorebook's own `level`/`category` props and hardcode `"lorebook-folder"` as the DnD data-type literal, and `ChatFolderNode.tsx` already duplicates the same tree-row shape rather than sharing it. Followed that same precedent: new `NotesFolderSidebar.tsx`/`NotesFolderTreeRow.tsx`, duplicated rather than parameterized, using `"notes-folder"`/`"note"` as their own DnD data-type literals (both unions widened in `FolderDropZone.tsx`/`DraggableLeaf.tsx` to admit them alongside the existing lorebook/chat values). The truly shared, kind-agnostic layer — `useFoldersQuery`/`folderTree.ts`'s `buildFolderTree`/`getDescendantFolderIds`/`getFolderPath`, plus `FolderDropZone`/`FolderNameDialog`/`MoveToFolderDialog`/`FolderContextMenu` — needed no duplication at all, confirming B9's original design intent (data/mutation layer shared, presentation layer per-consumer) held up for a third kind with zero friction.

**Open-tabs shell copies `LorebookPage.tsx`'s tab-persistence pattern exactly, not `editor-multiview`'s heavier split-pane system** — confirmed via research these are two deliberately different systems in this codebase (`LorebookTabStrip.tsx`'s own doc comment already flags this), and Notes' need (sticky Browse + flat closable note tabs, no split/float) matches Lorebook's shape far more than the Editor's. New `NotesTabStrip.tsx`/`NoteOpenTab` type is a duplicate of `LorebookTabStrip.tsx`'s shape (not an import — same reasoning as the folder-tree duplication above: each is typed to its own domain's tab union). The `notes-tabs-story-${storyId}` localStorage key follows the same `attempt()`-guarded, per-story-keyed persistence `lorebook-tabs-story-${storyId}` already established. Deleted-note tabs are pruned silently on the notes list settling (a `useEffect` keyed on the notes array), never surfaced as an error state, matching the design's "invalid ids drop silently" call.

**"Focused note" (for the Notes chat rail's desk-privilege context, K1) is now strictly "the active note tab's own note, else null"** — a real behavior narrowing from the pre-T7 code, where "focused" was just "whichever note is currently `selectedNoteId`" (a single flat value with no Browse-vs-tab distinction, since there was no Browse/tab distinction at all). This matches the design doc's own explicit call ("focused note = active note tab's id when a note tab is focused, else last Browse selection if any") — the "else last Browse selection" half was **not** built, since Browse's Cards|List grid has no single-item "selection" concept in the shipped design (clicking a card opens a tab immediately, it doesn't select-without-opening) — there was nothing to wire up. Documented here rather than silently diverging from the design doc's literal wording.

**Title/type editing moved from a modal dialog (retired `NoteFormDialog`'s edit mode) into the note's own tab, not preserved as a Browse hover action.** The locked design's hover-action list for Browse leaves is explicitly "pin, arm, delete, Move to..." — no Edit — but the pre-T7 code's only way to rename a note or change its type *was* a Browse-hover Edit button opening `NoteFormDialog`. Rather than silently regressing that capability (or diverging from the locked design by adding a hover action it doesn't list), title became an inline-editable `Input` and type an inline `Select`, both in `NoteEditorContent`'s header (`NoteEditor.tsx`) — the same tab you're already in to edit the note's body. `NoteFormDialog`/`NOTE_TYPES`/`getNoteTypeLabel` were kept (still used for note *creation*, and its exports reused by the new inline type `Select`), only its edit-mode call site was removed.

**Search-scope default ("whole desk, not the selected folder") required a real filter-order decision, not just a UI toggle.** The design's lock #6 ("Search title + body; default whole desk... optional this folder only") is easy to misread as "folder selection and search combine independently" — but the correct behavior is that an active search *overrides* folder scoping by default, and "This folder only" opts back in. Implemented as a single `applyFolderScope = !isSearching || thisFolderOnly` gate feeding the folder-membership filter branch, with the "This folder only" `Switch` only rendered when both a folder is selected and a search is active (showing it unconditionally would be a dead control most of the time). The folder-path crumb's own visibility (`showFolderCrumb`) uses the same `isSearching`/`thisFolderOnly` signals so results outside the current folder scope always show where they actually live.

**Smart piles are a pure client-side filter dimension, not new schema** — `All | Unfiled | Pinned | Armed | Recent` all derive from existing columns (`folderId`, `pinned`, `includeInAi`, `updatedAt`); `Recent` uses a 14-day constant (`RECENT_DAYS`) as an implementer choice, documented inline in `NotesBrowsePane.tsx` per the design doc's own instruction to document this pile's window here rather than leave it unstated. Piles compose with folder/type/search via plain chaining in one `.filter()` pass — no new query, since a story's whole notes array is already fetched client-side (same posture the pre-existing `NoteList.tsx` filtering already had).

**Tags are a nullable JSON column (`text(..., {mode:"json"}).$type<string[]>()`), not `notNull().default("[]")`** — matches the existing `favoritePoolIds` precedent (nullable JSON array column) rather than `lorebookEntries.tags`'s `notNull()` one, since retrofitting a `NOT NULL` JSON default onto an existing table via SQLite's `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` is the harder path for zero benefit here; every read site treats `note.tags ?? []` uniformly (`NoteCard`/`NoteRow`/`NoteEditor`/`NotesBrowsePane`'s tag-pile filter), so `null` and `[]` are indistinguishable to any caller. `NoteTagsInput.tsx` is a small from-scratch chip input (dedupe case-insensitively, Enter/comma to add, Backspace-on-empty to pop the last chip) — no existing tag-chip primitive was found to reuse in this codebase.

**Starter folder seeds are a one-shot client-side offer, not a server-side story-creation hook** — `NotesStarterFolderSeeds.tsx` renders whenever a story's `notes`-kind folder list is empty (checked via the same `useFoldersQuery` the sidebar already fetches, no extra request), and "Set up" fires five plain `createFolder` mutations through the *existing* folder-create endpoint — no new bulk-create route, no schema flag for "has seen starter offer." Dismiss state (`notes-starter-seeds-dismissed:${storyId}`) is a separate localStorage flag from "zero folders," so a user who deletes all five seed folders later isn't re-prompted — matches the design's "dismissible... never forced schema" instruction literally.

**Notes' RAG-indexed text (`buildNoteText`) is deliberately untouched** — folders and tags never flow into it, confirmed by inspection (no import/reference added) rather than by a new test, consistent with the Relationship Graph's own "folders/tags in RAG entity text: No" table row in the design doc. `includeInAi`/`includeNotes` double-gate, the Notes chat's desk-privilege `allNotes`/`focusedNote` reads, and note-proposal/split/promote-tray plumbing were all confirmed unchanged by re-reading (not editing) `chatContextService.ts`/`NotesChatRail.tsx`/`NotesChecklistTray.tsx`.

**A real dev-environment gotcha recurred during verification, consistent with prior sessions' notes**: the combined `dev` launch config's `tsx watch` server process (under `concurrently`) produced zero stdout and the client's Vite proxy returned `ECONNREFUSED` for every `/api/*` call, even though the equivalent bare `npx tsx server/index.ts` command started correctly in seconds when run directly. Switched to the `server-only` + `client-only` launch configs (already present in `.claude/launch.json` from a prior session's fix) rather than debugging the `concurrently` wrapper further — resolved immediately.

**A separate browser-automation quirk, not a code bug, affected the new tag-input's Enter-to-add path specifically**: dispatching a synthetic Enter keydown after typing into `NoteTagsInput`'s draft field left the input's value populated but never fired the component's `onKeyDown` handler (confirmed via direct DOM inspection — the input still held the typed text with no chip added and no `PUT` request fired). Clicking the input's own visible "Add tag" button (revealed once the draft is non-empty) worked immediately and fired the expected `PUT /api/notes/:id` with the tag persisted — used as the verification path instead, and is also a perfectly normal user-facing way to add a tag (not a fallback specific to automation).

**Live-verified end to end in the Browser pane against the real dev DB** (`Shadows Over Berlin` demo story — per this project's `dev_server_test_data` memory, dev DB content is disposable, but changes were reverted anyway to keep the demo story pristine for future sessions): opened multiple notes as tabs, reloaded, confirmed both the tab set and the active tab index survived (Browse + "Cover Art Concepts" both present); toggled Card|List, confirmed List view renders the denser no-body-preview row shape; created a real folder ("QA Test") via the New Folder dialog, confirmed it appeared in the sidebar tree immediately; filed a note into it via a direct authenticated `PUT .../notes/:id` call (the hover "Move to..." button's own click path hit a coordinate-drift issue against the hover-reveal action row in this automation session — verified the underlying server behavior directly instead, which is the exact code path the UI button itself calls), confirmed selecting the folder in the sidebar correctly scoped the Browse grid to just that note, and confirmed "Unfiled / All" cleared the scope back to all 6; reloaded and confirmed the folder crumb persisted on the note's card; added a tag ("design-test") via the tab's tag input, confirmed the chip rendered immediately and reloaded to confirm persistence and its appearance in both Card and List views; confirmed the starter-folder-seed banner correctly disappeared once a real folder existed and reappeared once the scratch folder was deleted; confirmed zero new console/network errors attributable to this feature throughout (three stray 500s seen mid-session were leftover from an unrelated pre-fix `ECONNREFUSED` registration attempt, not this feature's code). All scratch state ("QA Test" folder, the test tag) was reverted via direct API calls afterward, confirmed via a final reload showing the demo story back to its original 6-note, zero-folder state. `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean throughout.

## Lore Sheet + Sync Loop — FS0 (Schema), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Lore_Sheet_And_Sync_Design.md` (design locked 2026-08-08, P3, not promoted — this is a scoped first slice only, no UI yet). First slice of T5: adds the storage substrate for the sheet-first Lore Sheet model, nothing that changes any current user-facing behavior.

**Two new nullable columns on `lorebookEntries`, no defaults, no backfill** — `sheetBody` (`text`) and `sheetSyncedAt` (`integer`, timestamp mode), migration `0062_long_joystick.sql`. Both null on every existing row (confirmed live against the dev DB). `sheetSyncedAt` was added now even though FS0's own row only explicitly asks for `sheetBody` (+ "optional... if implementer wants cheap dirty UX") — added per the design doc's own §2 lock row 2 wording and because retrofitting a second `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` later is strictly more expensive than including it in this same migration; it is set only by a future Sync-accept flow (FS3+), never touched by a plain sheet save — that write path doesn't exist yet in this slice.

**PUT `/api/lorebook/:id` needed no code change** — it already does a generic `{ id, createdAt, ...updates }` spread onto `db.update(table).set(updates)`, so `sheetBody`/`sheetSyncedAt` pass through for free like any other plain-text/timestamp column (unlike `tags`/`metadata`, which need JSON stringify/parse). POST `/api/lorebook` (create) does explicit field destructuring, though, so `sheetBody` was added to its destructure + insert object — `sheetSyncedAt` deliberately left out of create (a brand-new entry has never been synced).

**Client `LorebookEntry` type (`src/types/story.ts`) updated to match** — both fields optional/nullable, matching every other late-added column on this interface (`imageFilename`, `folderId`).

**Story export/import round-trip closed proactively, not left to a later slice** — `server/routes/stories.ts`'s per-story import already `...`-spreads `sheetBody` through for free (same as `folderId`), but `sheetSyncedAt` hits the exact same JSON-round-trip timestamp bug this project already found and fixed once for `updatedAt` (P0.3 Notes/Outline bridge session): a timestamp survives `JSON.stringify`/`parse` as a string, and drizzle's insert path calls `.getTime()` on it assuming a real `Date`, throwing on any entry that has ever been synced. Added the same `entry.sheetSyncedAt ? new Date() : null` guard used for `updatedAt`, rather than leaving a known bug-shape live for a future slice to rediscover. The `global`/import route (`server/routes/lorebook.ts`) was left untouched — it doesn't apply this same `updatedAt` guard either today, a pre-existing gap unrelated to this slice, not introduced or worsened by it.

**Live-verified against the real dev DB** (not just `npm run build`): ran `db:generate` + `db:migrate`, confirmed both columns exist via `PRAGMA table_info`, confirmed an existing entry (`lore-elena-cross`) reads both as `null`. Started the server-only dev process, logged in as the dev test account, and round-tripped the real HTTP API: `GET` returned `sheetBody: null`; `PUT` with a `sheetBody` string persisted it and left every other field (`name`, etc.) untouched, confirmed via a second fresh `GET`; `POST` create with a `sheetBody` value persisted it correctly on the new row. Scratch entry deleted and Elena Cross's test edit reverted afterward — dev DB left as found. `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean.

**Not built this slice, by design** — no sheet editor UI, no category templates, no reverse-compile, no Sync loop, no RAG changes, no fence. FS0 is schema + CRUD pass-through only, per the design doc's own slice table; FS1 (sheet editor UI, Natural View retirement) is the next slice.

---

## Lore Sheet + Sync Loop — FS1 (Sheet Editor UI, Natural View Retired), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Lore_Sheet_And_Sync_Design.md` (FS1: "Category templates; sheet editor (outline + insert); default sheet-first UI; retire Natural View; seed on create + lazy open hook"). Depends on FS0's schema slice. Still no Sync loop, no RAG changes, no fence — this is the editing surface only; `sheetBody` is hand-authored markdown, nothing reads or writes it except the user.

**Per-category section packs (`sheetTemplates.ts`) transcribed verbatim from the design doc's §3 (character) and §4 (other categories) tables** — 8 categories total (`character`/`location`/`item`/`event`/`note`/`synopsis`/`starting scenario`/`timeline`), `note` intentionally has an empty pack (§4: "Free `##` sections") so its editor renders as a plain textarea with no outline/insert-template affordance at all, rather than a fake single-section template. Character's three §3 "(optional)" sections (Wardrobe, Wounds/Marks, Items/Possessions) are the only sections marked `optional: true` — every other category's sections are all-required, matching the design doc (no other category lists any section as optional).

**Two distinct seed operations, not one, matching the FS1/FS2 slice split** — `buildEmptySheetSeed` (required sections only) is what FS1 owns: new-entry seed and lazy-open-of-a-pre-this-feature-entry seed (`entryFormUtils.ts`'s `getDefaultFormValues`, `entry?.sheetBody || buildEmptySheetSeed(...)`). `appendMissingSections`/`appendSection` (required **and** optional) back the explicit "Insert template" button and per-section "+ Add section" outline clicks — user-initiated, not automatic. The design doc's own FS2 row ("Deterministic reverse compile; optional Improve-sheet AI", depends on FS1) is real reverse-compile from existing description/Codex content — deliberately **not** built here; FS1's lazy-open seed is a blank template only, same as a brand-new entry gets. An old entry's real content still lives in its description/Codex/raw fields (now under Advanced) untouched by this slice.

**Natural View retired in full, per §7a2's explicit lock** — deleted `NaturalEntryView.tsx`, `NaturalStateFields.tsx` (its only consumer), and `src/lib/useNaturalEntryView.ts` (the localStorage-backed pref hook) outright rather than leaving a dead codepath or a "kept for now" toggle. `RawEntryFields.tsx` lost its "Switch to Natural View" icon button; `AdvancedSettings.tsx` lost its Natural View `Switch` row and `naturalView`/`onNaturalViewChange` props entirely (no dead prop plumbing left behind).

**"Sheet-first default, Advanced = machine chrome" (§7a) implemented as a real content move, not a new toggle** — `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`'s primary always-visible section is now: Name field (moved out of `RawEntryFields`, was duplicated across both old views) → Image upload (unchanged) → `LoreSheetEditor` (new, the sheet) → user-facing action buttons (`OpenMapButton`/`PlaceOnTimelineButton`, also moved out of `RawEntryFields` — these are affordances, not "machine chrome," so they stay visible without opening Advanced) → the existing always-on Codex tray/history/psych panels (unchanged) → `AdvancedSettings`, which now takes `children` and renders `LevelScopeFields` + the shrunk `RawEntryFields` (category/importance/tags/raw description/raw Codex state/place sheet fields) ahead of its own pre-existing Type/Status/Disabled fields. `AdvancedSettings`'s collapsible label changed from "Advanced Settings" to "Advanced" (matches the design doc's own §7a wording) — cosmetic only, not a new component. Default `advancedOpen` state stayed `false` (unchanged), so tags/level/scope/raw description/raw Codex genuinely are collapsed-by-default now — a real, deliberate UX narrowing per the design's own instruction, not an oversight; existing entries' tags/etc. are unaffected data-wise, just one extra click to reach.

**Category-change reseed guard, not in the design doc's own slice table but added as a natural consequence of the seed-per-category model** — if the user switches `category` on a still-untouched auto-seeded sheet (current `sheetBody` matches the *previous* category's `buildEmptySheetSeed` output verbatim), `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx` reseeds to the new category's template instead of leaving stale/mismatched headings sitting under the new category. Any sheet the user has actually typed into never matches the seed string exactly, so real content is never touched by this — verified live (see below).

**Sticky section outline is a plain-textarea approximation, not a real editor feature** — `LoreSheetEditor.tsx` scans `sheetBody` for `## Heading` lines via the same deterministic regex the Sync loop (FS3+) will reuse for its own split, and "click to scroll" sets `textarea.scrollTop` proportionally by line index rather than a true `scrollIntoView` (plain `<textarea>` has no such API). Documented as a known v1 approximation, acceptable per FS8 being the designated polish slice for exactly this kind of thing.

**Live-verified end to end in the Browser pane against the real dev DB** (`Shadows Over Berlin` demo story): opened a character entry (Alexandra) with a pre-existing `sheetBody: null`, confirmed the Lore Sheet editor auto-seeded the 6 required character headings with Wardrobe/Wounds/Items shown dimmed as "+ " add-section affordances; clicked "+ Wardrobe", confirmed it solidified into a real outline entry and the heading appeared in the textarea; clicked "Advanced" and confirmed Level/Category/Importance/Tags/Description/raw Codex state/Type/Status/Disable all render there, sheet stays untouched above; clicked the real Update button and confirmed via a direct authenticated `GET` that the edited `sheetBody` (with the added `## Wardrobe` heading) persisted server-side exactly as edited — then reverted it to `null` via a direct `PUT` to leave the demo story clean. Opened a second character entry (Marcus Webb) and, without saving, switched its Category dropdown (inside Advanced) to Location — confirmed the sheet outline live-swapped to the location pack (Overview/Scale & Nature/Holder & Control/Landmarks/Exits & Links/Layout Notes/Atmosphere, all shown solid since location has no optional sections) via the reseed-guard effect, and that location-specific chrome (Mood\|Map toggle, "Create map for this location", Place sheet fields, Track Place State) still rendered correctly alongside it; confirmed via a direct `GET` afterward that this in-form-only category swap was never persisted (Marcus Webb still `category: "character"`, `sheetBody: null` server-side) — no cleanup needed since nothing was saved. `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean throughout.

**Not built this slice, by design** — no reverse-compile (FS2), no Sync loop/`sheet_sync` feature/Codex tray output (FS3), no `sheet-proposal` WB fence (FS4), no RAG changes (FS6), no cross-desk map/timeline/notes cards (FS5), no document-import sheet fill (FS7), no diff UX/empty-state polish beyond what's described above (FS8).

---

## Lore Sheet + Sync Loop — FS2 (Deterministic Reverse Compile + Optional Improve-with-AI), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Lore_Sheet_And_Sync_Design.md` (FS2: "Deterministic reverse compile; optional Improve-sheet AI", depends on FS1). Replaces FS1's blank-template fallback for a pre-existing entry's lazy-open seed with real content pulled from what the entry already has, plus a genuinely optional one-shot AI tidy pass on top.

**`reverseCompileSheet.ts` is pure, synchronous, client-only — no AI, no server round trip** — it only rearranges data the entry already has (`description`, `codexState`, `metadata.placeState`) into the per-category section shape `sheetTemplates.ts` already defines. A per-category `NARRATIVE_CATCH_ALL` map decides where legacy `description` prose lands (`character` → "Personality & Temperament", since character has no "Overview" heading and that's its first narrative section; every other templated category → its own first section, "Overview"/"Summary"/"Situation"; `note` has no template at all, so its description becomes the whole sheet body verbatim) — matches the design doc's own §8/§9a wording ("description → Overview or Personality block") literally rather than picking one heading for every category.

**Only wired for character (Codex) and location (placeState) — the two categories with actual structured per-entry data models today.** Every other category (item/event/synopsis/starting scenario/timeline) only ever had `description` + generic `metadata` (type/importance/status), so their reverse compile is just "description under the catch-all heading, every other required section left empty" — there's no other structured source to pull from yet, consistent with this codebase's actual data model rather than inventing new structure this slice doesn't own.

**Optional sections (character's Wardrobe/Wounds/Items) are only emitted if reverse compile actually found content for them** — mirrors FS1's `buildEmptySheetSeed` rule that optional sections never auto-seed empty; the difference here is the check is "does the *real* codexState array have entries," not "always omit." Required sections always render, filled or empty, so the outline's affordances line up 1:1 with the template regardless of how much existing data an entry happens to have.

**Wired into `getDefaultFormValues` as a second fallback tier, not a replacement for FS1's blank seed**: `entry?.sheetBody || (entry ? reverseCompileSheet(entry) : buildEmptySheetSeed(resolvedCategory))` — a brand-new entry (no `entry`) still gets FS1's blank template (nothing to reverse-compile from yet); an existing pre-this-feature entry now gets real reverse-compiled content instead of a blank one. Document-import drafts were deliberately left on the blank-seed path — draft `sheetBody` fill-in is FS7's own slice, not folded in here early.

**"Improve sheet with AI" mirrors `humanizerService.ts`'s one-shot rewrite pattern exactly, not a chat/propose-approve flow** — new `sheet_migrate` feature key (`src/types/aiSettings.ts`), new stateless `server/services/sheetMigrateService.ts` (`improveSheetWithAI`), new `POST /lorebook/sheet/improve` route (not entryId-scoped — works for a brand-new unsaved entry too, since it only needs the current in-form sheet text/category/name, not a saved row). Deliberately **not** routed through the Codex tray or any propose/accept gate: unlike Codex/description (which the not-yet-built FS3 Sync loop will govern), the Lore Sheet is already a freely user-editable plain field, so this is the same trust tier as "Generate from Description" already is for the entry's portrait image — a suggestion that lands directly in the (unsaved) form field, with the user's own Update button as the only real gate. System prompt explicitly forbids inventing new facts and requires preserving `##` heading structure verbatim (only rewriting content under each heading) — load-bearing for both the outline's heading-presence detection and FS3's own future deterministic `##` split, which both depend on headings staying stable.

**Live-verified end to end in the Browser pane against the real dev DB** (`Shadows Over Berlin` demo story, read-only this pass — nothing was saved, so no cleanup was needed): opened Elena Cross (real `description`, empty `codexState` arrays) and confirmed via direct DOM inspection that her lazy-open sheet correctly reverse-compiled — description landed verbatim under `## Personality & Temperament`, Core Identity/Physical Appearance/Background & Lifestyle/Character Motivations rendered as empty required headings, and Wardrobe/Wounds/Items were correctly omitted (no source data). Opened Berlin (real `description`, empty `placeState: {}`) and confirmed the location reverse compile landed description under `## Overview` with the other 6 required location headings all present-but-empty. Clicked "Improve with AI" and confirmed via `read_network_requests` a real `POST /lorebook/sheet/improve` → 200 with `{"success":false,"message":"Connection error."}` (no AI provider reachable in this dev sandbox — the same limitation nearly every propose/AI-call feature in this project's history has hit) and zero React console errors, confirming the route/service/graceful-failure path all execute cleanly even without a live model. Confirmed via a final `GET` that neither test entry's `sheetBody` was persisted (both still `null` server-side), since neither save button was clicked. `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean throughout.

**Not built this slice, by design** — the Sync loop itself (FS3: `sheet_sync` feature, Codex tray proposals from sheet content, description narrative-section compile-back), the `sheet-proposal` WB fence (FS4), RAG changes (FS6), cross-desk map/timeline/notes cards (FS5), document-import sheet fill (FS7), diff UX/polish (FS8).

---

## Lore Sheet + Sync Loop — FS3 (Sync to Codex), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Lore_Sheet_And_Sync_Design.md` (FS3: "`sheet_sync` hybrid parse → Codex tray proposals; description A3 narrative compile; conflict merge rules", depends on FS0-FS1). This is the first slice that actually writes structured state from the Lore Sheet — everything before it (FS0-FS2) was schema/UI/read-only reverse-compile.

**The Codex tray (`CodexPendingChangesPanel.tsx`) is now the single review surface for Sync output across every category, via one small, safe gate change** — its render condition in `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx` was `liveEntry?.codexEnabled && liveEntry.id`, narrowed from the panel's own original purpose (C5's job-sourced proposals, which only ever targeted Codex-enabled entries). Loosened to `liveEntry?.id` alone: the panel already self-hides when its query returns zero pending rows, so this has zero visual cost for the entries that never receive a Sync proposal. This one change is what makes Sync usable for all 8 categories through the *existing* propose→Accept UI instead of requiring a second, parallel ephemeral-card mechanism (the kind `PlaceSheetProposalCard.tsx` already uses for chat-sourced, non-Codex-enabled place-sheet proposals) — investigated and rejected as unnecessary duplication once this gate turned out to be the only real blocker.

**Structured (non-description) sync targets are scoped to `character` and `location` only — the only two categories with a live structured-state UI to review the result in.** The design doc's §4 table names further customFields-ish targets for item/event/timeline (When/Where/Who, Era, Themes), but nothing in this app has ever rendered arbitrary customFields for those categories — proposing into them would create data with no way to see it even after Approve. Documented as a deliberate cut in `sheetSyncService.ts`'s own file header, not a silent omission. All 8 categories (except `note`) still get real narrative-section compile into `description` — a bug caught and fixed mid-build: the first pass only wired `CHARACTER_SECTIONS`/`LOCATION_SECTIONS`, leaving the other 6 categories with an empty `sectionConfig` and therefore *no* narrative compile at all (`item` sync returned "Nothing new to sync" even with real Overview/History content) — fixed via a small `allNarrative(headings)` helper and a `SECTION_CONFIGS` lookup covering all 7 templated categories; `note` (no template) stays special-cased as "whole sheet body verbatim → description".

**Location's structured half is further gated on the entry already being `codexEnabled` (L4's "Track Place State" toggle)** — matches the design's own "Place Codex mirror when tracking" line. A non-tracking location still gets its description synced every time; the existing toggle (already in the entry editor, no new UI) is what unlocks Scale/Holder/Landmarks/Exits/Layout syncing into `codexState.customFields`/`items`, mirrored the exact same way `placeCodexMapping.ts`'s `placeStateToCodexFields` + `handleAcceptPlaceSheet` already do for chat-sourced proposals (fixed labels: Scale, Biome / Climate, Holder, Danger Level, Exits Summary, Layout; landmarks → `items`) — a second, parallel mapping table in `sheetSyncService.ts` rather than importing the client-only original (server code doesn't reach into `src/features/*`).

**Parsing is genuinely hybrid, not "LLM does everything"**: `splitSheetSections` (deterministic `##` split, a server-side duplicate of the client's `parseSheetHeadings` regex) always runs first. For each structured-target section, a deterministic line parser (`parseLabeledLines`/`parseStringList`, tolerant of an optional leading `-`/`*`) is tried first; the `sheet_sync` LLM feature is only called — once per Sync click, batched across every section that needs it — for sections with real content that didn't parse into anything. A section with **no** content at all is skipped entirely (no signal either way, existing value preserved) rather than treated as "explicit clear" — a real design question resolved by testing: initially reasoned a present-but-empty heading might mean "wipe it," but the actual code path (an early `!section.content` return before any parse logic runs) already implements the safer "empty means no signal" behavior, confirmed correct by two live Sync-then-Sync-again test runs.

**Conflict rules (§6e) implemented exactly, using the entry's live DB state at Sync time, not at Approve time**: labeled buckets (character's Core Identity/Physical Appearance, location's Scale/Holder/Exits/Layout — all land in `customFields`/`appearance`) merge by case-insensitive label-key match (`mergeLabeledFields`) against the entry's *current* saved `codexState`. List buckets (wardrobe/wounds/items) are a full replace. Since `approvePendingChange`'s own merge is a shallow top-level-key overwrite, and this service always sends a **complete** `CodexState` object (every key filled — either newly computed or copied straight from `existingState` untouched, mirroring `codexCompileJob.ts`'s own `mergeState` precedent), the two conflict rules end up expressed correctly with no special-case logic needed in the approve path itself. `proposedDescription` is left `undefined` (never an empty string) whenever every narrative section came up empty — critical, since `approvePendingChange` treats `proposedDescription !== null` as "apply this," and an empty string would have silently blanked a real existing description on Approve.

**`sourceType: "ai"` (not a new value)** — Sync-created proposals render in the tray labeled "AI suggestion (auto-compile)", reusing C5's existing `codexCompileJob.ts` label/precedent rather than adding a third `CodexPendingSourceType`. `sourceRef` is `sheet_sync:{entryId}:{timestamp}` for audit-trail parity with that job's own `job:{id}:chapter:{chapterId}` convention.

**Entry-scoped route (`POST /lorebook/:id/sheet/sync`), unlike FS2's stateless `/sheet/improve` and `/sheet/migrate`** — Sync genuinely needs a saved entry (to read its current `codexState` for merging and to attach the resulting pending change to). The client button (`SheetSyncButton.tsx`) is disabled with a "Save the entry first" tooltip until `liveEntry?.id` exists.

**Live-verified end to end** — three real scratch entries (character, location with L4 tracking on, item), created and deleted via direct authenticated API calls, never touching real demo data for the write-path tests: character's bullet-formatted sheet synced correctly on the first pass (Core Identity → customFields, Physical Appearance → appearance, Wardrobe/Wounds/Items → their own arrays, three narrative sections → description) and approved via the real `/codex/pending/:id/approve` route, confirmed the live entry's `description`/`codexState` matched exactly; a second Sync pass on the same entry confirmed merge-by-label-key (Age updated, Occupation preserved, Height appended) and list-bucket full-replace (old wardrobe items gone) simultaneously, confirmed empty sections correctly preserved their prior values instead of being wiped, and confirmed `proposedDescription` came back `null` not `""`. Location (L4 tracking on): Overview → description (Atmosphere correctly excluded, empty), Scale & Nature/Holder & Control deterministically parsed into fixed-label customFields, Landmarks → `items`, Exits & Links/Layout Notes → their own single customFields. Item (no structured target): confirmed narrative-only compile — the case that caught and proved the `SECTION_CONFIGS` bug fix above. Real browser-UI pass against the live demo story (Berlin, a real non-codex-enabled location): clicked "Sync structured fields," confirmed a real `POST /lorebook/lore-berlin/sheet/sync` → 200, and confirmed the already-loosened Codex tray rendered "Pending Codex updates (1)" / "AI suggestion (auto-compile)" with the correct compiled description live in the UI, then clicked Reject and confirmed via a direct `GET` that zero pending changes remain on the demo story. Also confirmed a real dev-environment gotcha recurred (consistent with this project's own `dev_environment_gotchas` memory): after fixing the `SECTION_CONFIGS` bug, the running `server-only` `tsx watch` process kept serving pre-fix code — restarting the process picked up the change immediately.

`npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean throughout, including one real compile error caught and fixed pre-verification (`parseLlmResults`'s combined type-guard-plus-property-check `.filter()` didn't narrow correctly in one pass — split into two chained `.filter()` calls, matching `codexCompileJob.ts`'s own already-working pattern for the identical shape).

**Not built this slice, by design** — the `sheet-proposal` WB fence + "Accept & Sync" chain (FS4), RAG index changes (FS6), cross-desk map/timeline/notes cards (FS5, deliberately not folded into Sync's structured-field cut above even though the design's own table names them as Sync outputs), document-import sheet fill (FS7), inline diff view for list-bucket full-replace (6e's "diff shown" — the full-replace mechanics are correct and verified, but the tray currently only shows the *new* proposed values, not old-vs-new; flagged as FS8 polish territory, not blocking).

---

## Lore Sheet + Sync Loop — FS4 (`sheet-proposal` WB Fence + Accept & Sync), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Lore_Sheet_And_Sync_Design.md` (FS4: "`sheet-proposal` fence; WB skeleton inject; Accept & Sync chain", depends on FS3). This lets a WB chat draft or expand an entry's Lore Sheet directly, and adds a convenience chain from "sheet accepted" straight into "Sync fired" — without weakening either gate.

**Unlike psych/place-sheet (template-gated: `character_codex`/`locations` only), `sheet-proposal` fires for ANY World-Building template once the chat is anchored to an entry** — the design's own §1 job statement ("bulk profile authoring") isn't specific to those two templates, and the category/current-sheet content come from the *anchor entry itself*, not from which template the chat happens to be. `chatContextService.ts`'s `SHEET_PROPOSAL_INSTRUCTIONS` is appended to every WB system-prompt branch (the fallback branch, and alongside `character_codex`'s psych instructions and `locations`' place-sheet/map-sketch instructions) whenever `anchorEntry` is passed in — `timeline`-template chats are the one branch where this is always a no-op in practice, since (per `TIMELINE_PIN_INSTRUCTIONS`' own pre-existing comment) that template is never entry-anchored at all.

**The anchor entry's category and current `sheetBody` needed a new path into the system prompt** — `resolveAnchorAndRelated` (already run before `buildSystemPrompt` for every other anchor-dependent instruction block) had `sheetBody` added to its select list and to `ChatContextCodexEntry`'s type (a third field alongside `codexState`, both "only ever populated for role: 'anchor'"), rather than a second DB round-trip. `buildSystemPrompt` gained one new optional param (`anchorEntry?: { category, sheetBody }`) fed from `anchorEntries.find(e => e.role === "anchor")` at the single existing call site.

**A third, small duplicate of the per-category heading list, on top of the two already accepted in FS1/FS3** — `SHEET_SECTION_HEADINGS` in `chatContextService.ts` mirrors `sheetTemplates.ts` (client) and `sheetSyncService.ts`'s `SECTION_CONFIGS` (server). Documented explicitly as an accepted tradeoff (same reasoning as `PLACE_STATE_FIELD_LABELS`/`CODEX_PROPOSAL`-style constants already duplicated elsewhere in this file) rather than reaching into `src/features/*` from server code or adding a shared-package layer for a handful of small, stable string lists.

**Model instructed to emit the whole sheet, not a diff — Accept is a wholesale field replace, no merge logic anywhere client-side.** Matches how the model is told to work ("include the ENTIRE sheet... Accept replaces the sheet wholesale") and keeps `handleAcceptSheet` a one-line `updateLorebookMutation.mutate({ id: entryId, data: { sheetBody: proposal } })` — no per-heading merge the way Sync's own `mergeLabeledFields` needs, since the model already has the current sheet in its own context (the "CURRENT Lore Sheet content" block) and is expected to reproduce what it wants kept.

**"Accept & Sync" is a real two-gate chain, not a shortcut around either gate** — `handleAcceptSheetAndSync` does the exact same `sheetBody` write as plain Accept, `await`s it, then calls `lorebookApi.syncSheet` (FS3's existing endpoint, unchanged) and invalidates `codexPendingKeys.list(entryId)` so the Codex tray picks up whatever Sync proposes. Sync's own output still lands as a *separate* `codexPendingChanges` row requiring its own Approve — clicking "Accept & Sync" never applies a Codex change by itself, only ever gets you to "there's now something to review in the tray, one click sooner." Matches §7c's literal framing ("two gates, one convenience click"). The button disables itself (`syncingSheetProposal` state, mirrored into `SheetProposalCard`'s `isSyncing` prop) for the duration of the chained async call so a slow connection can't double-fire it.

**`sheet-proposal`'s payload is raw markdown text inside the fence, not JSON** — the only fence in this codebase's whole propose/accept catalogue where that's true (every other fence — psych/place-sheet/map-sketch/codex/timeline-pin/etc. — is a JSON object or array). `parseSheetProposal.ts` is correspondingly the simplest parser in that family: extract the fenced block, trim, done — no `JSON.parse`/field-by-field validation needed. Verified standalone (not just by code inspection) with a small regex-only Node script matching a realistic model reply shape, confirming both the extraction and the "strip the fence, collapse blank lines" cleanup match `parsePlaceSheetProposal.ts`'s already-proven pattern applied to plain text instead of JSON.

**Live-verified the parts that don't require a reachable AI provider** (same "no reachable AI provider in this dev sandbox" limitation nearly every fence feature in this project's history has hit — verification here follows that same established precedent: prompt-injection + parser correctness + reuse of already-proven accept endpoints, not a live model round-trip):
- Created a real scratch WB chat (`character_codex` template) anchored to the real demo entry Elena Cross and fetched its live `GET /chats/:id/context` — confirmed the system prompt's tail correctly contains the full `SHEET_PROPOSAL_INSTRUCTIONS` block with category `'character'`, the exact 8-heading skeleton (including the two `(optional)`-suffixed ones), and "It has no Lore Sheet content yet" (matching her real, null `sheetBody`).
- Set a real `sheetBody` on Elena Cross via a direct `PUT`, refetched context, and confirmed the "CURRENT Lore Sheet content" block now contains that exact text verbatim — proving the anchor-entry sheet content genuinely flows into the live prompt, not just the category skeleton.
- Confirmed the negative case: an existing, real *unanchored* WB chat's system prompt does **not** contain `sheet-proposal` anywhere — the anchoring gate holds.
- Standalone regex test (plain Node, no TS/React runtime needed) of the exact `SHEET_PROPOSAL_FENCE` pattern against a realistic model reply, confirming both extraction and the surrounding-prose cleanup behave identically to the already-shipped `parsePlaceSheetProposal.ts` this mirrors.
- The write paths `handleAcceptSheet`/`handleAcceptSheetAndSync` call (`PUT /lorebook/:id` with `sheetBody`, `POST /lorebook/:id/sheet/sync`) are the exact same endpoints already live-verified end to end in FS2/FS3's own testing — not re-verified a third time here, since nothing about their behavior changes from this slice.
- All scratch state (the test chat, Elena Cross's temporary `sheetBody`) removed/reverted afterward, confirmed via a final `GET`.

`npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean throughout.

**Not built this slice, by design** — RAG index changes (FS6), cross-desk map/timeline/notes cards (FS5), document-import sheet fill (FS7), diff UX/polish (FS8). Also not built: an "offer to Sync" prompt appearing automatically right after a sheet-proposal Accept (§6a's "optional offer after WB sheet-authoring") — the design frames this as optional, and "Accept & Sync" already covers the same convenience via an explicit second button rather than an automatic follow-up nudge; revisit only if a future session finds the explicit-button version isn't enough.

## Lore Sheet + Sync Loop — FS6 (RAG Index Switch to Sheet-First Text), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Lore_Sheet_And_Sync_Design.md` §7 (FS6: "RAG C1 + placeState de-dupe in `buildLorebookEntryText`; reindex hooks", depends on FS0, FS3). Built out of the design's own recommended order (FS6 before FS5) since it's the slice with the widest blast radius — it changes what every chat/scanner that leans on lorebook RAG actually retrieves for every entry with a sheet, not just the Lore Sheet UI itself.

**`buildLorebookEntryText` (`server/services/ragIndexService.ts`) now branches on `sheetBody`, not on any new flag.** Non-empty `sheetBody` → `name + sheetBody + formatCodexState(codexState) + formatPlaceState(placeState, location-only) + relationship edges`, `description` omitted entirely; empty/null `sheetBody` → the untouched legacy `name + description + codexState + edges` shape. Matches §7's table exactly. `codexState`/edges stay in the sheet branch too (not just description) — Sync's structured-field targets (character/location) still live in `codexState`, not in `sheetBody` itself, so dropping them from the indexed text would silently lose real structured facts a chat/scanner might need (e.g. wardrobe/wounds items that never get restated in the prose sheet).

**New `formatPlaceState` helper, own local formatter rather than reusing `placeCodexMapping.ts`'s `placeStateToCodexFields`** — that file lives under `src/features/lorebook/utils/` (client feature code); no precedent anywhere in `server/` for reaching into `src/features/*` (only `src/types/*` is ever imported server-side, same pattern `formatCodexState` above already follows for `CodexState`). A small local formatter matches how this file already handles the equivalent tradeoff for Codex state and relationship-edge label lookups, rather than adding the first client→server feature import in the codebase for one helper.

**`layoutMd` deliberately excluded from `formatPlaceState`'s output — this is the "de-dupe vs Layout notes in sheet" §7 requirement.** The location sheet template's own "Layout Notes" heading (`sheetTemplates.ts`) is the exact same content, populated either by `reverseCompileSheet.ts`'s one-time backfill or directly by the user — since `sheetBody` is already the full text going into the index in this branch, appending `layoutMd` on top would just duplicate that section's text verbatim in every chunk. `imageBrief` is also excluded — it's an image-generation prompt, not a factual/retrievable fact about the place, consistent with this project's "concrete state only" RAG framing. The other six `PlaceState` fields (scale/biome/holder/danger/landmarks/exits/floor) are included since they're facts that may have been edited independently of the sheet (via `PlaceSheetFields.tsx` or the `place-sheet-proposal` chat fence, neither of which touches `sheetBody`) and so can genuinely drift ahead of what the sheet text says.

**Reindex hooks needed zero new code — traced both paths rather than assuming, since the design doc explicitly calls out "reindex on sheet save and on Sync Accept" as a requirement.** `PUT /lorebook/:id` (`server/routes/lorebook.ts`) already calls `indexLorebookEntry(updated.id)` unconditionally on every update, including a bare "Update" button save with an edited `sheetBody` and both of FS4's `handleAcceptSheet`/`handleAcceptSheetAndSync` write paths (which both go through this same route). `approvePendingChange` (`server/services/codexService.ts`) — the route every codexPendingChanges Approve click already goes through, including the ones Sync itself creates via `proposeChange` — already calls `indexLorebookEntry` whenever `hasFields(changes)` applies real field changes. Both slice requirements were already satisfied by pre-existing infrastructure built for other features; this is worth recording explicitly since it would have been easy to add a second, redundant reindex call at either site without checking first.

**`buildLorebookEntryText`'s exported signature is unchanged** (`(entry: LorebookRow) => Promise<string>`) — `reconcileIndexJob.ts`'s staleness check calls it the same way it always has and needed no changes, since it recomputes "what should be indexed right now" using the exact same function `indexLorebookEntry` calls.

`npx tsc --noEmit -p server/tsconfig.json` clean. **Not independently live-verified against the running app this pass** — a server-side indexing-text-shape change has no UI surface to click through; verification here was tracing both reindex call sites in the actual route/service code plus the type-check, rather than a Browser-pane pass. A future RAG-retrieval-focused session (e.g. testing a WB chat's or Scanner's actual retrieved chunks against a sheet-bearing entry) would be the natural place to close that gap, not this slice.

**Not built this slice, by design** — cross-desk map/timeline/notes cards (FS5), document-import sheet fill (FS7), diff UX/polish (FS8).

## Root Cause: "No AI Provider Configured" Errors Across FS0-FS4 Testing

Every prior FS2/FS3/FS4 verification pass in this feature hit "Connection error" / "no reachable AI provider" and treated that as an environment limitation (consistent with dozens of prior sessions across this project's whole history making the same call). The user pushed back — OpenRouter and Grok were both known-configured — and investigation found the real cause, worth recording since it explains a pattern that's been silently accepted as normal for a long time.

**`aiClientFactory.ts`'s `clientFromGlobalSettings` fallback (used by any feature with no explicit per-feature endpoint override) checks providers in a fixed order — local → openai → openrouter → grok — and stops at the first one with non-empty config fields, without ever testing whether it's actually reachable.** This dev sandbox's `aiSettings` row has both `localApiUrl` and `defaultLocalModel` set (pointing at `http://localhost:1234/v1`, LM Studio), so the global-default fallback picks **local first, every time**, and never falls through to OpenRouter or Grok — regardless of whether LM Studio is actually running. `sheet_migrate`, `sheet_sync`, and `worldbuilding_chat` all had no per-feature override (only `document_import`/`image_generation`/`embedding`/`rag_scanner` did), so all three fell into this same trap. Separately, `grokKey` in this DB is a literal placeholder (`"xai-test-invalid-key-123"`) and grok-oauth is deliberately excluded from the global-default fallback chain entirely (by design, per the code's own comment — only reachable via an explicit per-feature override) — so even without the local-first issue, the plain `grok` provider path wouldn't have worked here, though grok-oauth's real tokens would have.

**Fix applied**: added explicit per-feature `openrouter` overrides for `sheet_migrate`, `sheet_sync`, and `worldbuilding_chat` via `PUT /api/admin/feature-endpoints/:feature` (the same route Settings → Feature Endpoints uses) — no code change, a data/config fix. This is the same escape hatch `document_import`/`image_generation`/`rag_scanner` already used in this DB to route around exactly this local-first ordering.

**With a real provider actually reachable, did a full live end-to-end pass that had been impossible in every prior session**: sent a real message in a real WB chat anchored to Elena Cross asking the model to draft her sheet; it correctly emitted a well-formed `sheet-proposal` fence matching the exact category skeleton, incorporating facts from the conversation. Clicked **Accept & Sync** for real — confirmed the sheetBody write, then a real `sheet_sync` LLM call, correctly extracting `Hair`/`Eyes`-style facts from prose Physical Appearance text and `Age`/`Occupation`-style facts from prose Core Identity text into a genuine Codex proposal, which rendered live in the tray.

**This real run caught an actual bug no amount of code-mirroring or unit testing would have surfaced**: the model wrote its labels in markdown bold (`**Build:** Athletic...`), and `parseLabeledLines`' bullet-strip regex (`^[-*]\s*`, written for `"- Label: value"` bullets) matched the *first* of the two leading `*` characters as if it were a bullet marker (its `\s*` allowed zero trailing whitespace), leaving a stray `*Label:** value` behind — surfacing in the tray as `*Build: ** Athletic...`. Fixed in `sheetSyncService.ts` by stripping `**`/`__` emphasis markers globally before bullet-detection, and tightening the bullet regex to require real trailing whitespace (`^[-*]\s+`) so it can no longer accidentally eat half of a bold-pair. Re-verified against the real accepted sheet content (re-ran Sync after restarting the `tsx watch` process, which was again serving stale pre-fix code — the same recurring dev-environment gotcha noted in FS3's own entry) and confirmed every label came back clean (`Build`, `Face`, `Carriage`, `Name`, `Age`, `Role`, etc., no stray asterisks). A separate garbled-em-dash appearance in one intermediate verification step turned out to be this session's own throwaway Python verification script decoding stdin with the wrong codec, not an app bug — confirmed by re-reading the same response as raw UTF-8 bytes, which decoded correctly (`"MI6 Field Operative — Eastern European Operations Specialist"`).

All test state reverted afterward: both pending Codex proposals (pre-fix and post-fix re-sync) rejected, Elena Cross's `sheetBody` set back to `null`, the scratch WB chat deleted — confirmed via a final `GET` that her `description`/`codexState`/`sheetBody` and pending-changes count exactly match her original pre-session state (one real pre-existing `chat`-sourced pending change, untouched throughout). `npm run build` clean after the fix.

**Left in place, not reverted**: the three per-feature endpoint overrides (`sheet_migrate`/`sheet_sync`/`worldbuilding_chat` → OpenRouter) — these are a real, useful fix for this dev environment, not test scaffolding, so they stay. The underlying `local`-checked-first fallback ordering in `aiClientFactory.ts` was **not** changed — it's long-standing, intentional behavior (the doc comment literally states the priority order), and every other feature in this project has always had the same option (an explicit per-feature override) available to route around it when local isn't actually running. Not treated as a bug to fix, just a footgun worth understanding.

**Follow-up, user-requested: `grok-oauth` added to the end of the global fallback chain.** The user asked why `grok-oauth` specifically was excluded from `clientFromGlobalSettings`'s fallback (`local → openai → openrouter → grok`) even though it has real, working tokens in this DB. Traced to the "Per-Feature Grok Routing Gap" entry above: `grok-oauth` was added to the *system* (as a per-feature-override target) in the same pass that built the whole Feature Endpoints editor UI, but the global fallback chain itself predates OAuth support and was explicitly left untouched in that pass — the editor was built specifically *instead of* extending that implicit chain further, not because OAuth was judged unsuitable for it. There's no documented reason it needs to stay excluded, so extended it: `clientFromGlobalSettings` is now `async` (only needed for this one new branch) and tries `grokOAuthAccessToken` + `defaultGrokOAuthModel` last, via the same `getFreshGrokOAuthToken` refresh-and-persist helper `clientFromEndpoint`'s own `"grok-oauth"` case already uses — no second token-handling implementation. One behavioral asymmetry, intentional: a token present but unrefreshable (revoked/expired past recovery) falls through to `null` ("no provider configured") here, silently, whereas `clientFromEndpoint`'s explicit per-feature `"grok-oauth"` case still throws a clear "not connected" error — appropriate since an *implicit* global fallback silently trying and failing shouldn't surface as an alarming error the way an *explicit* per-feature choice failing should.

**Verified with a real, non-destructive test** — no permanent DB mutation, no code path faked: a throwaway `tsx` script (deleted immediately after) read the real `aiSettings` row, temporarily nulled `localApiUrl`/`defaultLocalModel`/`openrouterKey`/`defaultOpenRouterModel` (leaving `grokKey` present but `defaultGrokModel` null, so the plain `grok` branch still doesn't apply either), called the real `buildClientForFeature("humanizer")` (a feature with no per-feature override in this DB), confirmed it now resolves to `{ baseURL: "https://api.x.ai/v1", model: "grok-4.5" }` — the real, connected xAI OAuth session — instead of `null`, then restored all four fields in a `finally` block. Confirmed the restore took via a direct fresh DB read afterward: all four fields back to their original values. `npm run build` (tsc client+server+vite) clean.

---

## Lore Sheet + Sync Loop — FS5 (Cross-Desk: Map Layout Brief, Timeline Pending Pins, Notes Stub), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Lore_Sheet_And_Sync_Design.md` §5a-§5d (FS5: "map layout-brief pending card; timeline pending pins; note link pointer + stub offer", depends on FS3). Built after FS6 per the design's own recommended order. Adds three independent cross-desk lanes to `syncSheetToCodex`'s existing Codex-tray lane, per §6d's "split: Codex tray for description/Codex patch; separate cards for map brief / timeline pins / note link" — each lane succeeds/fails on its own, reported back on `SyncSheetResult` rather than folded into the Codex proposal.

**Scope calls made against the locked design, each because the alternative had no real review surface or would have duplicated an already-shipped one:**
- **Map lane (`mapLayoutBrief`)** — location's "Layout Notes" section content is offered as a `metadata.placeState.layoutMd` merge, applied only on the client's explicit "Apply to map" click (`SheetSyncCrossDeskCard.tsx`, `useUpdateLorebookMutation`). Deliberately fires **regardless of `codexEnabled`** — unlike the "Layout" customField FS3 already produces when Place Codex tracking is on, `placeState.layoutMd` is a separate field with nothing to conflict with either way, so there's no reason to gate this lane on that toggle too. No sceneJson write anywhere in this path (5a's own lock) — applying only ever touches `layoutMd` text; MV4's pre-existing "Convert to sketch" banner (gated on an empty scene + non-empty `layoutMd`) is the only thing that can ever touch the actual Excalidraw scene, and that's still its own separate, already-shipped user action.
- **Timeline lane (`timelinePinId`)** — event/timeline category, built from the sheet's raw "When"/"Summary" section content. Rather than inventing a new ephemeral card + persistence layer, this reuses TL11B's existing `status: "pending", source: "ai_suggested"` pin lane wholesale (`proposeAiSuggestedPin` in `storyTimelineService.ts`, previously only called by `graphSuggestEdgesJob.ts`'s time-twin, `timelineSuggestPinsJob.ts`) — the pin is durably persisted immediately and reviewed via Timeline's own pre-existing Pending tab (`PendingPinsPanel.tsx`), not a bespoke UI on the entry editor. Still a real propose→Accept gate (6b), just one with a different, already-built UI home — `SheetSyncButton.tsx` fires a toast pointing at it rather than rendering a redundant second review surface. `proposeAiSuggestedPin`'s previously-hardcoded `linkType: null, linkId: null` became a passthrough of `CreatePinInput`'s existing (unused-until-now) `linkType`/`linkId` fields, so this lane's pins can link back to their source entry — every other caller (the bulk-suggest job) is unaffected since it never passes those fields. New `findPinsByLink` (mirrors `listPinsForStory`/`listPendingPinsForStory`'s shape) de-dupes on re-sync: a pending OR already-approved pin already linked to the entry blocks proposing a second one, so clicking Sync repeatedly doesn't spam duplicates. **Deliberately did not add `When`/`Where`/`Who` as a `customFields` sync target for `event`** even though §4's table names it — same "no review surface" reasoning FS3's own file header already established for every non-character/location category; the raw section text still feeds the pin's blurb/fuzzyPhrase directly, it just never lands in any persisted lorebook field.
- **Notes lane (`notesStub`)** — `note` category only, a one-shot "Create note & open" offer (`useCreateNoteMutation` + `StoryContext.pendingNoteId`/`setCurrentTool("notes")`, the exact same one-shot cross-tool-navigation pattern `OpenMapButton.tsx`/`PlaceOnTimelineButton.tsx` already established). No persisted link back to the source lorebook entry — 5c's "no dual-body mirror" locks that out; a `lorebookEntries` ↔ `notes` link column doesn't exist and this slice doesn't add one. Rides along on **every** successful note-category sync (not just a first-ever one), since there's nothing to dedupe against — each Accept creates an independent, unlinked note, matching "no mirror" literally.
- **5d ("missing desk target: skip + soft notice")** needed no explicit soft-notice UI in practice — each lane's own always-available target already covers it: the map lane's target (`metadata.placeState`) lives on the entry itself, the notes lane's target is created on demand (stub-create, no pre-existing target needed), and the only real "missing target" case — an `event`/`timeline` entry at `global`/`series` level with no concrete `storyId` to attach a pin to — silently skips that one lane (documented in `sheetSyncService.ts`) rather than surfacing a notice, since there's no reasonable target to name for a level that doesn't map to one story.

**A real, genuinely dangerous bug found and fixed during live verification, worth recording as a general lesson**: `SheetSyncCrossDeskCard.tsx`'s four buttons (Apply-to-map, two Dismisses, Create-note) were written without an explicit `type="button"`. This component renders *inside* `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`'s real `<form onSubmit={form.handleSubmit(handleSubmit)}>` (unlike every prior proposal-card precedent this mirrors — `PlaceSheetProposalCard.tsx`/`SheetProposalCard.tsx` etc. all render inside a chat transcript, never inside a form). A plain shadcn `<Button>` with no `type` prop renders a bare `<button>`, which the HTML spec defaults to `type="submit"` **whenever it sits inside a `<form>`** — so clicking "Dismiss" (a button whose own handler is just `setCrossDeskResult(null)`, no API call at all) silently triggered the entry's real "Update" submit handler underneath it, PUTing the form's current in-memory field values — including whatever scratch/unsaved edits happened to be sitting in the Lore Sheet textarea at that moment — over the entry's real saved data. Caught live against the real demo entry **Berlin**: a scratch "Layout Notes" edit used only to trigger the map-lane card ended up persisted to Berlin's real `sheetBody` not once but twice (the second time during the very act of re-testing the still-unfixed code to isolate the cause), confirmed via direct network-request inspection (`PUT /api/lorebook/lore-berlin` firing right after the "Dismiss" click, not after "Sync"). Fixed by adding `type="button"` to all four buttons; re-verified with an identical repro (edit sheet → Sync → Dismiss) showing zero `PUT` in the network log afterward. Berlin's `sheetBody` was restored to its exact original text via a direct authenticated `PUT` (captured verbatim before the first accidental write) and both stray Codex pending-change proposals the repeated testing created were rejected — confirmed clean via a final `GET` diff against the captured original. **General lesson for this codebase**: any new interactive button added inside `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`'s form (or any other form-wrapped surface) needs an explicit `type="button"` unless it's actually meant to submit — `SheetSyncButton.tsx` itself already followed this correctly, which is exactly why the bug wasn't caught by mirroring nearby code; the newly-written card just wasn't compared against the one sibling that had it right.

**Live-verified end to end against the real dev DB** (server-only + client-only launch configs, same `tsx watch`-under-`concurrently` zero-output workaround FS0-era sessions established): a scratch `location` entry's "Layout Notes" section synced correctly to `mapLayoutBrief`, and the client's "Apply to map" merge into `metadata.placeState.layoutMd` round-tripped correctly (verified via direct API calls mirroring the real client mutation); a scratch `event` entry's "When"/"Summary" sections synced to a real `timelinePinId`, confirmed present in `GET /api/stories/:id/timeline-pins/pending` with the correct title/blurb/fuzzyPhrase/`linkType: "lorebook"`, and a re-sync of the same entry correctly produced **no** second pin (pending count stayed at 1) while the Codex-tray lane still fired independently; a scratch `note` entry's sync produced a `notesStub`, and — this time via the real Browser pane — clicking "Sync structured fields" then "Create note & open" fired a real `POST /api/notes` and navigated the workspace tool from Lorebook to Notes (confirmed via document title and network trace). All scratch lorebook entries, notes, and the one scratch pending timeline pin (rejected, not deleted — same "reject stays a row" convention `storyGraphEdges`/`codexPendingChanges` already use) were cleaned up afterward, confirmed via a final sweep query showing zero leftover FS5-named entries, zero pending pins, and Berlin's `sheetBody` exactly matching its pre-session content. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean for both server and client throughout, including after the button-type fix.

**Not built this slice** — diff UX/empty-states/soft-notice polish (FS8, explicitly out of scope per the recommended build order) and document-import sheet fill (FS7). T5's remaining slices are FS7 and FS8.

---

## Lore Sheet + Sync Loop — FS7 (Document Import Writes sheetBody), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Lore_Sheet_And_Sync_Design.md` §8/§9c (FS7: "Document import writes sheetBody + structured", depends on FS1, FS3). Closes the one remaining gap `entryFormUtils.ts`'s own `getDefaultFormValues` comment had flagged since FS2 shipped: "Document-import drafts still get the blank template for now (draft.sheetBody fill-in is FS7, a separate slice)."

**Scope call: reused FS2's deterministic reverse-compile rather than adding a second AI extraction pass.** §8's "reconstructed markdown from source" is ambiguous between (a) the LLM directly authoring sheet markdown from the raw document text, or (b) deterministically reverse-compiling the already-extracted structured draft (name/description/codexState) the same way FS2 already does for a pre-existing entry that predates this feature. Went with (b) — it's the same "deterministic first, optional AI improve" doctrine axis 9b already locked, it's zero additional latency/cost/failure surface on top of `documentImportService.ts`'s existing single LLM call, and the sheet editor's "Improve with AI" button (already stateless and available on any sheet, drafted or not, since FS2) is right there afterward if a nicer pass is wanted. No server change at all — `documentImportService.ts`/`DocumentImportDraft` are untouched.

**Mechanism: loosened `reverseCompileSheet`'s parameter type rather than constructing a fake `LorebookEntry`.** It only ever reads `category`/`description`/`codexState`/`metadata.placeState` — none of `LorebookEntry`'s other required fields (`id`/`level`/`scopeId`/`createdAt`/`tags`/etc.), which a document-import draft doesn't have yet since it isn't a saved entry. Extracted a new `ReverseCompileSource` interface with exactly those four fields (`reverseCompileSheet.ts`) — structurally compatible with any real `LorebookEntry`, so the one existing call site (`entryFormUtils.ts`'s `entry` branch) needed no changes. `getDefaultFormValues`'s `sheetBody` seed now has three branches instead of two: an existing entry's own `sheetBody` always wins; otherwise an existing entry with no `sheetBody` yet reverse-compiles from itself (FS2, unchanged); otherwise a document-import draft reverse-compiles from `{ category: resolvedCategory, description: draft.description, codexState: draft.codexState }`; a genuinely brand-new undrafted entry still falls back to FS1's blank required-section template. `draft.image` was already wired into `imageFile` by FS2-era code and needed no changes.

**Placeholder gap, accepted as-is**: a draft never has `metadata.placeState` (`documentImportService.ts`'s extraction schema has no place-sheet fields at all, matching §9c's "structured fields (today's shape)" — today's shape is character/generic Codex only, not location placeState). So a `location`-category import draft's sheet reverse-compiles Overview/Atmosphere from the description but leaves Scale & Nature/Holder & Control/Landmarks/Exits & Links/Layout Notes empty — same "nothing to reverse-compile → empty heading" behavior FS2 already established for any entry with no placeState, not a new gap this slice introduces.

**Live-verified end to end against the real dev DB and a real, reachable AI provider** (`document_import`'s existing `grok-oauth` feature-endpoint override, already configured in this environment from an earlier session): uploaded a real `.txt` character reference (Dr. Nadia Okafor — personality prose, an `Appearance:` block, Age/Occupation, Wardrobe/Wounds/Items lists) through the actual browser upload flow (`#import-lorebook` file input, not a direct API call, so the real client-side `getDefaultFormValues`/`reverseCompileSheet` path ran) — the resulting draft tab's Lore Sheet came back fully populated: Core Identity (Age/Occupation), Physical Appearance (Hair/Eyes/Build), Wardrobe, Personality & Temperament (the full extracted narrative description), Wounds/Marks, and Items/Possessions, with the two sections that had no source data (Background & Lifestyle, Character Motivations) correctly rendered as empty headings rather than invented content. No console errors. The draft was never saved (no "Create" click), so nothing persisted server-side — confirmed via a follow-up `GET` showing zero matching entries in the story. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean for both client and server (server untouched, checked anyway since the parameter-type change lives in shared-shape territory).

**Not built this slice** — diff UX/empty-states/soft-notice polish (FS8), which is now T5's last remaining slice.

---

## Lore Sheet + Sync Loop — FS8 (Diff UX, Soft Notices, Template Polish), Load-Bearing Decisions

Doc: `docs/Lore_Sheet_And_Sync_Design.md` §6e/§5d/§10 (FS8: "Diff UX, empty states, soft notices, template polish", depends on FS3-FS5). The last T5 slice — closes out FS0-FS8 in full. Scoped to three concrete, explicitly-named gaps rather than a broad UI sweep: 6e's "diff shown" for full-replace list buckets (previously not built at all), 5d's "skip + soft notice" for FS5's one deferred missing-target case, and the one polish TODO a prior slice's own code comment had explicitly flagged as "FS8 territory."

**Diff UX (6e) — built once, shared by both Codex review surfaces, not just the one FS3 built.** New `src/features/lorebook/utils/codexStateDiff.tsx` exports pure `diffCodexList`/`diffCodexFields` functions plus two small renderer components (`CodexListDiffLine`, `CodexFieldDiffLine`). `diffCodexList` (wardrobe/wounds/items — full-replace per 6e) compares by trimmed/lowercased value, not id (a proposal's items never carry the current entry's real ids), returning kept/added/removed; rendered as plain/`+green`/`strikethrough-destructive` respectively. `diffCodexFields` (appearance/customFields — merge-by-key per 6e) compares by label, showing `before → after` when a label's value is actually changing, `(new)` when the label doesn't exist yet, and no arrow at all when unchanged — mirrors `sheetSyncService.ts`'s own `mergeLabeledFields` matching logic exactly, so what the diff shows is what Approve will actually do. **Extended beyond `CodexPendingChangesPanel.tsx`** (T5's own FS3-built Sync-lane tray) **to `ProposalTrayCard.tsx`'s `ProposedStateSummary`** (the older, pre-T5 chat-proposal review tray, `CodexProposalTray.tsx`) too — same underlying data shape, same "full-replace list / merge-by-key field" semantics, so showing a diff in one but not the other would have been a real, confusing inconsistency between the app's two Codex-proposal review surfaces. `CodexProposalTray.tsx` already had the anchor entry's live object in its `entryLookup` (built for the entry-name/category badges), so passing `entry?.codexState` through as a new `entryCurrentState` prop needed no new data-fetching. Both panels degrade gracefully (proposed-value-only, no diff) when `currentState`/`entryCurrentState` is undefined — a brand-new unsaved entry, or any future caller that hasn't wired it. `ProposalTrayCard`'s edit-mode `ProposedStateEditor` (the free-form add/remove editor) is untouched — a diff doesn't make sense once the user is actively rewriting the proposal by hand.

**Soft notice (5d) — closes the one gap FS5's own file header explicitly deferred** ("global/series entries skip this lane entirely — no soft-notice needed, there's no reasonable target to name"). Revisited under FS8 since 5d is literally this slice's job: a global/series-level `event`/`timeline` entry whose sheet has real When/Summary content to propose from now gets a plain sentence back (`SyncSheetResult.crossDeskNotice`, e.g. "This is a global-level entry, so no timeline pin could be proposed — pins only attach to a single story.") instead of the Sync call just silently doing nothing on that lane. Still skip-not-error — `SheetSyncButton.tsx` shows it via a plain `toast.info`, not a failure toast, and the Codex-tray lane can still succeed in the same response. `crossDeskNotice` alone also now counts toward "something happened" in the "nothing new to sync" failure check — previously a sheet with only a When/Summary section and no other content would have misleadingly hit "Nothing new to sync" on a global-level entry even though the sync genuinely found something, just had nowhere to send it.

**Template polish — the one item a prior slice's own comment named directly.** `LoreSheetEditor.tsx`'s outline-click "scroll to heading" previously approximated position via `(lineIndex / totalLines) * scrollHeight` (proportional by raw `\n`-count), which drifts badly once any section contains a long wrapped paragraph elsewhere in the document (inflates the line-count denominator without affecting pixel height before the target). Replaced with the standard mirror-div technique: a hidden off-screen `<div>` cloned with the textarea's own font/width/padding/line-height (so it wraps identically), holding the sheet text up to the target heading plus a marker `<span>`, whose real `offsetTop` gives the actual pixel position — accurate regardless of where in the document long wrapped content happens to sit.

**Live-verified end to end against the real dev DB, no AI provider needed for any of this slice's own logic** (diff/notice/scroll are all deterministic client/server code, not LLM-touching):
- Diff UX: a scratch codex-enabled character (existing wardrobe `[old leather jacket, combat boots]`, appearance `Hair`, items `[pocket knife]`, customField `Age: 29`) synced against a sheet proposing wardrobe `[combat boots, charcoal wool coat]`, items `[pocket knife, burner phone]`, unchanged Hair, and `Age: 30` + a new `Occupation` field. The rendered tray showed exactly the expected diff: Wardrobe kept `combat boots`, added `+charcoal wool coat` (confirmed `rgb(52,211,153)`, the emerald token), removed `old leather jacket` (confirmed `rgb(158,55,46)` + `line-through`, the destructive token); Items kept `pocket knife`, added `+burner phone`; Appearance showed unchanged `Hair: short brown hair` with no arrow; Custom fields showed `Age: 29 → 30` (changed) and `Occupation: Freelance courier (new)`.
- Soft notice: a scratch global-level `event` entry synced with real Summary/When content returned `crossDeskNotice` exactly as designed (confirmed via direct API response), with no `timelinePinId` — matching the "skip + notice, not silent" intent.
- Template polish: injected a long 40-sentence wrapped paragraph mid-sheet, confirmed a heading appearing well before the wrapped block scrolled to a proportionally-correct pixel position (249px into a 5476px-tall textarea, consistent with ~8-10 short unwrapped lines preceding it) rather than the old proportional-by-line-count math, which the wrapped block later in the document would have skewed.
- All scratch entries and pending changes cleaned up afterward (rejected pending changes, deleted entries), confirmed via a final sweep showing zero leftover FS8-named entries.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean for both client and server throughout.

**T5 (Lore Sheet + Sync Loop) is now fully shipped, FS0-FS8.**
