## Lore Sheet — New "Sexuality" Character Section (2026-08-14)

User request, direct: add a "Sexuality" heading to the Character Lore Sheet's section outline. Added `{ heading: "Sexuality", optional: true }` to `sheetTemplates.ts`'s character pack, positioned between "Character Motivations" and "Wounds / Marks" (mirrors the source PP6 playbook packs' own placement of their "Sexuality & Power Dynamics" material near the end of the sheet, after personality/motivation content and before the purely physical/concrete trailing sections).

**Also added the matching entry to `sheetSyncService.ts`'s `CHARACTER_SECTIONS` map (`sexuality: { kind: "narrative" }`), not just the client-side template.** Confirmed by reading the sync loop's own section-processing code first: `syncSheetToCodex` does `const role = sectionConfig[section.heading.toLowerCase()]; if (!role || !section.content) return;` — a heading present in the sheet but absent from `CHARACTER_SECTIONS` is silently dropped from Sync entirely (not even folded into the narrative `description` compile), the same "real gap" class of bug FS3's own file header flagged and fixed for the six non-character/location categories. Marked narrative (compiles into `description` alongside Personality & Temperament/Background & Lifestyle/Character Motivations) rather than any structured Codex target, consistent with CLAUDE.md's Codex-is-concrete-state-only boundary — this section is characterization prose, not wardrobe/wounds/items list data.

`reverseCompileSheet.ts` and `LoreSheetEditor.tsx`'s outline rendering needed no changes — both are already fully generic over `getSheetTemplate(category)`, so the new optional section just works: omitted on reverse-compile when there's no source data (same as every other optional section), shown as an "Add ... section" affordance in the outline until content exists.

`docs/Lore_Sheet_And_Sync_Design.md` §3's character section-map table updated to match (a pre-existing minor doc/code drift was noticed in passing — the table was already missing the code's "Friends & Family" section — left alone, out of scope for this change).

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (client + server). Live-verified in the Browser pane against the real dev DB (Alexandra, demo story): the outline showed `+ Sexuality` positioned correctly right after Character Motivations and before Wounds/Marks; clicking it inserted a real `## Sexuality` heading into the sheet body at the right position. Confirmed via network inspection that the insert is unsaved client-only form state (no PUT fired) — a page reload discarded it cleanly, leaving the real entry untouched.

## Story Timeline — "Extract Pins" Action + Manuscript Status on Pins (2026-08-14)

User-identified gap, found live against the demo story's own "Story Timeline" lorebook entry (category `timeline`): its Summary section is a dense 8-day plot compression ("Day 1: ... Day 2: ... Day 8: ..."), and neither existing mechanism could turn it into real pins. T5 FS5's sheet-sync cross-desk timeline lane (`sheetSyncService.ts`) creates **at most one** pending pin per entry — title = the entry's own name, blurb = the first 500 characters of the Summary section (cut off mid-sentence) — and permanently deduplicates via `findPinsByLink`, so re-syncing never produces a second pin. TL11B's bulk "Suggest pins" job (`timelineSuggestPinsJob.ts`) does real multi-beat extraction, but reads each lorebook entry's `description` truncated to 240 characters, not `sheetBody` — this entry's actual Summary content may not even reach it. The user also asked two related questions, answered via `AskUserQuestion` before building: should Lorebook entries get a general "split into multiple entries" capability (mirroring Notes' `note-split-proposal`) — **user said yes, worth designing, but explicitly out of scope for this pass**, logged as a future item, not built; and should "planned vs. already written into the manuscript" be tracked on a pin — **user chose a new explicit user-set field**, not inferred from chapter-linking alone.

**`manuscriptStatus` (`"planned" | "written"`) added to `storyTimelinePins`** (migration `0068_overjoyed_blob.sql`, `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'planned'`). Deliberately decoupled from `linkType`/`linkId` per the user's own reasoning during the AskUserQuestion pass — a pin can be linked to a not-yet-drafted chapter (still planned) or represent something already established in prose with no chapter link at all (written), so "has a chapter link" is not a safe proxy. Threaded through the full stack: `rowToPin`, `CreatePinInput`/`ProposeAiSuggestedPinInput`/`UpdatePinInput` in `storyTimelineService.ts`, both `POST`/`PATCH` routes in `routes/storyTimeline.ts`, `storyTimelineClient.ts`. **AI-proposed pins never guess this value from prose tense** — every `proposeAiSuggestedPin` call defaults to `"planned"` unless a caller has a genuine signal. The one caller that does: `timelineSuggestPinsJob.ts`'s bulk job now tags each `CandidateSource` with `manuscriptStatus` at the source level — chapter-sourced beats (summary or full text, since that's manuscript prose or its own summary) default `"written"`; every other source (lorebook entries, notes, synopsis) stays `"planned"`, since the model has no reliable way to know whether background/world-building material describes something already drafted. `PinFormDialog.tsx` gets an explicit selector (default `"planned"`, except a brand-new pin created via "Place on timeline" on a **chapter** link, which defaults to `"written"` — the one case the UI genuinely knows better, still fully overridable). `PinCard.tsx` gets a click-to-toggle badge (green "Written" / muted "Planned") on every rendered pin, wired through the existing `useUpdatePinMutation`; `PendingPinsPanel.tsx` gets a matching read-only badge so a reviewer has the context before Approve/Reject.

**New dedicated "Extract pins" action, not a fix to either existing mechanism** — `server/services/timelineExtractPinsService.ts`, new `POST /lorebook/:id/timeline/extract-pins` route, new `timeline_extract_pins` feature key, `ExtractPinsButton.tsx` in the Lore Sheet editor (visible only for `timeline`/`event` categories, next to the existing "Sync structured fields" button). Deliberately **not** a change to Sync's one-pin lane (still useful as a "quick single pin" default for entries that are genuinely one beat) and deliberately **not** a fix to the bulk job's `description`-only reads (that job's whole-story scope makes shallow-per-source depth an already-made, correct cost tradeoff — deepening it to full `sheetBody` per entry across up to 40 entries would blow up its prompt size for a job that already sweeps a whole story). This action reads the **current form's full `sheetBody`** (not the truncated saved `description`) for **one entry** the user explicitly chose, and its system prompt explicitly instructs the model to decompose a sequenced structure ("Day 1: ... Day 2: ...") into one pin per beat rather than summarizing the whole document as one pin — same SYSTEM_PROMPT doctrine as `timelineSuggestPinsJob.ts`, written fresh rather than shared verbatim since the "already recorded, do not repeat" framing and single-vs-multi-source shape differ enough to not be worth a forced abstraction.

**Proposed pins are native (no `linkType`/`linkId`), matching TL7's chat fence and the bulk job — not FS5's single-pin lane, which does link back to its source entry.** Several distinct beats extracted from one document don't share a sensible 1:1 link back to that document; setting every extracted pin's `linkId` to the same source entry would also break `getPinForLink`'s implicit "one pin per source" assumption that `PlaceOnTimelineButton` relies on (a "Place on timeline" button checks that endpoint to decide whether to show Create vs. Edit for a given source — multiple same-link pins would make that check pick an arbitrary one). Every proposed pin lands `status: "pending", source: "ai_suggested", manuscriptStatus: "planned"` — reviewed through Timeline's existing Pending tab, same doctrine every other propose/accept surface in this app follows (never applied directly).

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (client + server) throughout. Live-verified end to end against the real dev DB and a real reachable model: clicking "Extract pins" on the demo story's actual "Story Timeline" entry (`lore-timeline`) first hit `ECONNREFUSED` (this feature key had no per-feature endpoint override yet, so the global fallback chain tried local/LM Studio first — same root-cause shape as FS4's own "No AI Provider Configured" incident); set a `grok-oauth` override for `timeline_extract_pins` via the admin API (matching every sibling timeline/sheet feature's own existing override) and re-ran — correctly decomposed the entry's 8-day Summary into **6 distinct pending pins** ("Berlin dead drop retrieval" / Day 1 through "Evidence review and PM message" / Day 8), each with a real title/blurb and `fuzzyPhrase` carrying the day marker, all `status: "pending"`, `manuscriptStatus: "planned"`, `linkType: null`. Confirmed via direct API read and via the Pending tab UI (title, day label, "Planned" badge, blurb, Approve/Reject all render correctly). Separately verified `PinCard.tsx`'s toggle button on a real pre-existing active pin (Planned → Written → Planned, restored to its original state after). Left the 6 pending pins in the demo story for the user to Approve/Reject themselves, since they directly answer the question that prompted this feature — not scratch data to clean up.

## Linked Identities, Codex Secrets, and Multi-Subject Document Import (2026-08-14)

User's real "Hareem of secrets profiles.md" reference document — a spy-story character bible describing ten distinct characters, one of them (Elizabeth "Lizzy" Hartley) explicitly a cover identity for another (Lizbet Anderson) — surfaced three real gaps in one conversation, confirmed and scoped via `AskUserQuestion` before building: (1) no way to represent "two entries are actually one person's linked identities," (2) no way to keep a fact tracked in the Codex but withheld from AI-generated prose until revealed, and (3) no way to import a multi-subject document as more than one entry (`documentImportService.ts`'s own prompt explicitly said "pick the single most central subject").

### 1. `alias_of` relationship-graph edge type

Simplest of the four pieces — the user chose **two linked entries** (not one entry with an alias slot) during the AskUserQuestion pass, so the fix is a new edge type, not a schema change. Added `"alias_of"` to `STORY_GRAPH_EDGE_TYPES`/`StoryGraphEdgeType` (`src/types/storyGraph.ts`) — directed from the alias/cover entry to the "real" one (`"Elizabeth Hartley" --alias_of--> "Lizbet Anderson"`), fitting the allowlist's existing "concrete/factual, no psychological types" doctrine since it's a factual identity relationship, not a personality one. Needed **zero other code changes** — every consuming surface (`storyGraphService.ts`'s validation, the Relations tool's create/edit UI, `ragIndexService.ts`'s `buildRelationshipText`) already derives from this one shared array/label map, so the edge rides for free on the existing "Relationships:" block every RAG-indexed entry already carries. Live-verified: `POST /graph/edges` accepted `edgeType: "alias_of"` (201), cleaned up after.

### 2. Codex "secrets" — manual toggle + chapter-scoped reveal

The user's choice from `AskUserQuestion`: an explicit, user-set field, decoupled from chapter-linking (not inferred from "is this pin linked to a chapter"). New optional `secrets?: CodexSecretItem[]` on `CodexState` (`src/types/codex.ts`) — `{id, value, revealed: boolean, revealedAtChapterId: string | null}`. `revealed` is always authoritative; `revealedAtChapterId` is an additive convenience that only ever *adds* visibility, never removes the requirement that either `revealed` is true or the current chapter's order has reached it.

**This is the security-sensitive piece — traced every server-side path that turns `codexState` into AI-facing text before writing any UI, since a bug here means a "secret" actually leaks:**
- `ragIndexService.ts`'s `formatCodexState` (feeds the static per-entry RAG index, used by every chat type's "search" results and any story-wide RAG query): only the manual `revealed` gate applies — the index is a request-independent blob computed once on save, so it structurally can't know "which chapter is this search happening for," and never tries to.
- `chatContextService.ts`'s `resolveAnchorAndRelated` (the one path that legitimately knows chapter context — Editor chats carry `anchorChapterId`): new `filterRevealedSecrets` helper resolves the anchor chapter's `order` and each secret's `revealedAtChapterId`'s `order`, applying chapter-scoped auto-reveal only here, only for Editor chats. WB/Outline/Brainstorm/Research/Notes chats have no anchor chapter, so they fall back to manual-only, same as the static index.
- `ChatInterface.tsx`'s own client-side `formatCodexState` (builds the `[CODEX]` block actually sent as prompt text): added a `Secrets (revealed)` line — safe by construction, since the server already stripped everything unrevealed before this payload was ever sent; the client formatter never makes its own decision about what's safe to show.
- `codexService.ts`'s `approvePendingChange` (the codex-proposal Approve path, `CODEX_PROPOSAL_INSTRUCTIONS`' own five enumerated keys never mention `secrets`): added a defensive strip anyway — `secrets: currentState.secrets` always wins over whatever a proposal's `proposedState` contains, so even a hallucinated `"secrets"` key in a model's JSON reply (or a future "Edit First" tray edit) can never create/reveal a secret as a side effect of an ordinary Approve. `sheetSyncService.ts`'s Sync-to-Codex proposals route through this same function, so they're covered by the same guard without a separate patch.

UI: new `SecretsBox` (`codexStateBoxes.tsx`) in `CodexStateEditor.tsx` — per-secret click-to-toggle Eye/EyeOff badge plus an optional "Reveal at chapter" picker (`useChaptersByStoryQuery`, only rendered when `storyId` is available). `entryFormUtils.ts`'s `EMPTY_CODEX_STATE`/`getDefaultFormValues` updated so `secrets` is always a defined array in the form (an entry saved before this feature has a real `codexState` with no `secrets` key at all — `useFieldArray` needs something to key off).

**Verification (live, against the real dev DB, the security-critical part):** created a scratch character entry with `secrets: [{value: "SHE IS SECRETLY A DOUBLE AGENT FOR MI6", revealed: false}]`. `POST /api/rag/search` for the entry confirmed it was indexed (`"Secret Test Entry\n\nA test character..."`) with the secret text **entirely absent** — proving the exclusion, not just an untested code path. Flipped `revealed: true`, re-saved, re-searched: the chunk now read `"...Secrets (revealed): SHE IS SECRETLY A DOUBLE AGENT FOR MI6"` — proving the reveal path too. (First attempt after editing `ragIndexService.ts` showed no change at all — the running `server-only` process predates the edit and doesn't watch/hot-reload; restarting it was the actual fix, a fresh instance of the same `dev_environment_gotchas` lesson.) Scratch entry deleted after.

### 3/4. Multi-subject Document Import + Brainstorm's shared entry point

User explicitly asked for both, built together sharing one extraction service, per the AskUserQuestion answer. `documentImportService.ts` gains `importEntriesFromDocument` (new `DOCUMENT_IMPORT_BATCH_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, capped at 20 subjects, reuses the existing `toDraft` per-array-element normalizer) — deliberately a **new, separate function**, not a change to `importEntryFromDocument`'s existing "pick the single most central subject" behavior, which stays the single-entry default for the common case. New route `POST /lorebook/import/document/batch`; new feature-key-free reuse of the existing `document_import` feature (no new AI Settings entry needed — same extraction call, same prompt doctrine, just asking for an array instead of one object).

**`MultiEntryImportDialog.tsx`** is the one piece of UI both entry points share: it supports an uncontrolled mode (Lorebook toolbar's own "Import multiple" button, owns its own file-upload step) and a controlled mode (`open`/`onOpenChange`/`initialDrafts` props — Brainstorm's checklist tray, drafts already extracted, no re-upload). Each accepted draft is created through the same `create` → `codexApi.enable` → `codexApi.recordState` → optional `uploadImage` sequence `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`'s own `handleSubmit` uses for a brand-new entry — real entries, not just pre-filled forms the user has to individually save (unlike the existing single-entry import's draft-tab flow).

**Brainstorm's entry point reuses the existing generic checklist infrastructure with zero schema change**, same "one more kind, same table" precedent every prior addition (`note_split`, `shuttle`/`shuttle_return`) already established: new `"character_batch"` `BrainstormChecklistKind` + `CharacterBatchPayload` (`{filename, drafts}`), allowlist bump in `routes/brainstorm.ts`'s `POST /checklist` validation. New `BrainstormCharacterImportPanel.tsx` (a `ChatToolsRail` panel, mirrors Outline chat's own OI6 file-attach precedent — hidden `<input type="file">` + button, no drag/drop) calls the same `lorebookApi.importDocumentBatch`, then persists the result as a durable checklist row via the existing generic `POST /brainstorm/checklist` route — no new server code needed beyond the one-line kind allowlist bump. `BrainstormChecklistTray.tsx` renders a new "Character imports" section; its "Review" button opens `MultiEntryImportDialog` in controlled mode (`key={openBatchItem?.id}` forces a remount per item, since the dialog's `initialDrafts` is only read on mount) and marks the row `"opened"` — same "Open/Accept perform the real action but only Mark done clears the queue" doctrine every other Brainstorm tray item follows; the real entry-creation happens inside the dialog, not as a side effect of Review.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (client + server). Live-verified against the real dev DB and a real reachable model using the user's actual document (uploaded via the real `POST /lorebook/import/document/batch` route, not a mock): correctly extracted **10 distinct drafts** — all nine named characters plus "Obsidian Order" pulled out as its own `note`-category entry rather than folded into a character's description, exactly matching the batch prompt's "an organization/faction with real described structure counts as its own subject" rule. Lizbet Anderson and Elizabeth Hartley came back as two separate character drafts, each with a cross-referencing custom field (`alter_ego` / `true_identity_link`) the model volunteered on its own — confirming the extraction correctly recognized the cover-identity relationship even before any `alias_of` edge is manually created between the two resulting entries. UI smoke-tested both entry points live: Lorebook toolbar's "Import multiple" button renders; Brainstorm's new "Import" rail panel opens and shows the attach prompt. Nothing was persisted to the demo story from this verification pass — `importEntriesFromDocument` never writes to the DB, only extracts.

## Story-Wide Secrets Panel (2026-08-14, same-session follow-up)

Direct follow-up question after shipping per-entry Codex secrets: "how does the user see secrets?" — the honest answer at the time was "only inside each entry's own Advanced → Codex panel, one at a time," with no way to answer "what's still hidden right now" without opening every character. User confirmed the Lorebook page as the right home (that's where the underlying Codex data already lives and gets edited) rather than a new top-level sidebar tool — secrets aren't a separate domain entity with their own table, just an array field on `lorebookEntries.codexState`, so a full separate tool felt disproportionate.

**New `{kind: "secrets"}` tab** added to `LorebookTabStrip.tsx`'s existing discriminated union (alongside `browse`/`entry`/`draft`/`new`) — a singleton like `browse` (only one ever open, re-selecting focuses the existing tab rather than duplicating), but closable unlike `browse` (falls under the existing `tab.kind !== "browse"` close-button condition, no special-casing needed). Opened via a new "Secrets" toolbar button in `LorebookBrowsePanel.tsx`, next to Export/Import.

**`LorebookSecretsPanel.tsx` needed zero new server routes** — the story's full entry list (already fetched by `LorebookPage.tsx` for the Browse tab, `codexState` included) is filtered client-side to `category === "character" && codexEnabled && secrets.length > 0`, flattened to one row per secret across every character at once. All/Hidden/Revealed tabs, click-to-jump to the owning entry, and the same reveal-toggle + reveal-at-chapter picker `SecretsBox` has — reading and writing through the same `codexApi.recordState` the per-entry editor uses (this panel edits one entry's `secrets` array at a time per toggle, same effective write as toggling from inside that entry's own form).

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Live-verified against the real dev DB: added a real secret to Marcus Webb's actual codexState via a direct API call (preserving his existing wardrobe/appearance fields — real story data, not a scratch entry), confirmed the panel's "1 hidden" badge and row rendered correctly with the entry-name link and chapter picker, clicked the reveal toggle and confirmed the badge flipped to "Revealed" and the hidden count cleared live (no reload). Reverted Marcus Webb's `secrets` to `[]` afterward, confirmed via a follow-up read that no trace of the test secret remained.

## Secrets Panel Quick-Add, With a Nudge Toward the Entry Editor (2026-08-14, same session)

Follow-up request: the panel could review/reveal secrets but not create them, only explain in text where to go. User asked for a real quick-add on this tab, while still recommending the per-entry path when the secret genuinely connects to established facts for that character. Built both, not one at the expense of the other.

**"Add secret" button next to the filter tabs opens an inline form**: a character picker (`Select`, `entries.filter(category === "character")`, sorted by name — no new server query, reuses the page's already-fetched entry list) + a free-text input + Add. Writes through the exact same primitives the toggle/reveal-at-chapter actions already use — `codexApi.enable` first if the chosen character hasn't got Codex tracking on yet (mirrors `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`'s own `handleSubmit` doing the same for a brand-new entry), then `codexApi.recordState` with the new `CodexSecretItem` appended (`revealed: false`, `revealedAtChapterId: null` — same defaults `SecretsBox`'s own add uses).

**The nudge is real, not just a courtesy line**: the form's own helper text explains *why* the entry's own Codex editor is often better — the quick-add form only ever takes free text with no visibility into that character's existing wardrobe/appearance/wounds/etc. to write the secret consistently against, while the entry's own editor has all of it right there. Deliberately not blocked or gated behind a confirmation — a real usable shortcut, just an honestly-reasoned recommendation to use the fuller editor when it matters.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Live-verified end to end against the real dev DB: opened the form, selected Marcus Webb (a real story character, not scratch data) via the character picker, typed a test secret, submitted — confirmed via a follow-up API read that his existing wardrobe/appearance fields survived untouched and the new secret landed correctly (`revealed: false`, real `id`), and the panel UI updated live showing "1 hidden" with the new row. Reverted his `secrets` back to `[]` afterward. One real automation-sandbox quirk hit during verification, not a product bug: the Radix `Select` trigger didn't open on a plain synthetic click — same category of issue `dev_environment_gotchas` already documents for Radix components in this Browser-pane sandbox — worked around with a full `pointerdown`/`mousedown`/`pointerup`/`mouseup`/`click` event sequence instead of a bare `.click()`.

## AI Review — Core v1 (AR0–AR4) (2026-08-14/15)

Promoted from the locked design (`docs/AI_Review_Design.md`) at the user's direction, scoped to Core v1 per an explicit `AskUserQuestion` choice: schema, Quick-mode job, findings list UI + actions, and the Editor entry point. Deep mode (AR5) and the Guide page (AR6) intentionally deferred.

- **Own tables, not `ragScans`/`ragScanIssues`** — `aiReviews`/`aiReviewFindings` (`server/db/schema.ts`), mirroring the Scanner's shape (status/progress row + child findings row) per design lock #11. Domain types in `src/types/aiReview.ts`. Migration `0069_ancient_speed_demon.sql`.
- **Quick mode is one LLM pass over the whole selection, not a per-chapter loop** — unlike RAG Scanner's `runChapterScan`, which calls the model once per chapter, AI Review's design explicitly calls Quick "a single LLM pass" over the chosen chapters. `aiReviewService.ts`'s `runQuickReview` assembles one combined prompt (chapter texts capped per-chapter + synopsis + RAG hits for entities across the selection, reusing `search()` from `ragIndexService.ts` verbatim) and resolves each returned finding's `chapterTitle` back to a `chapterId` via a title→id map (same "LLM names it, server resolves it" pattern as Scanner's `relatedEntityName`→`relatedEntityId`).
- **New `FeatureKey: "ai_review"`** (`src/types/aiSettings.ts`) and **new `AgentJobType: "ai_review_quick"`** (`src/types/agentJob.ts`), manual-trigger only (never auto-enqueued), same precedent as `distill_memory`/`timeline_suggest_pins`. Job handler `server/services/jobs/aiReviewJobs.ts` registered in `jobRunner.ts`'s `HANDLERS` map.
- **Triggering reuses the generic owner-gated job queue, not a dedicated route** — during implementation, found that RAG Scanner's own trigger UI never actually calls its synchronous `/api/rag/scan/*` routes; it goes through `agentJobsApi.enqueue()` → `POST /api/agent/jobs` (mounted `requireOwner`) for the job-tracking/retry machinery. AI Review's "Run Quick review" button does the same (`useTriggerQuickReviewMutation` in `useAiReviewQuery.ts`) rather than building a parallel `POST /api/ai-review/run` trigger endpoint, simplifying the original plan. `server/routes/aiReview.ts` (mounted `/api/ai-review`, editor-level, no `requireOwner` — matches `/api/rag`'s own read/triage routes) only serves `GET /findings/story/:storyId` (status/tag/chapterId filters) and `PATCH /findings/:findingId`, mirroring `ragClient.ts`'s "reads/triage only" split exactly.
- **New sidebar tool** (`ai-review` in `WorkspaceTool`, `Sidebar.tsx`, `MainContent.tsx`) — per design lock #6, unlike Scanner (which has a `"scanner"` `WorkspaceTool` value that was never actually wired into `Sidebar.tsx`'s nav array, a pre-existing gap noted but not touched here). `AiReviewPanel.tsx` mirrors `RagScannerPanel.tsx`'s status-Tabs/findings-list shape, plus a novel tag filter (`dev`/`continuity`/`voice`/`line`) via a `<Select>` — no existing panel combined a status filter with a second orthogonal filter, so this is new UI, not a reused pattern.
- **Editor "Review this chapter" entry point** navigates to the full AI Review tool (not an in-editor Sheet/drawer like Scanner's `ChapterScannerDrawer`) — a new one-shot `pendingAiReviewChapterId` context field (mirrors `pendingTimelineFocusPinId`) pre-checks that chapter in `AiReviewPanel`'s multi-select, consumed once.
- **Scribble append had no prior helper** — every existing caller of `chapters.notes` (`ChapterNotesEditor.tsx`) overwrites the whole blob. Since AI Review is genuinely a second append-style caller, extracted `useAppendToScribble.ts` (`formatScribbleBlock` + a thin `useUpdateChapterMutation` wrapper) rather than duplicating the concat-and-save logic inline. Scribble content is HTML (`react-simple-wysiwyg`), so the appended block is real markup (`<p>`/`<br>`), not raw newlines.
- **Editor chat composer seed required a real gap-fill** — `EditorChatRail.tsx` already accepted a `composerSeedText` prop, but nothing in `StoryEditor.tsx` consumed `StoryContext.pendingChatComposerSeed` for the `"editor"` tool (every other chat host — Research/Outline/Notes — already had this effect; Editor didn't, because until now nothing outside the Editor itself ever seeded its composer). Added the same one-shot consume-on-chat-selected effect pattern to `StoryEditor.tsx`.
- **Live-verified against a real reachable model** (Grok xAI OAuth, set as this story's `ai_review` feature-endpoint override during the test, then reverted): ran a real Quick review over "The Drop," got four genuinely well-reasoned findings (two `line`, two `dev`, correctly distinct from Scanner's factual-only lens) correctly tied to the right chapter; exercised Dismiss → Dismissed tab → Reopen; confirmed **Add to chapter scribble** appended without wiping the chapter's existing scribble content (verified via direct DB read before/after); confirmed **Send to Editor chat** prefilled the composer textarea without auto-sending; confirmed the Editor's "Review this chapter" button navigates to the AI Review tool. Also caught and worked around a known dev-environment issue (`tsx watch` under `concurrently` silently never binding the Express port — pre-existing "Dev Environment Gotchas" item, not caused by this change) by using the `server-only`/`client-only` launch configs instead. All test data (findings, review/job rows, the scribble test block, the temporary feature-endpoint override) cleaned up after verification. `npm run build` (tsc × 2 + vite) clean.
- **Deferred to a later pass:** AR5 (Deep staged mode — map → cross-chapter → voice pass → merge), AR6 (Guide page), and story export/import inclusion of completed findings (design doc's AR0 line item left as a documented omission — nothing wired it into `GET /stories/:id/export` this pass).

## AI Review — Deep Mode + Guide (AR5–AR6) (2026-08-15)

Follow-up to Core v1, user-requested directly ("ar5 an ar6"). Implements the design doc's staged Deep pipeline and the Guide page.

- **`aiReviews` gains an `options` json column** (nullable, migration `0070_careful_captain_universe.sql`) recording which context toggles a run used — `AiReviewOptions` (`src/types/aiReview.ts`): `includeMemory`/`includeTimeline`/`includeLine`/`includeCast` (UI-set) plus `castEntryIds` (server-resolved, audit only).
- **Deep is one job type covering all four stages**, not one job type per stage — `ai_review_deep` (`AgentJobType`), handler `runAiReviewDeepJob` in `aiReviewJobs.ts` creates the `aiReviews` row and delegates the whole pipeline to `aiReviewService.ts`'s `runDeepReview`, which takes an injected `onProgress` callback so the service owns pipeline orchestration while the job handler owns the actual `agentJobs` progress writes — same separation Quick already had, just with more checkpoints (`total = chapterIds.length + 3`).
- **Refactored `aiReviewService.ts` first**: extracted `callFindingsLlm` (the completion call + `parseFindings` JSON validation) and `toFindingParams` (parsed-finding → repository-insert mapping) so Quick and Deep's cross-chapter/voice stages share one parsing path instead of three near-duplicates. Also extracted `loadOrderedChapters` (chapter+synopsis fetch preserving caller order) since both Quick and Deep need the identical query.
- **Map stage produces plain-prose per-chapter summaries, not a new table** — one small LLM call per chapter (`runMapStage`), kept in memory for the run only, matching the design doc's own "internal or lightweight rows" framing. This is what lets Deep handle "whole book" selections: the cross-chapter stage reasons over compact summaries, not every chapter's full text.
- **Cross-chapter stage is `dev`/`continuity` only, from summaries; Voice stage is `voice` (+`line` when toggled), from raw text** — the design's four fixed stages don't call out a dedicated "line" stage, and line-level judgment needs actual prose, not a beat summary, so `line` findings live in the Voice stage's prompt rather than inventing a fifth stage. Voice's per-chapter char budget is split evenly across the selection (`VOICE_TOTAL_CHAR_BUDGET / chapterCount`, floor 1500) rather than a flat cap, so a large "whole book" run degrades gracefully instead of blowing the context window.
- **Merge/dedupe is programmatic, not a fifth LLM call** — `mergeAndDedupeFindings` concatenates the cross-chapter and voice arrays and drops exact `(chapterTitle, normalized title)` collisions in code. The two stages write disjoint tags in practice, so this is a safety net, not real dedup work — keeps Deep bounded/cheap and matches the design doc's explicit "not open agent swarm."
- **Cast auto-detection, no manual picker** — `resolveCastForChapters` substring-matches `category: "character"` Lorebook entries (same global+story+series scoping `resolveEntityIdsByName` already established) against the selection's combined text, ranks by mention count, caps to 8. **Reused `buildLorebookEntryText` (`ragIndexService.ts`, already exported) to format each matched entry's Codex/Sheet text — deliberately did not write a second codexState-to-text formatter**, since that function is the one place already trusted to filter unrevealed Codex secrets before any AI-facing text leaves the server; a second formatter would be a real risk of independently forgetting that filter. `gatherMemoryContext`/`gatherTimelineContext` (previously private in `ragScanner.ts`) were exported and reused the same way rather than reimplemented.
- **Frontend**: `AiReviewPanel.tsx` gained a Quick|Deep two-button toggle and, only when Deep is selected, four `Switch` toggles matching `RagScannerPanel.tsx`'s existing `includeMemory`/`includeTimeline` pattern. `useTriggerDeepReviewMutation` (`useAiReviewQuery.ts`) mirrors the Quick trigger mutation, `jobType: "ai_review_deep"`, `payload: {chapterIds, options}`.
- **Guide**: new top-level tab `src/features/guide/content/ai-review.mdx` (own tab, not folded into `advanced.mdx`, matching Story Timeline/Relationships/Locations & Maps precedent for full sidebar tools) registered in `GuideTabs.tsx`. Also had to register the new topic in three more places the Core v1 pass didn't touch (Guide search wasn't part of AR0-AR4): `guideSearchParser.ts`'s `GUIDE_TOPIC_META`, `guideSearchIndex.ts`'s `RAW_BY_TOPIC_ID` (+`?raw` import), and the server-side `guideSearchService.ts` — the last one needed no code change since it reads `GUIDE_TOPIC_META` + the file straight off disk by topic id.
- **Live-verified against a real reachable model** (Grok xAI OAuth, temporary story-level `ai_review` override, reverted after): ran a real Deep review over two chapters with all four toggles on — confirmed progress messages advanced through every stage (`Mapping chapters (1/2)…` → `Mapping chapters (2/2)…` → `Cross-chapter pass…` → `Voice pass…` → `Merging findings…` → `Review complete.`), got six findings with the expected tag/excerpt split (`dev`/`continuity` findings had `excerpt: null` as designed, `voice`/`line` findings had real verbatim excerpts — one of which was a genuine typo the model caught in the demo story's own prose), and the review row's `options.castEntryIds` correctly recorded four auto-detected character entries. Confirmed via direct DB check that none of the resolved cast entries had unrevealed secrets that could leak (and via code inspection that `gatherCastContext` only ever calls the secrets-safe `buildLorebookEntryText`). Re-ran Quick mode afterward as a regression check — completed cleanly, findings rendered correctly. Guide's new "AI Review" tab confirmed rendering with no MDX errors. All test findings/reviews/jobs and the temporary feature-endpoint override cleaned up afterward. `npm run build` (tsc × 2 + vite) clean.
- **Not done:** a manual cast picker (auto-detect only, per plan); a dedicated `ai_review_deep`-only feature key (Deep still shares the single `ai_review` key with Quick — no signal yet that they need different endpoints); persisting map-stage summaries anywhere durable (they're pipeline-internal only, as designed).

## Activity Stoplight (AS0–AS5) (2026-08-15)

Promoted from the locked design (`docs/Activity_Stoplight_Design.md`) directly on user request the same day it surfaced as the top not-yet-built P3 item. Global TopBar indicator over `agentJobs` — amber-pulse while anything is queued/running, red (wins) while any undismissed failure exists, hidden entirely when idle.

- **New `src/features/activity/` module**, not folded into the existing `src/features/ai/` job UI — `lib/jobPresentation.ts` extracts `JOB_TYPE_LABELS`/`STATUS_VARIANT` out of `RecentJobsCard.tsx` (now the shared source both surfaces import, no drift risk) and adds `JOB_TYPE_JUMP_TOOL`, a `Partial<Record<AgentJobType, WorkspaceTool>>` — deliberately partial, not total: `suggest_codex_updates` targets a specific chapter's pending-changes tray unreachable from a bare job row, and `reconcile_index`/`prune_history` are global housekeeping with no story-scoped surface at all, so all three are omitted (no Jump button) rather than pointed at a wrong/approximate destination.
- **Own query key (`["activityStoplight","jobs"]`), not the pre-existing `agentJobsKeys`** — traced every `agentJobsApi.enqueue()` call site (Scanner, Timeline suggest-pins, Graph suggest-edges, Codex compile, Memory distill, AI Review, reconcile) and confirmed none of them invalidate any job query on success. The existing `useRecentJobsQuery` fully **stops** polling (`refetchInterval: false`) once nothing queued/running is in its last-known data — correct for Settings' Recent Jobs (a card the user is actively looking at), wrong for a global stoplight that must notice work started from a completely different panel. `useActivityJobs.ts` polls 3s while active, 20s while idle instead of stopping — a deliberate divergence, documented inline, not an oversight.
- **`useRetryJobMutation` (`useAgentJobsQuery.ts`) extended to also invalidate `["activityStoplight"]`** on success, one extra `invalidateQueries` call, so retrying from either the stoplight popover or Settings' Recent Jobs refreshes both surfaces from a single mutation.
- **Owner-gated client-side, matching the API's own server-side gate** — `/api/agent/jobs` is mounted `requireOwner`; `TopBar.tsx` wraps the mount in `{isOwner && <ActivityStoplight />}` (same `useIsOwner()` pattern `RagScannerPanel`/`AiReviewPanel` already use) so a non-owner never fires the query at all rather than eating a guaranteed 403.
- **Dismiss is a client-local fingerprint set, not a server field** — per the design's own "lean" call, no schema change. **Two real bugs found and fixed live during verification, both timing-related and both would have shipped invisibly in a sandbox with a reachable AI provider** (jobs there don't fail-and-refail in seconds):
  1. The dismissed-id prune originally ran inside a `useState` lazy initializer, comparing stored ids against the *current* failed-job-id list — but that initializer runs before the query's first fetch resolves, so it always saw an empty list and silently wiped every real dismissal on the very next page load. Fixed by moving the prune into a `useEffect` gated on a `ready` flag (`data !== undefined` from the query) so it never runs against a not-yet-loaded state.
  2. Even after that fix, retrying a failed job (`POST /agent/jobs/:id/retry` mutates the same row in place, it doesn't create a new one) and having it fail again faster than the poll observes the intermediate `queued`/`running` state left the fresh failure wrongly suppressed by the old dismissal — confirmed live in this dev sandbox, where a retried `ai_review_quick` job with no reachable AI provider goes queued→failed in under a second, well inside even the 3s "active" poll interval. Fixed by fingerprinting each dismissal on `${job.id}::${job.completedAt}` instead of the bare id (`useDismissedJobIds.ts`), so a genuinely new failure (different `completedAt`) is never masked by an old dismissal of the same job row, independent of poll timing. An early "call `undismiss()` from the Retry button's `onSuccess`" attempt was reverted as dead code — a dismissed job's row is filtered out of the rendered list entirely, so its Retry button can never be clicked in the first place; the fingerprint approach is the only fix that actually reaches the bug.
- **`SettingsPage.tsx` gained a minimal `?section=` deep-link reader** (one-time initial `useState` read, validated against the existing `SECTIONS` tuple, not kept in sync with the URL afterward) so the popover's "All jobs →" link can land directly on the Logs tab instead of always resetting to Appearance.
- **Jump reuses `TransfersLogCard.tsx`'s existing cross-story navigation pattern** (`setCurrentStoryId` + `setCurrentTool` from `StoryContext`, no `navigate()` needed since the stoplight already lives inside the Workspace route) rather than inventing a new one.
- **Coverage matches the design lock exactly**: TopBar-only (not mounted in `MainLayout`, so Settings/Guide correctly show no stoplight — confirmed live, the popover's own "All jobs →" link is the only way from Settings back into job status), and implicitly absent during Deep Writing/`hideChrome` since the whole `TopBar` unmounts then.
- **Live-verified end to end against the real dev DB** with a real triggered `ai_review_quick` job (against `demo-story-shadows-berlin`'s real chapters, failed on the expected "no reachable AI provider" connection error, same limitation nearly every AI-call feature in this project's history has hit in this sandbox): amber pulse while queued/running → red-and-count-1 on fail → popover row (label "AI Review (Quick)", story crumb "Shadows Over Berlin", progress message, error text, Retry, Dismiss) → Dismiss hides the lamp and survives a full reload (localStorage) → the retry-then-instant-refail edge case above, reproduced with the bug present, then confirmed fixed → "All jobs →" navigates to `/settings?section=logs` landing directly on Logs with `RecentJobsCard` still rendering correctly off the now-shared `jobPresentation.ts` module → a fresh, previously-untouched browser tab showed zero console errors (the stale hook-order/HMR errors seen mid-session were confirmed, via a fresh tab, to be `read_console_messages`' own buffered history from live-edited hook signatures during debugging, not a real runtime bug — the actual fix works cleanly from a cold load). `npm run build` (tsc × 2 + vite) clean throughout every iteration. Test job row left in the dev DB (dismissed) rather than deleted — no delete endpoint exists for `agentJobs`, and leftover job history is this project's established convention (the dev DB already had dozens of routine `reconcile_index` rows before this session).

## Auto Humanizer (AH0–AH8) (2026-08-15)

Promoted from the locked design (`docs/Auto_Humanizer_Design.md`) directly on user request. A second, separate pipeline from the shipped manual Humanizer: an optional commit-time filter that runs local AI-detection + an LLM rewrite pass on AI prose as it's accepted into a chapter, so the manuscript only ever sees the final (humanized-or-original) text — hard silent, never a raw flash. Built in the design's own stated order (AH0 → AH1 → AH2 → AH3 → AH8 → AH4 → AH5 → AH6 → AH7), all nine slices in one pass.

- **`humanizePrompts.ts` extracted** (`server/services/`) — the light/medium/strong system prompts + temperatures moved out of `humanizerService.ts` unchanged (zero behavior change for Manual), now shared with a new `buildAutoHumanizeSystemPrompt(intensity, tone, customTone?, flaggedPhrases?)` that layers a tone sentence and an optional "avoid these phrases" clause (fed from the detector's own `matchedPhrases`) onto the same base prompt.
- **`autoHumanizerSettings`** — new single-global-row table (migration `0071_rich_monster_badoon.sql`), same shape/conventions as `humanizerSettings`. New `"auto_humanizer"` `FeatureKey` (own Settings card, own Feature Endpoints row — `FeatureEndpointsCard.tsx` needed zero code changes, it iterates `FEATURE_KEYS` generically).
- **`aiTextDetector.ts`** — pure-TS five-signal port of the altra/humanize heuristic family (flagged-phrase density 30%, sentence burstiness 25%, transition-word density 20%, paragraph symmetry 15%, average-sentence-length band 10%), 0–100 score, four verdict bands. No project test runner exists (checked — none outside `node_modules`), so verified via a disposable `tsx` script (this project's own established convention) rather than adding a test framework: a hand-written AI-flavored paragraph scored 73 ("likely_ai", 13 flagged phrases correctly matched), a clean narrative paragraph scored 0 ("human").
- **`autoHumanizerService.ts`'s `processAutoHumanize`** — the gate + rewrite core, "process owns gate server-side" (decision #17): disabled → skip; below `minChars` → skip; `mode: "threshold"` runs the detector and skips below `aiScoreThreshold`; `mode: "always"` skips the detector entirely and always rewrites. Always resolves (never throws) — a rewrite failure degrades to `{success:false, text: original, message}`, the caller's contract is to always use `result.text` and only toast when `success` is false.
- **A real, live-caught bug in the three-tier AI-connection fallback (decision #16: `auto_humanizer` override → `humanizer` override → global default → degrade)**: the naive implementation (`buildClientForFeature("auto_humanizer") ?? buildClientForFeature("humanizer") ?? buildClientForFeature()`) never actually reached the `humanizer` tier, because `buildClientForFeature(featureKey)` *always* falls through to the global default internally when that key has no override — it only returns `null` when the global default is also unconfigured. So an `auto_humanizer` call with no override "succeeded" via the (possibly broken) global default before the chain ever got a chance to try `humanizer`'s own override. Caught live: this session's global default pointed at an unreachable local endpoint while `humanizer` had a working `grok-oauth` override configured — `/api/humanizer/rewrite` worked, `/api/auto-humanizer/process` failed with the same underlying settings row. Fixed by adding `buildClientForFeatureOverrideOnly(featureKey)` to `aiClientFactory.ts` (returns `null` unless that exact feature has its own override — no global fallback baked in) and using it for the first two tiers, reserving the real `buildClientForFeature()` (global fallback included) for the final tier only. Re-verified after the fix: the same request correctly cascaded to `humanizer`'s working override and returned a real rewrite.
- **AH4 — Accept wired through `/process`, with a critical scope guard**: `ChatInterface.tsx`'s `applyProseProposal` (the shared choke point for both manual Accept and the auto-insert toggle path) also handles Selection-Rework Accept via its `proposal.target` branch — the design's decision #10 locks Rework Accept **out of scope** (before/after must stay visible), so the humanize call is gated on `!proposal.target` specifically, never firing for a rework replace. Converted `applyProseProposal` and both its callers (`handleAcceptProse`, the `onProseProposal` auto-insert branch) to `async`/`await` — confirmed safe first: `onProseProposal` is called fire-and-forget by `useChatMessageGeneration.ts` (never awaited), and `handleAcceptProse` is a plain `onClick` handler, so neither caller depended on a synchronous return. `ProseProposalCard.tsx` gained an `isBusy` prop (spinner + "Humanizing…", both buttons disabled) driven by a new `humanizingMessageId` state, only ever set for the non-rework path.
- **AH8 — Editor Humanize sheet**: new `"humanize"` `DrawerType`/`sidebarButtons` entry (`Wand2` icon) in `EditorToolsPanel.tsx`, always visible like Tags/Beats/Scanner (not gated on a selected chat). New `EditorHumanizeSheet.tsx` hosts both sections, bound to the exact same query/mutation hooks the Settings cards use (confirmed live: toggling Auto Humanizer's enable switch in Settings and reopening the sheet showed it already checked, no second write path). Section A's "Humanize selection" reuses `useHumanizeMutation`/`/api/humanizer/rewrite` verbatim — the **same** endpoint the floating toolbar calls, not a reimplementation — the only new piece is sourcing the Lexical editor via `getActiveChapterEditor(chapterId)` instead of `useLexicalComposerContext()`, since `EditorToolsPanel`'s sheets are siblings of the editor, not descendants (confirmed via `activeChapterEditorStore.ts`'s own doc comment, same bridge `chapterSelectionAdapter.ts` already uses for Rework). New `useActiveChapterSelection.ts` hook subscribes to the chapter's live editor via `registerUpdateListener` to reactively gate the button on "is there currently a non-collapsed selection," since a side-panel button (unlike the floating toolbar, which only ever appears *while* a selection exists) needs to track that itself.
- **`AutoHumanizerFields.tsx` extracted as a pure form component** specifically so the Settings card and the sheet's Section B render identically off identical hooks (design's explicit "no duplicate persistence" mandate) — Manual's two fields (toggle + intensity) were simple enough to leave inline in both places without extracting.
- **AH7 — Selection-Generate hook**: `FloatingTextFormatToolbarPlugin/index.tsx`'s `handleGenerateWithPrompt` now runs the streamed `fullText` through `/process` (if Auto Humanizer is enabled) before insertion — the **one** commit surface where Auto Humanizer deliberately *keeps* the italic marking (decision #2b), unlike Accept-prose and manual Humanize which are both never italic. Skip/fail falls through to today's raw-insert-with-italic behavior unchanged.
- **Live-verified end to end against the real dev DB**, with a real reachable provider this session (unlike most prior AI-feature sessions in this project's history): Settings card and Feature Endpoints row both render and persist across reload; Editor Humanize sheet opens with both sections, state matches Settings; a real chapter selection made the reactive "Humanize selection" button go from disabled+hint to enabled, and clicking it replaced the selection in place with a real rewrite (no italic) — confirmed via before/after DOM inspection, then reverted (the demo chapter's real text was touched during this test and restored verbatim via a direct API round-trip afterward); `/api/auto-humanizer/process` exercised directly for every gate branch — real AI-ish text (score 76) → real rewrite; short text → skipped, no LLM call; low-score human-ish text → skipped; `mode: "always"` → the same low-score text rewritten anyway, bypassing the detector entirely; disabled → skipped server-side too (defense-in-depth per decision #17) and confirmed zero `/process` calls fire client-side when disabled (the `applyProseProposal` gate check). Degrade path forced live (a temporary broken `auto_humanizer` Feature Endpoint override) and confirmed `{success:false, text: <original>, message: "Connection error."}` — exactly the contract AH4's caller relies on. All test overrides/settings reset back to clean defaults (`auto_humanizer` override deleted, `enabled:false`, `mode:"threshold"`) afterward. `npm run build` (tsc × 2 + vite) clean throughout every iteration, including after the fallback-chain fix.

## Sexuality Playbook (SX0–SX9) (2026-08-15)

Promoted from the locked design (`docs/Sexuality_Playbook_Design.md`) directly on user request — the last remaining locked-not-built design, closing that backlog bucket out entirely. An exact sibling of the shipped Character psych module: a dedicated `character_sexuality`/`any` playbook pack plus a structured `entry.metadata.sexualityProfile` field, proposed via a `sexuality-proposal` fence and accepted the same propose→accept way `psych-proposal` is — writing aid only, never Codex state. Built by tracing psych's implementation end-to-end (two parallel Explore agents, server-side and client-side) and replicating each piece deliberately, per the design doc's own repeated "mirror psych, don't reinvent" framing.

- **Content, upgraded mid-build**: `CHARACTER_SEXUALITY_ANY` (`src/data/playbookPackContent.ts`) was first drafted generically, then rewritten in full once the user supplied a real NSFW character-creation questionnaire mid-session (`G:\comfy\NFSW character creation questions.md`) — same "adapt real user-supplied material" precedent as PP6's starter playbook content. Restructured into the conversational interview-cue format (`##` sections, follow-up-style bullets) matching `CHARACTER_PSYCH_ANY`'s tone: orientation & identity, preferences (explicit, matching the source document's actual specificity rather than euphemism), kinks & fetishes (age play, pet play, medical play, sensory/impact/sensation play, exhibitionism — all from the source doc), experience & fantasies, hard limits (explicitly safety-framed, per design decision #5 — the one field with no psych analog), and a closing "how it reads differently up close" section tying back to already-established Codex/appearance facts without becoming Codex state itself.
- **Schema/types**: `PlaybookKey` gains `"character_sexuality"` (`src/types/playbookPack.ts`); `LorebookEntry.metadata.sexualityProfile: {orientation?, dynamic?, kinks?, limits?, blurb?}` (`src/types/story.ts`); new `aiChats.includeSexualityModule` boolean column (migration `0072_volatile_epoch.sql`), same shape/default as `includePsychModule`; `ChatContext.playbookPack` gains a third `sexuality` key (`src/types/worldbuilding.ts`).
- **`buildSystemPrompt` restructured from early-return to accumulator** (`chatContextService.ts`) — the pre-existing `character_codex` branch was a single `if (includePsychModule) return ...`; a second competing `if (includeSexualityModule) return ...` would have silently meant only whichever check ran first ever took effect when both modules are armed on the same chat. Fixed by building a `moduleAddendum` string that conditionally appends `PSYCH_MODULE_INSTRUCTIONS` and/or `SEXUALITY_MODULE_INSTRUCTIONS`, then one `character_codex` return using it — the one deliberate structural change in this feature, everything else additive. Live-verified both fences work in the same conversation.
- **Playbook-pack resolution mirrors psych's exactly**: `usePlaybookPack && includeSexualityModule ? resolvePlaybookPackContext(chat.storyId, "character_sexuality", "any") : Promise.resolve(null)`, a third parallel `Promise.all` entry, piggybacking on the same `usePlaybookPack` arm toggle psych's own lane uses (not a separate arm toggle) — same generic `resolvePlaybookPackContext(storyId, playbookKey, style)` function, no new resolution logic needed.
- **A real bug caught live, missed by both research agents' static tracing**: `server/services/chatRepository.ts` has its own separate `ChatUpdateFields` interface and its own explicit field-by-field allowlist (`if (fields.x !== undefined) updates.x = fields.x`), independent from `routes/chats.ts`'s `metaFields` object — a **third** touchpoint layer beyond the route's destructure/DTO-type/conditional-assign that neither exploration pass surfaced (both correctly found and mirrored the psych pattern in `schema.ts` and `chats.ts`, but `chatRepository.ts` sits between them and wasn't in either agent's search scope). Without `includeSexualityModule` added there too, `PATCH /api/chats/:id {includeSexualityModule: true}` silently dropped the field before it ever reached the DB write — confirmed live: the PATCH returned `200` with the field still `false`, the "Add sexuality prompt" button's own arm-on-click never actually took effect. Fixed with the same two-line pattern `chatRepository.ts` already had for `includePsychModule` (add to the `ChatUpdateFields` type, add one `if (fields.includeSexualityModule !== undefined) updates.includeSexualityModule = ...` line). Re-verified after the fix: the same PATCH correctly returned `true`.
- **Guided Setup button**: `handleAddSexualityPrompt` (`LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`) is a straight copy of `handleAddPsychPrompt` — seeds the composer with `SEXUALITY_PROMPT_TEXT`, PATCHes `includeSexualityModule: true` if not already armed. New "Add sexuality prompt" button sits next to "Add psych prompt" in the same `isCharacterTemplate`-gated Guided Setup panel. Deliberately **not** wired into `handleStyleChange`'s Grill-me auto-nudge (psych's own auto-arm-on-grill behavior) — confirmed live: setting `wbStyle: "grill"` + `includePsychModule: true` together left `includeSexualityModule` untouched at `false`.
- **Live-verified end to end against the real dev DB** with a real reachable provider: shipped pack seeded correctly on boot (5 packs total, up from 4); real chat turn on Marcus Webb (`lore-marcus-webb`) produced a genuine `sexuality-proposal` fence (orientation: bisexual, dynamic: leans dominant, kinks: knife play in trusted scenes only, plus a real blurb) matching the new pack content's depth and specificity; `SexualityProposalCard` rendered all fields correctly; Accept merged into the entry's real `metadata.sexualityProfile` via the existing generic `PUT /api/lorebook/:id`; `SexualityProfilePanel` rendered it read-only after reload, positioned beside `PsychProfilePanel`; both psych + sexuality instruction blocks confirmed present simultaneously in one chat's assembled `GET .../context` system prompt; the `character_sexuality` playbook-pack lane resolved real shipped body content once `usePlaybookPack` was on. All test artifacts reverted afterward: the 4 chat messages added during testing truncated back off, the entry's test `sexualityProfile` removed, `includeSexualityModule`/`includePsychModule`/`usePlaybookPack`/`wbStyle` all reset to their pre-test values. `npm run build` (tsc × 2 + vite) clean throughout, including after the `chatRepository.ts` fix.

## Glass Orchard QA Pass — Bugfix Round (B13–B18) (2026-08-15)

Fixed the 6 `[ERROR]`s from `docs/2026-08-15_Glass_Orchard_QA_Pass.md` (first full end-to-end pass against a real reachable model, Grok 4.5) — see that doc for the original repro steps and `docs/CURRENT_BACKLOG.md`'s P2 table (B13–B21) for the tracked entries. Friction items B19–B21 recorded in the backlog but not fixed this round.

- **B13 — live chat ignored Feature Routing.** `resolveChatDefaultModel.ts`'s `computeModeDefaultModelId`/`CLOUD_PRIORITY` fallback never consulted `aiSettings.featureEndpoints` at all (job-queue calls already did, via `aiClientFactory.ts`'s `buildClientForFeature`). Fixed by threading a new `featureOverrideModelId` param through `resolveChatDefaultModel` (checked after `lastUsedModelId`, before the mode-based fallback) and wiring it from `useChatSystemPrompt.ts` via the pre-existing `useFeatureEndpointsQuery()` — no new query or client-type change needed, that hook already existed for `FeatureEndpointsCard.tsx`. Only `promptType: "worldbuilding"`/`"editor"` map to a `FeatureKey` (`worldbuilding_chat`/`editor_chat`); Brainstorm/Outline/Notes/Research have no Feature Routing row and fall through unchanged. **Known residual limitation, out of scope for this fix**: a Feature Routing override with a *different* apiKey/apiUrl than the global connection for that provider (not the QA repro's own scenario, which just pointed at an already-globally-configured Grok) still won't route the actual live-chat *send* through that custom endpoint — only job-queue calls go through `buildClientForFeature` server-side. Client-side model *selection* now respects Feature Routing; actual per-request custom-endpoint routing for live chat would be a larger follow-up.
- **B14 — dead model hung silently, no visible error.** Two real bugs in `streamUtils.ts`/`useStreamingGeneration.ts`: (1) `processStreamedResponse` silently dropped any SSE line that failed `JSON.parse` — exactly what a mid-stream 504/gateway-timeout page looks like — continuing the read loop and eventually calling `onComplete()` as if generation had finished normally. Now calls `onError` immediately instead. (2) `useStreamingGeneration.ts`'s `!response.ok` branch discarded the actual HTTP status; now surfaces `"Generation timed out (504 Gateway Timeout)..."` specifically for 504, `"Failed to generate response (HTTP <status>)"` otherwise. Both toast call sites (`useStreamingGeneration.ts`, `useChatMessageGeneration.ts`) now show `error.message` instead of a hardcoded generic string.
- **B15 — Brainstorm's `overview-proposal`/`handoff-packet` fences almost never fired against a real model.** No code defect (wiring/regex/gating all traced clean) — the instruction wording itself (`chatContextService.ts`) was soft/conditional ("as the overview takes shape, propose capturing it") next to other fences in the same file using hard imperatives with concrete triggers. Rewrote both to "the instant X, you MUST emit the fence," added explicit trigger phrases ("save that", "lock it in", "formally propose this") and one worked example turn each, matching `NAME_PROPOSAL_INSTRUCTIONS`'s existing style.
- **B16 — Humanize selection could merge two words with no space.** `humanizerService.ts` blind-`.trim()`ed the model's reply; if the original selection had a boundary space (common with double-click/drag selection), the client's `insertText()` replaces the *whole* original range including that space, but the trimmed reply never restored it. Fixed by capturing the original selection's leading/trailing whitespace before the AI call and re-appending it to the trimmed output — fixes every caller (floating toolbar + Editor Humanize sheet) from one place.
- **B17 — MultiView "Split right" reportedly mislabeled a pane's tab.** Could not reproduce the exact repro cleanly in a follow-up live session — a first attempt via `computer`-tool coordinate clicks *appeared* to reproduce it (chapter flipped unexpectedly, no split occurred), but was traced to the ChapterSwitcher's Radix dropdown content staying `display: block`/hit-testable while `data-state="closed"` (a real minor CSS quirk, not investigated further — not one of the 6 tracked errors), intercepting the coordinate click. Retested with real DOM-dispatched clicks (`element.click()`, no coordinate ambiguity): switch to Chapter 2 → Split right → both panes correctly showed/labeled Chapter 2, no desync. Did find and fix a real, related, code-confirmed defect while investigating: `EditorLayoutContext.tsx`'s `addTab`/`closeTab`/`closePane` can all reassign which tab is "active" in a pane as a side effect (closing the active tab falls back to the last remaining one; closing the active pane hands off to the first surviving one; adding a tab always activates it) without ever calling `StoryContext.setCurrentChapterId` — only the click-driven paths (`TabButton.activate`, `EditorPane.focusPane`) did that sync. This is the confirmed root cause of the QA pass's separate **"Adding a chapter tab doesn't focus it"** friction finding (`addTab` via `AddTabMenu.tsx` updated the pane's `activeTabId` correctly but left the TopBar breadcrumb/download button etc. pointing at the old chapter). Fixed with a new `syncCurrentChapterFromPane` helper called from all three mutators whenever they affect the *active* pane. Live-verified the close-tab path directly (closed a non-active tab → no spurious chapter change, as expected). **B17 downgraded in the backlog to "fixed the confirmed related defect; original repro not reproduced" rather than closed as literally described** — if it recurs, get a screenshot/video from the reporter rather than coordinate-based automation, given the click-interception artifact found here.
- **B18 — EPUB export produced quiet repeated console errors.** `LoadChapterContent/index.tsx` called `editor.parseEditorState(currentChapter.content)` unconditionally; for a chapter with `content === ""` (a legitimate, expected state — not corruption) `JSON.parse("")` throws `"Unexpected end of JSON input"`, caught and recovered via the existing blank-doc fallback (no data loss, confirmed by the QA pass itself) but logged as a hard error every time — and EPUB export apparently reloads several idle background MultiView tabs, each hitting this once. Fixed by special-casing empty content *before* attempting to parse, building the blank doc directly with no `logger.error` call; genuinely malformed (non-empty, unparseable) content still hits the original error-logged recovery path unchanged.
- **Verification**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after every change. B13/B15/B16/B18 fixes are logic-level and narrowly scoped (type-checked, not independently live-exercised against a real model this round — Feature Routing/Brainstorm-fence/Humanizer-whitespace/empty-chapter behavior should be spot-checked against Grok next time any of those surfaces are touched). B17's investigation and its fix's non-regression (single close-tab path) were live-verified against the real "Glass Orchard" test story in the dev DB via direct DOM inspection (Browser pane screenshots were unavailable in this session's environment — the pane never composited frames — so verification relied on `read_page`/`get_page_text`/`javascript_tool` DOM queries instead of visual confirmation).

## Glass Orchard QA Pass — Friction Round (B19–B21) (2026-08-15, same-day follow-up)

Fixed the 3 `[FRICTION]` items left open from the bugfix round above, on direct user request right after it shipped. Same dev DB/test story, same DOM-query verification method (no Browser-pane screenshots available this session).

- **B19 — no bulk-accept for AI-suggested outline items; "stale" reject-all counter.** The counter half **did not reproduce** — `groupOutlineItems.ts`'s `pendingCount` is purely derived from the live `useOutlineQuery` result, and `useUpdateOutlineItemMutation`'s `onSuccess` already invalidates that exact query key; live-tested by accepting a real pending scene one at a time and watching "Reject All Suggestions (N)" correctly decrement 3→2 each time. Only the missing bulk-accept was real: added `POST /outline/story/:storyId/accept-all-pending` (mirrors `reject-all-pending` exactly, `status: "pending" → "confirmed"`, plus re-syncs each newly-confirmed row's RAG index entry via the existing `syncOutlineItemIndex`, since a bulk `UPDATE` can't go through the per-row hook the individual `PUT /:id` route uses), `outlineApi.acceptAllPending`, `useAcceptAllPendingOutlineMutation`, and an "Accept All Suggestions (N)" button next to the existing "Reject All" in `OutlinePage.tsx`. Live-verified against the real "Glass Orchard" outline (had 3 real pending AI-suggested scenes left over from the earlier bug-fix round's own testing): clicked Accept All, confirmed via direct API call that all 3 flipped to `confirmed` and the buttons correctly disappeared (pendingCount → 0).
- **B20 — new Lorebook entries didn't open their own tab after creation.** Root cause: `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`'s `handleSubmit` only called `onEntryCreated` (the callback that promotes a tab from draft to a real entry — pre-existing, but previously wired only to the lazy WB-chat-anchor path via `ensureLiveEntry`) for that one path; the ordinary Create-button submission never called it at all, so a new entry always finished mid-air with no tab of its own. Fixed by also calling `onEntryCreated` (and `setLiveEntry`) from the real create branch of `handleSubmit`. **Caught and fixed a second bug this surfaced live**: `LorebookPage.tsx`'s `onSaved={() => closeTab(activeTabIndex)}` for a "new" draft tab — previously harmless since nothing ever promoted that tab — now raced against the newly-firing `onEntryCreated`'s tab-promotion `setOpenTabs` call and closed the tab immediately after it was promoted to the real entry, landing the user right back where they started (confirmed live: `POST /api/lorebook` returned `201`, entry existed, but no tab appeared). Fixed by making `onSaved` a no-op for the "new" tab case (success is now `onEntryCreated`'s job; `onCancel` still closes for an explicit cancel). Also threaded `onEntryCreated` through `CreateEntryDialog` (the Sheet-based path used for chat/document-import handoffs) for the same open-on-create consistency. Live-verified end to end: created a real entry via the New Entry tab, confirmed its own tab opened and stayed open (not Browse); test entries deleted afterward via direct API calls.
- **B21 — long AI generations showed a static "Generating…" with no progress signal.** Found a bigger gap than the QA report described while investigating: `ChatMessageList.tsx`'s streaming bubble (`useChatMessages.ts` already merges live `streamingContent` into the placeholder message) was *unconditionally* rendered as a bare spinner for the entire generation — the real streamed text was already flowing into `message.content` the whole time but was never shown, not just missing a timer. Fixed by rendering the live content (via the existing `AssistantMessageContent`) with a small trailing spinner + "Generating…" once any text has arrived, falling back to the original spinner (now with an elapsed-seconds counter — `GeneratingIndicator`, a small extracted component so its 1s interval only runs while an actual streaming bubble is mounted) only for the pre-first-token gap. A Stop/cancel button already existed in the composer (`MessageInputArea.tsx`'s `onStop`/`abort`, wired but apparently not what the QA report meant by "no cancel button") — not duplicated near the message bubble. Live-verified against a real reachable model (Grok, same story): sent a message that produces a long reply, polled mid-generation and confirmed the growing streamed text ("...launch into climax. **Ch. 9...") rendering live under the "Generating…" spinner rather than a static placeholder — incidentally also re-confirming B13's chat-model-routing fix, since this same live session's chat correctly defaulted to Grok with no manual model switch needed.
- **Verification**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after every change (client + server). All three items live-verified against the real dev DB / "Glass Orchard" test story this same session, via `javascript_tool` DOM/API queries (no Browser-pane screenshots available).
